Ayaṃ kho, brāhmaṇa, pariyāyo, yena maṃ pariyāyena sammā vadamāno vadeyya – ‘apagabbho samaṇo gotamo’ti, no ca kho yaṃ tvaṃ sandhāya vadesi.

“The venerable Gotama does not return to a womb!” “There is indeed, brahmin, a way in which one speaking rightly might say of me: ‘The recluse Gotama does not return to a womb.’ For, brahmin, one for whom future lying in a womb and rebirth into a new existence has been abandoned, cut off at the root, made like a palm-stump, brought to non-existence, and is of a nature not to arise in the future—him I call ‘one who does not return to a womb.’ For the Tathāgata, brahmin, future lying in a womb and rebirth into a new existence has been abandoned, cut off at the root, made like a palm-stump, brought to non-existence, and is of a nature not to arise in the future. This, brahmin, is the way in which one speaking rightly might say of me: ‘The recluse Gotama does not return to a womb,’ but it is not in the sense that you mean.”

“Seyyathāpi, brāhmaṇa, kukkuṭiyā aṇḍāni aṭṭha vā dasa vā dvādasa vā. Tānāssu kukkuṭiyā sammā adhisayitāni sammā pariseditāni sammā paribhāvitāni. Yo nu kho tesaṃ kukkuṭacchāpakānaṃ paṭhamataraṃ pādanakhasikhāya vā mukhatuṇḍakena vā aṇḍakosaṃ padāletvā sotthinā abhinibbhijjeyya, kinti svāssa vacanīyo – ‘jeṭṭho vā kaniṭṭho vā’”ti? “Jeṭṭho tissa, bho gotama, vacanīyo. So hi nesaṃ, bho gotama, jeṭṭho hotī”ti.

“Suppose, brahmin, a hen has eight, ten, or twelve eggs, which she has properly sat on, properly warmed, and properly incubated. Now, among those chicks, whichever one first breaks through the eggshell with its claw-tip or beak and hatches safely—what should it be called: the eldest or the youngest?” “It should be called the eldest, venerable Gotama. For it is the eldest among them, venerable Gotama.”

“Evamevaṃ kho ahaṃ, brāhmaṇa, avijjāgatāya pajāya aṇḍabhūtāya pariyonaddhāya avijjaṇḍakosaṃ padāletvā ekova loke anuttaraṃ sammāsambodhiṃ abhisambuddho. Ahañhi, brāhmaṇa[V 3.0023], jeṭṭho seṭṭho lokassa. Āraddhaṃ kho pana me, brāhmaṇa, vīriyaṃ ahosi asallīnaṃ, upaṭṭhitā sati asammuṭṭhā, passaddho kāyo asāraddho, samāhitaṃ cittaṃ ekaggaṃ.

“In the same way, brahmin, amidst this generation that is in ignorance, that has become like an egg, that is enveloped, I, having broken open the eggshell of ignorance, am the one alone in the world who has fully awakened to the unsurpassed perfect enlightenment. For I, brahmin, am the eldest, the foremost in the world. And, brahmin, my energy was aroused and unremitting, my mindfulness was established and unconfused, my body was tranquil and unagitated, my mind was concentrated and one-pointed.”

“So [M 3.0022] kho ahaṃ, brāhmaṇa, vivicceva kāmehi vivicca akusalehi dhammehi savitakkaṃ savicāraṃ vivekajaṃ pītisukhaṃ paṭhamaṃ jhānaṃ upasampajja viharāmi; vitakkavicārānaṃ vūpasamā ajjhattaṃ sampasādanaṃ cetaso ekodibhāvaṃ avitakkaṃ avicāraṃ samādhijaṃ pītisukhaṃ dutiyaṃ [P 4.0177] jhānaṃ upasampajja viharāmi; pītiyā ca virāgā upekkhako ca viharāmi sato ca sampajāno sukhañca kāyena [T 4.0179] paṭisaṃvedemi yaṃ taṃ ariyā ācikkhanti – ‘upekkhako satimā sukhavihārī’ti tatiyaṃ jhānaṃ upasampajja viharāmi; sukhassa ca pahānā dukkhassa ca pahānā pubbeva somanassadomanassānaṃ atthaṅgamā adukkhamasukhaṃ upekkhāsatipārisuddhiṃ catutthaṃ jhānaṃ upasampajja viharāmi.

“Then I, brahmin, secluded from sensual pleasures, secluded from unwholesome states, entered and dwelled in the first jhāna, which is accompanied by applied and sustained thought, with rapture and happiness born of seclusion. With the stilling of applied and sustained thought, I entered and dwelled in the second jhāna, which has internal serenity and unification of mind, without applied and sustained thought, with rapture and happiness born of concentration. With the fading away of rapture, I dwelled equanimous, mindful, and clearly comprehending, and I experienced happiness with the body—that of which the noble ones declare, ‘Equanimous and mindful, one dwells in happiness’—and so I entered and dwelled in the third jhāna. With the abandoning of pleasure and pain, and with the earlier disappearance of joy and sorrow, I entered and dwelled in the fourth jhāna, which is neither-painful-nor-pleasant and has purity of mindfulness due to equanimity.”

“So evaṃ samāhite citte parisuddhe pariyodāte anaṅgaṇe vigatūpakkilese mudubhūte kammaniye ṭhite āneñjappatte pubbenivāsānussatiñāṇāya cittaṃ abhininnāmesiṃ. So anekavihitaṃ pubbenivāsaṃ anussarāmi, seyyathidaṃ – ekampi jātiṃ dvepi jātiyo tissopi jātiyo catassopi jātiyo pañcapi jātiyo dasapi jātiyo vīsampi jātiyo tiṃsampi jātiyo cattālīsampi jātiyo paññāsampi jātiyo jātisatampi jātisahassampi jātisatasahassampi anekepi saṃvaṭṭakappe anekepi vivaṭṭakappe anekepi saṃvaṭṭavivaṭṭakappe – ‘amutrāsiṃ evaṃnāmo evaṃgotto evaṃvaṇṇo evamāhāro evaṃsukhadukkhappaṭisaṃvedī evamāyupariyanto. So tato cuto amutra udapādiṃ; tatrāpāsiṃ evaṃnāmo evaṃgotto evaṃvaṇṇo evamāhāro evaṃsukhadukkhappaṭisaṃvedī evamāyupariyanto. So tato cuto idhūpapanno’ti. Iti sākāraṃ sauddesaṃ anekavihitaṃ pubbenivāsaṃ anussarāmi.

“With my mind thus concentrated, purified, bright, unblemished, rid of imperfections, malleable, workable, steady, and attained to imperturbability, I inclined and directed it to the knowledge of the recollection of past lives. I recollected my manifold past lives, that is: one birth, two births, three births, four births, five births, ten births, twenty births, thirty births, forty births, fifty births, a hundred births, a thousand births, a hundred thousand births, many eons of world-contraction, many eons of world-expansion, many eons of world-contraction and expansion. ‘There I was of such a name, such a clan, such an appearance, such was my food, such my experience of pleasure and pain, such my lifespan. Passing away from there, I was reborn there. There too I was of such a name, such a clan, such an appearance, such was my food, such my experience of pleasure and pain, such my lifespan. Passing away from there, I was reborn here.’ Thus I recollected my manifold past lives with their aspects and details.”

“Ayaṃ kho me, brāhmaṇa, rattiyā paṭhame yāme paṭhamā vijjā adhigatā; avijjā vihatā vijjā uppannā; tamo vihato āloko uppanno, yathā taṃ appamattassa ātāpino pahitattassa viharato. Ayaṃ kho me, brāhmaṇa, paṭhamā abhinibbhidā ahosi kukkuṭacchāpakasseva [T 4.0180] aṇḍakosamhā.

“This, brahmin, was the first knowledge attained by me in the first watch of the night. Ignorance was dispelled, knowledge arose; darkness was dispelled, light arose—as happens in one who dwells heedful, ardent, and resolute. This, brahmin, was my first breaking through, like a chick breaking out of its eggshell.”

“So [V 3.0024][P 4.0178] evaṃ samāhite citte parisuddhe pariyodāte anaṅgaṇe vigatūpakkilese mudubhūte kammaniye ṭhite āneñjappatte sattānaṃ cutūpapātañāṇāya cittaṃ abhininnāmesiṃ. So dibbena cakkhunā visuddhena atikkantamānusakena satte passāmi cavamāne upapajjamāne hīne paṇīte [M 3.0023] suvaṇṇe dubbaṇṇe, sugate duggate yathākammūpage satte pajānāmi – ‘ime vata bhonto sattā kāyaduccaritena samannāgatā, vacīduccaritena samannāgatā, manoduccaritena samannāgatā, ariyānaṃ upavādakā, micchādiṭṭhikā, micchādiṭṭhikammasamādānā. Te kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā apāyaṃ duggatiṃ vinipātaṃ nirayaṃ upapannāti. Ime vā pana bhonto sattā kāyasucaritena samannāgatā, vacīsucaritena samannāgatā, manosucaritena samannāgatā, ariyānaṃ anupavādakā, sammādiṭṭhikā, sammādiṭṭhikammasamādānā. Te kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā sugatiṃ saggaṃ lokaṃ upapannā’ti. Iti dibbena cakkhunā visuddhena atikkantamānusakena satte passāmi cavamāne upapajjamāne hīne paṇīte suvaṇṇe dubbaṇṇe, sugate duggate yathākammūpage satte pajānāmi.

“With my mind thus concentrated, purified, bright, unblemished, rid of imperfections, malleable, workable, steady, and attained to imperturbability, I inclined my mind to the knowledge of the passing away and reappearance of beings. With the divine eye, which is purified and surpasses the human, I see beings passing away and reappearing—inferior and superior, beautiful and ugly, in a good destination and a bad destination. I know beings who fare according to their kamma: ‘Indeed, these venerable beings were endowed with misconduct of body, speech, and mind, were revilers of the noble ones, held wrong view, and undertook action based on wrong view. At the breakup of the body, after death, they have been reborn in a state of deprivation, a bad destination, a place of downfall, in hell.’ Or: ‘Indeed, these venerable beings were endowed with good conduct of body, speech, and mind, were not revilers of the noble ones, held right view, and undertook action based on right view. At the breakup of the body, after death, they have been reborn in a good destination, in the heavenly world.’ Thus, with the divine eye, which is purified and surpasses the human, I see beings passing away and reappearing—inferior and superior, beautiful and ugly, in a good destination and a bad destination. I know beings who fare according to their kamma.”

“Ayaṃ kho me, brāhmaṇa, rattiyā majjhime yāme dutiyā vijjā adhigatā; avijjā vihatā vijjā uppannā; tamo vihato āloko uppanno, yathā taṃ appamattassa ātāpino pahitattassa viharato. Ayaṃ kho me, brāhmaṇa, dutiyā abhinibbhidā ahosi kukkuṭacchāpakasseva aṇḍakosamhā.

“This, brahmin, was the second knowledge attained by me in the middle watch of the night. Ignorance was dispelled, knowledge arose; darkness was dispelled, light arose, as it does for one who dwells heedful, ardent, and resolute. This, brahmin, was my second breakthrough, like that of a chick from its eggshell.”

“So evaṃ samāhite citte parisuddhe pariyodāte anaṅgaṇe [T 4.0181] vigatūpakkilese mudubhūte kammaniye ṭhite āneñjappatte āsavānaṃ khayañāṇāya cittaṃ abhininnāmesiṃ. So ‘idaṃ dukkha’nti yathābhūtaṃ abbhaññāsiṃ, ‘ayaṃ dukkhasamudayo’ti yathābhūtaṃ abbhaññāsiṃ, ‘ayaṃ dukkhanirodho’ti yathābhūtaṃ abbhaññāsiṃ, ‘ayaṃ dukkhanirodhagāminī paṭipadā’ti yathābhūtaṃ abbhaññāsiṃ; ‘ime āsavā’ti yathābhūtaṃ abbhaññāsiṃ, ‘ayaṃ [P 4.0179] āsavasamudayo’ti yathābhūtaṃ abbhaññāsiṃ, ‘ayaṃ āsavanirodho’ti yathābhūtaṃ abbhaññāsiṃ, ‘ayaṃ āsavanirodhagāminī paṭipadā’ti yathābhūtaṃ abbhaññāsiṃ. Tassa me evaṃ jānato evaṃ passato kāmāsavāpi cittaṃ vimuccittha, bhavāsavāpi cittaṃ vimuccittha, avijjāsavāpi cittaṃ vimuccittha. Vimuttasmiṃ vimuttamiti ñāṇaṃ ahosi. ‘Khīṇā jāti, vusitaṃ brahmacariyaṃ, kataṃ karaṇīyaṃ, nāparaṃ itthattāyā’ti abbhaññāsiṃ.

When my mind was thus concentrated, purified, bright, unblemished, rid of defilements, malleable, workable, steady, and had attained to imperturbability, I inclined my mind to the knowledge of the destruction of the taints. I knew with profound knowledge, in accordance with reality: ‘This is suffering.’ I knew with profound knowledge, in accordance with reality: ‘This is the origin of suffering.’ I knew with profound knowledge, in accordance with reality: ‘This is the cessation of suffering.’ I knew with profound knowledge, in accordance with reality: ‘This is the path leading to the cessation of suffering.’ I knew with profound knowledge, in accordance with reality: ‘These are the taints.’ I knew with profound knowledge, in accordance with reality: ‘This is the origin of the taints.’ I knew with profound knowledge, in accordance with reality: ‘This is the cessation of the taints.’ I knew with profound knowledge, in accordance with reality: ‘This is the path leading to the cessation of the taints.’ For me, thus knowing and thus seeing, my mind was liberated from the taint of sensual desire, my mind was liberated from the taint of existence, and my mind was liberated from the taint of ignorance. In the liberated, the knowledge arose: ‘It is liberated.’ I knew with profound knowledge: ‘Birth is destroyed, the noble life has been lived, what had to be done has been done, there is nothing further for this state of being.’

“Ayaṃ [V 3.0025] kho me, brāhmaṇa, rattiyā pacchime yāme tatiyā vijjā adhigatā; avijjā vihatā vijjā uppannā; tamo vihato āloko uppanno[M 3.0024], yathā taṃ appamattassa ātāpino pahitattassa viharato. Ayaṃ kho me, brāhmaṇa, tatiyā abhinibbhidā ahosi kukkuṭacchāpakasseva aṇḍakosamhā”ti.

Indeed, Brahmin, this was the third knowledge attained by me in the last watch of the night. Ignorance was dispelled, knowledge arose; darkness was dispelled, light arose, as it does for one who dwells heedful, ardent, and resolute. Indeed, Brahmin, this was my third breakthrough, like that of a chick from its eggshell.

Evaṃ vutte verañjo brāhmaṇo bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “jeṭṭho bhavaṃ gotamo, seṭṭho bhavaṃ gotamo. Abhikkantaṃ, bho gotama, abhikkantaṃ, bho gotama! Seyyathāpi, bho gotama, nikkujjitaṃ nikujjitaṃ (ka.) vā ukkujjeyya, paṭicchannaṃ vā vivareyya, mūḷhassa vā maggaṃ ācikkheyya, andhakāre vā telapajjotaṃ dhāreyya – cakkhumanto rūpāni dakkhantīti; evamevaṃ bhotā gotamena [T 4.0182] anekapariyāyena dhammo pakāsito. Esāhaṃ bhavantaṃ gotamaṃ saraṇaṃ gacchāmi dhammañca bhikkhusaṅghañca. Upāsakaṃ maṃ bhavaṃ gotamo dhāretu ajjatagge pāṇupetaṃ saraṇaṃ gata”nti. Paṭhamaṃ.

When this was said, the brahmin Verañja said this to the Blessed One: “The Venerable Gotama is the eldest! The Venerable Gotama is the best! Excellent, Venerable Gotama! Excellent, Venerable Gotama! Just as one might set upright what was overturned, or reveal what was concealed, or point out the way to one who was lost, or hold up an oil lamp in the darkness so that those with eyes might see forms; in the very same way, the Dhamma has been made clear by the Venerable Gotama in many ways. I go for refuge to the Venerable Gotama, to the Dhamma, and to the Bhikkhu Saṅgha. May the Venerable Gotama remember me as a lay follower who has gone for refuge from this day forward for as long as life lasts.”

2. Sīhasuttaṃ

2. The Sīha Sutta

12. Ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā vesāliyaṃ viharati mahāvane kūṭāgārasālāyaṃ. Tena kho pana samayena sambahulā abhiññātā abhiññātā licchavī santhāgāre sandhāgāre (ka.) sannisinnā sannipatitā anekapariyāyena buddhassa vaṇṇaṃ bhāsanti, dhammassa vaṇṇaṃ bhāsanti, saṅghassa vaṇṇaṃ bhāsanti.

12. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling in Vesālī, in the Great Wood, at the Hall with the Gabled Roof. Now at that time, many very well-known Licchavis had gathered and were seated together in the assembly hall, speaking in many ways in praise of the Buddha, in praise of the Dhamma, and in praise of the Saṅgha.

Tena [P 4.0180] kho pana samayena sīho senāpati nigaṇṭhasāvako tassaṃ parisāyaṃ nisinno hoti. Atha kho sīhassa senāpatissa etadahosi – “nissaṃsayaṃ kho so bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho bhavissati, tathā hime sambahulā abhiññātā abhiññātā licchavī santhāgāre sannisinnā sannipatitā anekapariyāyena buddhassa vaṇṇaṃ bhāsanti, dhammassa vaṇṇaṃ bhāsanti, saṅghassa vaṇṇaṃ bhāsanti. Yaṃnūnāhaṃ taṃ bhagavantaṃ dassanāya upasaṅkameyyaṃ arahantaṃ sammāsambuddha”nti. Atha kho sīho senāpati yena nigaṇṭho nāṭaputto nāthaputto (ka. sī.), nātaputto (ka. sī.) tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā nigaṇṭhaṃ nāṭaputtaṃ etadavoca – “icchāmahaṃ, bhante, samaṇaṃ gotamaṃ dassanāya upasaṅkamitu”nti.

At that time, the general Sīha, a disciple of the Nigaṇṭhas, was seated in that assembly. Then this thought arose in the general Sīha: 'Without a doubt, that Blessed One must be an Arahant, a Perfectly Enlightened One, for indeed these many very well-known Licchavis are gathered and seated together in the assembly hall, speaking in many ways in praise of the Buddha, the Dhamma, and the Saṅgha. It would be good if I were to approach that Blessed One, the Arahant, the Perfectly Enlightened One, for a seeing.' Then the general Sīha approached the Nigaṇṭha Nāṭaputta. Having approached, he said this to the Nigaṇṭha Nāṭaputta: 'Venerable sir, I wish to approach the ascetic Gotama for a seeing.'

“Kiṃ [V 3.0026][M 3.0025] pana tvaṃ, sīha, kiriyavādo samāno akiriyavādaṃ samaṇaṃ gotamaṃ dassanāya upasaṅkamissasi? Samaṇo hi, sīha, gotamo akiriyavādo, akiriyāya dhammaṃ deseti, tena ca sāvake vinetī”ti. Atha kho sīhassa [T 4.0183] senāpatissa yo ahosi gamiyābhisaṅkhāro gamikābhisaṅkhāro (ka. sī.) mahāva. 290 bhagavantaṃ dassanāya, so paṭippassambhi.

'Sīha, since you are a proponent of action, why would you approach the ascetic Gotama, a proponent of inaction, for a seeing? For, Sīha, the ascetic Gotama is a proponent of inaction; he teaches a doctrine for inaction, and by that he trains his disciples.' Then, whatever effort the general Sīha had to go for a seeing of the Blessed One, that subsided.

Dutiyampi kho sambahulā abhiññātā abhiññātā licchavī santhāgāre sannisinnā sannipatitā anekapariyāyena buddhassa…pe… dhammassa…pe… saṅghassa vaṇṇaṃ bhāsanti. Dutiyampi kho sīhassa senāpatissa etadahosi – “nissaṃsayaṃ kho so bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho bhavissati, tathā hime sambahulā abhiññātā abhiññātā licchavī santhāgāre sannisinnā sannipatitā anekapariyāyena buddhassa vaṇṇaṃ bhāsanti, dhammassa…pe… saṅghassa vaṇṇaṃ bhāsanti. Yaṃnūnāhaṃ taṃ bhagavantaṃ dassanāya upasaṅkameyyaṃ arahantaṃ [P 4.0181] sammāsambuddha”nti. Atha kho sīho senāpati yena nigaṇṭho nāṭaputto tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā nigaṇṭhaṃ nāṭaputtaṃ etadavoca – “icchāmahaṃ, bhante, samaṇaṃ gotamaṃ dassanāya upasaṅkamitu”nti.

For a second time, many very well-known Licchavis had gathered and were seated together in the assembly hall, speaking in many ways in praise of the Buddha, the Dhamma, and the Saṅgha. For a second time, this thought arose in the general Sīha: 'Without a doubt, that Blessed One must be an Arahant, a Perfectly Enlightened One, for indeed these many very well-known Licchavis are gathered and seated together in the assembly hall, speaking in many ways in praise of the Buddha, the Dhamma, and the Saṅgha. It would be good if I were to approach that Blessed One, the Arahant, the Perfectly Enlightened One, for a seeing.' Then the general Sīha approached the Nigaṇṭha Nāṭaputta. Having approached, he said this to the Nigaṇṭha Nāṭaputta: 'Venerable sir, I wish to approach the ascetic Gotama for a seeing.'

“Kiṃ pana tvaṃ, sīha, kiriyavādo samāno akiriyavādaṃ samaṇaṃ gotamaṃ dassanāya upasaṅkamissasi? Samaṇo hi, sīha, gotamo akiriyavādo akiriyāya dhammaṃ deseti, tena ca sāvake vinetī”ti. Dutiyampi kho sīhassa senāpatissa yo ahosi gamiyābhisaṅkhāro bhagavantaṃ dassanāya, so paṭippassambhi.

'Sīha, since you are a proponent of action, why would you approach the ascetic Gotama, a proponent of inaction, for a seeing? For, Sīha, the ascetic Gotama is a proponent of inaction; he teaches a doctrine for inaction, and by that he trains his disciples.' For a second time, whatever effort the general Sīha had to go for a seeing of the Blessed One, that subsided.

Tatiyampi kho sambahulā abhiññātā abhiññātā licchavī santhāgāre sannisinnā sannipatitā anekapariyāyena buddhassa…pe… dhammassa…pe… saṅghassa vaṇṇaṃ bhāsanti. Tatiyampi kho sīhassa [T 4.0184] senāpatissa etadahosi – “nissaṃsayaṃ kho so bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho bhavissati, tathā hime sambahulā abhiññātā abhiññātā licchavī santhāgāre sannisinnā sannipatitā anekapariyāyena buddhassa vaṇṇaṃ bhāsanti, dhammassa vaṇṇaṃ bhāsanti, saṅghassa vaṇṇaṃ bhāsanti. Kiṃ hime karissanti nigaṇṭhā [M 3.0026] apalokitā vā anapalokitā vā? Yaṃnūnāhaṃ anapaloketvāva nigaṇṭhe nigaṇṭhaṃ (syā. ka.) mahāva. 290 passitabbaṃ taṃ bhagavantaṃ dassanāya upasaṅkameyyaṃ arahantaṃ sammāsambuddha”nti.

For a third time, many very well-known Licchavis had gathered and were seated together in the assembly hall, speaking in many ways in praise of the Buddha... the Dhamma... the Sangha. For a third time, this thought arose in General Sīha: 'Without a doubt, that Blessed One must be an Arahant, a Perfectly Enlightened One, for indeed these many very well-known Licchavis are gathered and seated together in the assembly hall, speaking in many ways in praise of the Buddha, the Dhamma, and the Sangha. What can these Nigaṇṭhas do, whether I ask their leave or not? It would be good if I were to approach that Blessed One, the Arahant, the Perfectly Enlightened One, to see him, without first asking leave of the Nigaṇṭhas.'

Atha [V 3.0027] kho sīho senāpati pañcamattehi rathasatehi divādivassa vesāliyā niyyāsi bhagavantaṃ dassanāya. Yāvatikā yānassa bhūmi, yānena gantvā yānā paccorohitvā pattikova agamāsi. Atha kho sīho senāpati yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinno kho sīho senāpati bhagavantaṃ etadavoca –

Then General Sīha, with five hundred chariots, set out from Vesālī in the middle of the day to see the Blessed One. He went by chariot as far as the ground was passable for chariots, and then, having dismounted from his chariot, he proceeded on foot. Then General Sīha approached the Blessed One; having approached, he paid homage to the Blessed One and sat down to one side. Seated to one side, General Sīha said this to the Blessed One:

“Sutaṃ metaṃ, bhante – ‘akiriyavādo samaṇo gotamo, akiriyāya dhammaṃ [P 4.0182] deseti, tena ca sāvake vinetī’ti. Ye te, bhante, evamāhaṃsu – ‘akiriyavādo samaṇo gotamo, akiriyāya dhammaṃ deseti, tena ca sāvake vinetī’ti, kacci te, bhante, bhagavato vuttavādino na ca bhagavantaṃ abhūtena abbhācikkhanti dhammassa cānudhammaṃ byākaronti na ca koci sahadhammiko vādānuvādo vādānupāto (ka. sī. syā.) a. ni. 3.58; 5.5 gārayhaṃ ṭhānaṃ āgacchati? Anabbhakkhātukāmā hi mayaṃ, bhante, bhagavanta”nti.

“Venerable sir, I have heard this: ‘The ascetic Gotama is a proponent of inaction; he teaches a doctrine for inaction, and by that he trains his disciples.’ Venerable sir, those who have said this—‘The ascetic Gotama is a proponent of inaction; he teaches a doctrine for inaction, and by that he trains his disciples’—are they, venerable sir, reporting the Blessed One’s own words and not misrepresenting the Blessed One with what is untrue? Do they explain the Dhamma in accordance with the Dhamma, so that no reasonable ground for criticism and counter-criticism gives cause for censure? For we, venerable sir, have no wish to misrepresent the Blessed One.”

“Atthi, sīha, pariyāyo, yena maṃ pariyāyena sammā vadamāno vadeyya – ‘akiriyavādo samaṇo gotamo, akiriyāya dhammaṃ deseti, tena ca sāvake vinetī’”ti[T 4.0185].

“Sīha, there is a way in which one, speaking rightly, could say of me: ‘The ascetic Gotama is a proponent of inaction; he teaches a doctrine for inaction, and by that he trains his disciples.’”

“Atthi, sīha, pariyāyo, yena maṃ pariyāyena sammā vadamāno vadeyya – ‘kiriyavādo samaṇo gotamo, kiriyāya dhammaṃ deseti, tena ca sāvake vinetī’”ti.

“Sīha, there is a way in which one, speaking rightly, could say of me: ‘The ascetic Gotama is a proponent of action; he teaches a doctrine for action, and by that he trains his disciples.’”

“Atthi, sīha, pariyāyo, yena maṃ pariyāyena sammā vadamāno vadeyya – ‘ucchedavādo samaṇo gotamo, ucchedāya dhammaṃ deseti, tena ca sāvake vinetī’”ti.

“Sīha, there is a way in which, speaking rightly, one could say of me: ‘The ascetic Gotama is an annihilationist; he teaches the Dhamma for annihilation, and by that he trains his disciples.’”

“Atthi, sīha, pariyāyo, yena maṃ pariyāyena sammā vadamāno vadeyya – ‘jegucchī samaṇo gotamo, jegucchitāya dhammaṃ deseti, tena ca sāvake vinetī’”ti.

“Sīha, there is a way in which, speaking rightly, one could say of me: ‘The ascetic Gotama is one who is disgusted; he teaches the Dhamma for disgust, and by that he trains his disciples.’”

“Atthi[M 3.0027], sīha, pariyāyo, yena maṃ pariyāyena sammā vadamāno vadeyya – ‘venayiko samaṇo gotamo, vinayāya dhammaṃ deseti, tena ca sāvake vinetī’”ti.

“Sīha, there is a way in which, speaking rightly, one could say of me: ‘The ascetic Gotama is one who removes; he teaches the Dhamma for removal, and by that he trains his disciples.’”

“Atthi[V 3.0028], sīha, pariyāyo, yena maṃ pariyāyena sammā vadamāno vadeyya – ‘tapassī samaṇo gotamo, tapassitāya dhammaṃ deseti, tena ca sāvake vinetī’”ti.

“Sīha, there is a way in which, speaking rightly, one could say of me: ‘The ascetic Gotama is an ascetic; he teaches the Dhamma for asceticism, and by that he trains his disciples.’”

“Atthi, sīha, pariyāyo, yena maṃ pariyāyena sammā vadamāno vadeyya – ‘apagabbho samaṇo gotamo, apagabbhatāya dhammaṃ deseti, tena ca sāvake vinetī’”ti.

“Sīha, there is a way in which, speaking rightly, one could say of me: ‘The ascetic Gotama is one who will not return to a womb; he teaches the Dhamma for not returning to a womb, and by that he trains his disciples.’”

“Atthi, sīha, pariyāyo, yena maṃ pariyāyena sammā vadamāno vadeyya – ‘assāsako samaṇo gotamo, assāsāya dhammaṃ deseti, tena ca sāvake vinetī’”ti.

“Sīha, there is a way in which, speaking rightly, one could say of me: ‘The ascetic Gotama is one who brings comfort; he teaches the Dhamma for comfort, and by that he trains his disciples.’”

“Katamo ca, sīha, pariyāyo, yena maṃ pariyāyena sammā vadamāno vadeyya – ‘akiriyavādo samaṇo gotamo, akiriyāya [P 4.0183] dhammaṃ deseti, tena ca sāvake vinetī’ti? Ahañhi, sīha, akiriyaṃ vadāmi kāyaduccaritassa vacīduccaritassa manoduccaritassa; anekavihitānaṃ pāpakānaṃ akusalānaṃ dhammānaṃ [T 4.0186] akiriyaṃ vadāmi. Ayaṃ kho, sīha, pariyāyo, yena maṃ pariyāyena sammā vadamāno vadeyya – ‘akiriyavādo samaṇo gotamo, akiriyāya dhammaṃ deseti, tena ca sāvake vinetī’”ti.

“And in what way, Sīha, could one, speaking rightly, say of me: ‘The ascetic Gotama is a proponent of non-action; he teaches the Dhamma for non-action, and by that he trains his disciples’? I, Sīha, teach the non-doing of bodily misconduct, verbal misconduct, and mental misconduct; I teach the non-doing of many kinds of evil, unwholesome states. This, Sīha, is the way in which one, speaking rightly, could say of me: ‘The ascetic Gotama is a proponent of non-action; he teaches the Dhamma for non-action, and by that he trains his disciples.’”

“Katamo ca, sīha, pariyāyo, yena maṃ pariyāyena sammā vadamāno vadeyya – ‘kiriyavādo samaṇo gotamo, kiriyāya dhammaṃ deseti, tena ca sāvake vinetī’ti? Ahañhi, sīha, kiriyaṃ vadāmi kāyasucaritassa vacīsucaritassa manosucaritassa; anekavihitānaṃ kusalānaṃ dhammānaṃ kiriyaṃ vadāmi. Ayaṃ kho, sīha, pariyāyo, yena maṃ pariyāyena sammā vadamāno vadeyya – ‘kiriyavādo samaṇo gotamo, kiriyāya dhammaṃ deseti, tena ca sāvake vinetī’”ti.

“And in what way, Sīha, could one, speaking rightly, say of me: ‘The ascetic Gotama is a proponent of action; he teaches the Dhamma for action, and by that he trains his disciples’? I, Sīha, teach the doing of good bodily conduct, good verbal conduct, and good mental conduct; I teach the doing of many kinds of wholesome states. This, Sīha, is the way in which one, speaking rightly, could say of me: ‘The ascetic Gotama is a proponent of action; he teaches the Dhamma for action, and by that he trains his disciples.’”

“Katamo ca, sīha, pariyāyo, yena maṃ pariyāyena sammā vadamāno vadeyya – ‘ucchedavādo samaṇo gotamo, ucchedāya dhammaṃ deseti, tena ca sāvake vinetī’ti? Ahañhi, sīha, ucchedaṃ vadāmi rāgassa dosassa [M 3.0028] mohassa; anekavihitānaṃ pāpakānaṃ akusalānaṃ dhammānaṃ ucchedaṃ vadāmi. Ayaṃ kho, sīha, pariyāyo, yena maṃ pariyāyena sammā vadamāno [V 3.0029] vadeyya – ‘ucchedavādo samaṇo gotamo, ucchedāya dhammaṃ deseti, tena ca sāvake vinetī’”ti.

“And in what way, Sīha, could one, speaking rightly, say of me: ‘The ascetic Gotama is an annihilationist; he teaches the Dhamma for annihilation, and by that he trains his disciples’? I, Sīha, teach the annihilation of passion, aversion, and delusion; I teach the annihilation of many kinds of evil, unwholesome states. This, Sīha, is the way in which one, speaking rightly, could say of me: ‘The ascetic Gotama is an annihilationist; he teaches the Dhamma for annihilation, and by that he trains his disciples.’”

“Katamo ca, sīha, pariyāyo, yena maṃ pariyāyena sammā vadamāno vadeyya – ‘jegucchī samaṇo gotamo, jegucchitāya dhammaṃ deseti, tena ca sāvake vinetī’ti? Ahañhi, sīha, jigucchāmi kāyaduccaritena vacīduccaritena [T 4.0187] manoduccaritena; jigucchāmi anekavihitānaṃ pāpakānaṃ akusalānaṃ dhammānaṃ samāpattiyā. Ayaṃ kho, sīha, pariyāyo, yena maṃ pariyāyena sammā vadamāno vadeyya – ‘jegucchī samaṇo gotamo, jegucchitāya dhammaṃ deseti, tena ca sāvake vinetī’”ti.

And in what way, Sīha, could one, speaking rightly, say of me: ‘The ascetic Gotama is one who is disgusted; he teaches the Dhamma for disgust, and by that he trains his disciples’? I, Sīha, am disgusted by bodily misconduct, verbal misconduct, and mental misconduct; I am disgusted by the attainment of many kinds of evil, unwholesome states. This, Sīha, is the way in which one, speaking rightly, could say of me: ‘The ascetic Gotama is one who is disgusted; he teaches the Dhamma for disgust, and by that he trains his disciples.’

“Katamo ca, sīha, pariyāyo, yena maṃ pariyāyena sammā vadamāno vadeyya – ‘venayiko samaṇo gotamo, vinayāya dhammaṃ deseti, tena ca sāvake vinetī’ti? Ahañhi, sīha, vinayāya dhammaṃ desemi rāgassa dosassa mohassa; anekavihitānaṃ [P 4.0184] pāpakānaṃ akusalānaṃ dhammānaṃ vinayāya dhammaṃ desemi. Ayaṃ kho, sīha, pariyāyo, yena maṃ pariyāyena sammā vadamāno vadeyya – ‘venayiko samaṇo gotamo, vinayāya dhammaṃ deseti, tena ca sāvake vinetī’”ti.

And in what way, Sīha, could one, speaking rightly, say of me: ‘The ascetic Gotama is one who removes; he teaches the Dhamma for removal, and by that he trains his disciples’? I, Sīha, teach the Dhamma for the removal of passion, aversion, and delusion; I teach the Dhamma for the removal of many kinds of evil, unwholesome states. This, Sīha, is the way in which one, speaking rightly, could say of me: ‘The ascetic Gotama is one who removes; he teaches the Dhamma for removal, and by that he trains his disciples.’

“Katamo ca, sīha, pariyāyo, yena maṃ pariyāyena sammā vadamāno vadeyya – ‘tapassī samaṇo gotamo, tapassitāya dhammaṃ deseti, tena ca sāvake vinetī’ti? Tapanīyāhaṃ, sīha, pāpake akusale dhamme vadāmi kāyaduccaritaṃ vacīduccaritaṃ manoduccaritaṃ. Yassa kho, sīha, tapanīyā pāpakā akusalā dhammā pahīnā ucchinnamūlā tālāvatthukatā anabhāvaṃkatā āyatiṃ anuppādadhammā, tamahaṃ ‘tapassī’ti vadāmi. Tathāgatassa kho, sīha, tapanīyā pāpakā akusalā dhammā pahīnā ucchinnamūlā tālāvatthukatā anabhāvaṃkatā āyatiṃ anuppādadhammā. Ayaṃ kho, sīha, pariyāyo, yena maṃ pariyāyena sammā vadamāno vadeyya – ‘tapassī samaṇo [T 4.0188] gotamo, tapassitāya dhammaṃ deseti, tena ca sāvake vinetī’”ti.

And in what way, Sīha, could one, speaking rightly, say of me: ‘The ascetic Gotama is one who torments; he teaches the Dhamma for tormenting, and by that he trains his disciples’? I, Sīha, declare that evil, unwholesome states—bodily misconduct, verbal misconduct, and mental misconduct—are tormenting. One for whom, Sīha, these tormenting, evil, unwholesome states have been abandoned, cut off at the root, made like a palm stump, obliterated, and are no longer subject to future arising—him I call ‘one who torments.’ For the Tathāgata, Sīha, these tormenting, evil, unwholesome states have been abandoned, cut off at the root, made like a palm stump, obliterated, and are no longer subject to future arising. This, Sīha, is the way in which one, speaking rightly, could say of me: ‘The ascetic Gotama is one who torments; he teaches the Dhamma for tormenting, and by that he trains his disciples.’

“Katamo [M 3.0029] ca, sīha, pariyāyo, yena maṃ pariyāyena sammā vadamāno vadeyya – ‘apagabbho samaṇo gotamo, apagabbhatāya dhammaṃ deseti, tena ca sāvake vinetī’ti? Yassa kho[V 3.0030], sīha, āyatiṃ gabbhaseyyā punabbhavābhinibbatti pahīnā ucchinnamūlā tālāvatthukatā anabhāvaṃkatā āyatiṃ anuppādadhammā, tamahaṃ ‘apagabbho’ti vadāmi. Tathāgatassa kho, sīha, āyatiṃ gabbhaseyyā punabbhavābhinibbatti pahīnā ucchinnamūlā tālāvatthukatā anabhāvaṃkatā āyatiṃ anuppādadhammā. Ayaṃ kho, sīha, pariyāyo, yena maṃ pariyāyena sammā vadamāno vadeyya – ‘apagabbho samaṇo gotamo, apagabbhatāya dhammaṃ deseti, tena ca sāvake vinetī’”ti.

And in what way, Sīha, could one, speaking rightly, say of me: ‘The ascetic Gotama is free from the womb; he teaches the Dhamma for freedom from the womb, and by that he trains his disciples’? Whoever, Sīha, has abandoned future lying in a womb and rebirth in a new existence, has cut them off at the root, made them like a palm stump, brought them to a state of non-existence, so that they are of a nature not to arise in the future—that one I call ‘free from the womb.’ The Tathāgata, Sīha, has abandoned future lying in a womb and rebirth in a new existence, has cut them off at the root, made them like a palm stump, brought them to a state of non-existence, so that they are of a nature not to arise in the future. This, Sīha, is the way in which one, speaking rightly, could say of me: ‘The ascetic Gotama is free from the womb; he teaches the Dhamma for freedom from the womb, and by that he trains his disciples.’

“Katamo ca, sīha, pariyāyo, yena maṃ pariyāyena sammā vadamāno vadeyya – ‘assāsako samaṇo gotamo, assāsāya dhammaṃ deseti, tena ca sāvake vinetī’ti? Ahañhi, sīha, assāsako [P 4.0185] paramena assāsena, assāsāya dhammaṃ desemi, tena ca sāvake vinemi. Ayaṃ kho, sīha, pariyāyo, yena maṃ pariyāyena sammā vadamāno vadeyya – ‘assāsako samaṇo gotamo, assāsāya dhammaṃ deseti, tena ca sāvake vinetī’”ti.

And in what way, Sīha, could one, speaking rightly, say of me: ‘The ascetic Gotama is one who is at ease; he teaches the Dhamma for the sake of ease, and by that he trains his disciples’? For I, Sīha, am one who is at ease with the supreme ease; I teach the Dhamma for the sake of ease, and by that I train my disciples. This, Sīha, is the way in which one, speaking rightly, could say of me: ‘The ascetic Gotama is one who is at ease; he teaches the Dhamma for the sake of ease, and by that he trains his disciples.’

Evaṃ vutte sīho senāpati bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “abhikkantaṃ, bhante, abhikkantaṃ, bhante…pe… upāsakaṃ maṃ, bhante, bhagavā dhāretu ajjatagge [T 4.0189] pāṇupetaṃ saraṇaṃ gata”nti.

When this was said, the general Sīha said this to the Blessed One: “Excellent, venerable sir! Excellent, venerable sir! … Venerable sir, may the Blessed One regard me as a lay follower who has gone for refuge for life, from this day forward.”

“Anuviccakāraṃ kho, sīha, karohi. Anuviccakāro tumhādisānaṃ ñātamanussānaṃ sādhu hotī”ti. “Imināpāhaṃ, bhante, bhagavato bhiyyosomattāya attamano abhiraddho, yaṃ maṃ bhagavā evamāha – ‘anuviccakāraṃ kho, sīha, karohi. Anuviccakāro tumhādisānaṃ ñātamanussānaṃ sādhu hotī’ti. Mañhi, bhante, aññatitthiyā sāvakaṃ labhitvā kevalakappaṃ vesāliṃ paṭākaṃ parihareyyuṃ – ‘sīho amhākaṃ senāpati sāvakattaṃ upagato’ti. Atha ca pana bhagavā evamāha – ‘anuviccakāraṃ, sīha, karohi. Anuviccakāro tumhādisānaṃ ñātamanussānaṃ sādhu hotī’ti. Esāhaṃ, bhante, dutiyampi bhagavantaṃ saraṇaṃ gacchāmi dhammañca bhikkhusaṅghañca. Upāsakaṃ maṃ bhagavā dhāretu ajjatagge pāṇupetaṃ saraṇaṃ gata”nti.

“Sīha, you should act after careful investigation. Acting after careful investigation is proper for well-known people like you.” “By this, venerable sir, I am all the more pleased and delighted with the Blessed One, that the Blessed One said to me: ‘Sīha, you should act after careful investigation. Acting after careful investigation is proper for well-known people like you.’ For, venerable sir, if other sectarians were to gain me as a disciple, they would carry a banner throughout all of Vesālī, proclaiming: ‘The general Sīha has become our disciple!’ And yet the Blessed One says to me: ‘Sīha, you should act after careful investigation. Acting after careful investigation is proper for well-known people like you.’ For the second time, venerable sir, I go for refuge to the Blessed One, to the Dhamma, and to the Saṅgha of bhikkhus. May the Blessed One regard me as a lay follower who has gone for refuge for life, from this day forward.”

“Dīgharattaṃ [M 3.0030] kho te, sīha, nigaṇṭhānaṃ opānabhūtaṃ kulaṃ, yena nesaṃ upagatānaṃ piṇḍakaṃ dātabbaṃ maññeyyāsī”ti. “Imināpāhaṃ, bhante, bhagavato bhiyyosomattāya attamano abhiraddho, yaṃ maṃ bhagavā evamāha – ‘dīgharattaṃ kho te, sīha, nigaṇṭhānaṃ opānabhūtaṃ kulaṃ, yena [V 3.0031] nesaṃ upagatānaṃ piṇḍakaṃ dātabbaṃ maññeyyāsī’ti. Sutaṃ metaṃ, bhante – ‘samaṇo gotamo evamāha – mayhameva [P 4.0186] dānaṃ dātabbaṃ, mayhameva sāvakānaṃ dātabbaṃ; mayhameva dinnaṃ mahapphalaṃ, na aññesaṃ dinnaṃ mahapphalaṃ; mayhameva sāvakānaṃ dinnaṃ mahapphalaṃ, na aññesaṃ sāvakānaṃ dinnaṃ mahapphala’nti, atha ca pana maṃ bhagavā nigaṇṭhesupi dāne [T 4.0190] samādapeti samādāpeti (?). Api ca, bhante, mayamettha kālaṃ jānissāma. Esāhaṃ, bhante, tatiyampi bhagavantaṃ saraṇaṃ gacchāmi dhammañca bhikkhusaṅghañca. Upāsakaṃ maṃ, bhante, bhagavā dhāretu ajjatagge pāṇupetaṃ saraṇaṃ gata”nti.

“For a long time, Sīha, your family has been like a well for the Nigaṇṭhas. Therefore, you should consider that almsfood ought to be given to them when they approach.” “By this, venerable sir, I am all the more pleased and delighted with the Blessed One, that the Blessed One said to me: ‘For a long time, Sīha, your family has been like a well for the Nigaṇṭhas. Therefore, you should consider that almsfood ought to be given to them when they approach.’ I have heard this, venerable sir: ‘The ascetic Gotama says this: “Alms should be given only to me; alms should be given only to my disciples. What is given only to me is of great fruit, not what is given to others. What is given only to my disciples is of great fruit, not what is given to the disciples of others.”’ And yet the Blessed One encourages me in giving alms even to the Nigaṇṭhas. Moreover, venerable sir, in this matter we shall know the proper time. For the third time, venerable sir, I go for refuge to the Blessed One, to the Dhamma, and to the Saṅgha of bhikkhus. May the Blessed One regard me as a lay follower who has gone for refuge for life, from this day forward.”

Atha kho bhagavā sīhassa senāpatissa anupubbiṃ kathaṃ anupubbikathaṃ (sabbattha) kathesi, seyyathidaṃ – dānakathaṃ sīlakathaṃ saggakathaṃ, kāmānaṃ ādīnavaṃ okāraṃ saṃkilesaṃ nekkhamme ānisaṃsaṃ pakāsesi. Yadā bhagavā aññāsi sīhaṃ senāpatiṃ kallacittaṃ muducittaṃ vinīvaraṇacittaṃ udaggacittaṃ pasannacittaṃ, atha yā buddhānaṃ sāmukkaṃsikā dhammadesanā taṃ pakāsesi – dukkhaṃ samudayaṃ nirodhaṃ maggaṃ. Seyyathāpi nāma suddhaṃ vatthaṃ apagatakāḷakaṃ sammadeva rajanaṃ paṭiggaṇheyya; evamevaṃ sīhassa senāpatissa tasmiṃyeva āsane virajaṃ vītamalaṃ dhammacakkhuṃ udapādi – “yaṃ kiñci samudayadhammaṃ, sabbaṃ taṃ nirodhadhamma”nti.

Then the Blessed One gave General Sīha a gradual discourse, that is to say: a talk on giving, a talk on virtue, a talk on heaven; he explained the danger, the baseness, and the defilement of sensual pleasures, and the benefit of renunciation. When the Blessed One knew that General Sīha had a ready mind, a pliable mind, a mind free from hindrances, an uplifted mind, and a confident mind, he then explained that teaching of the Dhamma which is the special presentation of the Buddhas: suffering, its origin, its cessation, and the path. Just as a clean cloth, free from stains, would properly receive dye, even so, for General Sīha, while on that very seat, the dust-free, stainless Dhamma-eye arose: ‘Whatever has the nature to arise, all that has the nature to cease.’”

Atha kho sīho senāpati diṭṭhadhammo pattadhammo viditadhammo pariyogāḷhadhammo tiṇṇavicikiccho vigatakathaṃkatho vesārajjappatto aparappaccayo satthusāsane bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “adhivāsetu me, bhante, bhagavā svātanāya [P 4.0187] bhattaṃ saddhiṃ bhikkhusaṅghenā”ti. Adhivāsesi bhagavā tuṇhībhāvena.

Then General Sīha, having seen the Dhamma, attained the Dhamma, understood the Dhamma, penetrated the Dhamma, crossed over doubt, with perplexity removed, having attained confidence, and being independent of others in the Teacher’s dispensation, said this to the Blessed One: “Venerable sir, may the Blessed One consent to my meal for tomorrow, together with the Saṅgha of bhikkhus.” The Blessed One consented by remaining silent.

Atha kho sīho senāpati bhagavato adhivāsanaṃ viditvā uṭṭhāyāsanā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā padakkhiṇaṃ katvā pakkāmi. Atha kho sīho senāpati aññataraṃ purisaṃ āmantesi – “gaccha tvaṃ, ambho purisa[M 3.0031], pavattamaṃsaṃ jānāhī”ti. Atha kho sīho senāpati tassā rattiyā accayena sake nivesane paṇītaṃ khādanīyaṃ bhojanīyaṃ paṭiyādāpetvā bhagavato [T 4.0191] kālaṃ ārocāpesi – “kālo, bhante! Niṭṭhitaṃ bhatta”nti.

Then General Sīha, having understood the Blessed One’s consent, rose from his seat, paid homage to the Blessed One, circumambulated him keeping him to the right, and departed. Then General Sīha addressed a certain man: “Go, good man, and find out about available meat.” Then, after that night had passed, General Sīha, having had excellent hard and soft foods prepared in his own residence, had the time announced to the Blessed One: “It is time, venerable sir! The meal is ready.”

Atha kho bhagavā pubbaṇhasamayaṃ nivāsetvā pattacīvaramādāya yena sīhassa senāpatissa nivesanaṃ tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā paññatte āsane nisīdi saddhiṃ bhikkhusaṅghena. Tena kho pana [V 3.0032] samayena sambahulā nigaṇṭhā vesāliyaṃ rathikāya rathikaṃ rathiyāya rathiyaṃ (bahūsu) siṅghāṭakena siṅghāṭakaṃ bāhā paggayha kandanti – “ajja sīhena senāpatinā thūlaṃ pasuṃ vadhitvā samaṇassa gotamassa bhattaṃ kataṃ. Taṃ samaṇo gotamo jānaṃ uddissakataṃ maṃsaṃ paribhuñjati paṭiccakamma”nti.

Then the Blessed One, having dressed in the morning, took his bowl and robe and went to the residence of General Sīha. Having arrived, he sat down on the prepared seat together with the Saṅgha of bhikkhus. At that time, many Nigaṇṭhas in Vesālī were going from street to street and from crossroads to crossroads, raising their arms and wailing: “Today, General Sīha has slaughtered a fat animal and prepared a meal for the ascetic Gotama. The ascetic Gotama knowingly consumes meat prepared on his account, a deed connected to him.”

Atha kho aññataro puriso yena sīho senāpati tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā sīhassa senāpatissa upakaṇṇake ārocesi – “yagghe, bhante, jāneyyāsi! Ete sambahulā nigaṇṭhā vesāliyaṃ rathikāya rathikaṃ siṅghāṭakena siṅghāṭakaṃ bāhā paggayha kandanti – ‘ajja sīhena senāpatinā thūlaṃ pasuṃ vadhitvā samaṇassa gotamassa bhattaṃ kataṃ. Taṃ samaṇo gotamo jānaṃ uddissakataṃ maṃsaṃ paribhuñjati [P 4.0188] paṭiccakamma’nti. Alaṃ ayyo dīgharattañhi te āyasmanto avaṇṇakāmā buddhassa avaṇṇakāmā dhammassa avaṇṇakāmā saṅghassa. Na ca panete āyasmanto jiridanti taṃ bhagavantaṃ asatā tucchā musā abhūtena abbhācikkhituṃ; na ca mayaṃ jīvitahetupi sañcicca pāṇaṃ jīvitā voropeyyāmā”ti.

Then a certain man approached General Sīha and reported in his ear: “May it please you to know, venerable sir! These many Nigaṇṭhas in Vesālī are going from street to street and from crossroads to crossroads, raising their arms and wailing: ‘Today, General Sīha has slaughtered a fat animal and prepared a meal for the ascetic Gotama. The ascetic Gotama knowingly consumes meat prepared on his account, a deed connected to him!’” Sīha replied: “Enough, my good man. For a long time, those venerable ones have desired to speak ill of the Buddha, ill of the Dhamma, and ill of the Saṅgha. And those venerable ones do not shrink from accusing the Blessed One with what is non-existent, empty, false, and untrue. As for us, we would not intentionally deprive a living being of life, even for the sake of our own life.”

Atha kho sīho senāpati buddhappamukhaṃ bhikkhusaṅghaṃ paṇītena khādanīyena bhojanīyena sahatthā santappesi sampavāresi. Atha kho sīho senāpati [T 4.0192] bhagavantaṃ bhuttāviṃ onītapattapāṇiṃ ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinnaṃ kho sīhaṃ senāpatiṃ bhagavā dhammiyā kathāya sandassetvā samādapetvā samuttejetvā sampahaṃsetvā uṭṭhāyāsanā pakkāmīti. Dutiyaṃ.

Then General Sīha, with his own hand, served and satisfied the Saṅgha of bhikkhus headed by the Buddha with exquisite hard and soft foods. Then, when the Blessed One had finished his meal and had withdrawn his hand from the bowl, General Sīha sat down at one side. The Blessed One instructed, established, aroused, and gladdened General Sīha, who was sitting at one side, with a talk on the Dhamma, then rose from his seat and departed.

3. Assājānīyasuttaṃ

3. The Discourse on the Thoroughbred Horse

13. “Aṭṭhahi[M 3.0032], bhikkhave, aṅgehi samannāgato rañño bhaddo assājānīyo rājāraho hoti rājabhoggo, rañño aṅganteva saṅkhaṃ gacchati. Katamehi aṭṭhahi? Idha, bhikkhave, rañño bhaddo assājānīyo ubhato sujāto hoti – mātito ca pitito ca. Yassaṃ disāyaṃ aññepi bhaddā assājānīyā jāyanti, tassaṃ disāyaṃ jāto hoti. Yaṃ kho panassa bhojanaṃ denti – allaṃ vā sukkhaṃ vā – taṃ sakkaccaṃyeva paribhuñjati avikiranto. Jegucchī hoti uccāraṃ vā passāvaṃ vā abhinisīdituṃ vā abhinipajjituṃ vā. Sorato hoti [P 4.0189] sukhasaṃvāso, na ca aññe asse ubbejetā. Yāni kho panassa honti yāni kho panassa tāni (syā.) sāṭheyyāni kūṭeyyāni jimheyyāni vaṅkeyyāni, tāni yathābhūtaṃ sārathissa āvikattā hoti. Tesamassa sārathi [V 3.0033] abhinimmadanāya vāyamati. Vāhī kho pana hoti. ‘Kāmaññe assā vahantu vā mā vā, ahamettha vahissāmī’ti cittaṃ uppādeti. Gacchanto kho pana ujumaggeneva gacchati. Thāmavā hoti yāva jīvitamaraṇapariyādānā thāmaṃ upadaṃsetā. Imehi kho, bhikkhave, aṭṭhahi aṅgehi samannāgato rañño bhaddo assājānīyo rājāraho hoti rājabhoggo, rañño aṅganteva saṅkhaṃ gacchati.

13. “Bhikkhus, a king's good thoroughbred horse endowed with eight qualities is worthy of a king, fit for a king's use, and is reckoned as an attribute of the king. What eight? Here, bhikkhus, a king's good thoroughbred horse is well-born on both sides, from its mother and from its father. It is born in a region where other good thoroughbred horses are born. Whatever food they give it, whether fresh or dry, it eats it carefully without scattering it. It is disgusted to sit or lie down in its own excrement or urine. It is gentle and pleasant to associate with, and it does not alarm other horses. Whatever fraudulent, crooked, devious, or distorted traits it has, it reveals them to the charioteer as they really are. The charioteer then strives to subdue them. Furthermore, it is a good carrier. It generates the thought, ‘Whether other horses carry the load or not, I will carry it here.’ When going, it goes only by the straight path. It is strong and displays its strength right up to the end of its life. Bhikkhus, a king's good thoroughbred horse endowed with these eight qualities is worthy of a king, fit for a king's use, and is reckoned as an attribute of the king.”

“Evamevaṃ kho, bhikkhave, aṭṭhahi dhammehi [T 4.0193] samannāgato bhikkhu āhuneyyo hoti…pe… anuttaraṃ puññakkhettaṃ lokassa. Katamehi aṭṭhahi? Idha, bhikkhave, bhikkhu sīlavā hoti, pātimokkhasaṃvarasaṃvuto viharati ācāragocarasampanno aṇumattesu vajjesu bhayadassāvī, samādāya sikkhati sikkhāpadesu. Yaṃ kho panassa bhojanaṃ denti – lūkhaṃ vā paṇītaṃ vā – taṃ sakkaccaṃyeva paribhuñjati avihaññamāno. Jegucchī hoti kāyaduccaritena vacīduccaritena manoduccaritena; jegucchī hoti anekavihitānaṃ pāpakānaṃ akusalānaṃ dhammānaṃ samāpattiyā. Sorato hoti sukhasaṃvāso, na aññe bhikkhū ubbejetā. Yāni kho panassa honti sāṭheyyāni kūṭeyyāni [P 4.0190] jimheyyāni vaṅkeyyāni, tāni yathābhūtaṃ āvikattā hoti satthari vā viññūsu vā sabrahmacārīsu. Tesamassa satthā vā viññū vā sabrahmacārī abhinimmadanāya vāyamati. Sikkhitā kho pana hoti. ‘Kāmaññe [M 3.0033] bhikkhū sikkhantu vā mā vā, ahamettha sikkhissāmī’ti cittaṃ uppādeti. Gacchanto kho pana ujumaggeneva gacchati; tatrāyaṃ ujumaggo, seyyathidaṃ – sammādiṭṭhi…pe… sammāsamādhi. Āraddhavīriyo viharati – ‘kāmaṃ taco ca nhāru nahāru (sī. syā. kaṃ. pī.) ca aṭṭhi ca avasissatu, sarīre upasussatu maṃsalohitaṃ; yaṃ taṃ purisathāmena purisavīriyena purisaparakkamena pattabbaṃ, na taṃ apāpuṇitvā vīriyassa saṇṭhānaṃ bhavissatī’ti. Imehi kho, bhikkhave, aṭṭhahi dhammehi samannāgato bhikkhu āhuneyyo hoti…pe… anuttaraṃ puññakkhettaṃ lokassā”ti[T 4.0194]. Tatiyaṃ.

“In the same way, bhikkhus, a bhikkhu endowed with eight qualities is worthy of offerings… an unsurpassed field of merit for the world. What eight? Here, bhikkhus, a bhikkhu is virtuous; he dwells restrained by the Pātimokkha restraint, possessed of good conduct and resort, seeing danger in the slightest faults; he trains by undertaking the training rules. Whatever food they give him, whether coarse or fine, he consumes it carefully and without annoyance. He is disgusted by bodily, verbal, and mental misconduct; he is disgusted by the attainment of various evil, unwholesome states. He is gentle and pleasant to associate with, and he does not alarm other bhikkhus. Whatever fraudulent, crooked, devious, or distorted traits he has, he reveals them to the Teacher or to his wise companions in the holy life as they really are. The Teacher or his wise companions then strive to subdue them. He is dedicated to the training. He generates the thought, ‘Whether other bhikkhus train or not, I will train here.’ When going, he goes only by the straight path. Here, the straight path is this: right view… right concentration. He dwells with energy aroused, thinking: ‘Let only skin, sinews, and bones remain, and let the flesh and blood dry up in my body, but there will be no relaxing of my energy until I have attained what can be attained by human strength, human energy, and human effort.’ Bhikkhus, a bhikkhu endowed with these eight qualities is worthy of offerings… an unsurpassed field of merit for the world.”

4. Assakhaḷuṅkasuttaṃ

4. The Discourse on the Vicious Horse

14. “Aṭṭha ca aṭṭha (syā.), bhikkhave, assakhaḷuṅke assakhaluṅke (sī.) desessāmi aṭṭha ca assadose, aṭṭha ca purisakhaḷuṅke aṭṭha ca purisadose. Taṃ suṇātha, sādhukaṃ manasi karotha; bhāsissāmī”ti. “Evaṃ, bhante”ti kho te bhikkhū bhagavato paccassosuṃ. Bhagavā etadavoca –

14. “Bhikkhus, I will teach the eight kinds of vicious horses and the eight faults of a horse, and the eight kinds of vicious men and the eight faults of a man. Listen to that, pay close attention; I will speak.” “Yes, Venerable Sir,” those bhikkhus responded to the Blessed One. The Blessed One said this:

“Katame [V 3.0034] ca, bhikkhave, aṭṭha assakhaḷuṅkā aṭṭha ca assadosā? Idha, bhikkhave, ekacco assakhaḷuṅko ‘pehī’ti vutto, viddho samāno codito sārathinā pacchato paṭikkamati, piṭṭhito [P 4.0191] rathaṃ pavatteti. Evarūpopi, bhikkhave, idhekacco assakhaḷuṅko hoti. Ayaṃ, bhikkhave, paṭhamo assadoso.

“And what, bhikkhus, are the eight vicious horses and the eight faults of a horse? Here, bhikkhus, a certain vicious horse, when told ‘Go!’ by the charioteer, being prodded and urged on, retreats backward and makes the chariot go backward. Of such a nature, bhikkhus, is a certain vicious horse here. This, bhikkhus, is the first fault of a horse.

“Puna caparaṃ, bhikkhave, idhekacco assakhaḷuṅko ‘pehī’ti vutto, viddho samāno codito sārathinā pacchā laṅghati, kubbaraṃ hanati, tidaṇḍaṃ bhañjati. Evarūpopi, bhikkhave, idhekacco assakhaḷuṅko hoti. Ayaṃ, bhikkhave, dutiyo assadoso.

“Furthermore, bhikkhus, here a certain vicious horse, when told ‘Go!’ by the charioteer, being prodded and urged on, leaps backward, strikes the chariot’s guard-rail, and breaks the triple-bar. Of such a nature, bhikkhus, is a certain vicious horse here. This, bhikkhus, is the second fault of a horse.

“Puna caparaṃ, bhikkhave, idhekacco assakhaḷuṅko ‘pehī’ti vutto, viddho samāno codito sārathinā rathīsāya satthiṃ ussajjitvā rathīsaṃyeva ajjhomaddati. Evarūpopi, bhikkhave, idhekacco assakhaḷuṅko hoti. Ayaṃ, bhikkhave, tatiyo assadoso.

“Furthermore, bhikkhus, here a certain vicious horse, when told ‘Go!’ by the charioteer, being prodded and urged on, flings its thigh onto the chariot-pole and tramples upon the chariot-pole itself. Of such a nature, bhikkhus, is a certain vicious horse here. This, bhikkhus, is the third fault of a horse.

“Puna caparaṃ, bhikkhave, idhekacco assakhaḷuṅko ‘pehī’ti vutto, viddho samāno codito sārathinā ummaggaṃ gaṇhati, ubbaṭumaṃ rathaṃ karoti. Evarūpopi[M 3.0034], bhikkhave, idhekacco assakhaḷuṅko hoti. Ayaṃ, bhikkhave, catuttho assadoso.

“Furthermore, bhikkhus, here a certain vicious horse, when told ‘Go!’ by the charioteer, being prodded and urged on, takes a wrong path and leads the chariot off the road. Of such a nature, bhikkhus, is a certain vicious horse here. This, bhikkhus, is the fourth fault of a horse.

“Puna caparaṃ, bhikkhave, idhekacco assakhaḷuṅko ‘pehī’ti vutto, viddho samāno codito sārathinā laṅghati purimakāyaṃ paggaṇhati purime pāde. Evarūpopi, bhikkhave, idhekacco assakhaḷuṅko [T 4.0195] hoti. Ayaṃ, bhikkhave, pañcamo assadoso.

“Furthermore, bhikkhus, here a certain vicious horse, when told ‘Go!’ by the charioteer, being prodded and urged on, rears up its fore-body and lifts its forelegs. Of such a nature, bhikkhus, is a certain vicious horse here. This, bhikkhus, is the fifth fault of a horse.

“Puna caparaṃ, bhikkhave, idhekacco assakhaḷuṅko ‘pehī’ti vutto, viddho samāno codito sārathinā anādiyitvā sārathiṃ anādiyitvā patodalaṭṭhiṃ patodaṃ (sī. pī.), patodayaṭṭhiṃ (syā. kaṃ.) dantehi mukhādhānaṃ mukhāṭhānaṃ (ka.) vidhaṃsitvā [P 4.0192] yena kāmaṃ pakkamati. Evarūpopi, bhikkhave, idhekacco assakhaḷuṅko hoti. Ayaṃ, bhikkhave, chaṭṭho assadoso.

“Furthermore, monks, here a certain vicious horse, when told ‘Go!’ by the charioteer, being prodded and urged on, disregarding the charioteer, disregarding the goad-stick, chews through the bit with its teeth and goes off wherever it pleases. Of such a nature, monks, is a certain vicious horse here. This, monks, is the sixth fault of a horse.

“Puna caparaṃ, bhikkhave, idhekacco assakhaḷuṅko ‘pehī’ti vutto, viddho samāno codito [V 3.0035] sārathinā neva abhikkamati no paṭikkamati tattheva khīlaṭṭhāyī ṭhito hoti. Evarūpopi, bhikkhave, idhekacco assakhaḷuṅko hoti. Ayaṃ, bhikkhave, sattamo assadoso.

“Furthermore, monks, here a certain vicious horse, when told ‘Go!’ by the charioteer, being prodded and urged on, neither goes forward nor retreats, but stands right there, stock-still like a post. Of such a nature, monks, is a certain vicious horse here. This, monks, is the seventh fault of a horse.

“Puna caparaṃ, bhikkhave, idhekacco assakhaḷuṅko ‘pehī’ti vutto, viddho samāno codito sārathinā purime ca pāde saṃharitvā pacchime ca pāde saṃharitvā saṅkharitvā (ka.) tattheva cattāro pāde abhinisīdati. Evarūpopi, bhikkhave, idhekacco assakhaḷuṅko hoti. Ayaṃ, bhikkhave, aṭṭhamo assadoso. Ime kho, bhikkhave, aṭṭha assakhaḷuṅkā aṭṭha ca assadosā.

“Furthermore, monks, here a certain vicious horse, when told ‘Go!’ by the charioteer, being prodded and urged on, draws in its forelegs and its hind legs and sits down on all four legs right there. Of such a nature, monks, is a certain vicious horse here. This, monks, is the eighth fault of a horse. These, monks, are the eight vicious horses and the eight faults of a horse.

vibha. 956 “Katame ca, bhikkhave, aṭṭha purisakhaḷuṅkā aṭṭha ca purisadosā? Idha, bhikkhave, bhikkhū bhikkhuṃ āpattiyā codenti. So bhikkhu bhikkhūhi āpattiyā codiyamāno ‘na sarāmī’ti asatiyā nibbeṭheti. Seyyathāpi so, bhikkhave, assakhaḷuṅko ‘pehī’ti vutto, viddho samāno codito sārathinā pacchato paṭikkamati, piṭṭhito rathaṃ vatteti; tathūpamāhaṃ, bhikkhave, imaṃ puggalaṃ vadāmi. Evarūpopi, bhikkhave, idhekacco purisakhaḷuṅko hoti. Ayaṃ, bhikkhave, paṭhamo purisadoso.

“And what, monks, are the eight vicious men and the eight faults of a man? Here, monks, monks accuse a monk of an offense. That monk, being accused of an offense by the monks, evades by means of unmindfulness, saying, ‘I do not remember.’ Just as that vicious horse, monks, when told ‘Go!’ by the charioteer, being prodded and urged on, retreats backward and makes the chariot go backward; in the same way, monks, I say this person is comparable. Of such a nature, monks, is a certain vicious man here. This, monks, is the first fault of a man.

“Puna [M 3.0035] caparaṃ, bhikkhave[T 4.0196], bhikkhū bhikkhuṃ āpattiyā codenti. So bhikkhu bhikkhūhi āpattiyā codiyamāno codakaṃyeva [P 4.0193] paṭippharati – ‘kiṃ nu kho tuyhaṃ bālassa abyattassa bhaṇitena! Tvampi nāma bhaṇitabbaṃ maññasī’ti! Seyyathāpi so, bhikkhave, assakhaḷuṅko ‘pehī’ti vutto, viddho samāno codito sārathinā pacchā laṅghati, kubbaraṃ hanati, tidaṇḍaṃ bhañjati; tathūpamāhaṃ, bhikkhave, imaṃ puggalaṃ vadāmi. Evarūpopi, bhikkhave, idhekacco purisakhaḷuṅko hoti. Ayaṃ, bhikkhave, dutiyo purisadoso.

“Furthermore, monks, monks accuse a monk of an offense. That monk, being accused of an offense by the monks, retaliates against the accuser himself, saying: ‘What is to be done with the speech of a fool, an unskilled person like you! Do you really think you ought to speak?’ Just as that vicious horse, monks, when told ‘Go!’ by the charioteer, being prodded and urged on, leaps backward, strikes the chariot’s guard-rail, and breaks the triple-bar; in the same way, monks, I say this person is comparable. Of such a nature, monks, is a certain vicious man here. This, monks, is the second fault of a man.

“Puna caparaṃ, bhikkhave, bhikkhū bhikkhuṃ āpattiyā codenti. So bhikkhu bhikkhūhi āpattiyā codiyamāno codakasseva paccāropeti – ‘tvaṃ khosi itthannāmaṃ āpattiṃ āpanno, tvaṃ tāva paṭhamaṃ paṭikarohī’ti. Seyyathāpi so, bhikkhave, assakhaḷuṅko ‘pehī’ti vutto, viddho samāno codito sārathinā rathīsāya satthiṃ ussajjitvā rathīsaṃyeva ajjhomaddati; tathūpamāhaṃ, bhikkhave, imaṃ puggalaṃ vadāmi. Evarūpopi, bhikkhave, idhekacco purisakhaḷuṅko hoti. Ayaṃ, bhikkhave, tatiyo purisadoso.

“Furthermore, monks, monks accuse a monk of an offense. That monk, being accused of an offense by the monks, makes a counter-accusation against the accuser himself, saying: ‘Indeed, you have committed such-and-such an offense. You should make amends first!’ Just as that vicious horse, monks, when told ‘Go!’ by the charioteer, being prodded and urged on, flings its thigh onto the chariot-pole and tramples upon the chariot-pole itself; in the same way, monks, I say this person is comparable. Of such a nature, monks, is a certain vicious man here. This, monks, is the third fault of a man.”

“Puna [V 3.0036] caparaṃ, bhikkhave, bhikkhū bhikkhuṃ āpattiyā codenti. So bhikkhu bhikkhūhi āpattiyā codiyamāno aññenāññaṃ paṭicarati, bahiddhā kathaṃ apanāmeti, kopañca dosañca appaccayañca pātukaroti. Seyyathāpi so, bhikkhave, assakhaḷuṅko ‘pehī’ti vutto, viddho samāno codito sārathinā ummaggaṃ gaṇhati, ubbaṭumaṃ rathaṃ karoti; tathūpamāhaṃ, bhikkhave, imaṃ puggalaṃ vadāmi. Evarūpopi, bhikkhave, idhekacco purisakhaḷuṅko hoti. Ayaṃ, bhikkhave, catuttho purisadoso.

“Furthermore, monks, monks accuse a monk of an offense. That monk, being accused of an offense by the monks, speaks of one thing with another, deflects the talk to external matters, and makes apparent his anger, ill will, and displeasure. Just as that vicious horse, monks, when told ‘Go!’ by the charioteer, being prodded and urged on, takes a wrong way and drives the chariot off the path; in the same way, monks, I say this person is comparable. Of such a nature, monks, is a certain vicious man here. This, monks, is the fourth fault of a man.

“Puna caparaṃ, bhikkhave, bhikkhū bhikkhuṃ āpattiyā [T 4.0197] codenti. So bhikkhu bhikkhūhi āpattiyā codiyamāno saṅghamajjhe bāhuvikkhepaṃ karoti. Seyyathāpi so, bhikkhave[P 4.0194], assakhaḷuṅko ‘pehī’ti vutto, viddho samāno codito sārathinā laṅghati, purimakāyaṃ paggaṇhati purime pāde; tathūpamāhaṃ, bhikkhave, imaṃ puggalaṃ vadāmi. Evarūpopi, bhikkhave, idhekacco purisakhaḷuṅko hoti. Ayaṃ, bhikkhave, pañcamo purisadoso.

“Furthermore, monks, monks accuse a monk of an offense. That monk, being accused of an offense by the monks, waves his arms about in the midst of the Saṅgha. Just as that vicious horse, monks, when told ‘Go!’ by the charioteer, being prodded and urged on, leaps, rearing up its forebody and forefeet; in the same way, monks, I say this person is comparable. Of such a nature, monks, is a certain vicious man here. This, monks, is the fifth fault of a man.

“Puna [M 3.0036] caparaṃ, bhikkhave, bhikkhū bhikkhuṃ āpattiyā codenti. So bhikkhu bhikkhūhi āpattiyā codiyamāno anādiyitvā saṅghaṃ anādiyitvā codakaṃ sāpattikova yena kāmaṃ pakkamati. Seyyathāpi so, bhikkhave, assakhaḷuṅko ‘pehī’ti vutto, viddho samāno codito sārathinā anādiyitvā sārathiṃ anādiyitvā patodalaṭṭhiṃ dantehi mukhādhānaṃ vidhaṃsitvā yena kāmaṃ pakkamati; tathūpamāhaṃ, bhikkhave, imaṃ puggalaṃ vadāmi. Evarūpopi, bhikkhave, idhekacco purisakhaḷuṅko hoti. Ayaṃ, bhikkhave, chaṭṭho purisadoso.

“Furthermore, monks, monks accuse a monk of an offense. That monk, being accused of an offense by the monks, disregarding the Saṅgha and disregarding the accuser, while still having the offense, goes away to whatever place he desires. Just as that vicious horse, monks, when told ‘Go!’ by the charioteer, being prodded and urged on, disregarding the charioteer and disregarding the goad-stick, chews through the bit with its teeth and goes off wherever it pleases; in the same way, monks, I say this person is comparable. Of such a nature, monks, is a certain vicious man here. This, monks, is the sixth fault of a man.

“Puna caparaṃ, bhikkhave, bhikkhū bhikkhuṃ āpattiyā codenti. So bhikkhu bhikkhūhi āpattiyā codiyamāno ‘nevāhaṃ āpannomhi, na panāhaṃ āpannomhī’ti so tuṇhībhāvena saṅghaṃ viheṭheti viheseti (pī. ka.). Seyyathāpi so, bhikkhave, assakhaḷuṅko ‘pehī’ti vutto, viddho samāno codito sārathinā neva abhikkamati no paṭikkamati tattheva khīlaṭṭhāyī ṭhito hoti; tathūpamāhaṃ, bhikkhave, imaṃ puggalaṃ vadāmi. Evarūpopi, bhikkhave, idhekacco purisakhaḷuṅko hoti. Ayaṃ, bhikkhave, sattamo purisadoso.

Furthermore, monks, monks accuse a monk of an offense. That monk, being accused of an offense by the monks, thinking, 'I have not committed an offense; indeed, I have not committed an offense,' vexes the Saṅgha by his silence. Just as, monks, a bad horse, when told 'Go!', being prodded and urged on by the charioteer, neither goes forward nor retreats, but stands right there, standing immovably like a post; I say, monks, that this person is of such a likeness. In this world, monks, a certain bad man is of such a nature. This, monks, is the seventh fault of a man.

“Puna caparaṃ, bhikkhave, bhikkhū bhikkhuṃ āpattiyā codenti. So bhikkhu bhikkhūhi āpattiyā [V 3.0037] codiyamāno evamāha [T 4.0198][P 4.0195] – ‘kiṃ nu kho tumhe āyasmanto atibāḷhaṃ mayi byāvaṭā yāva idaṃ padaṃ sīhaḷapotthake natthi idānāhaṃ sikkhaṃ paccakkhāya hīnāyāvattissāmī’ti. So sikkhaṃ paccakkhāya hīnāyāvattitvā evamāha – ‘idāni kho tumhe āyasmanto attamanā hothā’ti? Seyyathāpi so, bhikkhave, assakhaḷuṅko ‘pehī’ti vutto, viddho samāno codito sārathinā purime ca pāde saṃharitvā pacchime ca pāde saṃharitvā tattheva cattāro pāde abhinisīdati; tathūpamāhaṃ, bhikkhave, imaṃ puggalaṃ vadāmi. Evarūpopi, bhikkhave, idhekacco purisakhaḷuṅko hoti. Ayaṃ, bhikkhave, aṭṭhamo purisadoso. Ime kho, bhikkhave, aṭṭha purisakhaḷuṅkā aṭṭha ca purisadosā”ti. Catutthaṃ.

Furthermore, monks, monks accuse a monk of an offense. That monk, being accused of an offense by the monks, says thus: 'Why are you venerables so excessively striving against me that I shall now renounce the training and return to the lower life?' He, having renounced the training and returned to the lower life, says thus: 'Now, indeed, are you venerables pleased?' Just as, monks, a bad horse, when told 'Go!', being prodded and urged on by the charioteer, having drawn in its forefeet and having drawn in its hind feet, sits down right there on all four feet; I say, monks, that this person is of such a likeness. In this world, monks, a certain bad man is of such a nature. This, monks, is the eighth fault of a man. These, indeed, monks, are the eight bad men and the eight faults of a man. The Fourth.

5. Malasuttaṃ

5. The Discourse on Stains

15. “Aṭṭhimāni[M 3.0037], bhikkhave, malāni. Katamāni aṭṭha? Asajjhāyamalā, bhikkhave, mantā; anuṭṭhānamalā, bhikkhave, gharā; malaṃ, bhikkhave, vaṇṇassa kosajjaṃ; pamādo, bhikkhave, rakkhato malaṃ; malaṃ, bhikkhave, itthiyā duccaritaṃ; maccheraṃ, bhikkhave, dadato malaṃ; malā, bhikkhave, pāpakā akusalā dhammā asmiṃ loke paramhi ca; tato tato ca (syā. pī.), bhikkhave, malā malataraṃ avijjā paramaṃ malaṃ. Imāni kho, bhikkhave, aṭṭha malānī”ti.

15. “Monks, there are these eight stains. Which eight? Monks, non-recitation is the stain of sacred texts; lack of exertion is the stain of households; laziness is the stain of beauty; heedlessness is the stain of one who guards; misconduct is the stain of a woman; stinginess is the stain of a giver; evil, unwholesome states are stains in this world and in the next. But, monks, a greater stain than these stains is ignorance, the supreme stain. These, monks, are the eight stains.”

“Asajjhāyamalā mantā, anuṭṭhānamalā gharā;

Malaṃ vaṇṇassa kosajjaṃ, pamādo rakkhato malaṃ.

“Non-recitation is the stain of sacred texts, lack of exertion is the stain of households; laziness is the stain of beauty, heedlessness is the stain of one who guards.”

“Malitthiyā duccaritaṃ, maccheraṃ dadato malaṃ;

Malā ve pāpakā dhammā, asmiṃ loke paramhi ca;

Tato malā malataraṃ, avijjā paramaṃ mala”nti. pañcamaṃ;

“Misconduct is the stain of a woman, stinginess is the stain of a giver; indeed, evil states are stains in this world and in the next. But a greater stain than these stains is ignorance, the supreme stain.” The Fifth.

6. Dūteyyasuttaṃ

6. The Discourse on the Envoy

16. cūḷava. 347 “Aṭṭhahi[T 4.0199][P 4.0196], bhikkhave, dhammehi samannāgato bhikkhu dūteyyaṃ gantumarahati. Katamehi aṭṭhahi? Idha, bhikkhave, bhikkhu sotā ca hoti, sāvetā ca, uggahetā ca, dhāretā ca, viññātā [V 3.0038] ca, viññāpetā ca, kusalo ca sahitāsahitassa, no ca kalahakārako – imehi kho, bhikkhave, aṭṭhahi dhammehi samannāgato bhikkhu dūteyyaṃ gantumarahati. Aṭṭhahi, bhikkhave, dhammehi samannāgato sāriputto dūteyyaṃ gantumarahati. Katamehi aṭṭhahi? Idha, bhikkhave, sāriputto sotā ca hoti, sāvetā ca, uggahetā ca, dhāretā ca, viññātā ca, viññāpetā ca, kusalo ca sahitāsahitassa, no ca kalahakārako. Imehi kho, bhikkhave, aṭṭhahi dhammehi samannāgato sāriputto dūteyyaṃ gantumarahatī”ti.

16. “Monks, a monk endowed with eight qualities is worthy to go on a mission. With which eight? Here, monks, a monk is one who listens and one who causes others to listen, one who learns and one who retains, one who understands and one who makes others understand, one who is skilled in what is beneficial and what is not, and one who is not a maker of quarrels. A monk endowed with these eight qualities, monks, is worthy to go on a mission. Monks, Sāriputta is endowed with eight qualities and is worthy to go on a mission. With which eight? Here, monks, Sāriputta is one who listens and one who causes others to listen, one who learns and one who retains, one who understands and one who makes others understand, one who is skilled in what is beneficial and what is not, and one who is not a maker of quarrels. Sāriputta, monks, being endowed with these eight qualities, is worthy to go on a mission.”

“Yo ve na byathati na vedhati (sī.), na byādhati (syā. pī.) patvā, parisaṃ uggavādiniṃ uggavādinaṃ (sī.), uggahavādinaṃ (syā. pī.), uggatavādiniṃ (ka.);

Na ca hāpeti vacanaṃ, na ca chādeti sāsanaṃ.

“He who, indeed, upon reaching an assembly of fierce debaters, is not perturbed, and neither diminishes the message nor conceals the teaching;”

“Asandiddhañca [M 3.0038] bhaṇati asandiddho ca akkhāti (cūḷava. 347), pucchito na ca kuppati;

Sa ve tādisako bhikkhu, dūteyyaṃ gantumarahatī”ti. chaṭṭhaṃ;

“He speaks without ambiguity, and when questioned, does not become angry; such a monk, indeed, is worthy to go on a mission.” The Sixth.

7. Paṭhamabandhanasuttaṃ

7. The First Discourse on Bonds

17. “Aṭṭhahi, bhikkhave, ākārehi itthī purisaṃ bandhati. Katamehi aṭṭhahi? Ruṇṇena, bhikkhave, itthī purisaṃ bandhati; hasitena, bhikkhave, itthī purisaṃ bandhati; bhaṇitena, bhikkhave, itthī purisaṃ bandhati; ākappena, bhikkhave, itthī purisaṃ bandhati[P 4.0197]; vanabhaṅgena, bhikkhave, itthī purisaṃ bandhati; gandhena, bhikkhave, itthī purisaṃ bandhati; rasena, bhikkhave, itthī purisaṃ bandhati; phassena, bhikkhave, itthī purisaṃ bandhati. Imehi kho, bhikkhave, aṭṭhahākārehi [T 4.0200] itthī purisaṃ bandhati. Te, bhikkhave, sattā subaddhā subandhā (sī. syā. ka.), ye yeva (syā. pī. ka.) phassena baddhā”ti bandhāti (sī. syā. ka.). Sattamaṃ.

17. “Monks, a woman binds a man in eight ways. What eight? By weeping, monks, a woman binds a man; by laughing, monks, a woman binds a man; by speaking, monks, a woman binds a man; by demeanor, monks, a woman binds a man; by a gift from the forest, monks, a woman binds a man; by scent, monks, a woman binds a man; by taste, monks, a woman binds a man; by touch, monks, a woman binds a man. These, monks, are the eight ways a woman binds a man. Those beings, monks, are well bound who are bound by touch.” The Seventh.

8. Dutiyabandhanasuttaṃ

8. The Second Discourse on Bonds

18. “Aṭṭhahi, bhikkhave, ākārehi puriso itthiṃ bandhati. Katamehi aṭṭhahi? Ruṇṇena, bhikkhave, puriso itthiṃ bandhati; hasitena, bhikkhave, puriso itthiṃ bandhati; bhaṇitena, bhikkhave, puriso itthiṃ bandhati; ākappena, bhikkhave, puriso itthiṃ bandhati; vanabhaṅgena, bhikkhave, puriso itthiṃ bandhati; gandhena, bhikkhave, puriso itthiṃ bandhati; rasena, bhikkhave[V 3.0039], puriso itthiṃ bandhati; phassena, bhikkhave, puriso itthiṃ bandhati. Imehi kho, bhikkhave, aṭṭhahākārehi puriso itthiṃ bandhati. Te, bhikkhave, sattā subaddhā, ye phassena baddhā”ti. Aṭṭhamaṃ.

18. “Monks, a man binds a woman in eight ways. What eight? By weeping, monks, a man binds a woman; by laughing, monks, a man binds a woman; by speaking, monks, a man binds a woman; by demeanor, monks, a man binds a woman; by a gift from the forest, monks, a man binds a woman; by scent, monks, a man binds a woman; by taste, monks, a man binds a woman; by touch, monks, a man binds a woman. These, monks, are the eight ways a man binds a woman. Those beings, monks, are well bound who are bound by touch.” The Eighth.

9. Pahārādasuttaṃ

9. The Discourse to Pahārāda

19. Ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā verañjāyaṃ vi harati naḷerupucimandamūle. Atha kho pahārādo asurindo yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ aṭṭhāsi. Ekamantaṃ ṭhitaṃ kho pahārādaṃ asurindaṃ bhagavā etadavoca –

19. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling in Verañjā at the foot of Naleru’s Pucimanda tree. Then Pahārāda, the lord of the asuras, went to the Blessed One, and after approaching and paying homage to him, stood to one side. The Blessed One said this to Pahārāda, the lord of the asuras, as he was standing to one side:

“Api [M 3.0039] kiṃ (ka.) pana, pahārāda[P 4.0198], asurā mahāsamudde abhiramantī”ti? “Abhiramanti, bhante, asurā mahāsamudde”ti. “Kati pana, pahārāda, mahāsamudde acchariyā abbhutā dhammā abbhutadhammā (syā. ka.) cūḷava. 384 passitabbaṃ, ye disvā disvā asurā mahāsamudde abhiramantī”ti? “Aṭṭha, bhante, mahāsamudde acchariyā abbhutā dhammā, ye disvā disvā asurā mahāsamudde abhiramanti. Katame aṭṭha? Mahāsamuddo, bhante, anupubbaninno anupubbapoṇo anupubbapabbhāro[T 4.0201], na āyatakeneva papāto. Yampi, bhante, mahāsamuddo anupubbaninno anupubbapoṇo anupubbapabbhāro, na āyatakeneva papāto. Ayaṃ, bhante, mahāsamudde paṭhamo acchariyo abbhuto dhammo, yaṃ disvā disvā asurā mahāsamudde abhiramanti.

“Pahārāda, do the asuras delight in the great ocean?” “They do, venerable sir, the asuras delight in the great ocean.” “And how many, Pahārāda, are the wonderful and marvelous things in the great ocean, seeing which again and again the asuras delight in it?” “There are eight wonderful and marvelous things in the great ocean, venerable sir, seeing which again and again the asuras delight in it. What are the eight? Venerable sir, the great ocean gradually slopes, gradually shelves, gradually inclines; it is not an abrupt precipice. That the great ocean gradually slopes, gradually shelves, gradually inclines, and is not an abrupt precipice—this, venerable sir, is the first wonderful and marvelous thing in the great ocean, seeing which again and again the asuras delight in it.

“Puna caparaṃ, bhante, mahāsamuddo ṭhitadhammo velaṃ nātivattati. Yampi, bhante, mahāsamuddo ṭhitadhammo velaṃ nātivattati; ayaṃ ayampi (ka.), bhante, mahāsamudde dutiyo acchariyo abbhuto dhammo yaṃ disvā disvā asurā mahāsamudde abhiramanti.

“Furthermore, venerable sir, the great ocean is of a stable nature and does not transgress its bounds. That the great ocean is of a stable nature and does not transgress its bounds—this, venerable sir, is the second wonderful and marvelous thing in the great ocean, seeing which again and again the asuras delight in it.

“Puna caparaṃ, bhante, mahāsamuddo na matena kuṇapena saṃvasati saṃvattati (syā.). Yaṃ hoti mahāsamudde mataṃ kuṇapaṃ, taṃ khippameva khippaṃyeva (sī.), khippaṃeva (pī.), khippaññeva (cūḷava. 384) tīraṃ vāheti, thalaṃ ussāreti. Yampi, bhante, mahāsamuddo na matena kuṇapena saṃvasati, yaṃ hoti mahāsamudde mataṃ kuṇapaṃ, taṃ khippameva tīraṃ vāheti, thalaṃ ussāreti; ayaṃ, bhante, mahāsamudde tatiyo acchariyo abbhuto dhammo, yaṃ disvā disvā asurā mahāsamudde abhiramanti.

“Furthermore, venerable sir, the great ocean does not remain with a dead corpse. Whatever dead corpse is in the great ocean, it quickly carries it to the shore and casts it onto dry land. That the great ocean does not remain with a dead corpse, and that whatever dead corpse is in the great ocean, it quickly carries it to the shore and casts it onto dry land—this, venerable sir, is the third wonderful and marvelous thing in the great ocean, seeing which again and again the asuras delight in it.

“Puna [V 3.0040] caparaṃ, bhante, yā kāci mahānadiyo, seyyathidaṃ – gaṅgā yamunā aciravatī sarabhū mahī, tā mahāsamuddaṃ patvā pattā (ka., cūḷava. 384) jahanti purimāni nāmagottāni, ‘mahāsamuddo’ [P 4.0199] tveva saṅkhaṃ gacchanti. Yampi, bhante, yā kāci mahānadiyo, seyyathidaṃ – gaṅgā yamunā aciravatī sarabhū mahī, tā mahāsamuddaṃ patvā jahanti purimāni nāmagottāni, ‘mahāsamuddo’ tveva saṅkhaṃ gacchanti; ayaṃ, bhante, mahāsamudde catuttho acchariyo abbhuto dhammo, yaṃ disvā disvā asurā mahāsamudde abhiramanti.

“Furthermore, venerable sir, whatever great rivers there are—such as the Gaṅgā, Yamunā, Aciravatī, Sarabhū, and Mahī—upon reaching the great ocean, they abandon their former names and lineages and are reckoned simply as ‘the great ocean.’ That these great rivers, such as the Gaṅgā, Yamunā, Aciravatī, Sarabhū, and Mahī, upon reaching the great ocean, abandon their former names and lineages and are reckoned simply as ‘the great ocean’—this, venerable sir, is the fourth wonderful and marvelous thing in the great ocean, seeing which again and again the asuras delight in it.

“Puna [M 3.0040] caparaṃ, bhante, yā ca yā kāci (syā. pī. ka.) loke savantiyo mahāsamuddaṃ [T 4.0202] appenti yā ca antalikkhā dhārā papatanti, na tena mahāsamuddassa ūnattaṃ vā pūrattaṃ vā paññāyati. Yampi, bhante, yā ca loke savantiyo mahāsamuddaṃ appenti yā ca antalikkhā dhārā papatanti, na tena mahāsamuddassa ūnattaṃ vā pūrattaṃ vā paññāyati; ayaṃ, bhante, mahāsamudde pañcamo acchariyo abbhuto dhammo, yaṃ disvā disvā asurā mahāsamudde abhiramanti.

“Furthermore, venerable sir, the rivers of the world flow into the great ocean and streams of rain fall from the sky, yet no emptying or filling of the great ocean is apparent on that account. That the rivers of the world flow into the great ocean and streams of rain fall from the sky, yet no emptying or filling of the great ocean is apparent on that account—this, venerable sir, is the fifth wonderful and marvelous thing in the great ocean, seeing which again and again the asuras delight in it.

“Puna caparaṃ, bhante, mahāsamuddo ekaraso loṇaraso. Yampi, bhante, mahāsamuddo ekaraso loṇaraso; ayaṃ, bhante, mahāsamudde chaṭṭho acchariyo abbhuto dhammo, yaṃ disvā disvā asurā mahāsamudde abhiramanti.

“Furthermore, venerable sir, the great ocean has a single taste, the taste of salt. That the great ocean has a single taste, the taste of salt—this, venerable sir, is the sixth wonderful and marvelous thing in the great ocean, seeing which again and again the asuras delight in it.

“Puna caparaṃ, bhante, mahāsamuddo bahuratano pahūtaratano (ka.) anekaratano. Tatrimāni ratanāni, seyyathidaṃ – muttā maṇi veḷuriyo saṅkho silā pavāḷaṃ rajataṃ jātarūpaṃ lohitako masāragallaṃ. Yampi, bhante, mahāsamuddo bahuratano anekaratano; tatrimāni ratanāni, seyyathidaṃ – muttā maṇi veḷuriyo saṅkho silā pavāḷaṃ rajataṃ jātarūpaṃ lohitako masāragallaṃ. Ayaṃ, bhante, mahāsamudde [P 4.0200] sattamo acchariyo abbhuto dhammo, yaṃ disvā disvā asurā mahāsamudde abhiramanti.

“Furthermore, venerable sir, the great ocean has many and various jewels. Therein are these jewels: pearl, gem, beryl, conch, crystal, coral, silver, gold, ruby, and cat's-eye. That the great ocean has many and various jewels, and that therein are these jewels—namely, pearl, gem, beryl, conch, crystal, coral, silver, gold, ruby, and cat's-eye—this, venerable sir, is the seventh wonderful and marvelous thing in the great ocean, seeing which again and again the asuras delight in it.

“Puna caparaṃ, bhante, mahāsamuddo mahataṃ bhūtānaṃ āvāso. Tatrime bhūtā – timi timiṅgalo timirapiṅgalo timitimiṅgalā timirapiṅgalā (sī.), timitimiṅgalā timiramiṅgalā (syā. pī.) asurā nāgā gandhabbā. Santi mahāsamudde yojanasatikāpi attabhāvā, dviyojanasatikāpi attabhāvā, tiyojanasatikāpi attabhāvā, catuyojanasatikāpi attabhāvā, pañcayojanasatikāpi attabhāvā. Yampi, bhante, mahāsamuddo mahataṃ [T 4.0203] bhūtānaṃ [V 3.0041] āvāso; tatrime bhūtā – timi timiṅgalo timirapiṅgalo asurā nāgā gandhabbā; santi mahāsamudde yojanasatikāpi attabhāvā…pe… dviyojana… tiyojana… catuyojana… pañcayojanasatikāpi attabhāvā; ayaṃ, bhante, mahāsamudde aṭṭhamo acchariyo abbhuto dhammo, yaṃ disvā disvā asurā mahāsamudde abhiramanti. Ime kho, bhante[M 3.0041], mahāsamudde aṭṭha acchariyā abbhutā dhammā, ye disvā disvā asurā mahāsamudde abhiramantīti.

“Furthermore, venerable sir, the great ocean is the abode of great beings. Therein are these beings: the timi, the timiṅgala, the timirapiṅgala, asuras, nāgas, and gandhabbas. In the great ocean there are beings with bodies of one hundred, two hundred, three hundred, four hundred, and five hundred yojanas. That the great ocean, venerable sir, is the abode of great beings; that therein are these beings—the timi, the timiṅgala, the timirapiṅgala, asuras, nāgas, and gandhabbas; and that in the great ocean there are beings with bodies of one hundred yojanas… two hundred… three hundred… four hundred… and five hundred yojanas—this, venerable sir, is the eighth wonderful and marvelous thing in the great ocean, seeing which again and again the asuras delight in it. These, venerable sir, are the eight wonderful and marvelous things in the great ocean, seeing which again and again the asuras delight in it.”

“Api pana, bhante, bhikkhū imasmiṃ dhammavinaye abhiramantī”ti? “Abhiramanti, pahārāda, bhikkhū imasmiṃ dhammavinaye”ti. “Kati pana, bhante, imasmiṃ dhammavinaye acchariyā abbhutā dhammā, ye disvā disvā bhikkhū imasmiṃ dhammavinaye abhiramantī”ti? “Aṭṭha, pahārāda, imasmiṃ dhammavinaye acchariyā abbhutā dhammā, ye disvā disvā bhikkhū imasmiṃ dhammavinaye abhiramanti. Katame aṭṭha? Seyyathāpi, pahārāda, mahāsamuddo anupubbaninno anupubbapoṇo anupubbapabbhāro, na āyatakeneva papāto[P 4.0201]; evamevaṃ kho, pahārāda, imasmiṃ dhammavinaye anupubbasikkhā anupubbakiriyā anupubbapaṭipadā, na āyatakeneva aññāpaṭivedho. Yampi, pahārāda, imasmiṃ dhammavinaye anupubbasikkhā anupubbakiriyā anupubbapaṭipadā, na āyatakeneva aññāpaṭivedho. Ayaṃ, pahārāda, imasmiṃ dhammavinaye paṭhamo acchariyo abbhuto dhammo, yaṃ disvā disvā bhikkhū imasmiṃ dhammavinaye abhiramanti.
