“Venerable sir, do the bhikkhus delight in this Dhamma and Discipline?” “They do, Pahārāda, the bhikkhus delight in this Dhamma and Discipline.” “How many, venerable sir, are the wonderful and marvelous qualities in this Dhamma and Discipline, seeing which again and again the bhikkhus delight in this Dhamma and Discipline?” “There are eight wonderful and marvelous qualities in this Dhamma and Discipline, Pahārāda, seeing which again and again the bhikkhus delight in this Dhamma and Discipline. What are the eight? Just as, Pahārāda, the great ocean gradually slopes, gradually deepens, and gradually inclines, without any abrupt precipice; even so, Pahārāda, in this Dhamma and Discipline there is a gradual training, a gradual practice, and a gradual path, with no sudden penetration to final knowledge. That in this Dhamma and Discipline, Pahārāda, there is a gradual training, a gradual practice, and a gradual path, with no sudden penetration to final knowledge—this, Pahārāda, is the first wonderful and marvelous quality in this Dhamma and Discipline, seeing which again and again the bhikkhus delight in this Dhamma and Discipline.”

“Seyyathāpi, pahārāda, mahāsamuddo ṭhitadhammo velaṃ nātivattati; evamevaṃ kho, pahārāda, yaṃ mayā sāvakānaṃ sikkhāpadaṃ paññattaṃ taṃ mama sāvakā [T 4.0204] jīvitahetupi nātikkamanti. Yampi, pahārāda, mayā sāvakānaṃ sikkhāpadaṃ paññattaṃ taṃ mama sāvakā jīvitahetupi nātikkamanti. Ayaṃ, pahārāda, imasmiṃ dhammavinaye dutiyo acchariyo abbhuto dhammo, yaṃ disvā disvā bhikkhū imasmiṃ dhammavinaye abhiramanti.

“Just as, Pahārāda, the great ocean is of a stable nature and does not transgress its boundary; even so, Pahārāda, whatever training rule has been laid down by me for my disciples, my disciples do not transgress it even for the sake of their lives. That my disciples, Pahārāda, do not transgress, even for the sake of their lives, the training rule laid down by me for them—this, Pahārāda, is the second wonderful and marvelous quality in this Dhamma and Discipline, seeing which again and again the bhikkhus delight in this Dhamma and Discipline.”

“Seyyathāpi, pahārāda, mahāsamuddo na matena kuṇapena saṃvasati. Yaṃ hoti mahāsamudde mataṃ kuṇapaṃ, taṃ khippameva tīraṃ vāheti thalaṃ ussāreti; evamevaṃ kho, pahārāda, yo so puggalo dussīlo pāpadhammo asucisaṅkassarasamācāro paṭicchannakammanto assamaṇo samaṇapaṭiñño [V 3.0042] abrahmacārī brahmacāripaṭiñño antopūti avassuto kasambujāto, na tena saṅgho saṃvasati; khippameva naṃ sannipatitvā ukkhipati.

“Just as, Pahārāda, the great ocean does not dwell with a dead corpse—whatever dead corpse is in the great ocean, it quickly carries it to the shore and casts it up on high ground; even so, Pahārāda, the Saṅgha does not dwell with a person who is immoral, of evil nature, impure, of suspect conduct, of secret actions, not a recluse but claiming to be a recluse, not living the holy life but claiming to live the holy life, rotten within, corrupt, and a rubbish heap; having assembled, they quickly expel him.”

“Kiñcāpi so hoti majjhe bhikkhusaṅghassa sannisinno, atha kho so ārakāva saṅghamhā saṅgho ca tena. Yampi, pahārāda, yo so puggalo [M 3.0042] dussīlo pāpadhammo asucisaṅkassarasamācāro paṭicchannakammanto assamaṇo samaṇapaṭiñño abrahmacārī brahmacāripaṭiñño antopūti avassuto kasambujāto, na [P 4.0202] tena saṅgho saṃvasati; khippameva naṃ sannipatitvā ukkhipati; kiñcāpi so hoti majjhe bhikkhusaṅghassa sannisinno, atha kho so ārakāva saṅghamhā saṅgho ca tena. Ayaṃ, pahārāda, imasmiṃ dhammavinaye tatiyo acchariyo abbhuto dhammo, yaṃ disvā disvā bhikkhū imasmiṃ dhammavinaye abhiramanti.

“And although he may be sitting in the midst of the community of bhikkhus, yet he is far from the Saṅgha, and the Saṅgha is far from him. That the Saṅgha, Pahārāda, does not dwell with a person who is immoral, of evil nature, impure, of suspect conduct, of secret actions, not a recluse but claiming to be a recluse, not living the holy life but claiming to live the holy life, rotten within, corrupt, and a rubbish heap; that having assembled, they quickly expel him; and that although he may be sitting in the midst of the community of bhikkhus, yet he is far from the Saṅgha, and the Saṅgha is far from him—this, Pahārāda, is the third wonderful and marvelous quality in this Dhamma and Discipline, seeing which again and again the bhikkhus delight in this Dhamma and Discipline.”

“Seyyathāpi, pahārāda, yā kāci mahānadiyo, seyyathidaṃ – gaṅgā yamunā aciravatī sarabhū mahī, tā mahāsamuddaṃ patvā jahanti purimāni nāmagottāni, ‘mahāsamuddo’ [T 4.0205] tveva saṅkhaṃ gacchanti; evamevaṃ kho, pahārāda, cattārome vaṇṇā – khattiyā, brāhmaṇā, vessā, suddā, te tathāgatappavedite dhammavinaye agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajitvā jahanti purimāni nāmagottāni, ‘samaṇā sakyaputtiyā’ tveva samaṇo sakyaputtiyo tveva (syā. ka.) saṅkhaṃ gacchanti. Yampi, pahārāda, cattārome vaṇṇā – khattiyā, brāhmaṇā, vessā, suddā, te tathāgatappavedite dhammavinaye agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajitvā jahanti purimāni nāmagottāni, ‘samaṇā sakyaputtiyā’ tveva saṅkhaṃ gacchanti. Ayaṃ, pahārāda, imasmiṃ dhammavinaye catuttho acchariyo abbhuto dhammo, yaṃ disvā disvā bhikkhū imasmiṃ dhammavinaye abhiramanti.

“Just as, Pahārāda, whatever great rivers there are—namely, the Ganges, Yamunā, Aciravatī, Sarabhū, and Mahī—upon reaching the great ocean, abandon their former names and clans and are reckoned simply as ‘the great ocean’; even so, Pahārāda, these four castes—khattiyas, brahmins, vessas, and suddas—having gone forth from the household life into homelessness in the Dhamma and Discipline proclaimed by the Tathāgata, abandon their former names and clans and are reckoned simply as ‘recluses, followers of the Sakyan son.’ That these four castes, Pahārāda—khattiyas, brahmins, vessas, and suddas—having gone forth from the household life into homelessness in the Dhamma and Discipline proclaimed by the Tathāgata, abandon their former names and clans and are reckoned simply as ‘recluses, followers of the Sakyan son’—this, Pahārāda, is the fourth wonderful and marvelous quality in this Dhamma and Discipline, seeing which again and again the bhikkhus delight in this Dhamma and Discipline.”

“Seyyathāpi, pahārāda, yā ca loke savantiyo mahāsamuddaṃ appenti yā ca antalikkhā dhārā papatanti, na tena mahāsamuddassa ūnattaṃ vā pūrattaṃ vā paññāyati; evamevaṃ kho, pahārāda, bahū cepi bhikkhū anupādisesāya nibbānadhātuyā parinibbāyanti, na tena nibbānadhātuyā ūnattaṃ vā pūrattaṃ vā paññāyati. Yampi, pahārāda, bahū cepi bhikkhū anupādisesāya nibbānadhātuyā [P 4.0203] parinibbāyanti, na tena nibbānadhātuyā ūnattaṃ vā pūrattaṃ vā paññāyati. Ayaṃ, pahārāda, imasmiṃ dhammavinaye pañcamo acchariyo abbhuto dhammo, yaṃ disvā disvā bhikkhū imasmiṃ dhammavinaye abhiramanti.

“Just as, Pahārāda, all the rivers in the world flow into the great ocean and the rains fall from the sky, yet by this no decrease or increase in the great ocean is perceived; even so, Pahārāda, though many bhikkhus attain final Nibbāna in the Nibbāna-element with no residue remaining, yet by this no decrease or increase in the Nibbāna-element is perceived. That though many bhikkhus, Pahārāda, attain final Nibbāna in the Nibbāna-element with no residue remaining, yet by this no decrease or increase in the Nibbāna-element is perceived—this, Pahārāda, is the fifth wonderful and marvelous quality in this Dhamma and Discipline, seeing which again and again the bhikkhus delight in this Dhamma and Discipline.”

“Seyyathāpi[V 3.0043], pahārāda, mahāsamuddo ekaraso loṇaraso; evamevaṃ kho, pahārāda, ayaṃ dhammavinayo ekaraso, vimuttiraso. Yampi pahārāda[M 3.0043], ayaṃ dhammavinayo ekaraso, vimuttiraso[T 4.0206]; ayaṃ, pahārāda, imasmiṃ dhammavinaye chaṭṭho acchariyo abbhuto dhammo, yaṃ disvā disvā bhikkhū imasmiṃ dhammavinaye abhiramanti.

“Just as, Pahārāda, the great ocean has one taste, the taste of salt; even so, Pahārāda, this Dhamma and Discipline has one taste, the taste of liberation. That this Dhamma and Discipline, Pahārāda, has one taste, the taste of liberation—this, Pahārāda, is the sixth wonderful and marvelous quality in this Dhamma and Discipline, seeing which again and again the bhikkhus delight in this Dhamma and Discipline.”

“Seyyathāpi, pahārāda, mahāsamuddo bahuratano anekaratano; tatrimāni ratanāni, seyyathidaṃ – muttā maṇi veḷuriyo saṅkho silā pavāḷaṃ rajataṃ jātarūpaṃ lohitako masāragallaṃ; evamevaṃ kho, pahārāda, ayaṃ dhammavinayo bahuratano anekaratano. Tatrimāni ratanāni, seyyathidaṃ – cattāro satipaṭṭhānā, cattāro sammappadhānā, cattāro iddhipādā, pañcindriyāni, pañca balāni, satta bojjhaṅgā, ariyo aṭṭhaṅgiko maggo. Yampi, pahārāda, ayaṃ dhammavinayo bahuratano anekaratano; tatrimāni ratanāni, seyyathidaṃ – cattāro satipaṭṭhānā, cattāro sammappadhānā, cattāro iddhipādā, pañcindriyāni, pañca balāni, satta bojjhaṅgā, ariyo aṭṭhaṅgiko maggo; ayaṃ, pahārāda, imasmiṃ dhammavinaye sattamo acchariyo abbhuto dhammo, yaṃ disvā disvā bhikkhū imasmiṃ dhammavinaye abhiramanti.

Just as, Pahārāda, the great ocean is rich in many treasures, a multitude of treasures—these treasures being pearls, gems, beryl, conch, crystal, coral, silver, gold, rubies, and cat's-eye gems; even so, Pahārāda, this Dhamma and Discipline is rich in many treasures, a multitude of treasures. These treasures are the four establishments of mindfulness, the four right strivings, the four bases of spiritual power, the five faculties, the five powers, the seven factors of enlightenment, and the Noble Eightfold Path. That this Dhamma and Discipline, Pahārāda, is rich in many treasures, a multitude of treasures—these treasures being the four establishments of mindfulness, the four right strivings, the four bases of spiritual power, the five faculties, the five powers, the seven factors of enlightenment, and the Noble Eightfold Path—this, Pahārāda, is the seventh wonderful and marvelous quality in this Dhamma and Discipline, seeing which again and again, the bhikkhus delight in this Dhamma and Discipline.

“Seyyathāpi, pahārāda, mahāsamuddo mahataṃ bhūtānaṃ āvāso; tatrime bhūtā – timi timiṅgalo timirapiṅgalo [P 4.0204] asurā nāgā gandhabbā; santi mahāsamudde yojanasatikāpi attabhāvā, dviyojanasatikāpi attabhāvā, tiyojanasatikāpi attabhāvā, catuyojanasatikāpi attabhāvā, pañcayojanasatikāpi attabhāvā; evamevaṃ kho, pahārāda, ayaṃ dhammavinayo mahataṃ bhūtānaṃ āvāso; tatrime bhūtā – sotāpanno sotāpattiphalasacchikiriyāya paṭipanno, sakadāgāmī sakadāgāmiphalasacchikiriyāya [T 4.0207] paṭipanno, anāgāmī anāgāmiphalasacchikiriyāya paṭipanno, arahā arahattāya paṭipanno. Yampi, pahārāda, ayaṃ dhammavinayo mahataṃ bhūtānaṃ āvāso; tatrime bhūtā – sotāpanno sotāpattiphalasacchikiriyāya paṭipanno, sakadāgāmī sakadāgāmiphalasacchikiriyāya paṭipanno, anāgāmī anāgāmiphalasacchikiriyāya paṭipanno, arahā arahattāya paṭipanno; ayaṃ, pahārāda, imasmiṃ dhammavinaye aṭṭhamo acchariyo abbhuto dhammo, yaṃ disvā disvā bhikkhū imasmiṃ dhammavinaye abhiramanti. Ime kho, pahārāda, imasmiṃ dhammavinaye aṭṭha [M 3.0044] acchariyā abbhutā dhammā, ye disvā disvā bhikkhū imasmiṃ dhammavinaye abhiramantī”ti. Navamaṃ.

Just as, Pahārāda, the great ocean is the abode of great beings—these beings being the timi, the timiṅgala, the timirapiṅgala, asuras, nāgas, and gandhabbas; and there are in the great ocean beings with bodies a hundred yojanas in size, beings with bodies two hundred yojanas in size, beings with bodies three hundred yojanas in size, beings with bodies four hundred yojanas in size, and beings with bodies five hundred yojanas in size; even so, Pahārāda, this Dhamma and Discipline is the abode of great beings. These beings are: the stream-enterer and the one practicing for the realization of the fruit of stream-entry; the once-returner and the one practicing for the realization of the fruit of once-returning; the non-returner and the one practicing for the realization of the fruit of non-returning; the Arahant and the one practicing for Arahantship. That this Dhamma and Discipline, Pahārāda, is the abode of great beings—these beings being the stream-enterer and the one practicing for the realization of the fruit of stream-entry; the once-returner and the one practicing for the realization of the fruit of once-returning; the non-returner and the one practicing for the realization of the fruit of non-returning; the Arahant and the one practicing for Arahantship—this, Pahārāda, is the eighth wonderful and marvelous quality in this Dhamma and Discipline, seeing which again and again, the bhikkhus delight in this Dhamma and Discipline. These, Pahārāda, are the eight wonderful and marvelous qualities in this Dhamma and Discipline, seeing which again and again, the bhikkhus delight in this Dhamma and Discipline. Ninth.

10. Uposathasuttaṃ

10. The Discourse on the Uposatha

20. cūḷava. 383; udā. 45; kathā. 346 Ekaṃ [V 3.0044] samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati pubbārāme migāramātupāsāde. Tena kho pana samayena bhagavā tadahuposathe bhikkhusaṅghaparivuto nisinno hoti. Atha kho āyasmā ānando abhikkantāya rattiyā, nikkhante paṭhame yāme, uṭṭhāyāsanā ekaṃsaṃ uttarāsaṅgaṃ karitvā yena bhagavā tenañjaliṃ paṇāmetvā bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “abhikkantā, bhante, ratti, nikkhanto paṭhamo yāmo, ciranisinno bhikkhusaṅgho. Uddisatu, bhante, bhagavā bhikkhūnaṃ pātimokkha”nti.

20. On one occasion the Blessed One was dwelling at Sāvatthī in the Eastern Park, at the palace of Migāra’s mother. Now on that occasion, it being the Uposatha day, the Blessed One was seated surrounded by the Saṅgha of bhikkhus. Then, when the night was advanced and the first watch had passed, the Venerable Ānanda rose from his seat, arranged his upper robe over one shoulder, extended his joined palms toward the Blessed One, and said to him: “Venerable sir, the night is advanced, the first watch has passed. The Saṅgha of bhikkhus has been seated for a long time. Venerable sir, may the Blessed One recite the Pātimokkha to the bhikkhus.”

Evaṃ vutte bhagavā tuṇhī ahosi. Dutiyampi [P 4.0205] kho āyasmā ānando abhikkantāya rattiyā, nikkhante majjhime yāme, uṭṭhāyāsanā ekaṃsaṃ uttarāsaṅgaṃ karitvā yena bhagavā tenañjaliṃ paṇāmetvā bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “abhikkantā, bhante[T 4.0208], ratti, nikkhanto majjhimo yāmo, ciranisinno bhikkhusaṅgho. Uddisatu, bhante, bhagavā bhikkhūnaṃ pātimokkha”nti. Dutiyampi kho bhagavā tuṇhī ahosi. Tatiyampi kho āyasmā ānando abhikkantāya rattiyā, nikkhante pacchime yāme, uddhaste aruṇe, nandimukhiyā rattiyā uṭṭhāyāsanā ekaṃsaṃ uttarāsaṅgaṃ karitvā yena bhagavā tenañjaliṃ paṇāmetvā bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “abhikkantā, bhante, ratti, nikkhanto pacchimo yāmo, uddhastaṃ aruṇaṃ, nandimukhī ratti; ciranisinno bhikkhusaṅgho. Uddisatu, bhante, bhagavā bhikkhūnaṃ pātimokkha”nti. “Aparisuddhā, ānanda, parisā”ti.

When this was said, the Blessed One remained silent. For a second time, when the night was advanced and the middle watch had passed, the Venerable Ānanda rose from his seat, arranged his upper robe over one shoulder, extended his joined palms toward the Blessed One, and said to him: “Venerable sir, the night is advanced, the middle watch has passed. The Saṅgha of bhikkhus has been seated for a long time. Venerable sir, may the Blessed One recite the Pātimokkha to the bhikkhus.” For a second time, the Blessed One remained silent. For a third time, when the night was advanced, the last watch had passed, the dawn had risen, and the face of the night was joyful, the Venerable Ānanda rose from his seat, arranged his upper robe over one shoulder, extended his joined palms toward the Blessed One, and said to him: “Venerable sir, the night is advanced, the last watch has passed, the dawn has risen, and the face of the night is joyful. The Saṅgha of bhikkhus has been seated for a long time. Venerable sir, may the Blessed One recite the Pātimokkha to the bhikkhus.” “Ānanda, the assembly is not pure.”

Atha kho āyasmato mahāmoggallānassa etadahosi – “kaṃ nu kho bhagavā puggalaṃ sandhāya evamāha – ‘aparisuddhā, ānanda, parisā’”ti? Atha kho āyasmā mahāmoggallāno sabbāvantaṃ bhikkhusaṅghaṃ cetasā ceto paricca manasākāsi. Addasā kho āyasmā mahāmoggallāno taṃ puggalaṃ dussīlaṃ pāpadhammaṃ asuciṃ saṅkassarasamācāraṃ paṭicchannakammantaṃ assamaṇaṃ samaṇapaṭiññaṃ abrahmacāriṃ brahmacāripaṭiññaṃ antopūtiṃ avassutaṃ kasambujātaṃ majjhe bhikkhusaṅghassa nisinnaṃ; disvāna uṭṭhāyāsanā yena so puggalo tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā taṃ puggalaṃ [M 3.0045] etadavoca – “uṭṭhehāvuso, diṭṭhosi bhagavatā. Natthi te bhikkhūhi saddhiṃ saṃvāso”ti.

Then this thought occurred to the Venerable Mahā Moggallāna: “In reference to what person, I wonder, did the Blessed One say, ‘Ānanda, the assembly is not pure’?” Then the Venerable Mahā Moggallāna directed his mind, encompassing the entire Saṅgha of bhikkhus with his own mind. The Venerable Mahā Moggallāna saw that person—immoral, of evil character, impure, of suspect conduct, of concealed deeds, not an ascetic but claiming to be an ascetic, not a celibate but claiming to be a celibate, rotten within, oozing with defilements, a rubbish-heap—seated in the midst of the Saṅgha of bhikkhus. Having seen him, he rose from his seat, approached that person, and said to him: “Arise, friend. You have been seen by the Blessed One. There is no living in communion for you with the bhikkhus.”

Evaṃ [V 3.0045] vutte so puggalo tuṇhī ahosi. Dutiyampi kho āyasmā mahāmoggallāno taṃ puggalaṃ etadavoca – “uṭṭhehāvuso, diṭṭhosi bhagavatā. Natthi [T 4.0209] te bhikkhūhi saddhiṃ saṃvāso”ti. Dutiyampi kho so puggalo tuṇhī ahosi. Tatiyampi kho āyasmā mahāmoggallāno taṃ puggalaṃ etadavoca [P 4.0206] – “uṭṭhehāvuso, diṭṭhosi bhagavatā. Natthi te bhikkhūhi saddhiṃ saṃvāso”ti. Tatiyampi kho so puggalo tuṇhī ahosi.

When this was said, that person remained silent. For a second time, the Venerable Mahā Moggallāna said this to that person: “Arise, friend. You have been seen by the Blessed One. There is no communion for you with the monks.” For a second time, that person remained silent. For a third time, the Venerable Mahā Moggallāna said this to that person: “Arise, friend. You have been seen by the Blessed One. There is no communion for you with the monks.” For a third time, that person remained silent.

Atha kho āyasmā mahāmoggallāno taṃ puggalaṃ bāhāyaṃ gahetvā bahidvārakoṭṭhakā nikkhāmetvā sūcighaṭikaṃ datvā yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “nikkhāmito so, bhante, puggalo mayā. Parisuddhā parisā. Uddisatu, bhante, bhagavā bhikkhūnaṃ pātimokkha”nti. “Acchariyaṃ, moggallāna, abbhutaṃ, moggallāna! Yāva bāhā gahaṇāpi nāma so moghapuriso āgamissatī”ti!

Then the Venerable Mahā Moggallāna, taking that person by the arm, expelling him outside the gatehouse, and bolting the gate, approached the Blessed One. Having approached, he said this to the Blessed One: “Venerable Sir, that person has been expelled by me. The assembly is pure. May the Blessed One, Venerable Sir, recite the Pātimokkha to the monks.” “It is wonderful, Moggallāna, it is amazing, Moggallāna, that this worthless man would wait until he was taken by the arm!”

Atha kho bhagavā bhikkhū āmantesi – “tumheva dāni, bhikkhave, uposathaṃ kareyyātha, pātimokkhaṃ uddiseyyātha. Na dānāhaṃ, bhikkhave, ajjatagge uposathaṃ karissāmi, pātimokkhaṃ uddisissāmi. Aṭṭhānametaṃ, bhikkhave, anavakāso yaṃ tathāgato aparisuddhāya parisāya pātimokkhaṃ uddiseyya”.

Then the Blessed One addressed the monks: “Monks, from now on you yourselves should perform the Uposatha and recite the Pātimokkha. From today onwards, monks, I will not perform the Uposatha nor recite the Pātimokkha. It is impossible, monks, it cannot be, that a Tathāgata should recite the Pātimokkha in an impure assembly.”

“Aṭṭhime, bhikkhave, mahāsamudde acchariyā abbhutā dhammā, ye disvā disvā asurā mahāsamudde abhiramanti. Katame aṭṭha? Mahāsamuddo, bhikkhave, anupubbaninno anupubbapoṇo anupubbapabbhāro, na āyatakeneva papāto. Yampi, bhikkhave, mahāsamuddo anupubbaninno anupubbapoṇo anupubbapabbhāro, na āyatakeneva papāto; ayaṃ, bhikkhave, mahāsamudde paṭhamo [T 4.0210] acchariyo abbhuto dhammo, yaṃ disvā disvā asurā mahāsamudde abhiramanti…pe… (yathā purime tathā vitthāretabbo).

“Monks, there are these eight wonderful and amazing qualities of the great ocean, seeing which again and again the asuras delight in the great ocean. What are the eight? Monks, the great ocean gradually slopes, gradually shelves, gradually inclines; there is no abrupt precipice. Monks, that the great ocean gradually slopes, gradually shelves, gradually inclines, and there is no abrupt precipice—this, monks, is the first wonderful and amazing quality of the great ocean, seeing which again and again the asuras delight in the great ocean... (to be elaborated in full as in the previous discourse).”

“Puna caparaṃ, bhikkhave, mahāsamuddo mahataṃ bhūtānaṃ āvāso. Tatrime bhūtā – timi timiṅgalo timirapiṅgalo [P 4.0207] asurā nāgā gandhabbā. Vasanti mahāsamudde yojanasatikāpi attabhāvā…pe… pañcayojanasatikāpi attabhāvā[M 3.0046]. Yampi, bhikkhave, mahāsamuddo mahataṃ bhūtānaṃ āvāso; tatrime bhūtā – timi timiṅgalo timirapiṅgalo asurā nāgā gandhabbā; vasanti [V 3.0046] mahāsamudde yojanasatikāpi attabhāvā…pe… pañcayojanasatikāpi attabhāvā; ayaṃ, bhikkhave, mahāsamudde aṭṭhamo acchariyo abbhuto dhammo, yaṃ disvā disvā asurā mahāsamudde abhiramanti. Ime kho, bhikkhave, mahāsamudde aṭṭha acchariyā abbhutā dhammā, yaṃ disvā disvā asurā mahāsamudde abhiramanti.

“Furthermore, monks, the great ocean is the abode of great beings. These beings are—the timi, the timiṅgala, the timirapiṅgala, the asuras, the nāgas, and the gandhabbas. There are in the great ocean beings with bodies of one hundred yojanas... up to... five hundred yojanas. That the great ocean is the abode of great beings—these beings being the timi, the timiṅgala, the timirapiṅgala, the asuras, the nāgas, and the gandhabbas; and that there are in the great ocean beings with bodies of one hundred yojanas... up to... five hundred yojanas—this, monks, is the eighth wonderful and amazing quality of the great ocean, seeing which again and again the asuras delight in the great ocean. These, monks, are the eight wonderful and amazing qualities of the great ocean, seeing which again and again the asuras delight in the great ocean.”

“Evamevaṃ kho, bhikkhave, aṭṭha imasmiṃ dhammavinaye acchariyā abbhutā dhammā, ye disvā disvā bhikkhū imasmiṃ dhammavinaye abhiramanti. Katame aṭṭha? Seyyathāpi, bhikkhave, mahāsamuddo anupubbaninno anupubbapoṇo anupubbapabbhāro, na āyatakeneva papāto; evamevaṃ kho, bhikkhave, imasmiṃ dhammavinaye anupubbasikkhā anupubbakiriyā anupubbapaṭipadā, na āyatakeneva aññāpaṭivedho. Yampi, bhikkhave, imasmiṃ dhammavinaye anupubbasikkhā anupubbakiriyā anupubbapaṭipadā, na āyatakeneva aññāpaṭivedho; ayaṃ, bhikkhave, imasmiṃ dhammavinaye paṭhamo acchariyo abbhuto dhammo, yaṃ disvā disvā [T 4.0211] bhikkhū imasmiṃ dhammavinaye abhiramanti…pe… seyyathāpi, bhikkhave, mahāsamuddo mahataṃ bhūtānaṃ āvāso; tatrime bhūtā – timi timiṅgalo timirapiṅgalo asurā nāgā gandhabbā, vasanti mahāsamudde yojanasatikāpi attabhāvā…pe… pañcayojanasatikāpi [P 4.0208] attabhāvā; evamevaṃ kho, bhikkhave, ayaṃ dhammavinayo mahataṃ bhūtānaṃ āvāso. Tatrime bhūtā – sotāpanno sotāpattiphalasacchikiriyāya paṭipanno…pe… arahā arahattāya paṭipanno. Yampi, bhikkhave, ayaṃ dhammavinayo mahataṃ bhūtānaṃ āvāso; tatrime bhūtā – sotāpanno sotāpattiphalasacchikiriyāya paṭipanno…pe… arahā arahattāya paṭipanno; ayaṃ, bhikkhave, imasmiṃ dhammavinaye aṭṭhamo acchariyo abbhuto dhammo, yaṃ disvā disvā bhikkhū imasmiṃ dhammavinaye abhiramanti. Ime kho, bhikkhave, imasmiṃ dhammavinaye aṭṭha acchariyā abbhutā dhammā, ye disvā disvā bhikkhū imasmiṃ dhammavinaye abhiramantī”ti. Dasamaṃ.

“In the very same way, monks, there are eight wonderful and amazing qualities in this Dhamma and Discipline, seeing which again and again the monks delight in this Dhamma and Discipline. What are the eight? Just as, monks, the great ocean gradually slopes, gradually shelves, gradually inclines, with no abrupt precipice; in the very same way, monks, in this Dhamma and Discipline there is a gradual training, a gradual practice, a gradual path, with no abrupt penetration to final knowledge. That in this Dhamma and Discipline there is a gradual training, a gradual practice, a gradual path, with no abrupt penetration to final knowledge—this, monks, is the first wonderful and amazing quality in this Dhamma and Discipline, seeing which again and again the monks delight in this Dhamma and Discipline… Just as, monks, the great ocean is the abode of great beings—these beings being the timi, the timiṅgala, the timirapiṅgala, the asuras, the nāgas, and the gandhabbas; and there are in the great ocean beings with bodies of one hundred yojanas... up to... five hundred yojanas; in the very same way, monks, this Dhamma and Discipline is the abode of great beings. These beings are: the stream-enterer and the one practicing for the realization of the fruit of stream-entry… the Arahant and the one practicing for Arahantship. That this Dhamma and Discipline is the abode of great beings—these beings being the stream-enterer and the one practicing for the realization of the fruit of stream-entry… the Arahant and the one practicing for Arahantship—this, monks, is the eighth wonderful and amazing quality in this Dhamma and Discipline, seeing which again and again the monks delight in this Dhamma and Discipline. These, monks, are the eight wonderful and amazing qualities in this Dhamma and Discipline, seeing which again and again the monks delight in this Dhamma and Discipline.” The Tenth Sutta.

Mahāvaggo dutiyo.

The Second Great Chapter.

Tassuddānaṃ –

The summary of its contents:

Verañjo [M 3.0047] sīho ājaññaṃ, khaḷuṅkena malāni ca;

Dūteyyaṃ dve ca bandhanā, pahārādo uposathoti.

Verañja, Sīha, the thoroughbred, with the bit, and stains; the mission, the two bonds, Pahārāda, and the Uposatha.

3. Gahapativaggo

3. The Householder Chapter

1. Paṭhamauggasuttaṃ

1. The First Discourse on Ugga

21. Ekaṃ [V 3.0047] samayaṃ bhagavā vesāliyaṃ viharati mahāvane kūṭāgārasālāyaṃ[T 4.0212]. Tatra kho bhagavā bhikkhū āmantesi “aṭṭhahi, bhikkhave, acchariyehi abbhutehi dhammehi abbhutadhammehi (syā. ka.) samannāgataṃ uggaṃ gahapatiṃ vesālikaṃ dhārethā”ti. Idamavoca [P 4.0209] bhagavā. Idaṃ vatvāna sugato uṭṭhāyāsanā vihāraṃ pāvisi.

21. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Vesālī, in the Great Wood, at the Hall with the Peaked Roof. There the Blessed One addressed the bhikkhus: “Bhikkhus, remember Ugga the householder of Vesālī as one endowed with eight amazing and wonderful qualities.” This is what the Blessed One said. Having said this, the Well-Farer rose from his seat and entered his dwelling.

Atha kho aññataro bhikkhu pubbaṇhasamayaṃ nivāsetvā pattacīvaramādāya yena uggassa gahapatino vesālikassa nivesanaṃ tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā paññatte āsane nisīdi. Atha kho uggo gahapati vesāliko yena so bhikkhu tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā taṃ bhikkhuṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinnaṃ kho uggaṃ gahapatiṃ vesālikaṃ so bhikkhu etadavoca –

Then a certain bhikkhu, having dressed in the morning, taking his bowl and robe, went to the residence of Ugga, the householder of Vesālī. Having approached, he sat down on the prepared seat. Then Ugga, the householder of Vesālī, approached that bhikkhu; having approached, he paid homage to that bhikkhu and sat down to one side. To Ugga, the householder of Vesālī, who was sitting to one side, that bhikkhu said this:

“Aṭṭhahi kho tvaṃ, gahapati, acchariyehi abbhutehi dhammehi samannāgato bhagavatā byākato. Katame te, gahapati, aṭṭha acchariyā abbhutā dhammā, yehi tvaṃ samannāgato bhagavatā byākato”ti? “Na kho ahaṃ, bhante, jānāmi – katamehi aṭṭhahi acchariyehi abbhutehi dhammehi samannāgato bhagavatā byākatoti. Api ca, bhante, ye me aṭṭha acchariyā abbhutā dhammā saṃvijjanti, taṃ suṇohi, sādhukaṃ manasi karohi; bhāsissāmī”ti. “Evaṃ, gahapatī”ti kho so bhikkhu uggassa gahapatino vesālikassa paccassosi. Uggo gahapati vesāliko etadavoca – “yadāhaṃ, bhante, bhagavantaṃ paṭhamaṃ dūratova addasaṃ; saha dassaneneva me, bhante[M 3.0048], bhagavato cittaṃ pasīdi. Ayaṃ kho me, bhante, paṭhamo acchariyo abbhuto dhammo saṃvijjati”.

“Householder, you have been declared by the Blessed One as one endowed with eight amazing and wonderful qualities. What, householder, are those eight amazing and wonderful qualities with which you are endowed and have been so declared by the Blessed One?” “Venerable sir, I do not know with which eight amazing and wonderful qualities I have been declared by the Blessed One as being endowed. However, venerable sir, there are eight amazing and wonderful qualities that are found in me. Listen to that, attend to it well; I will speak.” “So be it, householder,” that bhikkhu replied to Ugga, the householder of Vesālī. Ugga, the householder of Vesālī, said this: “Venerable sir, when I first saw the Blessed One from afar, just at the sight of the Blessed One my mind gained confidence. This, venerable sir, is the first amazing and wonderful quality that is found in me.”

“So kho ahaṃ, bhante, pasannacitto bhagavantaṃ payirupāsiṃ. Tassa me bhagavā anupubbiṃ kathaṃ [V 3.0048] kathesi[T 4.0213], seyyathidaṃ – dānakathaṃ sīlakathaṃ saggakathaṃ; kāmānaṃ ādīnavaṃ okāraṃ saṃkilesaṃ, nekkhamme ānisaṃsaṃ pakāsesi. Yadā maṃ bhagavā aññāsi kallacittaṃ muducittaṃ vinīvaraṇacittaṃ udaggacittaṃ pasannacittaṃ, atha yā buddhānaṃ [P 4.0210] sāmukkaṃsikā dhammadesanā taṃ pakāsesi – dukkhaṃ, samudayaṃ, nirodhaṃ, maggaṃ. Seyyathāpi nāma suddhaṃ vatthaṃ apagatakāḷakaṃ sammadeva rajanaṃ paṭiggaṇheyya; evamevaṃ kho me tasmiṃyeva āsane virajaṃ vītamalaṃ dhammacakkhuṃ udapādi – ‘yaṃ kiñci samudayadhammaṃ, sabbaṃ taṃ nirodhadhamma’nti. So kho ahaṃ, bhante, diṭṭhadhammo pattadhammo viditadhammo pariyogāḷhadhammo tiṇṇavicikiccho vigatakathaṃkatho vesārajjappatto aparappaccayo satthusāsane tattheva buddhañca dhammañca saṅghañca saraṇaṃ agamāsiṃ, brahmacariyapañcamāni ca sikkhāpadāni samādiyiṃ. Ayaṃ kho me, bhante, dutiyo acchariyo abbhuto dhammo saṃvijjati.

“Venerable sir, with a confident mind, I attended upon the Blessed One. The Blessed One gave me a gradual discourse, that is to say: a discourse on giving, a discourse on virtue, and a discourse on heaven; he explained the danger, the inferiority, and the defilement of sensual pleasures, and the benefit of renunciation. When the Blessed One knew my mind to be ready, pliable, free from hindrances, uplifted, and confident, he then explained the Dhamma teaching that is special to the Buddhas: suffering, its origin, its cessation, and the path. Just as a clean cloth free from stains would properly take dye, so too, right there on that very seat, the dust-free, stainless Dhamma-eye arose in me: ‘Whatever is subject to arising is all subject to cessation.’ Venerable sir, having seen the Dhamma, attained the Dhamma, understood the Dhamma, and penetrated the Dhamma, I crossed beyond doubt, became free from perplexity, attained self-confidence, and became independent of others in the Teacher’s dispensation. Right there, I went for refuge to the Buddha, the Dhamma, and the Saṅgha, and I undertook the training rules with celibacy as the fifth. This, venerable sir, is the second amazing and wonderful quality found in me.”

“Tassa mayhaṃ, bhante, catasso komāriyo pajāpatiyo ahesuṃ. Atha khvāhaṃ, bhante, yena tā pajāpatiyo tenupasaṅkamiṃ; upasaṅkamitvā tā pajāpatiyo etadavacaṃ – ‘mayā kho, bhaginiyo, brahmacariyapañcamāni sikkhāpadāni samādinnāni samādiṇṇāni (sī. ka.). Yā icchati sā idheva bhoge ca bhuñjatu puññāni ca karotu, sakāni vā ñātikulāni gacchatu. Hoti vā pana purisādhippāyo, kassa vo dammī’ti? Evaṃ vutte sā, bhante, jeṭṭhā pajāpati maṃ etadavoca – ‘itthannāmassa maṃ, ayyaputta, purisassa dehī’ti. Atha kho ahaṃ, bhante, taṃ purisaṃ pakkosāpetvā vāmena hatthena pajāpatiṃ gahetvā [T 4.0214] dakkhiṇena hatthena bhiṅgāraṃ gahetvā tassa purisassa oṇojesiṃ. Komāriṃ kho panāhaṃ, bhante, dāraṃ pariccajanto nābhijānāmi cittassa aññathattaṃ. Ayaṃ kho me, bhante, tatiyo acchariyo abbhuto dhammo saṃvijjati.

“Venerable sir, I had four young wives. Then, venerable sir, I approached those wives and said to them: ‘Sisters, I have undertaken the training rules with celibacy as the fifth. Whoever wishes may remain here, enjoy these riches, and make merit, or she may return to her own relatives’ families. Or, if she desires another man, to whom shall I give you?’ When this was said, venerable sir, the eldest wife said to me: ‘My lord, give me to the man named so-and-so.’ Then, venerable sir, having had that man summoned, I took my wife with my left hand and a water pot with my right hand, and gave her over to that man. And, venerable sir, while giving away my young wife, I was not aware of any alteration in my mind. This, venerable sir, is the third amazing and wonderful quality found in me.”

“Saṃvijjanti [P 4.0211] kho pana me, bhante, kule bhogā. Te ca kho appaṭivibhattā sīlavantehi kalyāṇadhammehi. Ayaṃ kho me, bhante, catuttho acchariyo abbhuto dhammo saṃvijjati.

“Furthermore, venerable sir, there are riches in my family, and they are shared with the virtuous who are of good character. This, venerable sir, is the fourth amazing and wonderful quality that is found in me.

“Yaṃ [M 3.0049] kho panāhaṃ, bhante, bhikkhuṃ payirupāsāmi; sakkaccaṃyeva payirupāsāmi, no asakkaccaṃ. Ayaṃ kho me, bhante, pañcamo acchariyo abbhuto dhammo saṃvijjati.

“Furthermore, venerable sir, whatever bhikkhu I attend upon, I attend upon him respectfully, not disrespectfully. This, venerable sir, is the fifth amazing and wonderful quality that is found in me.

“So [V 3.0049] ce, bhante, me āyasmā dhammaṃ deseti; sakkaccaṃyeva suṇomi, no asakkaccaṃ. No ce me so āyasmā dhammaṃ deseti, ahamassa dhammaṃ desemi. Ayaṃ kho me, bhante chaṭṭho acchariyo abbhuto dhammo saṃvijjati.

“Venerable sir, if that venerable one teaches me the Dhamma, I listen respectfully, not disrespectfully. If that venerable one does not teach me the Dhamma, I teach him the Dhamma. This, venerable sir, is the sixth amazing and wonderful quality that is found in me.”

“Anacchariyaṃ kho pana maṃ, bhante, devatā upasaṅkamitvā ārocenti – ‘svākkhāto, gahapati, bhagavatā dhammo’ti. Evaṃ vutte ahaṃ, bhante, tā devatā evaṃ vadāmi – ‘vadeyyātha vā evaṃ kho tumhe devatā no vā vadeyyātha, atha kho svākkhāto bhagavatā dhammo’ti. Na kho panāhaṃ, bhante, abhijānāmi tatonidānaṃ cittassa unnatiṃ uṇṇatiṃ (ka.) dha. sa. 1121; vibha. 843, 845 passitabbaṃ – ‘maṃ vā devatā upasaṅkamanti, ahaṃ vā devatāhi saddhiṃ sallapāmī’ti. Ayaṃ kho me, bhante, sattamo acchariyo abbhuto dhammo saṃvijjati.

“Venerable sir, it is no wonder that deities approach me and announce: ‘Well-proclaimed, householder, is the Dhamma by the Blessed One.’ When this is said, venerable sir, I say this to those deities: ‘Whether you deities say so or not, the Dhamma is indeed well-proclaimed by the Blessed One.’ Yet, venerable sir, I am not aware of any elation of mind on that account—that ‘deities approach me,’ or ‘I converse with deities.’ This, venerable sir, is the seventh amazing and wonderful quality that is found in me.

“Yānimāni, bhante, bhagavatā desitāni pañcorambhāgiyāni saṃyojanāni, nāhaṃ tesaṃ kiñci attani appahīnaṃ samanupassāmi. Ayaṃ [T 4.0215] kho me, bhante, aṭṭhamo acchariyo abbhuto dhammo saṃvijjati. Ime [P 4.0212] kho me, bhante, aṭṭha acchariyā abbhutā dhammā saṃvijjanti. Na ca kho ahaṃ jānāmi – katamehi cāhaṃ katamehipahaṃ (sī.), katamehipāhaṃ (pī. ka.) aṭṭhahi acchariyehi abbhutehi dhammehi samannāgato bhagavatā byākato”ti.

Venerable sir, regarding these five lower fetters taught by the Blessed One, I do not see any among them that is unabandoned in myself. This, venerable sir, is the eighth wonderful and marvelous quality that is found in me. Indeed, venerable sir, these eight wonderful and marvelous qualities are found in me. And I do not know with which eight wonderful and marvelous qualities the Blessed One has declared me to be endowed.

Atha kho so bhikkhu uggassa gahapatino vesālikassa nivesane piṇḍapātaṃ gahetvā uṭṭhāyāsanā pakkāmi. Atha kho so bhikkhu pacchābhattaṃ piṇḍapātapaṭikkanto yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinno kho so bhikkhu yāvatako ahosi uggena gahapatinā vesālikena saddhiṃ kathāsallāpo, taṃ sabbaṃ bhagavato ārocesi.

Then that bhikkhu, having received almsfood at the house of Ugga the householder of Vesālī, rose from his seat and departed. Then, after the meal, having returned from his alms-round, he approached the Blessed One. Having approached, he paid homage to the Blessed One and sat down to one side. Seated to one side, that bhikkhu reported to the Blessed One the entire conversation that had taken place with Ugga the householder of Vesālī.

“Sādhu sādhu, bhikkhu! Yathā taṃ uggo gahapati vesāliko sammā byākaramāno byākareyya, imeheva kho, bhikkhu, aṭṭhahi acchariyehi abbhutehi [M 3.0050] dhammehi samannāgato uggo gahapati vesāliko mayā byākato. Imehi ca pana, bhikkhu, aṭṭhahi acchariyehi abbhutehi dhammehi samannāgataṃ uggaṃ gahapatiṃ vesālikaṃ dhārehī”ti. Paṭhamaṃ.

“Good, good, bhikkhu! Ugga the householder of Vesālī has declared it rightly. Indeed, bhikkhu, it was with these very eight wonderful and marvelous qualities that Ugga the householder of Vesālī was declared by me to be endowed. And, bhikkhu, remember Ugga the householder of Vesālī as one endowed with these eight wonderful and marvelous qualities.” The First.

2. Dutiyauggasuttaṃ

2. The Second Sutta on Ugga

22. Ekaṃ [V 3.0050] samayaṃ bhagavā vajjīsu viharati hatthigāme. Tatra kho bhagavā bhikkhū āmantesi – “aṭṭhahi, bhikkhave, acchariyehi abbhutehi dhammehi samannāgataṃ uggaṃ gahapatiṃ hatthigāmakaṃ dhārethā”ti. Idamavoca bhagavā. Idaṃ vatvāna sugato uṭṭhāyāsanā vihāraṃ pāvisi.

22. On one occasion the Blessed One was dwelling among the Vajjis at Hatthigāma. There the Blessed One addressed the bhikkhus: “Bhikkhus, remember Ugga the householder of Hatthigāma as possessing eight wonderful and amazing qualities.” This the Blessed One said. Having said this, the Sugata rose from his seat and entered his dwelling.

Atha kho aññataro bhikkhu pubbaṇhasamayaṃ nivāsetvā pattacīvaramādāya [T 4.0216] yena uggassa gahapatino hatthigāmakassa nivesanaṃ tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā paññatte āsane nisīdi. Atha kho uggo gahapati hatthigāmako yena so bhikkhu tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā taṃ bhikkhuṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinnaṃ kho uggaṃ gahapatiṃ hatthigāmakaṃ so bhikkhu etadavoca – “aṭṭhahi [P 4.0213] kho tvaṃ, gahapati, acchariyehi abbhutehi dhammehi samannāgato bhagavatā byākato. Katame te, gahapati, aṭṭha acchariyā abbhutā dhammā, yehi tvaṃ samannāgato bhagavatā byākato”ti?

Then a certain bhikkhu, having dressed in the morning and taking his bowl and robe, went to the residence of Ugga the householder of Hatthigāma. Having approached, he sat down on the prepared seat. Then Ugga the householder of Hatthigāma approached that bhikkhu, paid homage to him, and sat down to one side. To Ugga the householder of Hatthigāma sitting to one side, that bhikkhu said this: “Householder, you have been declared by the Blessed One as being endowed with eight wonderful and amazing qualities. What, householder, are those eight wonderful and amazing qualities with which you have been declared by the Blessed One to be endowed?”

“Na kho ahaṃ, bhante, jānāmi – katamehi aṭṭhahi acchariyehi abbhutehi dhammehi samannāgato bhagavatā byākatoti. Api ca, bhante, ye me aṭṭha acchariyā abbhutā dhammā saṃvijjanti, taṃ suṇāhi, sādhukaṃ manasi karohi; bhāsissāmī”ti. “Evaṃ, gahapatī”ti kho so bhikkhu uggassa gahapatino hatthigāmakassa paccassosi. Uggo gahapati hatthigāmako etadavoca – “yadāhaṃ, bhante, nāgavane paricaranto bhagavantaṃ paṭhamaṃ dūratova addasaṃ; saha dassaneneva me, bhante, bhagavato cittaṃ pasīdi, surāmado ca pahīyi. Ayaṃ kho me, bhante, paṭhamo acchariyo abbhuto dhammo saṃvijjati.

“Venerable sir, I do not know with which eight wonderful and amazing qualities I was declared by the Blessed One to be endowed. However, venerable sir, there are eight wonderful and amazing qualities that exist in me. Listen to them and pay close attention; I will speak.” “So be it, householder,” that bhikkhu replied to Ugga the householder of Hatthigāma. Ugga the householder of Hatthigāma said this: “Venerable sir, when I was wandering in the Nāgavana for pleasure, I saw the Blessed One for the first time from afar. Venerable sir, with the very seeing of the Blessed One, my mind became serene and my intoxication from liquor vanished. This, venerable sir, is the first wonderful and amazing quality that exists in me.

“So kho ahaṃ, bhante, pasannacitto bhagavantaṃ payirupāsiṃ. Tassa me bhagavā anupubbiṃ kathaṃ kathesi, seyyathidaṃ – dānakathaṃ sīlakathaṃ saggakathaṃ; kāmānaṃ ādīnavaṃ okāraṃ saṃkilesaṃ, nekkhamme ānisaṃsaṃ pakāsesi. Yadā maṃ bhagavā aññāsi kallacittaṃ muducittaṃ vinīvaraṇacittaṃ udaggacittaṃ pasannacittaṃ, atha yā [M 3.0051] buddhānaṃ sāmukkaṃsikā dhammadesanā [T 4.0217] taṃ pakāsesi – dukkhaṃ, samudayaṃ, nirodhaṃ, maggaṃ. Seyyathāpi nāma suddhaṃ vatthaṃ apagatakāḷakaṃ sammadeva [V 3.0051] rajanaṃ paṭiggaṇheyya; evamevaṃ kho me tasmiṃyeva āsane virajaṃ vītamalaṃ dhammacakkhuṃ udapādi – ‘yaṃ kiñci samudayadhammaṃ, sabbaṃ taṃ nirodhadhamma’nti. So kho ahaṃ, bhante, diṭṭhadhammo pattadhammo viditadhammo pariyogāḷhadhammo tiṇṇavicikiccho vigatakathaṃkatho vesārajjappatto aparappaccayo satthusāsane tattheva [P 4.0214] buddhañca dhammañca saṅghañca saraṇaṃ agamāsiṃ, brahmacariyapañcamāni ca sikkhāpadāni samādiyiṃ. Ayaṃ kho me, bhante, dutiyo acchariyo abbhuto dhammo saṃvijjati.

“Then, venerable sir, with a serene mind, I attended upon the Blessed One. The Blessed One gave me a graduated discourse, that is to say: a talk on giving, a talk on virtue, a talk on heaven; he revealed the danger, degradation, and defilement in sensual pleasures, and the benefit of renunciation. When the Blessed One knew my mind to be ready, soft, free from hindrances, uplifted, and serene, he then revealed that teaching of the Dhamma which is the special instruction of the Buddhas: suffering, its origin, its cessation, and the path. Just as a clean cloth, free of dark spots, would rightly take the dye, so too, on that very seat, the dust-free, stainless Dhamma-eye arose in me: ‘Whatever is of the nature to arise, all that is of the nature to cease.’ Then, venerable sir, having seen the Dhamma, attained the Dhamma, known the Dhamma, plunged into the Dhamma, I crossed over doubt, became free from perplexity, attained confidence, and became independent of others in the Teacher’s dispensation. Right there I went for refuge to the Buddha, the Dhamma, and the Saṅgha, and undertook the training rules with celibacy as the fifth. This, venerable sir, is the second wonderful and amazing quality that exists in me.

“Tassa mayhaṃ, bhante, catasso komāriyo pajāpatiyo ahesuṃ. Atha khvāhaṃ, bhante, yena tā pajāpatiyo tenupasaṅkamiṃ; upasaṅkamitvā tā pajāpatiyo etadavacaṃ – ‘mayā kho, bhaginiyo, brahmacariyapañcamāni sikkhāpadāni samādinnāni. Yā icchati sā idheva bhoge ca bhuñjatu puññāni ca karotu, sakāni vā ñātikulāni gacchatu. Hoti vā pana purisādhippāyo, kassa vo dammī’ti? Evaṃ vutte sā, bhante, jeṭṭhā pajāpati maṃ etadavoca – ‘itthannāmassa maṃ, ayyaputta, purisassa dehī’ti. Atha kho ahaṃ, bhante, taṃ purisaṃ pakkosāpetvā vāmena hatthena pajāpatiṃ gahetvā dakkhiṇena hatthena bhiṅgāraṃ gahetvā tassa purisassa oṇojesiṃ. Komāriṃ kho panāhaṃ, bhante, dāraṃ pariccajanto nābhijānāmi cittassa aññathattaṃ. Ayaṃ kho me, bhante, tatiyo acchariyo abbhuto dhammo saṃvijjati.

“Venerable sir, I had four young wives. Then, venerable sir, I approached those wives and said to them: ‘Sisters, I have undertaken the training rules with celibacy as the fifth. Whichever of you wishes may remain here enjoying wealth and making merit, or may go to her own relatives’ families. Or if there is a desire for a man, to whom shall I give you?’ When this was said, venerable sir, the eldest wife said to me: ‘Master, give me to the man named so-and-so.’ Then, venerable sir, having had that man summoned, taking my wife with my left hand and a water pitcher with my right hand, I dedicated her to that man. Venerable sir, in giving away my young wife, I am not aware of any alteration of mind. This, venerable sir, is the third wonderful and amazing quality that exists in me.

“Saṃvijjanti kho pana [T 4.0218] me, bhante, kule bhogā. Te ca kho appaṭivibhattā sīlavantehi kalyāṇadhammehi. Ayaṃ kho me, bhante, catuttho acchariyo abbhuto dhammo saṃvijjati.

“Venerable sir, there is wealth in my family, and it is shared without distinction with those who are virtuous and of good character. This, venerable sir, is the fourth wonderful and amazing quality that exists in me.

“Yaṃ [P 4.0215] kho panāhaṃ, bhante, bhikkhuṃ payirupāsāmi; sakkaccaṃyeva payirupāsāmi, no asakkaccaṃ. So ce me āyasmā dhammaṃ deseti; sakkaccaṃyeva suṇomi, no asakkaccaṃ. No ce me so āyasmā dhammaṃ deseti, ahamassa dhammaṃ desemi. Ayaṃ kho me, bhante, pañcamo acchariyo abbhuto dhammo saṃvijjati.

“Venerable sir, whatever monk I attend upon, I attend upon him respectfully, not disrespectfully. If that venerable one teaches me the Dhamma, I listen respectfully, not disrespectfully. If that venerable one does not teach me the Dhamma, I teach him the Dhamma. This, venerable sir, is the fifth wonderful and amazing quality that exists in me.

“Anacchariyaṃ [M 3.0052] kho pana, bhante, saṅghe nimantite devatā upasaṅkamitvā ārocenti – ‘asuko, gahapati, bhikkhu ubhatobhāgavimutto asuko paññāvimutto asuko kāyasakkhī asuko [V 3.0052] diṭṭhippatto diṭṭhappatto (ka.) asuko saddhāvimutto asuko dhammānusārī asuko saddhānusārī asuko sīlavā kalyāṇadhammo asuko dussīlo pāpadhammo’ti. Saṅghaṃ kho panāhaṃ, bhante, parivisanto nābhijānāmi evaṃ cittaṃ uppādento – ‘imassa vā thokaṃ demi imassa vā bahuka’nti. Atha khvāhaṃ, bhante, samacittova demi. Ayaṃ kho me, bhante, chaṭṭho acchariyo abbhuto dhammo saṃvijjati.

“It is no wonder, venerable sir, that when the Saṅgha has been invited, deities approach and inform me: ‘Householder, such-and-such a monk is liberated both ways, such-and-such a monk is liberated by wisdom, such-and-such a monk is a body-witness, such-and-such a monk is attained to view, such-and-such a monk is liberated by faith, such-and-such a monk is a Dhamma-follower, such-and-such a monk is a faith-follower, such-and-such a monk is virtuous, with good qualities, such-and-such a monk is unvirtuous, with bad qualities.’ But, venerable sir, when I serve the Saṅgha, I do not recall producing such a thought as: ‘I will give a little to this one or a lot to that one.’ Rather, venerable sir, I give with an even mind. This, venerable sir, is the sixth wonderful and amazing quality that exists in me.”

“Anacchariyaṃ kho pana maṃ, bhante, devatā upasaṅkamitvā ārocenti – ‘svākkhāto, gahapati, bhagavatā dhammo’ti. Evaṃ vutte ahaṃ, bhante, tā devatā evaṃ vademi – ‘vadeyyātha vā evaṃ kho tumhe devatā no vā vadeyyātha, atha kho svākkhāto bhagavatā dhammo’ti. Na kho panāhaṃ, bhante, abhijānāmi tatonidānaṃ cittassa unnatiṃ – ‘maṃ tā devatā upasaṅkamanti, ahaṃ vā [T 4.0219] devatāhi saddhiṃ sallapāmī’ti. Ayaṃ kho me, bhante, sattamo acchariyo abbhuto dhammo saṃvijjati.

“It is no wonder, venerable sir, that deities approach me and announce: ‘Householder, the Dhamma is well proclaimed by the Blessed One.’ When this is said, venerable sir, I say to those deities: ‘Whether you deities say so or not, the Dhamma is indeed well proclaimed by the Blessed One.’ But, venerable sir, I do not recognize any mental elevation arising from that reason, thinking: ‘The deities approach me, and I converse with the deities.’ This, venerable sir, is the seventh wonderful and amazing quality that exists in me.”

“Sace [P 4.0216] kho panāhaṃ, bhante, bhagavato paṭhamataraṃ kālaṃ kareyyaṃ, anacchariyaṃ kho panetaṃ yaṃ maṃ bhagavā evaṃ byākareyya – ‘natthi taṃ saṃyojanaṃ yena saṃyutto uggo gahapati hatthigāmako puna imaṃ lokaṃ āgaccheyyā’ti. Ayaṃ kho me, bhante, aṭṭhamo acchariyo abbhuto dhammo saṃvijjati. Ime kho me, bhante, aṭṭha acchariyā abbhutā dhammā saṃvijjanti. Na ca kho ahaṃ jānāmi – katamehi cāhaṃ aṭṭhahi acchariyehi abbhutehi dhammehi samannāgato bhagavatā byākato”ti.

“Venerable sir, if I were to die before the Blessed One, it would be no wonder if the Blessed One were to declare of me thus: ‘There is no fetter by which Ugga the householder of Hatthigāma is bound that would bring him back to this world.’ This, venerable sir, is the eighth wonderful and amazing quality that exists in me. These, venerable sir, are the eight wonderful and amazing qualities that exist in me. But I do not know by which eight wonderful and amazing qualities the Blessed One has declared me to be endowed.”

“Atha kho so bhikkhu uggassa gahapatino hatthigāmakassa nivesane piṇḍapātaṃ gahetvā uṭṭhāyāsanā pakkāmi. Atha kho so bhikkhu pacchābhattaṃ piṇḍapātapaṭikkanto yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinno kho so bhikkhu yāvatako ahosi uggena gahapatinā hatthigāmakena saddhiṃ kathāsallāpo, taṃ sabbaṃ bhagavato ārocesi.

Then that monk, having received almsfood at the residence of Ugga the householder of Hatthigāma, rose from his seat and departed. Then, after the meal, having returned from the almsround, that monk approached the Blessed One; having approached, he paid homage to the Blessed One and sat down to one side. Sitting to one side, that monk reported to the Blessed One the entire conversation he had with Ugga the householder of Hatthigāma.

“Sādhu [M 3.0053] sādhu, bhikkhu! Yathā taṃ uggo gahapati hatthigāmako sammā byākaramāno byākareyya, imeheva kho bhikkhu, aṭṭhahi acchariyehi abbhutehi dhammehi samannāgato uggo gahapati [V 3.0053] hatthigāmako mayā byākato. Imehi ca pana, bhikkhu, aṭṭhahi acchariyehi abbhutehi dhammehi samannāgataṃ uggaṃ gahapatiṃ hatthigāmakaṃ dhārehī”ti. Dutiyaṃ.

“Good, good, monk! It is just as Ugga the householder of Hatthigāma, declaring rightly, would declare. It is as one endowed with these very eight wonderful and amazing qualities, monk, that Ugga the householder of Hatthigāma has been declared by me. Therefore, monk, remember Ugga the householder of Hatthigāma as endowed with these eight wonderful and amazing qualities.” Second.

3. Paṭhamahatthakasuttaṃ

3. The First Discourse concerning Hatthaka

23. Ekaṃ [T 4.0220] samayaṃ bhagavā āḷaviyaṃ viharati aggāḷave cetiye. Tatra kho bhagavā bhikkhū āmantesi – “sattahi[P 4.0217], bhikkhave, acchariyehi abbhutehi dhammehi samannāgataṃ hatthakaṃ āḷavakaṃ dhāretha. Katamehi sattahi? Saddho hi, bhikkhave, hatthako āḷavako; sīlavā, bhikkhave, hatthako āḷavako; hirīmā, bhikkhave, hatthako āḷavako; ottappī, bhikkhave, hatthako āḷavako; bahussuto, bhikkhave, hatthako āḷavako; cāgavā, bhikkhave, hatthako āḷavako; paññavā, bhikkhave, hatthako āḷavako – imehi kho, bhikkhave, sattahi acchariyehi abbhutehi dhammehi samannāgataṃ hatthakaṃ āḷavakaṃ dhārethā”ti. Idamavoca bhagavā. Idaṃ vatvāna sugato uṭṭhāyāsanā vihāraṃ pāvisi.

23. On one occasion, the Blessed One was dwelling in Āḷavī at the Aggāḷava shrine. There, the Blessed One addressed the bhikkhus: “Bhikkhus, you should bear in mind that Hatthaka of Āḷavī is endowed with seven amazing and wonderful qualities. With which seven? Bhikkhus, Hatthaka of Āḷavī has faith; bhikkhus, Hatthaka of Āḷavī is virtuous; bhikkhus, Hatthaka of Āḷavī has a sense of shame; bhikkhus, Hatthaka of Āḷavī has fear of wrongdoing; bhikkhus, Hatthaka of Āḷavī is learned; bhikkhus, Hatthaka of Āḷavī is generous; bhikkhus, Hatthaka of Āḷavī is wise. Bhikkhus, you should bear in mind that Hatthaka of Āḷavī is endowed with these seven amazing and wonderful qualities.” This the Blessed One said. Having said this, the Sugata rose from his seat and entered the monastery.

Atha kho aññataro bhikkhu pubbaṇhasamayaṃ nivāsetvā pattacīvaramādāya yena hatthakassa āḷavakassa nivesanaṃ tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā paññatte āsane nisīdi. Atha kho hatthako āḷavako yena so bhikkhu tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā taṃ bhikkhuṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinnaṃ kho hatthakaṃ āḷavakaṃ so bhikkhu etadavoca –

Then, a certain bhikkhu, having dressed in the morning and taking his bowl and robe, approached the residence of Hatthaka of Āḷavī. Having approached, he sat down on the prepared seat. Then, Hatthaka of Āḷavī approached that bhikkhu. Having approached and paid homage to that bhikkhu, he sat down to one side. As Hatthaka of Āḷavī was sitting to one side, that bhikkhu said this to him:

“Sattahi kho tvaṃ, āvuso, acchariyehi abbhutehi dhammehi samannāgato bhagavatā byākato. Katamehi sattahi? ‘Saddho, bhikkhave, hatthako āḷavako; sīlavā…pe… hirimā… ottappī… bahussuto… cāgavā… paññavā, bhikkhave, hatthako āḷavako’ti. Imehi kho tvaṃ, āvuso, sattahi acchariyehi abbhutehi dhammehi samannāgato bhagavatā byākato”ti. “Kaccittha, bhante, na koci gihī ahosi odātavasano”ti? “Na hettha, āvuso[T 4.0221], koci gihī ahosi odātavasano”ti. “Sādhu, bhante, yadettha na koci gihī ahosi odātavasano”ti.

“Friend, you have been declared by the Blessed One as one endowed with seven amazing and wonderful qualities. With which seven? ‘Bhikkhus, Hatthaka of Āḷavī has faith; is virtuous… has a sense of shame… has fear of wrongdoing… is learned… is generous… bhikkhus, Hatthaka of Āḷavī is wise.’ Friend, you have been declared by the Blessed One as one endowed with these seven amazing and wonderful qualities.” “Venerable sir, was there by any chance no layperson in white robes present there?” “No, friend, there was no layperson in white robes present there.” “It is good, venerable sir, that there was no layperson in white robes present there.”

Atha [M 3.0054] kho so bhikkhu hatthakassa āḷavakassa nivesane piṇḍapātaṃ gahetvā uṭṭhāyāsanā pakkāmi[V 3.0054]. Atha kho so bhikkhu pacchābhattaṃ piṇḍapātapaṭikkanto yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami[P 4.0218]; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinno kho so bhikkhu bhagavantaṃ etadavoca –

Then, that bhikkhu, having received almsfood at the residence of Hatthaka of Āḷavī, rose from his seat and departed. Then, that bhikkhu, after the meal, having returned from the alms-round, approached the Blessed One. Having approached and paid homage to the Blessed One, he sat down to one side. Sitting to one side, that bhikkhu said this to the Blessed One:

“Idhāhaṃ, bhante, pubbaṇhasamayaṃ nivāsetvā pattacīvaramādāya yena hatthakassa āḷavakassa nivesanaṃ tenupasaṅkamiṃ; upasaṅkamitvā paññatte āsane nisīdiṃ. Atha kho, bhante, hatthako āḷavako yenāhaṃ tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā maṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinnaṃ kho ahaṃ, bhante, hatthakaṃ āḷavakaṃ etadavacaṃ – ‘sattahi kho tvaṃ, āvuso, acchariyehi abbhutehi dhammehi samannāgato bhagavatā byākato. Katamehi sattahi? Saddho, bhikkhave, hatthako āḷavako; sīlavā…pe… hirimā… ottappī… bahussuto… cāgavā… paññavā, bhikkhave, hatthako āḷavakoti. Imehi kho tvaṃ, āvuso, sattahi acchariyehi abbhutehi dhammehi samannāgato bhagavatā byākato’ti.

“Venerable sir, this morning, having dressed and taking my bowl and robe, I approached the residence of Hatthaka of Āḷavī. Having approached, I sat down on the prepared seat. Then, venerable sir, Hatthaka of Āḷavī approached me. Having approached and paid homage to me, he sat down to one side. As he was sitting to one side, venerable sir, I said this to Hatthaka of Āḷavī: ‘Friend, you have been declared by the Blessed One as one endowed with seven amazing and wonderful qualities. With which seven? “Bhikkhus, Hatthaka of Āḷavī has faith; is virtuous… has a sense of shame… has fear of wrongdoing… is learned… is generous… bhikkhus, Hatthaka of Āḷavī is wise.” Friend, you have been declared by the Blessed One as one endowed with these seven amazing and wonderful qualities.’”

“Evaṃ vutte, bhante, hatthako maṃ etadavoca – ‘kaccittha, bhante, na koci gihī ahosi odātavasano’ti? ‘Na hettha, āvuso, koci gihī ahosi odātavasano’ti. ‘Sādhu, bhante, yadettha na koci gihī ahosi odātavasano’”ti.

“When this was said, venerable sir, Hatthaka of Āḷavī said this to me: ‘Venerable sir, was there by any chance no layperson in white robes present there?’ ‘No, friend, there was no layperson in white robes present there.’ ‘It is good, venerable sir, that there was no layperson in white robes present there.’”

“Sādhu sādhu, bhikkhu! Appiccho so, bhikkhu, kulaputto[T 4.0222]. Santeyeva attani kusaladhamme na icchati parehi ñāyamāne paññāpayamāne (ka.). Tena hi tvaṃ, bhikkhu, imināpi aṭṭhamena acchariyena abbhutena dhammena samannāgataṃ hatthakaṃ āḷavakaṃ dhārehi, yadidaṃ appicchatāyā”ti. Tatiyaṃ.

“Good, good, bhikkhu! That clansman, bhikkhu, is of few desires. He does not wish for the wholesome qualities that he possesses to be known by others. Therefore, bhikkhu, you should also bear in mind that Hatthaka of Āḷavī is endowed with this eighth amazing and wonderful quality, that is, being of few desires.” The Third.

4. Dutiyahatthakasuttaṃ

4. The Second Hatthaka Sutta

24. Ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā āḷaviyaṃ viharati aggāḷave cetiye. Atha kho hatthako āḷavako pañcamattehi upāsakasatehi [P 4.0219] parivuto yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinnaṃ kho hatthakaṃ āḷavakaṃ bhagavā [M 3.0055] etadavoca – “mahatī kho tyāyaṃ, hatthaka, parisā. Kathaṃ pana tvaṃ, hatthaka, imaṃ mahatiṃ parisaṃ saṅgaṇhāsī”ti? “Yānimāni, bhante, bhagavatā desitāni a. ni. 4.32; dī. ni. 3.313 cattāri saṅgahavatthūni, tehāhaṃ tenāhaṃ (sī.) imaṃ mahatiṃ [V 3.0055] parisaṃ saṅgaṇhāmi. Ahaṃ, bhante, yaṃ jānāmi – ‘ayaṃ dānena saṅgahetabbo’ti, taṃ dānena saṅgaṇhāmi; yaṃ jānāmi – ‘ayaṃ peyyavajjena saṅgahetabbo’ti, taṃ peyyavajjena saṅgaṇhāmi; yaṃ jānāmi – ‘ayaṃ atthacariyāya saṅgahetabbo’ti, taṃ atthacariyāya saṅgaṇhāmi; yaṃ jānāmi – ‘ayaṃ samānattatāya saṅgahetabbo’ti, taṃ samānattatāya saṅgaṇhāmi. Saṃvijjanti kho pana me, bhante, kule bhogā. Daliddassa kho no tathā sotabbaṃ maññantī”ti. “Sādhu sādhu, hatthaka! Yoni kho tyāyaṃ, hatthaka, mahatiṃ parisaṃ saṅgahetuṃ. Ye hi keci, hatthaka, atītamaddhānaṃ mahatiṃ parisaṃ saṅgahesuṃ, sabbe te imeheva catūhi saṅgahavatthūhi mahatiṃ parisaṃ saṅgahesuṃ. Yepi hi keci, hatthaka, anāgatamaddhānaṃ mahatiṃ parisaṃ saṅgaṇhissanti[T 4.0223], sabbe te imeheva catūhi saṅgahavatthūhi mahatiṃ parisaṃ saṅgaṇhissanti. Yepi hi keci, hatthaka, etarahi mahatiṃ parisaṃ saṅgaṇhanti, sabbe te imeheva catūhi saṅgahavatthūhi mahatiṃ parisaṃ saṅgaṇhantī”ti.

24. On one occasion the Blessed One was dwelling at Āḷavī, at the Aggāḷava Shrine. Then Hatthaka of Āḷavī, surrounded by about five hundred lay followers, approached the Blessed One. Having approached, he paid homage to the Blessed One and sat down to one side. The Blessed One said to Hatthaka of Āḷavī, who was sitting to one side: “Large, indeed, Hatthaka, is this assembly of yours. How, then, Hatthaka, do you gather this large assembly?” “Venerable sir, I gather this large assembly by means of the four bases of beneficence taught by the Blessed One. Venerable sir, when I know, ‘This person is to be gathered by generosity,’ I gather them by generosity. When I know, ‘This person is to be gathered by kind speech,’ I gather them by kind speech. When I know, ‘This person is to be gathered by beneficial conduct,’ I gather them by beneficial conduct. When I know, ‘This person is to be gathered by impartiality,’ I gather them by impartiality. Moreover, venerable sir, there are riches in my family. For they do not think that the words of a poor person should be heeded.” “Good, good, Hatthaka! This is indeed the proper way, Hatthaka, to gather a large assembly. For whoever, Hatthaka, in the past gathered a large assembly, all of them gathered a large assembly by these same four bases of beneficence. And whoever, Hatthaka, in the future will gather a large assembly, all of them will gather a large assembly by these same four bases of beneficence. And whoever, Hatthaka, at present gathers a large assembly, all of them gather a large assembly by these same four bases of beneficence.”

Atha kho hatthako āḷavako bhagavatā dhammiyā kathāya sandassito samādapito samuttejito sampahaṃsito uṭṭhāyāsanā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā padakkhiṇaṃ katvā pakkāmi[P 4.0220]. Atha kho bhagavā acirapakkante hatthake āḷavake bhikkhū āmantesi – “aṭṭhahi, bhikkhave, acchariyehi abbhutehi dhammehi samannāgataṃ hatthakaṃ āḷavakaṃ dhāretha. Katamehi aṭṭhahi? Saddho, bhikkhave, hatthako āḷavako; sīlavā, bhikkhave…pe… hirīmā… ottappī… bahussuto… cāgavā… paññavā, bhikkhave, hatthako āḷavako; appiccho, bhikkhave, hatthako āḷavako. Imehi kho, bhikkhave, aṭṭhahi acchariyehi abbhutehi dhammehi samannāgataṃ hatthakaṃ āḷavakaṃ dhārethā”ti. Catutthaṃ.

Then Hatthaka of Āḷavī, having been instructed, encouraged, roused, and gladdened by the Blessed One with a talk on the Dhamma, rose from his seat, paid homage to the Blessed One, circumambulated him keeping his right side toward him, and departed. Soon after Hatthaka of Āḷavī had left, the Blessed One addressed the monks: “Monks, you should remember Hatthaka of Āḷavī as one endowed with eight wonderful and marvelous qualities. What eight? Monks, Hatthaka of Āḷavī has faith; he is virtuous, monks… he has a sense of shame… he has fear of wrongdoing… he is learned… he is generous… he is wise, monks; Hatthaka of Āḷavī has few wishes, monks. You should remember Hatthaka of Āḷavī, monks, as one endowed with these eight wonderful and marvelous qualities.”

5. Mahānāmasuttaṃ

5. The Discourse to Mahānāma

25. Ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sakkesu viharati kapilavatthusmiṃ nigrodhārāme. Atha kho mahānāmo sakko yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā [M 3.0056] bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinno kho mahānāmo sakko bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “kittāvatā nu kho, bhante, upāsako hotī”ti? “Yato kho, mahānāma, buddhaṃ saraṇaṃ gato hoti, dhammaṃ saraṇaṃ gato hoti, saṅghaṃ saraṇaṃ gato hoti; ettāvatā kho, mahānāma, upāsako hotī”ti.

25. On one occasion the Blessed One was dwelling among the Sakyans at Kapilavatthu in the Banyan Tree Monastery. Then Mahānāma the Sakyan approached the Blessed One; having approached, he paid homage to the Blessed One and sat down to one side. Sitting to one side, Mahānāma the Sakyan said to the Blessed One: “To what extent, venerable sir, is one a lay follower?” “When, Mahānāma, one has gone for refuge to the Buddha, gone for refuge to the Dhamma, and gone for refuge to the Saṅgha; to that extent, Mahānāma, one is a lay follower.”

“Kittāvatā [V 3.0056] pana, bhante, upāsako sīlavā hotī”ti? “Yato kho, mahānāma[T 4.0224], upāsako pāṇātipātā paṭivirato hoti, adinnādānā paṭivirato hoti, kāmesumicchācārā paṭivirato hoti, musāvādā paṭivirato hoti, surāmerayamajjapamādaṭṭhānā paṭivirato hoti; ettāvatā kho, mahānāma, upāsako sīlavā hotī”ti.

“And to what extent, venerable sir, is a lay follower virtuous?” “When, Mahānāma, a lay follower abstains from the destruction of life, abstains from taking what is not given, abstains from misconduct in sensual pleasures, abstains from false speech, and abstains from liquors, wines, and intoxicants which are a cause for heedlessness; to that extent, Mahānāma, a lay follower is virtuous.”

“Kittāvatā pana, bhante, upāsako attahitāya paṭipanno hoti, no parahitāyā”ti? “Yato [P 4.0221] kho, mahānāma, upāsako attanāva saddhāsampanno hoti, no paraṃ saddhāsampadāya samādapeti samādāpeti (?); attanāva sīlasampanno hoti, no paraṃ sīlasampadāya samādapeti; attanāva cāgasampanno hoti, no paraṃ cāgasampadāya samādapeti; attanāva bhikkhūnaṃ dassanakāmo hoti, no paraṃ bhikkhūnaṃ dassane samādapeti; attanāva saddhammaṃ sotukāmo hoti, no paraṃ saddhammassavane samādapeti; attanāva sutānaṃ dhammānaṃ dhāraṇajātiko hoti, no paraṃ dhammadhāraṇāya samādapeti; attanāva sutānaṃ dhammānaṃ atthūpaparikkhitā hoti, no paraṃ atthūpaparikkhāya samādapeti; attanāva atthamaññāya dhammamaññāya dhammānudhammappaṭipanno hoti, no paraṃ dhammānudhammappaṭipattiyā samādapeti. Ettāvatā kho, mahānāma, upāsako attahitāya paṭipanno hoti, no parahitāyā”ti.

“And to what extent, venerable sir, is a lay follower practicing for his own welfare, but not for the welfare of others?” “When, Mahānāma, a lay follower is himself accomplished in faith, but does not encourage others in the accomplishment of faith; is himself accomplished in virtue, but does not encourage others in the accomplishment of virtue; is himself accomplished in generosity, but does not encourage others in the accomplishment of generosity; himself desires to see the monks, but does not encourage others to see the monks; himself desires to hear the good Dhamma, but does not encourage others to hear the good Dhamma; is himself of a nature to retain the teachings he has heard, but does not encourage others to retain the teachings; is himself an examiner of the meaning of the teachings he has heard, but does not encourage others to examine the meaning; and having himself understood the meaning and understood the Dhamma, he practices in accordance with the Dhamma, but does not encourage others to practice in accordance with the Dhamma. To this extent, Mahānāma, a lay follower is practicing for his own welfare, but not for the welfare of others.”

“Kittāvatā pana, bhante, upāsako attahitāya ca paṭipanno hoti parahitāya cā”ti? “Yato kho, mahānāma, upāsako attanā ca saddhāsampanno hoti, parañca saddhāsampadāya samādapeti; attanā ca sīlasampanno [T 4.0225] hoti, parañca sīlasampadāya samādapeti; attanā ca cāgasampanno hoti, parañca cāgasampadāya samādapeti; attanā ca bhikkhūnaṃ dassanakāmo hoti, parañca bhikkhūnaṃ dassane samādapeti; attanā ca saddhammaṃ [M 3.0057] sotukāmo hoti, parañca saddhammassavane samādapeti; attanā ca sutānaṃ dhammānaṃ dhāraṇajātiko hoti, parañca dhammadhāraṇāya samādapeti; attanā ca sutānaṃ dhammānaṃ atthūpaparikkhitā hoti, parañca atthūpaparikkhāya samādapeti, attanā ca atthamaññāya dhammamaññāya [P 4.0222] dhammānudhammappaṭipanno hoti, parañca dhammānudhammappaṭipattiyā samādapeti. Ettāvatā kho, mahānāma, upāsako attahitāya ca paṭipanno hoti parahitāya cā”ti. Pañcamaṃ.

“Venerable sir, to what extent is a lay follower one who practices for his own benefit and for the benefit of others?” “Mahānāma, when a lay follower is himself accomplished in faith and also encourages others in the accomplishment of faith; when he is himself accomplished in virtue and also encourages others in the accomplishment of virtue; when he is himself accomplished in generosity and also encourages others in the accomplishment of generosity; when he himself wishes to see the bhikkhus and also encourages others to see the bhikkhus; when he himself wishes to hear the true Dhamma and also encourages others to hear the true Dhamma; when he himself is one who retains the teachings he has heard and also encourages others to retain the Dhamma; when he himself investigates the meaning of the teachings he has heard and also encourages others to investigate the meaning; when he himself, having understood the meaning and understood the Dhamma, practices in accordance with the Dhamma, and also encourages others in the practice in accordance with the Dhamma—to this extent, Mahānāma, a lay follower is one who practices for his own benefit and for the benefit of others.” The fifth.

6. Jīvakasuttaṃ

6. The Discourse with Jīvaka

26. Ekaṃ [V 3.0057] samayaṃ bhagavā rājagahe viharati jīvakambavane. Atha kho jīvako komārabhacco yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinno kho jīvako komārabhacco bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “kittāvatā nu kho, bhante, upāsako hotī”ti? “Yato kho, jīvaka, buddhaṃ saraṇaṃ gato hoti, dhammaṃ saraṇaṃ gato hoti, saṅghaṃ saraṇaṃ gato hoti; ettāvatā kho jīvaka, upāsako hotī”ti.

26. On one occasion the Blessed One was dwelling at Rājagaha in Jīvaka’s Mango Grove. Then Jīvaka Komārabhacca approached the Blessed One, paid homage to him, and sat down to one side. Sitting to one side, Jīvaka Komārabhacca said to the Blessed One: “To what extent, venerable sir, is one a lay follower?” “Jīvaka, when one has gone for refuge to the Buddha, gone for refuge to the Dhamma, and gone for refuge to the Saṅgha—to this extent, Jīvaka, one is a lay follower.”

“Kittāvatā pana, bhante, upāsako sīlavā hotī”ti? “Yato kho, jīvaka, upāsako pāṇātipātā paṭivirato hoti…pe… surāmerayamajjapamādaṭṭhānā [T 4.0226] paṭivirato hoti; ettāvatā kho, jīvaka, upāsako sīlavā hotī”ti.

“To what extent, venerable sir, is a lay follower virtuous?” “Jīvaka, when a lay follower abstains from taking life... abstains from intoxicants which are a cause of heedlessness—to this extent, Jīvaka, a lay follower is virtuous.”

“Kittāvatā pana, bhante, upāsako attahitāya paṭipanno hoti, no parahitāyā”ti? “Yato kho, jīvaka, upāsako attanāva saddhāsampanno hoti, no paraṃ saddhāsampadāya samādapeti…pe… attanāva atthamaññāya dhammamaññāya dhammānudhammappaṭipanno hoti, no paraṃ dhammānudhammappaṭipattiyā samādapeti. Ettāvatā kho, jīvaka, upāsako attahitāya paṭipanno hoti, no parahitāyā”ti.

“But to what extent, venerable sir, is a lay follower one who practices for his own benefit, but not for the benefit of others?” “Jīvaka, when a lay follower is himself accomplished in faith, but does not establish others in the accomplishment of faith; ... when he himself, having understood the meaning and the Dhamma, practices in accordance with the Dhamma, but does not establish others in the practice in accordance with the Dhamma—to this extent, Jīvaka, a lay follower is one who practices for his own benefit, but not for the benefit of others.”

“Kittāvatā pana, bhante, upāsako attahitāya ca paṭipanno hoti parahitāya cā”ti? “Yato [P 4.0223] kho, jīvaka, upāsako attanā ca saddhāsampanno hoti, parañca saddhāsampadāya samādapeti; attanā ca sīlasampanno hoti, parañca sīlasampadāya samādapeti; attanā ca cāgasampanno [M 3.0058] hoti, parañca cāgasampadāya samādapeti; attanā ca bhikkhūnaṃ dassanakāmo hoti, parañca bhikkhūnaṃ dassane samādapeti; attanā ca saddhammaṃ sotukāmo hoti, parañca saddhammassavane samādapeti; attanā ca sutānaṃ dhammānaṃ dhāraṇajātiko hoti, parañca dhammadhāraṇāya samādapeti; attanā ca sutānaṃ dhammānaṃ atthūpaparikkhitā hoti, parañca atthūpaparikkhāya samādapeti; attanā ca atthamaññāya dhammamaññāya dhammānudhammappaṭipanno hoti, parañca dhammānudhammappaṭipattiyā samādapeti. Ettāvatā kho, jīvaka, upāsako attahitāya ca paṭipanno hoti parahitāya [T 4.0227] cā”ti. Chaṭṭhaṃ.

“But to what extent, venerable sir, is a lay follower one who practices for his own benefit and for the benefit of others?” “Jīvaka, when a lay follower is himself accomplished in faith, and also establishes others in the accomplishment of faith; when he is himself accomplished in virtue, and also establishes others in the accomplishment of virtue; when he is himself accomplished in generosity, and also establishes others in the accomplishment of generosity; when he himself desires to see the bhikkhus, and also establishes others in seeing the bhikkhus; when he himself desires to hear the good Dhamma, and also establishes others in hearing the good Dhamma; when he himself is of a nature to retain the teachings he has heard, and also establishes others in retaining the Dhamma; when he himself is one who investigates the meaning of the teachings he has heard, and also establishes others in investigating the meaning; when he himself, having understood the meaning and the Dhamma, practices in accordance with the Dhamma, and also establishes others in the practice in accordance with the Dhamma—to this extent, Jīvaka, a lay follower is one who practices for his own benefit and for the benefit of others.” Sixth.

7. Paṭhamabalasuttaṃ

7. The First Discourse on Powers

27. “Aṭṭhimāni[V 3.0058], bhikkhave, balāni. Katamāni aṭṭha? Ruṇṇabalā, bhikkhave, dārakā, kodhabalā mātugāmā, āvudhabalā corā, issariyabalā rājāno, ujjhattibalā bālā, nijjhattibalā paṇḍitā, paṭisaṅkhānabalā bahussutā, khantibalā samaṇabrāhmaṇā – imāni kho, bhikkhave, aṭṭha balānī”ti. Sattamaṃ.

27. “Bhikkhus, there are these eight powers. Which eight? Crying is the power of children, anger is the power of women, weapons are the power of thieves, sovereignty is the power of kings, scorn is the power of fools, reflection on what is beneficial is the power of the wise, careful consideration is the power of the learned, and patience is the power of ascetics and brahmins—these, bhikkhus, are the eight powers.” Seventh.

8. Dutiyabalasuttaṃ

8. The Second Discourse on Powers

28. Atha kho āyasmā sāriputto yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinnaṃ kho āyasmantaṃ sāriputtaṃ [P 4.0224] bhagavā etadavoca – “kati nu kho, sāriputta, khīṇāsavassa bhikkhuno balāni, yehi balehi samannāgato khīṇāsavo bhikkhu āsavānaṃ khayaṃ paṭijānāti – ‘khīṇā me āsavā’”ti? “Aṭṭha, bhante, khīṇāsavassa bhikkhuno balāni, yehi balehi samannāgato khīṇāsavo bhikkhu āsavānaṃ khayaṃ paṭijānāti – ‘khīṇā me āsavā’”ti.

28. Then the Venerable Sāriputta approached the Blessed One; having approached, he paid homage to the Blessed One and sat down to one side. As he was sitting to one side, the Blessed One said to the Venerable Sāriputta: “Sāriputta, how many powers does a monk whose taints are destroyed possess, endowed with which powers a monk whose taints are destroyed declares the destruction of the taints, saying: ‘My taints are destroyed’?” “Venerable sir, there are eight powers that a monk whose taints are destroyed possesses, endowed with which powers a monk whose taints are destroyed declares the destruction of the taints, saying: ‘My taints are destroyed.’”

“Katamāni aṭṭha? a. ni. 10.90; paṭi. ma. 2.44 Idha, bhante, khīṇāsavassa bhikkhuno aniccato sabbe saṅkhārā yathābhūtaṃ sammappaññāya sudiṭṭhā honti. Yampi, bhante, khīṇāsavassa bhikkhuno aniccato sabbe saṅkhārā yathābhūtaṃ sammappaññāya sudiṭṭhā honti, idampi, bhante, khīṇāsavassa bhikkhuno balaṃ hoti, yaṃ balaṃ āgamma khīṇāsavo bhikkhu āsavānaṃ khayaṃ paṭijānāti – ‘khīṇā me āsavā’”ti.

“Which eight? Here, venerable sir, for a monk whose taints are destroyed, all formations are well seen with right wisdom as they truly are: as impermanent. That for a monk whose taints are destroyed all formations are well seen with right wisdom as they truly are, as impermanent—this too, venerable sir, is a power for a monk whose taints are destroyed, a power relying on which a monk whose taints are destroyed declares the destruction of the taints, saying: ‘My taints are destroyed.’”

“Puna [M 3.0059] caparaṃ, bhante, khīṇāsavassa bhikkhuno aṅgārakāsūpamā kāmā yathābhūtaṃ sammappaññāya sudiṭṭhā honti. Yampi, bhante, khīṇāsavassa bhikkhuno aṅgārakāsūpamā [T 4.0228] kāmā yathābhūtaṃ sammappaññāya sudiṭṭhā honti, idampi, bhante, khīṇāsavassa bhikkhuno balaṃ hoti, yaṃ balaṃ āgamma khīṇāsavo bhikkhu āsavānaṃ khayaṃ paṭijānāti – ‘khīṇā me āsavā’”ti.

“Furthermore, venerable sir, for a monk whose taints are destroyed, sensual pleasures are well seen with right wisdom as they truly are: like a pit of embers. That, venerable sir, for a monk whose taints are destroyed, sensual pleasures are well seen with right wisdom as they truly are, like a pit of embers—this too, venerable sir, is a power for a monk whose taints are destroyed, a power relying on which a monk whose taints are destroyed declares the destruction of the taints: ‘My taints are destroyed.’”

“Puna [V 3.0059] caparaṃ, bhante, khīṇāsavassa bhikkhuno vivekaninnaṃ cittaṃ hoti vivekapoṇaṃ vivekapabbhāraṃ vivekaṭṭhaṃ nekkhammābhirataṃ byantibhūtaṃ sabbaso āsavaṭṭhāniyehi dhammehi. Yampi, bhante, khīṇāsavassa bhikkhuno vivekaninnaṃ cittaṃ hoti vivekapoṇaṃ vivekapabbhāraṃ vivekaṭṭhaṃ nekkhammābhirataṃ byantibhūtaṃ sabbaso āsavaṭṭhāniyehi dhammehi, idampi, bhante, khīṇāsavassa bhikkhuno balaṃ hoti, yaṃ balaṃ āgamma khīṇāsavo bhikkhu āsavānaṃ khayaṃ paṭijānāti – ‘khīṇā me āsavā’”ti.

“Furthermore, venerable sir, for a monk whose taints are destroyed, his mind inclines to seclusion, slopes to seclusion, tends to seclusion, is established in seclusion, delights in renunciation, and is in every way aloof from states that are grounds for the taints. That, venerable sir, for a monk whose taints are destroyed, his mind inclines to seclusion, slopes to seclusion, tends to seclusion, is established in seclusion, delights in renunciation, and is in every way aloof from states that are grounds for the taints—this too, venerable sir, is a power for a monk whose taints are destroyed, a power relying on which a monk whose taints are destroyed declares the destruction of the taints: ‘My taints are destroyed.’”

“Puna caparaṃ, bhante, khīṇāsavassa bhikkhuno cattāro satipaṭṭhānā bhāvitā honti subhāvitā. Yampi, bhante, khīṇāsavassa [P 4.0225] bhikkhuno cattāro satipaṭṭhānā bhāvitā honti subhāvitā, idampi, bhante, khīṇāsavassa bhikkhuno balaṃ hoti, yaṃ balaṃ āgamma khīṇāsavo bhikkhu āsavānaṃ khayaṃ paṭijānāti – ‘khīṇā me āsavā’”ti.

“Furthermore, venerable sir, for a monk whose taints are destroyed, the four foundations of mindfulness are developed and well-developed. That, venerable sir, for a monk whose taints are destroyed, the four foundations of mindfulness are developed and well-developed—this too, venerable sir, is a power for a monk whose taints are destroyed, a power relying on which a monk whose taints are destroyed declares the destruction of the taints: ‘My taints are destroyed.’”

“Puna caparaṃ, bhante, khīṇāsavassa bhikkhuno cattāro iddhipādā bhāvitā honti subhāvitā…pe… pañcindriyāni bhāvitāni honti subhāvitāni…pe… satta bojjhaṅgā bhāvitā honti subhāvitā…pe… ariyo aṭṭhaṅgiko maggo bhāvito hoti subhāvito. Yampi, bhante, khīṇāsavassa bhikkhuno ariyo aṭṭhaṅgiko maggo bhāvito hoti subhāvito, idampi, bhante, khīṇāsavassa bhikkhuno balaṃ hoti, yaṃ balaṃ āgamma khīṇāsavo bhikkhu āsavānaṃ [T 4.0229] khayaṃ paṭijānāti – ‘khīṇā me āsavā’”ti.

“Furthermore, venerable sir, for a monk whose taints are destroyed, the four bases of power are developed and well-developed... the five faculties are developed and well-developed... the seven factors of enlightenment are developed and well-developed... the noble eightfold path is developed and well-developed. That, venerable sir, for a monk whose taints are destroyed, the noble eightfold path is developed and well-developed—this too, venerable sir, is a power for a monk whose taints are destroyed, a power relying on which a monk whose taints are destroyed declares the destruction of the taints: ‘My taints are destroyed.’”

“Imāni kho, bhante, aṭṭha khīṇāsavassa bhikkhuno balāni, yehi balehi samannāgato khīṇāsavo bhikkhu āsavānaṃ khayaṃ paṭijānāti – ‘khīṇā me āsavā’”ti. Aṭṭhamaṃ.

“Venerable sir, these indeed are the eight powers of a monk whose taints are destroyed, endowed with which a monk whose taints are destroyed declares the destruction of the taints: ‘My taints are destroyed.’” Eighth.

9. Akkhaṇasuttaṃ

9. The Discourse on Inopportune Moments

29. “‘Khaṇakicco [M 3.0060] loko, khaṇakicco loko’ti, bhikkhave, assutavā puthujjano bhāsati, no ca kho so jānāti khaṇaṃ vā akkhaṇaṃ vā. Aṭṭhime, bhikkhave, akkhaṇā asamayā brahmacariyavāsāya. Katame aṭṭha? Idha, bhikkhave, tathāgato ca loke uppanno hoti arahaṃ sammāsambuddho vijjācaraṇasampanno sugato lokavidū anuttaro purisadammasārathi satthā devamanussānaṃ [V 3.0060] buddho bhagavā, dhammo ca desiyati opasamiko parinibbāniko sambodhagāmī sugatappavedito; ayañca puggalo nirayaṃ upapanno hoti. Ayaṃ, bhikkhave, paṭhamo akkhaṇo asamayo brahmacariyavāsāya.

29. “‘The world has its timely duties, the world has its timely duties,’ so, monks, the uninstructed worldling says, but they do not know what is an opportune moment and what is an inopportune moment.
