There are, monks, these eight inopportune and untimely moments for living the holy life. What are the eight? Here, monks, a Tathāgata has arisen in the world—an Arahant, a Perfectly Self-Enlightened One, perfect in knowledge and conduct, the Well-farer, the Knower of the world, the unsurpassed trainer of persons to be tamed, the teacher of gods and humans, the Buddha, the Blessed One—and the Dhamma is taught, which is calming, leading to Nibbāna, conducive to enlightenment, proclaimed by the Well-farer; but this person has been reborn in hell. This, monks, is the first inopportune and untimely moment for living the holy life.

“Puna [P 4.0226] caparaṃ, bhikkhave, tathāgato ca loke uppanno hoti…pe… satthā devamanussānaṃ buddho bhagavā, dhammo ca desiyati opasamiko parinibbāniko sambodhagāmī sugatappavedito; ayañca puggalo tiracchānayoniṃ upapanno hoti…pe….

“Furthermore, monks, a Tathāgata has arisen in the world… the teacher of gods and humans, the Buddha, the Blessed One, and the Dhamma is taught, which is calming, leading to Nibbāna, conducive to enlightenment, proclaimed by the Well-farer; but this person has been reborn in the animal realm….

“Puna caparaṃ, bhikkhave…pe… ayañca puggalo pettivisayaṃ upapanno hoti…pe….

“Furthermore, monks… this person has been reborn in the realm of the petas….

“Puna caparaṃ, bhikkhave…pe… ayañca puggalo aññataraṃ dīghāyukaṃ devanikāyaṃ upapanno hoti…pe….

“Furthermore, monks… this person has been reborn in a certain long-lived group of devas….

“Puna caparaṃ, bhikkhave…pe… ayañca puggalo paccantimesu janapadesu paccājāto hoti, so ca hoti aviññātāresu milakkhesu milakkhūsu (syā. ka.) dī. ni. 3.358, yattha natthi gati bhikkhūnaṃ bhikkhunīnaṃ upāsakānaṃ upāsikānaṃ…pe… pañcamo akkhaṇo asamayo brahmacariyavāsāya.

“Furthermore, monks… this person is reborn in the border regions, among ignorant barbarians, where there is no access for monks, nuns, laymen, or laywomen… this is the fifth inopportune and untimely moment for living the holy life.

“Puna caparaṃ, bhikkhave…pe… ayañca puggalo majjhimesu janapadesu paccājāto hoti, so ca hoti micchādiṭṭhiko [T 4.0230] viparītadassano – ‘natthi dinnaṃ, natthi yiṭṭhaṃ, natthi hutaṃ, natthi sukatadukkaṭānaṃ kammānaṃ phalaṃ vipāko, natthi ayaṃ loko, natthi paro loko, natthi mātā, natthi pitā, natthi sattā opapātikā, natthi loke samaṇabrāhmaṇā sammaggatā sammā paṭipannā ye imañca lokaṃ parañca lokaṃ sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā pavedentī’ti…pe….

“Furthermore, monks… this person is reborn in the middle countries, yet he holds wrong view and has a distorted perspective: ‘There is nothing given, nothing offered, nothing sacrificed. There is no fruit or result of good and bad actions. There is no this world, no other world. There is no mother, no father. There are no spontaneously arisen beings. There are in the world no ascetics or brahmins who are proceeding rightly and practicing rightly, who proclaim this world and the other world, having realized them for themselves by direct knowledge.’…

“Puna [M 3.0061] caparaṃ, bhikkhave…pe… ayañca puggalo majjhimesu janapadesu paccājāto hoti[V 3.0061], so ca hoti duppañño jaḷo eḷamūgo appaṭibalo subhāsitadubbhāsitassa atthamaññātuṃ. Ayaṃ, bhikkhave, sattamo akkhaṇo asamayo brahmacariyavāsāya.

“Furthermore, monks… this person is reborn in the middle countries, yet he is unwise, dull, a slobbering idiot, and unable to understand the meaning of what is well-spoken and ill-spoken. This, monks, is the seventh inopportune and untimely moment for living the holy life.

“Puna caparaṃ, bhikkhave, tathāgato ca loke anuppanno hoti arahaṃ sammāsambuddho…pe… satthā devamanussānaṃ buddho bhagavā. Dhammo ca na desiyati [P 4.0227] opasamiko parinibbāniko sambodhagāmī sugatappavedito. Ayañca puggalo majjhimesu janapadesu paccājāto hoti, so ca hoti paññavā ajaḷo aneḷamūgo paṭibalo subhāsitadubbhāsitassa atthamaññātuṃ. Ayaṃ, bhikkhave, aṭṭhamo akkhaṇo asamayo brahmacariyavāsāya. ‘Ime kho, bhikkhave, aṭṭha akkhaṇā asamayā brahmacariyavāsāya’”.

“Furthermore, monks, a Tathāgata has not arisen in the world—an Arahant, a Perfectly Enlightened One… a teacher of gods and humans, a Buddha, the Blessed One. And the Dhamma is not taught—which is calming, leading to complete Nibbāna, conducive to enlightenment, proclaimed by the Well-farer. And this person is reborn in the middle lands, and is wise, not foolish, not simple-minded, and is capable of understanding the meaning of what is well-spoken and ill-spoken. This, monks, is the eighth inopportune and untimely moment for living the holy life. These, monks, are the eight inopportune and untimely moments for living the holy life.”

“Ekova kho, bhikkhave, khaṇo ca samayo ca brahmacariyavāsāya. Katamo eko? Idha, bhikkhave, tathāgato ca loke uppanno hoti arahaṃ sammāsambuddho vijjācaraṇasampanno sugato lokavidū anuttaro purisadammasārathi satthā devamanussānaṃ buddho bhagavā. Dhammo ca desiyati opasamiko parinibbāniko sambodhagāmī sugatappavedito. Ayañca puggalo majjhimesu [T 4.0231] janapadesu paccājāto hoti, so ca hoti paññavā ajaḷo aneḷamūgo paṭibalo subhāsitadubbhāsitassa atthamaññātuṃ. Ayaṃ, bhikkhave, ekova khaṇo ca samayo ca brahmacariyavāsāyā”ti.

“Monks, there is only one opportune and timely moment for living the holy life. What is that one? Here, monks, a Tathāgata has arisen in the world—an Arahant, a Perfectly Enlightened One, perfect in knowledge and conduct, the Well-farer, the Knower of the world, the unsurpassed trainer of persons to be tamed, the teacher of gods and humans, the Buddha, the Blessed One. And the Dhamma is taught, which is calming, leading to complete Nibbāna, conducive to enlightenment, proclaimed by the Well-farer. And this person is reborn in the middle lands, and is wise, not foolish, not simple-minded, and is capable of understanding the meaning of what is well-spoken and ill-spoken. This, monks, is the one opportune and timely moment for living the holy life.”

“Manussalābhaṃ manussalokaṃ (syā.) laddhāna, saddhamme suppavedite;

Ye khaṇaṃ nādhigacchanti, atināmenti te khaṇaṃ.

“Having obtained a human birth, when the good Dhamma is well-proclaimed, those who do not realize the opportune moment, they let that moment pass them by.

“Bahū hi akkhaṇā vuttā, maggassa antarāyikā;

Kadāci karahaci loke, uppajjanti tathāgatā.

“Many inopportune moments have been spoken of, which are obstacles to the path; only sometimes, on rare occasions, do Tathāgatas arise in the world.

“Tayidaṃ tassidaṃ (ka.) sammukhībhūtaṃ, yaṃ lokasmiṃ sudullabhaṃ;

Manussapaṭilābho ca, saddhammassa ca desanā;

Alaṃ vāyamituṃ tattha, attakāmena atthakāmena (sī. syā. ka.) jantunā.

“That which is so very rare in the world is now present: the attainment of a human state and the teaching of the good Dhamma. It is fitting for a person who desires their own welfare to strive therein.

“Kathaṃ [V 3.0062][M 3.0062][P 4.0228] vijaññā saddhammaṃ, khaṇo ve vo (syā.) mā upaccagā;

Khaṇātītā hi socanti, nirayamhi samappitā.

“How is one to understand the good Dhamma? Let not this opportune moment pass you by! For those who have let the moment pass grieve, having been consigned to hell.

“Idha ce naṃ virādheti, saddhammassa niyāmataṃ niyāmitaṃ (syā.);

Vāṇijova atītattho, cirattaṃ cirantaṃ (ka.) anutapissati.

“If one here should fail in the fixed course of the good Dhamma, like a merchant who has missed his profit, for a long time one will repent.

“Avijjānivuto poso, saddhammaṃ aparādhiko;

Jātimaraṇasaṃsāraṃ, ciraṃ paccanubhossati.

“A person enveloped in ignorance, failing in the good Dhamma, will long experience the saṃsāra of birth and death.

“Ye ca laddhā manussattaṃ, saddhamme suppavedite;

Akaṃsu satthu vacanaṃ, karissanti karonti vā.

“But those who, having gained a human state when the good Dhamma is well-proclaimed, have followed the Teacher’s word, will follow it, or are following it now.

“Khaṇaṃ paccaviduṃ loke, brahmacariyaṃ anuttaraṃ;

Ye maggaṃ paṭipajjiṃsu, tathāgatappaveditaṃ.

“Those who have practiced the path proclaimed by the Tathāgata, the unsurpassed holy life, have penetrated the opportune moment in this world.

“Ye [T 4.0232] saṃvarā cakkhumatā, desitādiccabandhunā;

Tesu tesaṃ (ka.) gutto sadā sato, vihare anavassuto.

“In those restraints taught by the One with Vision, the Kinsman of the Sun, one should dwell ever guarded, mindful, and untainted.

“Sabbe anusaye chetvā, māradheyyaparānuge;

Te ve pāraṅgatā pāragatā (sī. syā. pī.) loke, ye pattā āsavakkhaya”nti. navamaṃ;

“Having cut off all latent tendencies that lead one into Māra’s domain, they have truly crossed to the far shore in this world—they who have attained the destruction of the taints.”

10. Anuruddhamahāvitakkasuttaṃ

10. The Discourse on the Great Thoughts of Anuruddha

30. Ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā bhaggesu viharati suṃsumāragire bhesakaḷāvane migadāye. Tena kho pana samayena āyasmā anuruddho cetīsu viharati pācīnavaṃsadāye. Atha kho āyasmato anuruddhassa rahogatassa paṭisallīnassa evaṃ cetaso parivitakko udapādi – “appicchassāyaṃ dhammo, nāyaṃ dhammo mahicchassa; santuṭṭhassāyaṃ dhammo[P 4.0229], nāyaṃ dhammo asantuṭṭhassa; pavivittassāyaṃ dhammo, nāyaṃ dhammo saṅgaṇikārāmassa; āraddhavīriyassāyaṃ dhammo, nāyaṃ dhammo kusītassa; upaṭṭhitassatissāyaṃ [V 3.0063] upaṭṭhitasatissāyaṃ (sī. syā. pī.) dhammo, nāyaṃ dhammo muṭṭhassatissa muṭṭhasatissa (sī. syā. pī.); samāhitassāyaṃ dhammo, nāyaṃ dhammo asamāhitassa; paññavato ayaṃ dhammo, nāyaṃ dhammo duppaññassā”ti.

30. At one time, the Blessed One was dwelling among the Bhaggas in the Bhesakaḷā Forest, a deer sanctuary at Suṃsumāragira. At that time, the Venerable Anuruddha was dwelling among the Cetīs in the Eastern Bamboo Grove. Then, while the Venerable Anuruddha was in solitude and seclusion, this thought arose in his mind: ‘This Dhamma is for one with few wishes, not for one with great wishes; this Dhamma is for the contented, not for the discontented; this Dhamma is for the secluded, not for one who delights in company; this Dhamma is for one with aroused energy, not for the lazy; this Dhamma is for one with established mindfulness, not for one with lapsed mindfulness; this Dhamma is for the concentrated, not for the unconcentrated; this Dhamma is for one with wisdom, not for one without wisdom.’”

Atha [M 3.0063] kho bhagavā āyasmato anuruddhassa cetasā cetoparivitakkamaññāya – seyyathāpi nāma balavā puriso samiñjitaṃ vā bāhaṃ pasāreyya, pasāritaṃ vā bāhaṃ samiñjeyya; evamevaṃ – bhaggesu suṃsumāragire bhesakaḷāvane migadāye antarahito cetīsu pācīnavaṃsadāye āyasmato anuruddhassa sammukhe pāturahosi. Nisīdi bhagavā paññatte āsane. Āyasmāpi kho anuruddho bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ

Then the Blessed One, knowing with his mind the thought in the Venerable Anuruddha’s mind, just as a strong man might extend his bent arm or bend his extended arm, vanished from the Bhesakaḷā Forest, the deer sanctuary at Suṃsumāragira among the Bhaggas, and reappeared before the Venerable Anuruddha in the Eastern Bamboo Grove among the Cetīs. The Blessed One sat down on the prepared seat. The Venerable Anuruddha, having paid homage to the Blessed One, also sat down at one side.

[T 4.0233] Nisinnaṃ kho āyasmantaṃ anuruddhaṃ bhagavā etadavoca –

To the Venerable Anuruddha, who was seated at one side, the Blessed One said this:

“Sādhu sādhu, anuruddha! Sādhu kho tvaṃ, anuruddha, (yaṃ taṃ mahāpurisavitakkaṃ) satta mahāpurisavitakke (sī. pī.) dī. ni. 3.358 vitakkesi – ‘appicchassāyaṃ dhammo, nāyaṃ dhammo mahicchassa; santuṭṭhassāyaṃ dhammo, nāyaṃ dhammo asantuṭṭhassa; pavivittassāyaṃ dhammo, nāyaṃ dhammo saṅgaṇikārāmassa; āraddhavīriyassāyaṃ dhammo, nāyaṃ dhammo kusītassa; upaṭṭhitassatissāyaṃ dhammo, nāyaṃ dhammo muṭṭhassatissa; samāhitassāyaṃ dhammo, nāyaṃ dhammo asamāhitassa; paññavato ayaṃ dhammo, nāyaṃ dhammo duppaññassā’ti. Tena hi tvaṃ, anuruddha, imampi aṭṭhamaṃ mahāpurisavitakkaṃ vitakkehi – ‘nippapañcārāmassāyaṃ dhammo nippapañcaratino, nāyaṃ dhammo papañcārāmassa papañcaratino’”ti.

“Good, good, Anuruddha! It is good, Anuruddha, that you have thought these thoughts of a great man: ‘This Dhamma is for one with few wishes, not for one with great wishes; this Dhamma is for the contented, not for the discontented; this Dhamma is for the secluded, not for one who delights in company; this Dhamma is for one with aroused energy, not for the lazy; this Dhamma is for one with established mindfulness, not for one with lapsed mindfulness; this Dhamma is for the concentrated, not for the unconcentrated; this Dhamma is for one with wisdom, not for one without wisdom.’ Therefore, Anuruddha, you should also think this eighth thought of a great man: ‘This Dhamma is for one who delights in non-proliferation and rejoices in non-proliferation; this Dhamma is not for one who delights in proliferation and rejoices in proliferation.’”

“Yato kho tvaṃ, anuruddha, ime aṭṭha mahāpurisavitakke vitakkessasi, tato tvaṃ, anuruddha, yāvadeva yāvade (saṃ. ni. 2.152) ākaṅkhissasi, vivicceva kāmehi vivicca akusalehi dhammehi [P 4.0230] savitakkaṃ savicāraṃ vivekajaṃ pītisukhaṃ paṭhamaṃ jhānaṃ upasampajja viharissasi.

“When, Anuruddha, you reflect upon these eight thoughts of a great man, then, for as long as you wish, secluded from sensual pleasures, secluded from unwholesome states, you will enter and dwell in the first jhāna, which has initial and sustained thought, with rapture and happiness born of seclusion.

“Yato kho tvaṃ, anuruddha, ime aṭṭha mahāpurisavitakke vitakkessasi, tato tvaṃ, anuruddha, yāvadeva ākaṅkhissasi, vitakkavicārānaṃ vūpasamā ajjhattaṃ sampasādanaṃ cetaso ekodibhāvaṃ avitakkaṃ avicāraṃ samādhijaṃ pītisukhaṃ dutiyaṃ jhānaṃ upasampajja viharissasi.

When, Anuruddha, you reflect upon these eight thoughts of a great man, then, for as long as you wish, with the stilling of applied and sustained thought, with internal confidence and unification of mind, you will enter and dwell in the second jhāna, which is without applied and sustained thought, with rapture and happiness born of concentration.

“Yato kho tvaṃ, anuruddha, ime aṭṭha mahāpurisavitakke vitakkessasi, tato tvaṃ, anuruddha[V 3.0064], yāvadeva ākaṅkhissasi, pītiyā ca virāgā upekkhako ca viharissasi sato ca sampajāno sukhañca kāyena paṭisaṃvedissasi [M 3.0064] yaṃ taṃ ariyā ācikkhanti – ‘upekkhako [T 4.0234] satimā sukhavihārī’ti tatiyaṃ jhānaṃ upasampajja viharissasi.

When, Anuruddha, you reflect upon these eight thoughts of a great man, then, for as long as you wish, with the fading away of rapture, you will dwell equanimous, mindful, and clearly comprehending, and you will experience happiness with the body. You will enter and dwell in the third jhāna, of which the noble ones declare: ‘Equanimous and mindful, one dwells happily.’

“Yato kho tvaṃ, anuruddha, ime aṭṭha mahāpurisavitakke vitakkessasi, tato tvaṃ, anuruddha, yāvadeva ākaṅkhissasi, sukhassa ca pahānā dukkhassa ca pahānā pubbeva somanassadomanassānaṃ atthaṅgamā adukkhamasukhaṃ upekkhāsatipārisuddhiṃ catutthaṃ jhānaṃ upasampajja viharissasi.

When, Anuruddha, you reflect upon these eight thoughts of a great man, then, for as long as you wish, with the abandoning of pleasure and pain, and with the previous disappearance of joy and sorrow, you will enter and dwell in the fourth jhāna, which is neither-painful-nor-pleasant and has purity of mindfulness due to equanimity.

“Yato kho tvaṃ, anuruddha, ime ca aṭṭha mahāpurisavitakke vitakkessasi, imesañca catunnaṃ jhānānaṃ ābhicetasikānaṃ diṭṭhadhammasukhavihārānaṃ nikāmalābhī bhavissasi akicchalābhī akasiralābhī, tato tuyhaṃ, anuruddha, seyyathāpi nāma gahapatissa vā gahapatiputtassa vā nānārattānaṃ dussānaṃ dussakaraṇḍako pūro; evamevaṃ te paṃsukūlacīvaraṃ khāyissati santuṭṭhassa viharato ratiyā aparitassāya phāsuvihārāya okkamanāya nibbānassa.

When, Anuruddha, you reflect upon these eight thoughts of a great man, and you are a gainer at will, without difficulty, and without trouble of these four jhānas belonging to the higher mind, pleasant abidings in the present life, then, Anuruddha, to you who are dwelling contentedly, your rag-robe will appear pleasing—just as a householder’s or a householder’s son’s clothes chest is full of clothes of various colors—for the sake of delight, for freedom from vexation, for a comfortable abiding, and for entry into Nibbāna.

“Yato [P 4.0231] kho tvaṃ, anuruddha, ime ca aṭṭha mahāpurisavitakke vitakkessasi, imesañca catunnaṃ jhānānaṃ ābhicetasikānaṃ diṭṭhadhammasukhavihārānaṃ nikāmalābhī bhavissasi akicchalābhī akasiralābhī, tato tuyhaṃ, anuruddha, seyyathāpi nāma gahapatissa vā gahapatiputtassa vā sālīnaṃ odano vicitakāḷako anekasūpo anekabyañjano; evamevaṃ te piṇḍiyālopabhojanaṃ khāyissati santuṭṭhassa viharato ratiyā aparitassāya phāsuvihārāya okkamanāya nibbānassa.

When, Anuruddha, you reflect upon these eight thoughts of a great man, and you are a gainer at will, without difficulty, and without trouble of these four jhānas belonging to the higher mind, pleasant abidings in the present life, then, Anuruddha, to you who are dwelling contentedly, your alms-food will appear pleasing—just as for a householder or a householder’s son there is fine rice with the black grains removed, with many soups and curries—for the sake of delight, for freedom from vexation, for a comfortable abiding, and for entry into Nibbāna.

“Yato kho tvaṃ, anuruddha, ime ca aṭṭha mahāpurisavitakke vitakkessasi, imesañca catunnaṃ jhānānaṃ ābhicetasikānaṃ diṭṭhadhammasukhavihārānaṃ nikāmalābhī bhavissasi akicchalābhī akasiralābhī, tato tuyhaṃ, anuruddha, seyyathāpi [T 4.0235] nāma gahapatissa vā gahapatiputtassa vā kūṭāgāraṃ ullittāvalittaṃ nivātaṃ phusitaggaḷaṃ pihitavātapānaṃ; evamevaṃ te rukkhamūlasenāsanaṃ khāyissati santuṭṭhassa viharato ratiyā aparitassāya phāsuvihārāya okkamanāya nibbānassa.

When, Anuruddha, you reflect upon these eight thoughts of a great man, and you are a gainer at will, without difficulty, and without trouble of these four jhānas belonging to the higher mind, pleasant abidings in the present life, then, Anuruddha, to you who are dwelling contentedly, the lodging at the foot of a tree will appear pleasing—just as a gabled mansion, plastered inside and out, free from wind, with bolted doors and closed windows, appears pleasing to a householder or a householder's son—for the sake of delight, for freedom from vexation, for a comfortable abiding, and for entry into Nibbāna.

“Yato [V 3.0065] kho tvaṃ, anuruddha, ime ca aṭṭha mahāpurisavitakke vitakkessasi, imesañca catunnaṃ jhānānaṃ ābhicetasikānaṃ diṭṭhadhammasukhavihārānaṃ nikāmalābhī bhavissasi akicchalābhī akasiralābhī, tato tuyhaṃ, anuruddha[M 3.0065], seyyathāpi nāma gahapatissa vā gahapatiputtassa vā pallaṅko gonakatthato paṭikatthato paṭalikatthato kadalimigapavarapaccattharaṇo kādali… paccattharaṇo (sī.) sauttaracchado ubhatolohitakūpadhāno; evamevaṃ te tiṇasanthārakasayanāsanaṃ khāyissati santuṭṭhassa viharato ratiyā aparitassāya phāsuvihārāya okkamanāya nibbānassa.

When, Anuruddha, you reflect upon these eight thoughts of a great man, and you are a gainer at will, without difficulty, and without trouble of these four jhānas belonging to the higher mind, pleasant abidings in the present life, then, Anuruddha, to you who are dwelling contentedly, a bed and seat made of a spread of grass will appear pleasing—just as for a householder or a householder's son, a couch spread with a long-fleeced cover, a white woolen cover, a cover with floral designs, a supreme covering made from the hide of a kadali deer, with a canopy above and red cushions at both ends, appears pleasing—for the sake of delight, for freedom from vexation, for a comfortable abiding, and for entry into Nibbāna.

“Yato [P 4.0232] kho tvaṃ, anuruddha, ime ca aṭṭha mahāpurisavitakke vitakkessasi, imesañca catunnaṃ jhānānaṃ ābhicetasikānaṃ diṭṭhadhammasukhavihārānaṃ nikāmalābhī bhavissasi akicchalābhī akasiralābhī, tato tuyhaṃ, anuruddha, seyyathāpi nāma gahapatissa vā gahapatiputtassa vā nānābhesajjāni, seyyathidaṃ – sappi navanītaṃ telaṃ madhu phāṇitaṃ; evamevaṃ te pūtimuttabhesajjaṃ khāyissati santuṭṭhassa viharato ratiyā aparitassāya phāsuvihārāya okkamanāya nibbānassa. Tena hi tvaṃ, anuruddha, āyatikampi vassāvāsaṃ idheva cetīsu pācīnavaṃsadāye vihareyyāsī”ti. “Evaṃ, bhante”ti kho āyasmā anuruddho bhagavato paccassosi.

When, Anuruddha, you reflect upon these eight thoughts of a great man, and you are a gainer at will, without difficulty, and without trouble of these four jhānas belonging to the higher mind, pleasant abidings in the present life, then, Anuruddha, to you who are dwelling contentedly, the medicine of fermented urine will appear pleasing—just as for a householder or a householder's son, various medicines, namely: ghee, fresh butter, oil, honey, and molasses, appear pleasing—for the sake of delight, for freedom from vexation, for a comfortable abiding, and for entry into Nibbāna. Therefore, Anuruddha, you should dwell for the rains-residence in the future right here among the Cetīs in the Eastern Bamboo Grove.” “So be it, venerable sir,” the Venerable Anuruddha assented to the Blessed One.

Atha kho bhagavā āyasmantaṃ anuruddhaṃ iminā ovādena ovaditvā – seyyathāpi nāma balavā [T 4.0236] puriso samiñjitaṃ vā bāhaṃ pasāreyya, pasāritaṃ vā bāhaṃ samiñjeyya, evamevaṃ – cetīsu pācīnavaṃsadāye antarahito bhaggesu suṃsumāragire bhesakaḷāvane migadāye pāturahosīti. Nisīdi bhagavā paññatte āsane. Nisajja kho bhagavā bhikkhū āmantesi – “aṭṭha kho, bhikkhave, mahāpurisavitakke desessāmi, taṃ suṇātha…pe… katame ca, bhikkhave, aṭṭha mahāpurisavitakkā? Appicchassāyaṃ, bhikkhave, dhammo, nāyaṃ dhammo mahicchassa; santuṭṭhassāyaṃ, bhikkhave, dhammo, nāyaṃ dhammo asantuṭṭhassa; pavivittassāyaṃ, bhikkhave, dhammo, nāyaṃ dhammo saṅgaṇikārāmassa; āraddhavīriyassāyaṃ, bhikkhave, dhammo, nāyaṃ dhammo kusītassa; upaṭṭhitassatissāyaṃ, bhikkhave, dhammo, nāyaṃ dhammo muṭṭhassatissa; samāhitassāyaṃ, bhikkhave, dhammo, nāyaṃ dhammo asamāhitassa; paññavato ayaṃ, bhikkhave, dhammo, nāyaṃ [P 4.0233] dhammo duppaññassa; nippapañcārāmassāyaṃ, bhikkhave, dhammo nippapañcaratino, nāyaṃ dhammo papañcārāmassa papañcaratino”.

Then the Blessed One, having advised the Venerable Anuruddha with this exhortation, just as a strong man might stretch out his bent arm or bend his stretched-out arm, disappeared from the Eastern Bamboo Grove among the Cetīs and reappeared in the Bhesakaḷā Grove, the deer park at Suṃsumāragira, among the Bhaggas. The Blessed One sat down on the prepared seat. Having sat down, the Blessed One addressed the monks: 'Monks, I will teach the eight thoughts of a great man. Listen to that... And what, monks, are the eight thoughts of a great man? This Dhamma, monks, is for one with few wishes; this Dhamma is not for one with many wishes. This Dhamma, monks, is for the contented; this Dhamma is not for the discontented. This Dhamma, monks, is for the secluded; this Dhamma is not for one who delights in company. This Dhamma, monks, is for one with aroused energy; this Dhamma is not for the lazy. This Dhamma, monks, is for one with established mindfulness; this Dhamma is not for one with lapsed mindfulness. This Dhamma, monks, is for the concentrated; this Dhamma is not for the unconcentrated. This Dhamma, monks, is for the wise; this Dhamma is not for the unwise. This Dhamma, monks, is for one who delights in non-proliferation and rejoices in non-proliferation; this Dhamma is not for one who delights in proliferation and rejoices in proliferation.'

“‘Appicchassāyaṃ[M 3.0066][V 3.0066], bhikkhave, dhammo, nāyaṃ dhammo mahicchassā’ti, iti kho panetaṃ vuttaṃ. Kiñcetaṃ paṭicca vuttaṃ? Idha, bhikkhave, bhikkhu appiccho samāno ‘appicchoti maṃ jāneyyu’nti na icchati, santuṭṭho samāno ‘santuṭṭhoti maṃ jāneyyu’nti na icchati, pavivitto samāno ‘pavivittoti maṃ jāneyyu’nti na icchati, āraddhavīriyo samāno ‘āraddhavīriyoti maṃ jāneyyu’nti na icchati, upaṭṭhitassati samāno ‘upaṭṭhitassatīti maṃ jāneyyu’nti na icchati, samāhito samāno ‘samāhitoti [T 4.0237] maṃ jāneyyu’nti na icchati, paññavā samāno ‘paññavāti maṃ jāneyyu’nti na icchati, nippapañcārāmo samāno ‘nippapañcārāmoti maṃ jāneyyu’nti na icchati. ‘Appicchassāyaṃ, bhikkhave, dhammo, nāyaṃ dhammo mahicchassā’ti, iti yaṃ taṃ vuttaṃ idametaṃ paṭicca vuttaṃ.

'Monks, this Dhamma is for one with few wishes, not for one with many wishes.' Thus, indeed, was this said. And in reference to what was this said? Here, monks, a monk, being one with few wishes, does not wish, 'May they know me as one with few wishes.' Being contented, he does not wish, 'May they know me as contented.' Being secluded, he does not wish, 'May they know me as secluded.' Being one with aroused energy, he does not wish, 'May they know me as one with aroused energy.' Being one with established mindfulness, he does not wish, 'May they know me as one with established mindfulness.' Being concentrated, he does not wish, 'May they know me as concentrated.' Being wise, he does not wish, 'May they know me as wise.' Delighting in non-proliferation, he does not wish, 'May they know me as one who delights in non-proliferation.' 'Monks, this Dhamma is for one with few wishes, not for one with many wishes'—it was in reference to this that this was said.

“‘Santuṭṭhassāyaṃ, bhikkhave, dhammo, nāyaṃ dhammo asantuṭṭhassā’ti, iti kho panetaṃ vuttaṃ, kiñcetaṃ paṭicca vuttaṃ? Idha, bhikkhave, bhikkhu santuṭṭho hoti itarītaracīvarapiṇḍapātasenāsanagilānapaccayabhesajjaparikkhārena. ‘Santuṭṭhassāyaṃ, bhikkhave, dhammo, nāyaṃ dhammo asantuṭṭhassā’ti, iti yaṃ taṃ vuttaṃ idametaṃ paṭicca vuttaṃ.

'Monks, this Dhamma is for the contented, not for the discontented.' Thus, indeed, was this said. And in reference to what was this said? Here, monks, a monk is contented with whatever kind of robes, almsfood, lodging, and medicinal requisites for the sick. 'Monks, this Dhamma is for the contented, not for the discontented'—it was in reference to this that this was said.

“‘Pavivittassāyaṃ, bhikkhave, dhammo, nāyaṃ dhammo saṅgaṇikārāmassā’ti, iti kho panetaṃ vuttaṃ, kiñcetaṃ paṭicca vuttaṃ? Idha, bhikkhave, bhikkhuno pavivittassa viharato bhavanti upasaṅkamitāro bhikkhū bhikkhuniyo upāsakā upāsikāyo rājāno rājamahāmattā titthiyā titthiyasāvakā. Tatra bhikkhu vivekaninnena cittena vivekapoṇena vivekapabbhārena vivekaṭṭhena nekkhammābhiratena aññadatthu uyyojanikapaṭisaṃyuttaṃyeva kathaṃ kattā pavattā (ka.) hoti. ‘Pavivittassāyaṃ[P 4.0234], bhikkhave, dhammo, nāyaṃ dhammo saṅgaṇikārāmassā’ti, iti yaṃ taṃ vuttaṃ idametaṃ paṭicca vuttaṃ.

'Monks, this Dhamma is for one who is secluded, not for one who delights in company.' Thus, indeed, was this said. And in reference to what was this said? Here, monks, when a monk is dwelling in seclusion, monks, nuns, laymen, laywomen, kings, royal ministers, sectarians, and their disciples come to visit him. There, with a mind inclined to seclusion, sloping towards seclusion, descending towards seclusion, established in seclusion, and delighting in renunciation, he is one who speaks only what is connected with dismissal. 'Monks, this Dhamma is for one who is secluded, not for one who delights in company.' It was with reference to this that this was said.

“‘Āraddhavīriyassāyaṃ, bhikkhave, dhammo, nāyaṃ dhammo kusītassā’ti, iti kho panetaṃ vuttaṃ, kiñcetaṃ paṭicca vuttaṃ? Idha, bhikkhave, bhikkhu āraddhavīriyo viharati akusalānaṃ dhammānaṃ pahānāya kusalānaṃ dhammānaṃ upasampadāya [T 4.0238] thāmavā daḷhaparakkamo anikkhittadhuro kusalesu dhammesu. ‘Āraddhavīriyassāyaṃ[M 3.0067], bhikkhave, dhammo, nāyaṃ dhammo kusītassā’ti, iti yaṃ taṃ vuttaṃ idametaṃ paṭicca vuttaṃ.

'Monks, this Dhamma is for one with aroused energy, not for one who is lazy.' Thus, indeed, was this said. And in reference to what was this said? Here, monks, a monk dwells with aroused energy for the abandoning of unwholesome states and for the acquisition of wholesome states; he is strong, of steadfast exertion, not shirking the responsibility in regard to wholesome states. 'Monks, this Dhamma is for one with aroused energy, not for one who is lazy.' It was with reference to this that this was said.

“‘Upaṭṭhitassatissāyaṃ[V 3.0067], bhikkhave, dhammo, nāyaṃ dhammo muṭṭhassatissā’ti, iti kho panetaṃ vuttaṃ. Kiñcetaṃ paṭicca vuttaṃ? Idha, bhikkhave, bhikkhu satimā hoti paramena satinepakkena samannāgato, cirakatampi cirabhāsitampi saritā anussaritā. ‘Upaṭṭhitassatissāyaṃ, bhikkhave, dhammo, nāyaṃ dhammo, muṭṭhassatissā’ti, iti yaṃ taṃ vuttaṃ idametaṃ paṭicca vuttaṃ.

'Monks, this Dhamma is for one with established mindfulness, not for one with lapsed mindfulness.' Thus, indeed, was this said. And in reference to what was this said? Here, monks, a monk is mindful, endowed with supreme mindfulness and prudence, one who remembers and recollects what was done and said long ago. 'Monks, this Dhamma is for one with established mindfulness, not for one with lapsed mindfulness.' It was with reference to this that this was said.

“‘Samāhitassāyaṃ, bhikkhave, dhammo, nāyaṃ dhammo asamāhitassā’ti, iti kho panetaṃ vuttaṃ. Kiñcetaṃ paṭicca vuttaṃ? Idha, bhikkhave, bhikkhu vivicceva kāmehi…pe… catutthaṃ jhānaṃ upasampajja viharati. ‘Samāhitassāyaṃ, bhikkhave, dhammo, nāyaṃ dhammo asamāhitassā’ti, iti yaṃ taṃ vuttaṃ idametaṃ paṭicca vuttaṃ.

'Monks, this Dhamma is for one who is concentrated, not for one who is unconcentrated.' Thus, indeed, was this said. And in reference to what was this said? Here, monks, a monk, secluded from sensual pleasures… enters and dwells in the fourth jhāna. 'Monks, this Dhamma is for one who is concentrated, not for one who is unconcentrated.' It was with reference to this that this was said.

“‘Paññavato ayaṃ, bhikkhave, dhammo, nāyaṃ dhammo duppaññassā’ti, iti kho panetaṃ vuttaṃ. Kiñcetaṃ paṭicca vuttaṃ? Idha, bhikkhave, bhikkhu paññavā hoti udayatthagāminiyā paññāya samannāgato ariyāya nibbedhikāya sammā dukkhakkhayagāminiyā. ‘Paññavato ayaṃ, bhikkhave, dhammo, nāyaṃ dhammo duppaññassā’ti, iti yaṃ taṃ vuttaṃ idametaṃ paṭicca vuttaṃ.

“‘Monks, this Dhamma is for one with wisdom, not for one without wisdom.’ Thus was it said. And with reference to what was this said? Here, monks, a monk is wise, endowed with the wisdom that discerns arising and passing away, which is noble, penetrative, and leads to the complete destruction of suffering. ‘Monks, this Dhamma is for one with wisdom, not for one without wisdom.’ It was with reference to this that this was said.

“‘Nippapañcārāmassāyaṃ[P 4.0235], bhikkhave, dhammo nippapañcaratino, nāyaṃ dhammo papañcārāmassa papañcaratino’ti, iti kho panetaṃ vuttaṃ. Kiñcetaṃ [T 4.0239] paṭicca vuttaṃ? Idha, bhikkhave, bhikkhuno papañcanirodhe cittaṃ pakkhandati pasīdati santiṭṭhati vimuccati. ‘Nippapañcārāmassāyaṃ, bhikkhave, dhammo, nippapañcaratino, nāyaṃ dhammo papañcārāmassa papañcaratino’ti, iti yaṃ taṃ vuttaṃ idametaṃ paṭicca vutta”nti.

“‘Monks, this Dhamma is for one who delights in non-proliferation and rejoices in non-proliferation, not for one who delights in proliferation and rejoices in proliferation.’ Thus was it said. And with reference to what was this said? Here, monks, a monk’s mind leaps forward to, becomes tranquil in, is established in, and is liberated in the cessation of proliferation. ‘Monks, this Dhamma is for one who delights in non-proliferation and rejoices in non-proliferation, not for one who delights in proliferation and rejoices in proliferation.’ It was with reference to this that this was said.”

Atha kho āyasmā anuruddho āyatikampi vassāvāsaṃ tattheva cetīsu pācīnavaṃsadāye vihāsi. Atha kho āyasmā anuruddho eko vūpakaṭṭho appamatto ātāpī pahitatto viharanto nacirasseva – yassatthāya kulaputtā sammadeva agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajanti, tadanuttaraṃ – brahmacariyapariyosānaṃ diṭṭheva dhamme sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja vihāsi. “Khīṇā jāti, vusitaṃ brahmacariyaṃ, kataṃ karaṇīyaṃ, nāparaṃ itthattāyā”ti abbhaññāsi. Aññataro ca panāyasmā anuruddho arahataṃ [M 3.0068] ahosīti. Atha kho āyasmā anuruddho arahattappatto tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imā gāthāyo abhāsi –

Then the Venerable Anuruddha spent the next rains-residence right there among the Cetis in the Eastern Bamboo Grove. Then the Venerable Anuruddha, dwelling alone, withdrawn, heedful, ardent, and with a dedicated mind, in no long time realized for himself with direct knowledge, in this very life, that unsurpassed culmination of the holy life for which clansmen rightly go forth from the household life into homelessness, and dwelt having attained it. He knew directly: “Birth is destroyed, the holy life has been lived, what had to be done has been done, there is nothing further for this state of being.” And the Venerable Anuruddha became one of the arahants. Then the Venerable Anuruddha, having attained arahantship, at that time uttered these verses:

theragā. 901-903 “Mama [V 3.0068] saṅkappamaññāya, satthā loke anuttaro;

Manomayena kāyena, iddhiyā upasaṅkami.

“Knowing my thought, the Teacher, unsurpassed in the world, approached by means of psychic power with a mind-made body.

“Yathā me ahu saṅkappo, tato uttari desayi;

Nippapañcarato buddho, nippapañcaṃ adesayi.

He taught beyond what my thought had been. The Buddha, who delights in non-proliferation, taught non-proliferation.

“Tassāhaṃ dhammamaññāya, vihāsiṃ sāsane rato;

Tisso vijjā anuppattā, kataṃ buddhassa sāsana”nti. dasamaṃ;

Having understood his Dhamma, I dwelt delighting in his teaching. The three knowledges have been attained; the Buddha’s instruction has been done.” Tenth.

Gahapativaggo tatiyo.

The Chapter on Householders, the Third.

Tassuddānaṃ –

The summary thereof:

Dve [T 4.0240] uggā dve ca hatthakā, mahānāmena jīvako;

Dve balā akkhaṇā vuttā, anuruddhena te dasāti.

Two on Ugga, and two on Hatthaka, Mahānāma, and Jīvaka; two on Powers, the Inopportune Moment, and Anuruddha—these are the ten.

4. Dānavaggo

4. The Chapter on Giving

1. Paṭhamadānasuttaṃ

1. The First Discourse on Giving

31. dī. ni. 3.336 “Aṭṭhimāni[V 3.0069][P 4.0236], bhikkhave, dānāni. Katamāni aṭṭha? Āsajja dānaṃ deti, bhayā dānaṃ deti, ‘adāsi me’ti dānaṃ deti, ‘dassati me’ti dānaṃ deti, ‘sāhu dāna’nti dānaṃ deti, ‘ahaṃ pacāmi, ime na pacanti; nārahāmi pacanto apacantānaṃ dānaṃ adātu’nti dānaṃ deti, ‘imaṃ me dānaṃ dadato kalyāṇo kittisaddo abbhuggacchatī’ti dānaṃ deti, cittālaṅkāracittaparikkhāratthaṃ dānaṃ deti. Imāni kho, bhikkhave, aṭṭha dānānī”ti. Paṭhamaṃ.

31. “Bhikkhus, there are these eight kinds of giving. Which eight? One gives a gift to someone who has approached; one gives a gift out of fear; one gives a gift thinking, ‘He gave to me’; one gives a gift thinking, ‘He will give to me’; one gives a gift thinking, ‘Giving is good’; one gives a gift thinking, ‘I cook, but these do not cook; it is not proper for me, who cooks, not to give to those who do not cook’; one gives a gift thinking, ‘By giving this gift, a good reputation will spread for me’; one gives a gift for the sake of the adornment and requisite of the mind. These, bhikkhus, are indeed the eight kinds of giving.” First.

2. Dutiyadānasuttaṃ

2. The Second Discourse on Giving

32.

32.

kathā. 480 “Saddhā hiriyaṃ kusalañca dānaṃ,

Dhammā ete sappurisānuyātā;

Etañhi maggaṃ diviyaṃ vadanti,

Etena hi gacchati devaloka”nti. dutiyaṃ;

“Faith, moral shame, and wholesome giving—these three qualities are followed by the good. This, indeed, they call the divine path; by this one goes to the heavenly world.” Second.

3. Dānavatthusuttaṃ

3. The Discourse on the Grounds for Giving

33. “Aṭṭhimāni[M 3.0069], bhikkhave, dānavatthūni. Katamāni aṭṭha? Chandā dānaṃ deti, dosā dānaṃ deti, mohā dānaṃ deti, bhayā dānaṃ deti, ‘dinnapubbaṃ katapubbaṃ pitupitāmahehi, nārahāmi porāṇaṃ kulavaṃsaṃ hāpetu’nti dānaṃ deti, ‘imāhaṃ dānaṃ datvā kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā sugatiṃ [T 4.0241] saggaṃ lokaṃ upapajjissāmī’ti dānaṃ deti, ‘imaṃ me dānaṃ dadato cittaṃ pasīdati, attamanatā [P 4.0237] somanassaṃ upajāyatī’ti dānaṃ deti, cittālaṅkāracittaparikkhāratthaṃ dānaṃ deti. Imāni kho, bhikkhave, aṭṭha dānavatthūnī”ti. Tatiyaṃ.

33. “Bhikkhus, there are these eight grounds for giving. Which eight? One gives a gift out of desire; one gives a gift out of hatred; one gives a gift out of delusion; one gives a gift out of fear; one gives a gift thinking, ‘This was given and done before by my fathers and grandfathers; it is not proper for me to let the ancient family lineage decline’; one gives a gift thinking, ‘Having given this gift, with the breakup of the body, after death, I will be reborn in a good destination, a heavenly world’; one gives a gift thinking, ‘When I give this gift, my mind becomes serene, and gladness and joy arise’; one gives a gift for the sake of the adornment and requisite of the mind. These, bhikkhus, are indeed the eight grounds for giving.” The third.

4. Khettasuttaṃ

4. The Discourse on the Field

34. “Aṭṭhaṅgasamannāgate[V 3.0070], bhikkhave, khette bījaṃ vuttaṃ na mahapphalaṃ hoti na mahassādaṃ na phātiseyyaṃ na phātiseyyanti (sī. syā. ka.), na phātiseyyā (katthaci). Kathaṃ aṭṭhaṅgasamannāgate? Idha, bhikkhave, khettaṃ unnāmaninnāmi ca hoti, pāsāṇasakkharikañca hoti, ūsarañca hoti, na ca gambhīrasitaṃ hoti, na āyasampannaṃ hoti, na apāyasampannaṃ hoti, na mātikāsampannaṃ hoti, na mariyādasampannaṃ hoti. Evaṃ aṭṭhaṅgasamannāgate, bhikkhave, khette bījaṃ vuttaṃ na mahapphalaṃ hoti na mahassādaṃ na phātiseyyaṃ.

34. “Bhikkhus, seed sown in a field possessing eight factors is not of great fruit, not of great flavor, and does not lead to growth. Which eight factors? Here, bhikkhus, a field is uneven, with stones and gravel, and saline; it is not deeply ploughed, not endowed with a water inlet, not endowed with a water outlet, not endowed with irrigation channels, and not endowed with boundaries. Bhikkhus, seed sown in a field possessing these eight factors is not of great fruit, not of great flavor, and does not lead to growth.

“Evamevaṃ kho, bhikkhave, aṭṭhaṅgasamannāgatesu samaṇabrāhmaṇesu dānaṃ dinnaṃ na mahapphalaṃ hoti na mahānisaṃsaṃ na mahājutikaṃ na mahāvipphāraṃ. Kathaṃ aṭṭhaṅgasamannāgatesu? Idha, bhikkhave, samaṇabrāhmaṇā micchādiṭṭhikā honti, micchāsaṅkappā, micchāvācā, micchākammantā, micchāājīvā, micchāvāyāmā, micchāsatino, micchāsamādhino. Evaṃ aṭṭhaṅgasamannāgatesu, bhikkhave, samaṇabrāhmaṇesu dānaṃ dinnaṃ na mahapphalaṃ hoti na mahānisaṃsaṃ na mahājutikaṃ na mahāvipphāraṃ.

“Just so, bhikkhus, a gift given to ascetics and brahmins who possess eight factors is not of great fruit, not of great benefit, not of great radiance, and not of great pervasiveness. Which eight factors? Here, bhikkhus, ascetics and brahmins are of wrong view, wrong intention, wrong speech, wrong action, wrong livelihood, wrong effort, wrong mindfulness, and wrong concentration. Bhikkhus, a gift given to ascetics and brahmins who possess these eight factors is not of great fruit, not of great benefit, not of great radiance, and not of great pervasiveness.

“Aṭṭhaṅgasamannāgate, bhikkhave, khette bījaṃ vuttaṃ mahapphalaṃ hoti mahassādaṃ [T 4.0242] phātiseyyaṃ. Kathaṃ aṭṭhaṅgasamannāgate? Idha, bhikkhave, khettaṃ anunnāmāninnāmi ca hoti, apāsāṇasakkharikañca hoti, anūsarañca hoti, gambhīrasitaṃ [P 4.0238] hoti, āyasampannaṃ hoti, apāyasampannaṃ hoti, mātikāsampannaṃ [M 3.0070] hoti, mariyādasampannaṃ hoti. Evaṃ aṭṭhaṅgasamannāgate, bhikkhave, khette bījaṃ vuttaṃ mahapphalaṃ hoti mahassādaṃ phātiseyyaṃ.

Bhikkhus, seed sown in a field possessing eight factors is of great fruit, of great flavor, and leads to growth. What eight factors? Here, bhikkhus, a field is level, without stones and gravel, and not saline; it is deeply ploughed, endowed with a water inlet, endowed with a water outlet, endowed with irrigation channels, and endowed with boundaries. Bhikkhus, seed sown in a field possessing these eight factors is of great fruit, of great flavor, and leads to growth.

“Evamevaṃ kho, bhikkhave, aṭṭhaṅgasamannāgatesu samaṇabrāhmaṇesu dānaṃ dinnaṃ mahapphalaṃ hoti mahānisaṃsaṃ mahājutikaṃ mahāvipphāraṃ. Kathaṃ aṭṭhaṅgasamannāgatesu? Idha, bhikkhave, samaṇabrāhmaṇā sammādiṭṭhikā honti, sammāsaṅkappā, sammāvācā, sammākammantā, sammāājīvā, sammāvāyāmā, sammāsatino, sammāsamādhino. Evaṃ aṭṭhaṅgasamannāgatesu, bhikkhave, samaṇabrāhmaṇesu dānaṃ dinnaṃ mahapphalaṃ hoti mahānisaṃsaṃ mahājutikaṃ mahāvipphāra”nti.

Just so, bhikkhus, a gift given to ascetics and brahmins who possess eight factors is of great fruit, of great benefit, of great radiance, and of great pervasiveness. What eight factors? Here, bhikkhus, ascetics and brahmins are of right view, right intention, right speech, right action, right livelihood, right effort, right mindfulness, and right concentration. Bhikkhus, a gift given to ascetics and brahmins who possess these eight factors is of great fruit, of great benefit, of great radiance, and of great pervasiveness.

“Yathāpi [V 3.0071] khette sampanne, pavuttā bījasampadā;

Deve sampādayantamhi sañjāyantamhi (ka.), hoti dhaññassa sampadā.

Just as in a perfected field, with the perfection of sown seed, when the rain provides sufficiently, there is a perfection of grain.

“Anītisampadā hoti, virūḷhī bhavati sampadā;

Vepullasampadā hoti, phalaṃ ve hoti sampadā.

The perfection of freedom from calamity occurs; growth becomes a perfection; there is the perfection of abundance; and the fruit is indeed a perfection.

“Evaṃ sampannasīlesu, dinnā bhojanasampadā;

Sampadānaṃ upaneti, sampannaṃ hissa taṃ kataṃ.

Thus, the perfection of food given to those perfected in virtue leads to the perfection of wholesome qualities; for that deed of his is indeed perfected.

“Tasmā sampadamākaṅkhī, sampannatthūdha puggalo;

Sampannapaññe [T 4.0243] sevetha, evaṃ ijjhanti sampadā.

Therefore, a person in this world who has perfected requisites for offering and desires perfection should associate with those of perfected wisdom; thus are the perfections achieved.

“Vijjācaraṇasampanne, laddhā cittassa sampadaṃ;

Karoti kammasampadaṃ, labhati catthasampadaṃ.

For one perfected in knowledge and conduct, having attained perfection of mind, one performs a perfected action and obtains the perfection of the goal.

“Lokaṃ ñatvā yathābhūtaṃ, pappuyya diṭṭhisampadaṃ;

Maggasampadamāgamma, yāti sampannamānaso.

That one with a perfected mind, having known the world as it truly is, having arrived at the perfection of view, and having resorted to the perfection of the path, proceeds.

“Odhunitvā [P 4.0239] malaṃ sabbaṃ, patvā nibbānasampadaṃ;

Muccati sabbadukkhehi, sā hoti sabbasampadā”ti. catutthaṃ;

Having shaken off every stain, having reached the perfection of Nibbāna, one is released from all suffering. That is the perfection of all things. Fourth.

5. Dānūpapattisuttaṃ

5. The Discourse on Rebirths from Giving

35. dī. ni. 3.337 “Aṭṭhimā, bhikkhave, dānūpapattiyo. Katamā aṭṭha? Idha, bhikkhave, ekacco dānaṃ deti samaṇassa vā brāhmaṇassa vā annaṃ pānaṃ vatthaṃ yānaṃ [M 3.0071] mālāgandhavilepanaṃ seyyāvasathapadīpeyyaṃ. So yaṃ deti taṃ paccāsīsati paccāsiṃsati (sī. syā. kaṃ. pī.). So passati khattiyamahāsāle vā brāhmaṇamahāsāle vā gahapatimahāsāle vā pañcahi kāmaguṇehi samappite samaṅgībhūte paricārayamāne. Tassa evaṃ hoti – ‘aho vatāhaṃ kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā khattiyamahāsālānaṃ [V 3.0072] vā brāhmaṇamahāsālānaṃ vā gahapatimahāsālānaṃ vā sahabyataṃ upapajjeyya’nti! So taṃ cittaṃ dahati, taṃ cittaṃ adhiṭṭhāti, taṃ cittaṃ bhāveti. Tassa taṃ cittaṃ hīne vimuttaṃ hīnedhimuttaṃ (syā. pī.) vimuttanti adhimuttaṃ, vimuttanti vā vissaṭṭhaṃ (ṭīkāsaṃvaṇṇanā), uttari abhāvitaṃ, tatrūpapattiyā saṃvattati. Kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā khattiyamahāsālānaṃ vā brāhmaṇamahāsālānaṃ vā gahapatimahāsālānaṃ vā sahabyataṃ upapajjati. Tañca kho sīlavato vadāmi, no dussīlassa. Ijjhati, bhikkhave, sīlavato cetopaṇidhi visuddhattā.

35. Bhikkhus, there are these eight rebirths from giving. What eight? Here, bhikkhus, a certain person gives a gift to an ascetic or a brahmin—food, drink, clothing, a vehicle, garlands, scents, and ointments, a bed, a dwelling, and a lamp. He gives aspiring for a return. He sees great nobles, great brahmins, or great householders furnished and endowed with the five strands of sensual pleasure, enjoying themselves. It occurs to him: ‘Oh, that with the breakup of the body, after death, I might be reborn into companionship with great nobles, great brahmins, or great householders!’ He sets his mind on that, resolves upon it, and develops it. That mind of his, being inclined to the inferior and not developed further, leads to rebirth there. With the breakup of the body, after death, he is reborn into companionship with great nobles, great brahmins, or great householders. And this, I say, is for the virtuous, not for the immoral. Bhikkhus, the aspiration of the virtuous succeeds because of their purity.

“Idha pana, bhikkhave, ekacco dānaṃ deti [T 4.0244] samaṇassa vā brāhmaṇassa vā annaṃ pānaṃ vatthaṃ yānaṃ mālāgandhavilepanaṃ seyyāvasathapadīpeyyaṃ. So yaṃ deti taṃ paccāsīsati. Tassa sutaṃ hoti – ‘cātumahārājikā cātummahārājikā (sī. syā. kaṃ. pī.) devā [P 4.0240] dīghāyukā vaṇṇavanto sukhabahulā’ti. Tassa evaṃ hoti – ‘aho vatāhaṃ kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā cātumahārājikānaṃ devānaṃ sahabyataṃ upapajjeyya’nti. So taṃ cittaṃ dahati, taṃ cittaṃ adhiṭṭhāti, taṃ cittaṃ bhāveti. Tassa taṃ cittaṃ hīne vimuttaṃ, uttari abhāvitaṃ, tatrūpapattiyā saṃvattati. Kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā cātumahārājikānaṃ devānaṃ sahabyataṃ upapajjati. Tañca kho sīlavato vadāmi, no dussīlassa. Ijjhati, bhikkhave, sīlavato cetopaṇidhi visuddhattā.

Furthermore, bhikkhus, here a certain person gives a gift to an ascetic or a brahmin—food, drink, clothing, a vehicle, garlands, scents, and ointments, a bed, a dwelling, and a lamp. He gives aspiring for a return. He has heard: ‘The devas of the realm of the Four Great Kings are long-lived, beautiful, and have much happiness.’ It occurs to him: ‘Oh, that with the breakup of the body, after death, I might be reborn into companionship with the devas of the realm of the Four Great Kings!’ He sets his mind on that, resolves upon it, and develops it. That mind of his, being inclined to the inferior and not developed further, leads to rebirth there. With the breakup of the body, after death, he is reborn into companionship with the devas of the realm of the Four Great Kings. And this, I say, is for the virtuous, not for the immoral. Bhikkhus, the aspiration of the virtuous succeeds because of their purity.

“Idha pana, bhikkhave, ekacco dānaṃ deti samaṇassa vā brāhmaṇassa vā annaṃ pānaṃ vatthaṃ yānaṃ mālāgandhavilepanaṃ seyyāvasathapadīpeyyaṃ. So yaṃ deti taṃ paccāsīsati. Tassa sutaṃ hoti – tāvatiṃsā devā…pe… yāmā devā… tusitā devā… nimmānaratī devā… paranimmitavasavattī devā dīghāyukā vaṇṇavanto sukhabahulāti. Tassa evaṃ hoti – ‘aho vatāhaṃ kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā paranimmitavasavattīnaṃ devānaṃ sahabyataṃ upapajjeyya’nti. So taṃ cittaṃ dahati, taṃ cittaṃ adhiṭṭhāti[M 3.0072], taṃ cittaṃ bhāveti. Tassa taṃ cittaṃ hīne vimuttaṃ, uttari abhāvitaṃ, tatrūpapattiyā saṃvattati. Kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā paranimmitavasavattīnaṃ devānaṃ sahabyataṃ upapajjati. Tañca kho sīlavato vadāmi, no dussīlassa. Ijjhati, bhikkhave, sīlavato cetopaṇidhi visuddhattā.

Furthermore, bhikkhus, here a certain person gives a gift to an ascetic or a brahmin—food, drink, clothing, a vehicle, garlands, scents, and ointments, a bed, a dwelling, and a lamp. He gives aspiring for a return. He has heard: ‘The devas of the realm of the Thirty-three… the Yāma devas… the Tusita devas… the Nimmānaratī devas… the Paranimmitavasavattī devas are long-lived, beautiful, and have much happiness.’ It occurs to him: ‘Oh, that with the breakup of the body, after death, I might be reborn into companionship with the Paranimmitavasavattī devas!’ He sets his mind on that, resolves upon it, and develops it. That mind of his, being inclined to the inferior and not developed further, leads to rebirth there. With the breakup of the body, after death, he is reborn into companionship with the Paranimmitavasavattī devas. And this, I say, is for the virtuous, not for the immoral. Bhikkhus, the aspiration of the virtuous succeeds because of their purity.

“Idha pana, bhikkhave, ekacco [T 4.0245] dānaṃ deti samaṇassa vā brāhmaṇassa vā annaṃ pānaṃ vatthaṃ yānaṃ mālāgandhavilepanaṃ seyyāvasathapadīpeyyaṃ. So yaṃ deti taṃ paccāsīsati. Tassa sutaṃ hoti [V 3.0073] – ‘brahmakāyikā devā dīghāyukā [P 4.0241] vaṇṇavanto sukhabahulā’ti. Tassa evaṃ hoti – ‘aho vatāhaṃ kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā brahmakāyikānaṃ devānaṃ sahabyataṃ upapajjeyya’nti. So taṃ cittaṃ dahati, taṃ cittaṃ adhiṭṭhāti, taṃ cittaṃ bhāveti. Tassa taṃ cittaṃ hīne vimuttaṃ, uttari abhāvitaṃ, tatrūpapattiyā saṃvattati. Kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā brahmakāyikānaṃ devānaṃ sahabyataṃ upapajjati. Tañca kho sīlavato vadāmi, no dussīlassa; vītarāgassa, no sarāgassa. Ijjhati, bhikkhave, sīlavato cetopaṇidhi vītarāgattā. Imā kho, bhikkhave, aṭṭha dānūpapattiyo”ti. Pañcamaṃ.

“Furthermore, Bhikkhus, here a certain person gives a gift to a recluse or a brahmin—food, drink, clothing, a vehicle, garlands, scents, and ointments, a bed, a dwelling, and a lamp. He gives aspiring for a return. He has heard: ‘The devas of Brahma’s company are long-lived, beautiful, and have much happiness.’ It occurs to him: ‘Oh, that with the breakup of the body, after death, I might be reborn into the companionship of the devas of Brahma’s company!’ He sets his mind on that, resolves upon it, and develops it. That mind of his, being devoted to the inferior and not developed for what is higher, leads to rebirth there. With the breakup of the body, after death, he is reborn into the companionship of the devas of Brahma’s company. And this, I say, is for one who is virtuous, not for one who is immoral; for one free from passion, not for one with passion. Bhikkhus, the aspiration of one who is virtuous succeeds because of freedom from passion. These, Bhikkhus, are the eight rebirths resulting from giving.” Fifth.

6. Puññakiriyavatthusuttaṃ

6. The Discourse on the Bases of Meritorious Action

36. “Tīṇimāni, bhikkhave, puññakiriyavatthūni. Katamāni tīṇi? Dānamayaṃ puññakiriyavatthu puññakiriyavatthuṃ (sī. pī.) evamuparipi, sīlamayaṃ puññakiriyavatthu, bhāvanāmayaṃ puññakiriyavatthu. Idha, bhikkhave, ekaccassa dānamayaṃ puññakiriyavatthu parittaṃ kataṃ hoti, sīlamayaṃ puññakiriyavatthu parittaṃ kataṃ hoti, bhāvanāmayaṃ puññakiriyavatthuṃ puññakiriyavatthu (syā.) nābhisambhoti. So kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā manussadobhagyaṃ upapajjati.

36. “Bhikkhus, there are these three bases of meritorious action. What three? The basis of meritorious action consisting of giving, the basis of meritorious action consisting of virtue, and the basis of meritorious action consisting of development. Here, Bhikkhus, for a certain person the basis of meritorious action consisting of giving is done to a limited extent, the basis of meritorious action consisting of virtue is done to a limited extent, and the basis of meritorious action consisting of development is not accomplished. With the breakup of the body, after death, he is reborn in an unfortunate human state.

“Idha pana, bhikkhave, ekaccassa dānamayaṃ puññakiriyavatthu mattaso kataṃ hoti, sīlamayaṃ puññakiriyavatthu mattaso kataṃ hoti, bhāvanāmayaṃ puññakiriyavatthuṃ nābhisambhoti. So kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā manussasobhagyaṃ upapajjati.

“Furthermore, Bhikkhus, for a certain person the basis of meritorious action consisting of giving is done to a moderate extent, the basis of meritorious action consisting of virtue is done to a moderate extent, but the basis of meritorious action consisting of development is not accomplished. With the breakup of the body, after death, he is reborn in a fortunate human state.

“Idha [M 3.0073] pana, bhikkhave[T 4.0246], ekaccassa dānamayaṃ puññakiriyavatthu adhimattaṃ kataṃ hoti, sīlamayaṃ puññakiriyavatthu [P 4.0242] adhimattaṃ kataṃ hoti, bhāvanāmayaṃ puññakiriyavatthuṃ nābhisambhoti. So kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā cātumahārājikānaṃ devānaṃ sahabyataṃ upapajjati. Tatra, bhikkhave, cattāro mahārājāno dānamayaṃ puññakiriyavatthuṃ atirekaṃ karitvā, sīlamayaṃ puññakiriyavatthuṃ atirekaṃ karitvā, cātumahārājike deve dasahi ṭhānehi adhigaṇhanti – dibbena āyunā, dibbena vaṇṇena, dibbena sukhena, dibbena yasena, dibbena ādhipateyyena, dibbehi rūpehi, dibbehi saddehi, dibbehi gandhehi, dibbehi rasehi, dibbehi phoṭṭhabbehi.

“Furthermore, Bhikkhus, for a certain person the basis of meritorious action consisting of giving is done to an abundant extent, the basis of meritorious action consisting of virtue is done to an abundant extent, but the basis of meritorious action consisting of development is not accomplished. With the breakup of the body, after death, he is reborn into companionship with the devas of the Four Great Kings. There, Bhikkhus, the Four Great Kings, having performed the basis of meritorious action consisting of giving to an excellent degree and the basis of meritorious action consisting of virtue to an excellent degree, surpass the devas of the Four Great Kings in ten respects: in divine lifespan, divine beauty, divine happiness, divine retinue, divine dominion, divine sights, divine sounds, divine scents, divine tastes, and divine tactile sensations.

“Idha [V 3.0074] pana, bhikkhave, ekaccassa dānamayaṃ puññakiriyavatthu adhimattaṃ kataṃ hoti, sīlamayaṃ puññakiriyavatthu adhimattaṃ kataṃ hoti, bhāvanāmayaṃ puññakiriyavatthuṃ nābhisambhoti. So kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā tāvatiṃsānaṃ devānaṃ sahabyataṃ upapajjati. Tatra, bhikkhave, sakko devānamindo dānamayaṃ puññakiriyavatthuṃ atirekaṃ karitvā sīlamayaṃ puññakiriyavatthuṃ atirekaṃ karitvā tāvatiṃse deve dasahi ṭhānehi adhigaṇhāti – dibbena āyunā…pe… dibbehi phoṭṭhabbehi.

“Furthermore, Bhikkhus, for a certain person the basis of meritorious action consisting of giving is done to an abundant extent, the basis of meritorious action consisting of virtue is done to an abundant extent, but the basis of meritorious action consisting of development is not accomplished. With the breakup of the body, after death, he is reborn into companionship with the Tāvatiṃsa devas. There, Bhikkhus, Sakka, lord of the devas, having performed the basis of meritorious action consisting of giving to an excellent degree and the basis of meritorious action consisting of virtue to an excellent degree, surpasses the Tāvatiṃsa devas in ten respects: with divine lifespan...and with divine tactile sensations.

“Idha pana, bhikkhave, ekaccassa dānamayaṃ puññakiriyavatthu adhimattaṃ kataṃ hoti, sīlamayaṃ puññakiriyavatthu adhimattaṃ kataṃ hoti, bhāvanāmayaṃ puññakiriyavatthuṃ nābhisambhoti. So kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā yāmānaṃ devānaṃ sahabyataṃ upapajjati. Tatra, bhikkhave, suyāmo devaputto dānamayaṃ puññakiriyavatthuṃ atirekaṃ karitvā[T 4.0247], sīlamayaṃ puññakiriyavatthuṃ atirekaṃ karitvā, yāme deve dasahi ṭhānehi adhigaṇhāti – dibbena āyunā…pe… dibbehi phoṭṭhabbehi.

“Furthermore, Bhikkhus, for a certain person the basis of meritorious action consisting of giving is done to an abundant extent, the basis of meritorious action consisting of virtue is done to an abundant extent, but the basis of meritorious action consisting of development is not accomplished. With the breakup of the body, after death, he is reborn into companionship with the Yāma devas. There, Bhikkhus, the deva Suyāma, having performed the basis of meritorious action consisting of giving to an excellent degree and the basis of meritorious action consisting of virtue to an excellent degree, surpasses the Yāma devas in ten respects: with divine lifespan...and with divine tactile sensations.

“Idha pana, bhikkhave, ekaccassa dānamayaṃ puññakiriyavatthu adhimattaṃ kataṃ hoti, sīlamayaṃ puññakiriyavatthu adhimattaṃ kataṃ hoti, bhāvanāmayaṃ puññakiriyavatthuṃ nābhisambhoti. So kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā tusitānaṃ devānaṃ sahabyataṃ upapajjati. Tatra[P 4.0243], bhikkhave, santusito devaputto dānamayaṃ puññakiriyavatthuṃ atirekaṃ karitvā, sīlamayaṃ puññakiriyavatthuṃ atirekaṃ karitvā, tusite deve dasahi ṭhānehi adhigaṇhāti – dibbena āyunā…pe… dibbehi phoṭṭhabbehi.

“Furthermore, Bhikkhus, for a certain person the basis of meritorious action consisting of giving is done to an abundant extent, the basis of meritorious action consisting of virtue is done to an abundant extent, but the basis of meritorious action consisting of development is not accomplished. With the breakup of the body, after death, he is reborn into companionship with the Tusita devas. There, Bhikkhus, the deva Santusita, having performed the basis of meritorious action consisting of giving to an excellent degree and the basis of meritorious action consisting of virtue to an excellent degree, surpasses the Tusita devas in ten respects: with divine lifespan...and with divine tactile sensations.

“Idha [M 3.0074] pana, bhikkhave, ekaccassa dānamayaṃ puññakiriyavatthu adhimattaṃ kataṃ hoti, sīlamayaṃ puññakiriyavatthu adhimattaṃ kataṃ hoti, bhāvanāmayaṃ puññakiriyavatthuṃ nābhisambhoti. So kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā nimmānaratīnaṃ devānaṃ sahabyataṃ upapajjati. Tatra, bhikkhave, sunimmito devaputto dānamayaṃ puññakiriyavatthuṃ atirekaṃ karitvā, sīlamayaṃ puññakiriyavatthuṃ atirekaṃ karitvā, nimmānaratīdeve dasahi ṭhānehi adhigaṇhāti – dibbena āyunā…pe… dibbehi phoṭṭhabbehi.

“Furthermore, Bhikkhus, for a certain person the basis of meritorious action consisting of giving is done to an abundant extent, the basis of meritorious action consisting of virtue is done to an abundant extent, but the basis of meritorious action consisting of development is not accomplished. With the breakup of the body, after death, he is reborn into companionship with the Nimmānaratī devas. There, Bhikkhus, the deva Sunimmita, having performed the basis of meritorious action consisting of giving to an excellent degree and the basis of meritorious action consisting of virtue to an excellent degree, surpasses the Nimmānaratī devas in ten respects: with divine lifespan...and with divine tactile sensations.

“Idha [V 3.0075] pana, bhikkhave, ekaccassa dānamayaṃ puññakiriyavatthu adhimattaṃ kataṃ hoti, sīlamayaṃ puññakiriyavatthu adhimattaṃ kataṃ hoti, bhāvanāmayaṃ puññakiriyavatthuṃ nābhisambhoti. So kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā paranimmitavasavattīnaṃ devānaṃ sahabyataṃ upapajjati. Tatra, bhikkhave, vasavattī devaputto dānamayaṃ puññakiriyavatthuṃ atirekaṃ [T 4.0248] karitvā, sīlamayaṃ puññakiriyavatthuṃ atirekaṃ karitvā, paranimmitavasavattīdeve dasahi ṭhānehi adhigaṇhāti – dibbena āyunā, dibbena vaṇṇena, dibbena sukhena, dibbena yasena, dibbena ādhipateyyena, dibbehi rūpehi, dibbehi saddehi, dibbehi gandhehi, dibbehi rasehi, dibbehi phoṭṭhabbehi. Imāni kho, bhikkhave, tīṇi puññakiriyavatthūnī”ti. Chaṭṭhaṃ.

“Here, bhikkhus, for a certain person the basis of meritorious action consisting of giving is done to an abundant extent, the basis of meritorious action consisting of virtue is done to an abundant extent, but the basis of meritorious action consisting of development is not accomplished. With the breakup of the body, after death, he is reborn into companionship with the devas of the realm of Those Who Wield Power over the Creations of Others. There, bhikkhus, the deva Vasavattī, having performed the basis of meritorious action consisting of giving to an excellent degree and the basis of meritorious action consisting of virtue to an excellent degree, surpasses the devas of the realm of Those Who Wield Power over the Creations of Others in ten respects: in divine lifespan, divine beauty, divine happiness, divine glory, divine dominion, divine forms, divine sounds, divine scents, divine tastes, and divine tactile sensations. These, bhikkhus, are the three bases of meritorious action.” Sixth.

7. Sappurisadānasuttaṃ

7. The Discourse on the Giving of a Good Person

37. “Aṭṭhimāni, bhikkhave, sappurisadānāni. Katamāni aṭṭha? Suciṃ [P 4.0244] deti, paṇītaṃ deti, kālena deti, kappiyaṃ deti, viceyya deti, abhiṇhaṃ deti, dadaṃ cittaṃ pasādeti, datvā attamano hoti. Imāni kho, bhikkhave, aṭṭha sappurisadānānī”ti.

37. “Bhikkhus, there are these eight kinds of giving of a good person. What are the eight? One gives what is pure, one gives what is choice, one gives at a suitable time, one gives what is allowable, one gives after careful consideration, one gives frequently, while giving one gladdens one's mind, and having given, one is joyful. These, bhikkhus, are the eight kinds of giving of a good person.”

“Suciṃ paṇītaṃ kālena, kappiyaṃ pānabhojanaṃ;

Abhiṇhaṃ dadāti dānaṃ, sukhettesu sukhette (sī. pī.) brahmacārisu.

“One frequently gives a pure, choice, timely, and allowable gift of drink and food to those who lead the holy life, who are like a fertile field.

“Neva na ca (sī. pī.) vippaṭisārissa, cajitvā āmisaṃ bahuṃ;

Evaṃ dinnāni dānāni, vaṇṇayanti vipassino.

“Having given away much material wealth, one has no regret; gifts given in this way are praised by the discerning.

“Evaṃ yajitvā medhāvī, saddho muttena cetasā;

Abyābajjhaṃ abyāpajjhaṃ (ka.) a. ni. 4.40; 6.37 sukhaṃ lokaṃ, paṇḍito upapajjatī”ti. sattamaṃ;

“Thus having made offerings with a liberated mind, the faithful, intelligent, and wise person is reborn into a happy, unafflicted world.” Seventh.

8. Sappurisasuttaṃ

8. The Discourse on a Good Person

38. “Sappuriso[M 3.0075], bhikkhave, kule jāyamāno bahuno janassa atthāya hitāya sukhāya hoti – mātāpitūnaṃ atthāya hitāya sukhāya hoti, puttadārassa atthāya hitāya sukhāya hoti[V 3.0076], dāsakammakaraporisassa atthāya hitāya sukhāya hoti, mittāmaccānaṃ atthāya hitāya sukhāya hoti, pubbapetānaṃ atthāya hitāya sukhāya hoti, rañño [T 4.0249] atthāya hitāya sukhāya hoti, devatānaṃ atthāya hitāya sukhāya hoti, samaṇabrāhmaṇānaṃ atthāya hitāya sukhāya hoti.

38. “Bhikkhus, when a good person is born into a family, it is for the benefit, welfare, and happiness of many people: for the benefit, welfare, and happiness of his mother and father; for the benefit, welfare, and happiness of his children and wife; for the benefit, welfare, and happiness of his slaves, workers, and servants; for the benefit, welfare, and happiness of his friends and companions; for the benefit, welfare, and happiness of his departed ancestors; for the benefit, welfare, and happiness of the king; for the benefit, welfare, and happiness of the devas; and for the benefit, welfare, and happiness of ascetics and brahmins.

“Seyyathāpi, bhikkhave, mahāmegho sabbasassāni sampādento bahuno janassa atthāya hitāya sukhāya hitāya…pe… (syā. ka.) hoti; evamevaṃ kho, bhikkhave, sappuriso kule jāyamāno bahuno janassa atthāya hitāya sukhāya hoti – mātāpitūnaṃ atthāya hitāya sukhāya hoti, puttadārassa atthāya hitāya sukhāya hoti, dāsakammakaraporisassa atthāya hitāya sukhāya hoti, mittāmaccānaṃ atthāya hitāya sukhāya hoti, pubbapetānaṃ atthāya hitāya sukhāya hoti, rañño atthāya hitāya sukhāya [P 4.0245] hoti, devatānaṃ atthāya hitāya sukhāya hoti, samaṇabrāhmaṇānaṃ atthāya hitāya sukhāya hotī”ti.

“Just as, bhikkhus, a great rain cloud, bringing all crops to fruition, is for the benefit, welfare, and happiness of many people; so too, bhikkhus, a good person born into a family is for the benefit, welfare, and happiness of many people: for the benefit, welfare, and happiness of his mother and father; for the benefit, welfare, and happiness of his children and wife; for the benefit, welfare, and happiness of his slaves, workers, and servants; for the benefit, welfare, and happiness of his friends and companions; for the benefit, welfare, and happiness of his departed ancestors; for the benefit, welfare, and happiness of the king; for the benefit, welfare, and happiness of the devas; and for the benefit, welfare, and happiness of ascetics and brahmins.”

“Bahūnaṃ bahunnaṃ (sī. pī.) vata atthāya, sappañño gharamāvasaṃ;

Mātaraṃ pitaraṃ pubbe, rattindivamatandito.

“A wise person dwelling at home is truly for the benefit of many. Untiring day and night, he first attends to his mother and father.

“Pūjeti sahadhammena, pubbekatamanussaraṃ;

Anāgāre pabbajite, apace brahmacārayo brahmacārino (syā.).

“Remembering what they have done in the past, he honors them in accordance with the Dhamma. He pays respect to the homeless ones who have gone forth, those who lead the holy life.

“Niviṭṭhasaddho pūjeti, ñatvā dhamme ca pesalo pesale (ka.);

Rañño hito devahito, ñātīnaṃ sakhinaṃ hito.

“With established faith, knowing the Dhamma and being amiable, he gives honor. He is one who promotes the welfare of the king, the devas, relatives, and friends.

“Sabbesaṃ sabbesu (ka.) so sa (syā. pī. ka.) hito hoti, saddhamme suppatiṭṭhito;

Vineyya maccheramalaṃ, sa lokaṃ bhajate siva”nti. aṭṭhamaṃ;

“He promotes the welfare of all, being well-established in the Good Dhamma. Having dispelled the stain of stinginess, he resorts to the auspicious world.” Eighth.

9. Abhisandasuttaṃ

9. The Discourse on Flows of Merit

39. “Aṭṭhime[M 3.0076], bhikkhave, puññābhisandā kusalābhisandā sukhassāhārā [T 4.0250] sovaggikā sukhavipākā [V 3.0077] saggasaṃvattanikā, iṭṭhāya kantāya manāpāya hitāya sukhāya saṃvattanti. Katame aṭṭha? Idha, bhikkhave, ariyasāvako buddhaṃ saraṇaṃ gato hoti. Ayaṃ, bhikkhave, paṭhamo puññābhisando kusalābhisando sukhassāhāro sovaggiko sukhavipāko saggasaṃvattaniko, iṭṭhāya kantāya manāpāya hitāya sukhāya saṃvattati.

39. “Bhikkhus, there are these eight flows of merit, flows of the wholesome, which bring happiness, are heavenly, ripen in happiness, and are conducive to heaven; they lead to what is desirable, agreeable, pleasing, beneficial, and happy. What are the eight? Here, bhikkhus, a noble disciple has gone to the Buddha for refuge. This, bhikkhus, is the first flow of merit, flow of the wholesome, which brings happiness, is heavenly, ripens in happiness, and is conducive to heaven; it leads to what is desirable, agreeable, pleasing, beneficial, and happy.

“Puna caparaṃ, bhikkhave, ariyasāvako dhammaṃ saraṇaṃ gato hoti. Ayaṃ, bhikkhave, dutiyo puññābhisando…pe… saṃvattati.

“Furthermore, bhikkhus, a noble disciple has gone to the Dhamma for refuge. This, bhikkhus, is the second flow of merit… and so on… it leads to what is desirable, agreeable, pleasing, beneficial, and happy.

“Puna caparaṃ, bhikkhave, ariyasāvako saṅghaṃ saraṇaṃ gato hoti. Ayaṃ, bhikkhave, tatiyo puññābhisando kusalābhisando sukhassāhāro sovaggiko sukhavipāko saggasaṃvattaniko, iṭṭhāya kantāya manāpāya hitāya sukhāya saṃvattati.

“Furthermore, bhikkhus, a noble disciple has gone to the Saṅgha for refuge. This, bhikkhus, is the third flow of merit, flow of the wholesome, which brings happiness, is heavenly, ripens in happiness, and is conducive to heaven; it leads to what is desirable, agreeable, pleasing, beneficial, and happy.”

kathā. 480 “Pañcimāni[P 4.0246], bhikkhave, dānāni mahādānāni aggaññāni rattaññāni vaṃsaññāni porāṇāni asaṃkiṇṇāni asaṃkiṇṇapubbāni, na saṃkiyanti na saṃkiyissanti, appaṭikuṭṭhāni appatikuṭṭhāni (sī.) samaṇehi brāhmaṇehi viññūhi. Katamāni pañca? Idha, bhikkhave, ariyasāvako pāṇātipātaṃ pahāya pāṇātipātā paṭivirato hoti. Pāṇātipātā paṭivirato, bhikkhave, ariyasāvako aparimāṇānaṃ sattānaṃ abhayaṃ deti, averaṃ deti, abyābajjhaṃ abyāpajjhaṃ (ka.) evamuparipi deti. Aparimāṇānaṃ sattānaṃ abhayaṃ datvā averaṃ datvā abyābajjhaṃ datvā aparimāṇassa abhayassa averassa abyābajjhassa bhāgī hoti. Idaṃ, bhikkhave, paṭhamaṃ dānaṃ mahādānaṃ aggaññaṃ rattaññaṃ vaṃsaññaṃ porāṇaṃ asaṃkiṇṇaṃ asaṃkiṇṇapubbaṃ, na saṃkiyati na saṃkiyissati, appaṭikuṭṭhaṃ samaṇehi [T 4.0251] brāhmaṇehi viññūhi. Ayaṃ, bhikkhave, catuttho puññābhisando kusalābhisando sukhassāhāro sovaggiko sukhavipāko saggasaṃvattaniko, iṭṭhāya kantāya manāpāya hitāya sukhāya saṃvattati.

“Bhikkhus, there are these five great gifts—foremost, of long standing, of ancient lineage, ancient, uncorrupted, never before corrupted, not being corrupted, and not to be corrupted, unreproached by wise ascetics and brahmins. Which five? Here, bhikkhus, a noble disciple, having abandoned the destruction of life, abstains from the destruction of life. By abstaining from the destruction of life, bhikkhus, a noble disciple gives to immeasurable beings freedom from fear, freedom from hostility, and freedom from affliction. Having given to immeasurable beings freedom from fear, freedom from hostility, and freedom from affliction, he partakes in immeasurable freedom from fear, freedom from hostility, and freedom from affliction. This, bhikkhus, is the first great gift—foremost, of long standing, of ancient lineage, ancient, uncorrupted, never before corrupted, not being corrupted, and not to be corrupted, unreproached by wise ascetics and brahmins. This, bhikkhus, is the fourth stream of merit, stream of the wholesome, nourishment for happiness—heavenly, ripening in happiness, conducive to heaven—that leads to what is desirable, agreeable, pleasing, beneficial, and pleasant.”

“Puna caparaṃ, bhikkhave, ariyasāvako adinnādānaṃ pahāya adinnādānā paṭivirato hoti…pe… kāmesumicchācāraṃ pahāya kāmesumicchācārā paṭivirato [M 3.0077] hoti…pe… musāvādaṃ pahāya musāvādā paṭivirato hoti…pe… surāmerayamajjapamādaṭṭhānaṃ pahāya surāmerayamajjapamādaṭṭhānā paṭivirato hoti. Surāmerayamajjapamādaṭṭhānā paṭivirato, bhikkhave, ariyasāvako [V 3.0078] aparimāṇānaṃ sattānaṃ abhayaṃ deti averaṃ deti abyābajjhaṃ deti. Aparimāṇānaṃ sattānaṃ abhayaṃ datvā averaṃ datvā abyābajjhaṃ datvā, aparimāṇassa abhayassa averassa abyābajjhassa bhāgī hoti. Idaṃ, bhikkhave, pañcamaṃ dānaṃ mahādānaṃ aggaññaṃ rattaññaṃ vaṃsaññaṃ porāṇaṃ asaṃkiṇṇaṃ asaṃkiṇṇapubbaṃ, na saṃkiyati na saṃkiyissati, appaṭikuṭṭhaṃ samaṇehi brāhmaṇehi viññūhi. Ayaṃ kho, bhikkhave, aṭṭhamo puññābhisando kusalābhisando [P 4.0247] sukhassāhāro sovaggiko sukhavipāko saggasaṃvattaniko, iṭṭhāya kantāya manāpāya hitāya sukhāya saṃvattati. Ime kho, bhikkhave, aṭṭha puññābhisandā kusalābhisandā sukhassāhārā sovaggikā sukhavipākā saggasaṃvattanikā, iṭṭhāya kantāya manāpāya hitāya sukhāya saṃvattantī”ti. Navamaṃ.

“Furthermore, bhikkhus, a noble disciple, having abandoned taking what is not given, abstains from taking what is not given… and so on… having abandoned sexual misconduct, abstains from sexual misconduct… and so on… having abandoned false speech, abstains from false speech… and so on… having abandoned distilled and fermented intoxicants which are the basis for heedlessness, abstains from distilled and fermented intoxicants which are the basis for heedlessness. By abstaining from distilled and fermented intoxicants which are the basis for heedlessness, bhikkhus, a noble disciple gives to immeasurable beings freedom from fear, freedom from hostility, and freedom from affliction. Having given to immeasurable beings freedom from fear, freedom from hostility, and freedom from affliction, he partakes in immeasurable freedom from fear, freedom from hostility, and freedom from affliction. This, bhikkhus, is the fifth great gift—foremost, of long standing, of ancient lineage, ancient, uncorrupted, never before corrupted, not being corrupted, and not to be corrupted, unreproached by wise ascetics and brahmins. This, bhikkhus, is the eighth stream of merit, stream of the wholesome, nourishment for happiness—heavenly, ripening in happiness, conducive to heaven—that leads to what is desirable, agreeable, pleasing, beneficial, and pleasant. These, bhikkhus, are the eight streams of merit, streams of the wholesome, nourishment for happiness—heavenly, ripening in happiness, conducive to heaven—that lead to what is desirable, agreeable, pleasing, beneficial, and pleasant.” Ninth.

10. Duccaritavipākasuttaṃ

10. The Discourse on the Results of Misconduct

40. “Pāṇātipāto, bhikkhave, āsevito bhāvito bahulīkato nirayasaṃvattaniko [T 4.0252] tiracchānayonisaṃvattaniko pettivisayasaṃvattaniko. Yo sabbalahuso sabbalahusoti sabbalahuko (syā. aṭṭha.) pāṇātipātassa vipāko, manussabhūtassa appāyukasaṃvattaniko hoti.

40. “Bhikkhus, the taking of life, when pursued, developed, and frequently practiced, leads to hell, to the animal realm, and to the sphere of the departed. The very lightest result of the taking of life, for one who has become a human, is that it conduces to a short lifespan.”

“Adinnādānaṃ, bhikkhave, āsevitaṃ bhāvitaṃ bahulīkataṃ nirayasaṃvattanikaṃ tiracchānayonisaṃvattanikaṃ pettivisayasaṃvattanikaṃ. Yo sabbalahuso adinnādānassa vipāko, manussabhūtassa bhogabyasanasaṃvattaniko hoti.

“Bhikkhus, taking what is not given, when pursued, developed, and frequently practiced, leads to hell, to the animal realm, and to the sphere of the departed. The very lightest result of taking what is not given, for one who has become a human, is that it conduces to the destruction of wealth.”

“Kāmesumicchācāro, bhikkhave, āsevito bhāvito bahulīkato nirayasaṃvattaniko tiracchānayonisaṃvattaniko pettivisayasaṃvattaniko. Yo sabbalahuso kāmesumicchācārassa vipāko, manussabhūtassa sapattaverasaṃvattaniko hoti.

“Bhikkhus, misconduct in sensual pleasures, when pursued, developed, and frequently practiced, leads to hell, to the animal realm, and to the sphere of the departed. The very lightest result of misconduct in sensual pleasures, for one who has become a human, is that it conduces to having enemies and animosity.”

“Musāvādo[M 3.0078], bhikkhave, āsevito bhāvito bahulīkato nirayasaṃvattaniko tiracchānayonisaṃvattaniko pettivisayasaṃvattaniko. Yo sabbalahuso musāvādassa vipāko, manussabhūtassa abhūtabbhakkhānasaṃvattaniko hoti.

“Bhikkhus, false speech, when pursued, developed, and frequently practiced, leads to hell, to the animal realm, and to the sphere of the departed. The very lightest result of false speech, for one who has become a human, is that it conduces to being baselessly accused.”

“Pisuṇā[V 3.0079], bhikkhave, vācā āsevitā bhāvitā bahulīkatā nirayasaṃvattanikā tiracchānayonisaṃvattanikā pettivisayasaṃvattanikā. Yo sabbalahuso pisuṇāya vācāya vipāko, manussabhūtassa mittehi bhedanasaṃvattaniko hoti.

“Bhikkhus, divisive speech, when pursued, developed, and frequently practiced, leads to hell, to the animal realm, and to the sphere of the departed. The very lightest result of divisive speech, for one who has become a human, is that it conduces to the breaking of friendships.”

“Pharusā[P 4.0248], bhikkhave, vācā āsevitā bhāvitā bahulīkatā nirayasaṃvattanikā tiracchānayonisaṃvattanikā pettivisayasaṃvattanikā. Yo sabbalahuso pharusāya vācāya vipāko, manussabhūtassa amanāpasaddasaṃvattaniko hoti.

“Bhikkhus, harsh speech, when pursued, developed, and frequently practiced, leads to hell, to the animal realm, and to the sphere of the departed. The very lightest result of harsh speech, for one who has become a human, is that it conduces to hearing disagreeable sounds.”

“Samphappalāpo, bhikkhave, āsevito bhāvito bahulīkato nirayasaṃvattaniko tiracchānayonisaṃvattaniko pettivisayasaṃvattaniko. Yo sabbalahuso samphappalāpassa vipāko, manussabhūtassa anādeyyavācāsaṃvattaniko hoti.

“Bhikkhus, idle chatter, when pursued, developed, and frequently practiced, leads to hell, to the animal realm, and to the sphere of the departed. The very lightest result of idle chatter, for one who has become a human, is that it conduces to one's words being unacceptable.”

“Surāmerayapānaṃ, bhikkhave[T 4.0253], āsevitaṃ bhāvitaṃ bahulīkataṃ nirayasaṃvattanikaṃ tiracchānayonisaṃvattanikaṃ pettivisayasaṃvattanikaṃ. Yo sabbalahuso surāmerayapānassa vipāko, manussabhūtassa ummattakasaṃvattaniko hotī”ti. Dasamaṃ.

“Bhikkhus, the drinking of spirits and intoxicants, when pursued, developed, and frequently practiced, leads to hell, to the animal realm, and to the sphere of the departed. The very lightest result of drinking spirits and intoxicants, for one who has become a human, is that it conduces to madness.” Tenth.

Dānavaggo catuttho.

The Chapter on Giving, the Fourth.

Tassuddānaṃ –

The summary for this is:

Dve dānāni vatthuñca, khettaṃ dānūpapattiyo;

Kiriyaṃ dve sappurisā, abhisando vipāko cāti.

Two (suttas on) Giving, and Grounds, Field, Rebirths due to Giving; Deed, two (suttas on the) Good Person, Outflow, and Result.

5. Uposathavaggo

5. The Chapter on the Uposatha

1. Saṅkhittūposathasuttaṃ

1. The Abridged Discourse on the Uposatha

41. Evaṃ [M 3.0079][V 3.0080] me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme. Tatra kho bhagavā bhikkhū āmantesi – “bhikkhavo”ti. “Bhadante”ti te bhikkhū bhagavato paccassosuṃ. Bhagavā etadavoca –

41. Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling in Sāvatthī, in Jeta’s Grove, Anāthapiṇḍika’s Park. There, the Blessed One addressed the bhikkhus: “Bhikkhus!” “Venerable sir,” those bhikkhus responded to the Blessed One. The Blessed One said this:

“Aṭṭhaṅgasamannāgato, bhikkhave, uposatho upavuttho mahapphalo hoti mahānisaṃso mahājutiko mahāvipphāro. Kathaṃ upavuttho ca, bhikkhave, aṭṭhaṅgasamannāgato uposatho mahapphalo hoti mahānisaṃso mahājutiko mahāvipphāro? Idha[P 4.0249], bhikkhave, ariyasāvako iti paṭisañcikkhati – ‘yāvajīvaṃ arahanto pāṇātipātaṃ pahāya pāṇātipātā paṭiviratā nihitadaṇḍā nihitasatthā lajjī dayāpannā, sabbapāṇabhūtahitānukampino viharanti. Ahaṃ pajja imañca rattiṃ imañca divasaṃ pāṇātipātaṃ pahāya pāṇātipātā paṭivirato nihitadaṇḍo nihitasattho lajjī dayāpanno, sabbapāṇabhūtahitānukampī [T 4.0254] viharāmi. Imināpaṅgena imināpi aṅgena (sī. pī.) a. ni. 3.71 arahataṃ anukaromi, uposatho ca me upavuttho bhavissatī’ti. Iminā paṭhamena aṅgena samannāgato hoti.

“Bhikkhus, the Uposatha, when observed and endowed with eight factors, is of great fruit, of great benefit, of great splendor, and of great pervasiveness. And how, bhikkhus, is the Uposatha, when observed and endowed with eight factors, of great fruit, of great benefit, of great splendor, and of great pervasiveness? Here, bhikkhus, a noble disciple reflects thus: ‘For as long as they live, the arahants, having abandoned the taking of life, abstain from the taking of life. With rod and weapon laid aside, they are conscientious, merciful, and dwell compassionate for the welfare of all living beings. Today, for this night and this day, I too, having abandoned the taking of life, abstain from the taking of life. With rod and weapon laid aside, I am conscientious, merciful, and dwell compassionate for the welfare of all living beings. By this factor too, I emulate the arahants, and my Uposatha will have been observed.’ He is endowed with this first factor.

“‘Yāvajīvaṃ arahanto adinnādānaṃ pahāya adinnādānā paṭiviratā dinnādāyī dinnapāṭikaṅkhī, athenena sucibhūtena attanā viharanti. Ahaṃ pajja imañca rattiṃ imañca divasaṃ adinnādānaṃ pahāya adinnādānā paṭivirato dinnādāyī dinnapāṭikaṅkhī, athenena sucibhūtena attanā viharāmi. Imināpaṅgena arahataṃ anukaromi, uposatho ca me upavuttho bhavissatī’ti. Iminā dutiyena aṅgena samannāgato hoti.

‘For as long as they live, the arahants, having abandoned taking what is not given, abstain from taking what is not given. Taking only what is given, expecting only what is given, they dwell with a self that is pure and free from theft. Today, for this night and this day, I too, having abandoned taking what is not given, abstain from taking what is not given. Taking only what is given, expecting only what is given, I dwell with a self that is pure and free from theft. By this factor too, I emulate the arahants, and my Uposatha will have been observed.’ He is endowed with this second factor.

“‘Yāvajīvaṃ arahanto abrahmacariyaṃ pahāya brahmacārino ārācārino viratā methunā gāmadhammā. Ahaṃ pajja imañca rattiṃ imañca divasaṃ abrahmacariyaṃ pahāya brahmacārī ārācārī anācārī (ka.) virato [V 3.0081] methunā gāmadhammā. Imināpaṅgena arahataṃ anukaromi, uposatho ca me upavuttho bhavissatī’ti. Iminā tatiyena aṅgena samannāgato hoti.

‘For as long as they live, the arahants, having abandoned unchastity, are celibate, keeping remote, and abstaining from sexual intercourse, a practice of villagers. Today, for this night and this day, I too, having abandoned unchastity, am celibate, keeping remote, and abstaining from sexual intercourse, a practice of villagers.
