By this factor too, I emulate the arahants, and my Uposatha will have been observed.’ He is endowed with this third factor.

“‘Yāvajīvaṃ [M 3.0080] arahanto musāvādaṃ pahāya musāvādā paṭiviratā saccavādino saccasandhā thetā paccayikā avisaṃvādako lokassa. Ahaṃ pajja imañca rattiṃ imañca divasaṃ musāvādaṃ pahāya musāvādā paṭivirato saccavādī [P 4.0250] saccasandho theto paccayiko avisaṃvādako lokassa. Imināpaṅgena arahataṃ anukaromi, uposatho ca me upavuttho bhavissatī’ti. Iminā catutthena aṅgena samannāgato hoti.

‘For as long as they live, the arahants, having abandoned false speech, abstain from false speech. They are speakers of truth, devoted to truth, steadfast, trustworthy, and do not deceive the world. Today, for this night and this day, I too, having abandoned false speech, abstain from false speech. I am a speaker of truth, devoted to truth, steadfast, trustworthy, and do not deceive the world. By this factor too, I emulate the arahants, and my Uposatha will have been observed.’ He is endowed with this fourth factor.

“‘Yāvajīvaṃ arahanto surāmerayamajjapamādaṭṭhānaṃ pahāya surāmerayamajjapamādaṭṭhānā paṭiviratā. Ahaṃ [T 4.0255] pajja imañca rattiṃ imañca divasaṃ surāmerayamajjapamādaṭṭhānaṃ pahāya surāmerayamajjapamādaṭṭhānā paṭivirato. Imināpaṅgena arahataṃ anukaromi, uposatho ca me upavuttho bhavissatī’ti. Iminā pañcamena aṅgena samannāgato hoti.

‘For as long as they live, the arahants, having abandoned intoxicants—liquors and spirits that are a cause for heedlessness—abstain from them. Today, for this night and this day, I too, having abandoned intoxicants—liquors and spirits that are a cause for heedlessness—abstain from them. By this factor too, I emulate the arahants, and my Uposatha will have been observed.’ He is endowed with this fifth factor.

“‘Yāvajīvaṃ arahanto ekabhattikā rattūparatā viratā vikālabhojanā. Ahaṃ pajja imañca rattiṃ imañca divasaṃ ekabhattiko rattūparato virato vikālabhojanā. Imināpaṅgena arahataṃ anukaromi, uposatho ca me upavuttho bhavissatī’ti. Iminā chaṭṭhena aṅgena samannāgato hoti.

‘For as long as they live, the arahants eat only one meal a day, abstaining from eating at night and refraining from eating at the wrong time. Today, for this night and this day, I too eat only one meal a day, abstaining from eating at night and refraining from eating at the wrong time. By this factor too, I emulate the arahants, and my Uposatha will have been observed.’ He is endowed with this sixth factor.

“‘Yāvajīvaṃ arahanto naccagītavāditavisūkadassanamālāgandhavilepanadhāraṇamaṇḍanavibhūsanaṭṭhānaṃ pahāya naccagītavāditavisūkadassanamālāgandhavilepanadhāraṇamaṇḍanavibhūsanaṭṭhānā paṭiviratā. Ahaṃ pajja imañca rattiṃ imañca divasaṃ naccagītavāditavisūkadassanamālāgandhavilepanadhāraṇamaṇḍanavibhūsanaṭṭhānaṃ pahāya naccagītavāditavisūkadassanamālāgandhavilepanadhāraṇamaṇḍanavibhūsanaṭṭhānā paṭivirato. Imināpaṅgena arahataṃ anukaromi, uposatho ca me upavuttho bhavissatī’ti. Iminā sattamena aṅgena samannāgato hoti.

‘For as long as they live, the arahants, having abandoned dancing, singing, music, watching shows, wearing garlands, using perfumes and cosmetics, and occasions for adornment and beautification, abstain from them. Today, for this night and this day, I too, having abandoned dancing, singing, music, watching shows, wearing garlands, using perfumes and cosmetics, and occasions for adornment and beautification, abstain from them. By this factor too, I emulate the arahants, and my Uposatha will have been observed.’ He is endowed with this seventh factor.

“‘Yāvajīvaṃ arahanto uccāsayanamahāsayanaṃ pahāya uccāsayanamahāsayanā paṭiviratā nīcaseyyaṃ kappenti – mañcake vā tiṇasanthārake vā. Ahaṃ pajja imañca rattiṃ imañca divasaṃ [V 3.0082] uccāsayanamahāsayanaṃ pahāya uccāsayanamahāsayanā paṭivirato nīcaseyyaṃ kappemi – mañcake vā tiṇasanthārake vā. Imināpaṅgena arahataṃ anukaromi[P 4.0251], uposatho ca me upavuttho bhavissatī’ti. Iminā aṭṭhamena aṅgena samannāgato hoti[M 3.0081]. Evaṃ upavuttho kho, bhikkhave, aṭṭhaṅgasamannāgato uposatho mahapphalo hoti mahānisaṃso mahājutiko mahāvipphāro”ti. Paṭhamaṃ.

‘For as long as they live, the arahants, having abandoned high and luxurious beds, abstain from high and luxurious beds. They make use of a low sleeping place—either a small cot or a straw mat. Today, for this night and this day, I too, having abandoned high and luxurious beds, abstain from high and luxurious beds. I make use of a low sleeping place—either a small cot or a straw mat. By this factor too, I emulate the arahants, and my Uposatha will have been observed.’ He is endowed with this eighth factor. Thus, bhikkhus, the Uposatha, when observed and endowed with eight factors, is of great fruit, of great benefit, of great splendor, and of great pervasiveness.” The first.

2. Vitthatūposathasuttaṃ

2. The Detailed Uposatha Discourse

42. “Aṭṭhaṅgasamannāgato[T 4.0256], bhikkhave, uposatho upavuttho mahapphalo hoti mahānisaṃso mahājutiko mahāvipphāro. Kathaṃ upavuttho ca, bhikkhave, aṭṭhaṅgasamannāgato uposatho mahapphalo hoti mahānisaṃso mahājutiko mahāvipphāro? Idha, bhikkhave, ariyasāvako iti paṭisañcikkhati – ‘yāvajīvaṃ arahanto pāṇātipātaṃ pahāya pāṇātipātā paṭiviratā nihitadaṇḍā nihitasatthā lajjī dayāpannā, sabbapāṇabhūtahitānukampino viharanti. Ahaṃ pajja imañca rattiṃ imañca divasaṃ pāṇātipātaṃ pahāya pāṇātipātā paṭivirato nihitadaṇḍo nihitasattho lajjī dayāpanno, sabbapāṇabhūtahitānukampī viharāmi. Imināpaṅgena arahataṃ anukaromi, uposatho ca me upavuttho bhavissatī’ti. Iminā paṭhamena aṅgena samannāgato hoti…pe….

42. “Monks, the Uposatha endowed with eight factors, when observed, is of great fruit, of great benefit, of great radiance, and of great pervasiveness. And how, monks, is the Uposatha endowed with eight factors, when observed, of great fruit, of great benefit, of great radiance, and of great pervasiveness? Here, monks, a noble disciple reflects thus: ‘For as long as life lasts, the arahants, having abandoned the taking of life, abstain from the taking of life; with rod and weapon laid aside, scrupulous, compassionate, they dwell sympathetic to the welfare of all living beings. Today, for this night and day, I too, having abandoned the taking of life, abstain from the taking of life; with rod and weapon laid aside, scrupulous, compassionate, dwell sympathetic to the welfare of all living beings. By this factor I emulate the arahants, and my Uposatha will be observed.’ Thus one is endowed with this first factor… (and so on).

“‘Yāvajīvaṃ arahanto uccāsayanamahāsayanaṃ pahāya uccāsayanamahāsayanā paṭiviratā nīcaseyyaṃ kappenti – mañcake vā tiṇasanthārake vā. Ahaṃ pajja imañca rattiṃ imañca divasaṃ uccāsayanamahāsayanaṃ pahāya uccāsayanamahāsayanā paṭivirato nīcaseyyaṃ kappemi – mañcake vā tiṇasanthārake vā. Imināpaṅgena arahataṃ anukaromi, uposatho ca me upavuttho bhavissatī’ti. Iminā aṭṭhamena aṅgena samannāgato hoti. Evaṃ upavuttho kho, bhikkhave, aṭṭhaṅgasamannāgato uposatho mahapphalo hoti mahānisaṃso mahājutiko mahāvipphāro[P 4.0252].

“‘For as long as life lasts, the arahants, having abandoned high and great beds, abstain from high and great beds; they make use of a low sleeping place—either on a small couch or a grass mat. Today, for this night and day, I too, having abandoned high and great beds, abstain from high and great beds; I make use of a low sleeping place—either on a small couch or a grass mat. By this factor I emulate the arahants, and my Uposatha will be observed.’ Thus one is endowed with this eighth factor. Monks, the Uposatha endowed with eight factors, thus observed, is of great fruit, of great benefit, of great radiance, and of great pervasiveness.”

“Kīvamahapphalo hoti kīvamahānisaṃso kīvamahājutiko kīvamahāvipphāro? Seyyathāpi, bhikkhave, yo imesaṃ soḷasannaṃ mahājanapadānaṃ pahūtarattaratanānaṃ [T 4.0257] pahūtasattaratanānaṃ (sī. syā. kaṃ. pī.) a. ni. 3.71 pāḷiyā ṭīkāyaṃ dassitapāḷiyeva. tadaṭṭhakathāpi passitabbā issariyādhipaccaṃ rajjaṃ kāreyya, seyyathidaṃ – aṅgānaṃ magadhānaṃ kāsīnaṃ kosalānaṃ vajjīnaṃ mallānaṃ cetīnaṃ vaṅgānaṃ kurūnaṃ pañcālānaṃ macchānaṃ majjānaṃ (ka.) sūrasenānaṃ assakānaṃ avantīnaṃ gandhārānaṃ kambojānaṃ, aṭṭhaṅgasamannāgatassa [M 3.0082][V 3.0083] uposathassa etaṃ ekaṃ (ka.) kalaṃ nāgghati soḷasiṃ. Taṃ kissa hetu? Kapaṇaṃ, bhikkhave, mānusakaṃ rajjaṃ dibbaṃ sukhaṃ upanidhāya.

“To what extent is its fruit great, to what extent is its benefit great, to what extent is its radiance great, to what extent is its pervasiveness great? Suppose, monks, someone were to exercise sovereign kingship over these sixteen great countries abounding in many precious treasures—namely, Aṅga, Magadha, Kāsī, Kosala, Vajjī, Malla, Cetī, Vaṅga, Kuru, Pañcāla, Maccha, Sūrasena, Assaka, Avantī, Gandhāra, and Kamboja—this is not worth a sixteenth part of the Uposatha endowed with eight factors. For what reason? Because, monks, human kingship is paltry when compared to divine happiness.”

“Yāni, bhikkhave, mānusakāni paññāsa vassāni, cātumahārājikānaṃ devānaṃ eso eko rattindivo rattidivo (ka.). Tāya rattiyā tiṃsarattiyo māso. Tena māsena dvādasamāsiyo saṃvaccharo. Tena saṃvaccharena dibbāni pañca vassasatāni cātumahārājikānaṃ devānaṃ āyuppamāṇaṃ. Ṭhānaṃ kho panetaṃ, bhikkhave, vijjati yaṃ idhekacco itthī vā puriso vā aṭṭhaṅgasamannāgataṃ uposathaṃ upavasitvā kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā cātumahārājikānaṃ devānaṃ sahabyataṃ upapajjeyya. Idaṃ kho panetaṃ, bhikkhave, sandhāya bhāsitaṃ – ‘kapaṇaṃ mānusakaṃ rajjaṃ dibbaṃ sukhaṃ upanidhāya’”.

“Monks, what are fifty human years is one day and night for the devas of the Cātumahārājika realm. Thirty such days and nights make a month. Twelve such months make a year. Five hundred such divine years is the lifespan of the devas of the Cātumahārājika realm. It is possible, monks, that some woman or man here, having observed the Uposatha endowed with eight factors, upon the breakup of the body, after death, might be reborn into the companionship of the devas of the Cātumahārājika realm. It was with reference to this, monks, that it was said: ‘Human kingship is paltry when compared to divine happiness.’”

“Yāni, bhikkhave, mānusakāni vassasatāni, tāvatiṃsānaṃ devānaṃ eso eko rattindivo. Tāya rattiyā tiṃsarattiyo māso. Tena māsena dvādasamāsiyo saṃvaccharo. Tena saṃvaccharena dibbaṃ vassasahassaṃ tāvatiṃsānaṃ devānaṃ [P 4.0253] āyuppamāṇaṃ. Ṭhānaṃ kho panetaṃ, bhikkhave, vijjati yaṃ idhekacco itthī vā puriso vā aṭṭhaṅgasamannāgataṃ uposathaṃ upavasitvā kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā tāvatiṃsānaṃ devānaṃ sahabyataṃ upapajjeyya. Idaṃ kho panetaṃ, bhikkhave, sandhāya bhāsitaṃ – ‘kapaṇaṃ [T 4.0258] mānusakaṃ rajjaṃ dibbaṃ sukhaṃ upanidhāya’”.

“Monks, what are one hundred human years is one day and night for the devas of the Tāvatiṃsa realm. Thirty such days and nights make a month. Twelve such months make a year. One thousand such divine years is the lifespan of the devas of the Tāvatiṃsa realm. It is possible, monks, that some woman or man here, having observed the Uposatha endowed with eight factors, upon the breakup of the body, after death, might be reborn into the companionship of the devas of the Tāvatiṃsa realm. It was with reference to this, monks, that it was said: ‘Human kingship is paltry when compared to divine happiness.’”

“Yāni, bhikkhave, mānusakāni dve vassasatāni, yāmānaṃ devānaṃ eso eko rattindivo. Tāya rattiyā tiṃsarattiyo māso. Tena māsena dvādasamāsiyo saṃvaccharo. Tena saṃvaccharena dibbāni dve vassasahassāni yāmānaṃ devānaṃ āyuppamāṇaṃ. Ṭhānaṃ kho panetaṃ, bhikkhave, vijjati yaṃ idhekacco itthī vā puriso vā aṭṭhaṅgasamannāgataṃ uposathaṃ upavasitvā kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā yāmānaṃ devānaṃ sahabyataṃ upapajjeyya. Idaṃ kho panetaṃ, bhikkhave, sandhāya bhāsitaṃ – ‘kapaṇaṃ mānusakaṃ rajjaṃ dibbaṃ sukhaṃ upanidhāya’”.

“Monks, what are two hundred human years is one day and night for the devas of the Yāma realm. Thirty such days and nights make a month. Twelve such months make a year. Two thousand such divine years is the lifespan of the devas of the Yāma realm. It is possible, monks, that some woman or man here, having observed the Uposatha endowed with eight factors, upon the breakup of the body, after death, might be reborn into the companionship of the devas of the Yāma realm. It was with reference to this, monks, that it was said: ‘Human kingship is paltry when compared to divine happiness.’”

“Yāni, bhikkhave, mānusakāni cattāri vassasatāni, tusitānaṃ devānaṃ eso eko rattindivo. Tāya rattiyā tiṃsarattiyo māso. Tena māsena dvādasamāsiyo saṃvaccharo. Tena saṃvaccharena dibbāni cattāri vassasahassāni [M 3.0083] tusitānaṃ devānaṃ āyuppamāṇaṃ. Ṭhānaṃ kho panetaṃ, bhikkhave, vijjati yaṃ idhekacco itthī vā puriso vā aṭṭhaṅgasamannāgataṃ uposathaṃ upavasitvā kāyassa [V 3.0084] bhedā paraṃ maraṇā tusitānaṃ devānaṃ sahabyataṃ upapajjeyya. Idaṃ kho panetaṃ, bhikkhave, sandhāya bhāsitaṃ – ‘kapaṇaṃ mānusakaṃ rajjaṃ dibbaṃ sukhaṃ upanidhāya’”.

“Monks, what are four hundred human years is one day and night for the devas of the Tusita realm. Thirty such days and nights make a month. Twelve such months make a year. Four thousand such divine years is the lifespan of the devas of the Tusita realm. It is possible, monks, that some woman or man here, having observed the Uposatha endowed with eight factors, upon the breakup of the body, after death, might be reborn into the companionship of the devas of the Tusita realm. It was with reference to this, monks, that it was said: ‘Human kingship is paltry when compared to divine happiness.’”

“Yāni, bhikkhave, mānusakāni aṭṭha vassasatāni, nimmānaratīnaṃ devānaṃ eso eko rattindivo. Tāya rattiyā tiṃsarattiyo māso. Tena māsena dvādasamāsiyo saṃvaccharo. Tena saṃvaccharena dibbāni aṭṭha vassasahassāni nimmānaratīnaṃ devānaṃ āyuppamāṇaṃ. Ṭhānaṃ kho panetaṃ, bhikkhave, vijjati yaṃ idhekacco itthī vā puriso vā aṭṭhaṅgasamannāgataṃ [P 4.0254] uposathaṃ upavasitvā [T 4.0259] kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā nimmānaratīnaṃ devānaṃ sahabyataṃ upapajjeyya. Idaṃ kho panetaṃ, bhikkhave, sandhāya bhāsitaṃ – ‘kapaṇaṃ mānusakaṃ rajjaṃ dibbaṃ sukhaṃ upanidhāya’”.

“Bhikkhus, what are eight hundred human years is one day and night for the devas of the Nimmānaratī realm. Thirty such nights make a month. Twelve such months make a year. Eight thousand such divine years is the lifespan of the devas of the Nimmānaratī realm. It is possible, bhikkhus, that some woman or man here, having undertaken the Uposatha endowed with eight factors, upon the breaking up of the body, after death, might be reborn into the companionship of the devas of the Nimmānaratī realm. This, bhikkhus, is what was spoken by me in reference to this: ‘Human kingship is paltry when compared to divine happiness.’”

“Yāni, bhikkhave, mānusakāni soḷasa vassasatāni, paranimmitavasavattīnaṃ devānaṃ eso eko rattindivo. Tāya rattiyā tiṃsarattiyo māso. Tena māsena dvādasamāsiyo saṃvaccharo. Tena saṃvaccharena dibbāni soḷasa vassasahassāni paranimmitavasavattīnaṃ devānaṃ āyuppamāṇaṃ. Ṭhānaṃ kho panetaṃ, bhikkhave, vijjati yaṃ idhekacco itthī vā puriso vā aṭṭhaṅgasamannāgataṃ uposathaṃ upavasitvā kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā paranimmitavasavattīnaṃ devānaṃ sahabyataṃ upapajjeyya. Idaṃ kho panetaṃ, bhikkhave, sandhāya bhāsitaṃ – ‘kapaṇaṃ mānusakaṃ rajjaṃ dibbaṃ sukhaṃ upanidhāyā’”ti.

“Bhikkhus, what are sixteen hundred human years is one day and night for the devas of the Paranimmitavasavattī realm. Thirty such nights make a month. Twelve such months make a year. Sixteen thousand such divine years is the lifespan of the devas of the Paranimmitavasavattī realm. It is possible, bhikkhus, that some woman or man here, having undertaken the Uposatha endowed with eight factors, upon the breaking up of the body, after death, might be reborn into the companionship of the devas of the Paranimmitavasavattī realm. This, bhikkhus, is what was spoken by me in reference to this: ‘Human kingship is paltry when compared to divine happiness.’”

“Pāṇaṃ na haññe hāne (sī.), hena (ka.) a. ni. 3.71 na cadinnamādiye,

Musā na bhāse na ca majjapo siyā;

Abrahmacariyā virameyya methunā,

Rattiṃ na bhuñjeyya vikālabhojanaṃ.

“One should not kill a living being, nor take what is not given; one should not speak falsehood, nor should one be a drinker of intoxicants. One should refrain from unchastity, from sexual intercourse; one should not eat at night, nor eat food at the wrong time.

“Mālaṃ na dhāre na ca gandhamācare gandhamādhare (ka.),

Mañce chamāyaṃ va sayetha santhate;

Etañhi aṭṭhaṅgikamāhuposathaṃ,

Buddhena dukkhantagunā pakāsitaṃ.

“One should not wear garlands nor use perfumes; one should lie on a couch or on a mat spread on the ground. This indeed is the eight-factored Uposatha, declared by the Buddha, who has reached the end of suffering.

“Cando [M 3.0084][V 3.0085] ca suriyo ca ubho sudassanā,

Obhāsayaṃ [T 4.0260] anupariyanti yāvatā;

Tamonudā te pana antalikkhagā,

Nabhe pabhāsanti disāvirocanā.

“The moon and the sun, both beautiful to see, circle around, illuminating as far as they go; dispelling darkness, they travel through the sky, shining in the heavens, making the directions resplendent.

“Etasmiṃ [P 4.0255] yaṃ vijjati antare dhanaṃ,

Muttā maṇi veḷuriyañca bhaddakaṃ;

Siṅgīsuvaṇṇaṃ atha vāpi kañcanaṃ,

Yaṃ jātarūpaṃ haṭakanti vuccati.

“Whatever wealth exists within this space—pearls, gems, and excellent beryl; Jambu-river gold or also other gold, that which is called `jātarūpa` or `haṭaka`—

“Aṭṭhaṅgupetassa uposathassa,

Kalampi te nānubhavanti soḷasiṃ;

Candappabhā tāragaṇā ca sabbe.

all that wealth, the moon’s radiance, and all the hosts of stars do not amount to a sixteenth part of the Uposatha endowed with eight factors.

“Tasmā hi nārī ca naro ca sīlavā,

Aṭṭhaṅgupetaṃ upavassuposathaṃ;

Puññāni katvāna sukhudrayāni,

Aninditā saggamupenti ṭhāna”nti. dutiyaṃ;

“Therefore, a virtuous woman and man should observe the Uposatha endowed with eight factors. Having performed meritorious deeds that result in happiness, being blameless, they go to the heavenly state.” The Second Sutta.

3. Visākhāsuttaṃ

3. The Discourse to Visākhā

43. a. ni. 3.71 Ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati pubbārāme migāramātupāsāde. Atha kho visākhā migāramātā yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinnaṃ kho visākhaṃ migāramātaraṃ bhagavā etadavoca – “aṭṭhaṅgasamannāgato kho, visākhe, uposatho upavuttho mahapphalo hoti mahānisaṃso mahājutiko mahāvipphāro. Kathaṃ upavuttho ca, visākhe, aṭṭhaṅgasamannāgato uposatho [T 4.0261] mahapphalo hoti mahānisaṃso mahājutiko mahāvipphāro? Idha, visākhe, ariyasāvako iti paṭisañcikkhati – ‘yāvajīvaṃ arahanto pāṇātipātaṃ pahāya pāṇātipātā paṭiviratā nihitadaṇḍā [V 3.0086] nihitasatthā lajjī dayāpannā, sabbapāṇabhūtahitānukampino viharanti. Ahaṃ pajja imañca rattiṃ imañca divasaṃ pāṇātipātaṃ pahāya pāṇātipātā paṭivirato nihitadaṇḍo nihitasattho lajjī dayāpanno, sabbapāṇabhūtahitānukampī viharāmi. Imināpaṅgena arahataṃ anukaromi[P 4.0256], uposatho [M 3.0085] ca me upavuttho bhavissatī’”ti. Iminā paṭhamena aṅgena samannāgato hoti…pe….

43. On one occasion the Blessed One was dwelling at Sāvatthī, in the Eastern Park, in the palace of Migāra's mother. Then Visākhā, Migāra's mother, approached the Blessed One; having approached, she paid homage to the Blessed One and sat down to one side. To Visākhā, Migāra's mother, who was sitting to one side, the Blessed One said this: "Visākhā, the Uposatha endowed with its eight factors, having been observed, is of great fruit, of great benefit, of great splendor, and of great pervasiveness. And how, Visākhā, is the Uposatha endowed with its eight factors, having been observed, of great fruit, of great benefit, of great splendor, and of great pervasiveness? Here, Visākhā, a noble disciple reflects thus: 'For as long as they live, the Arahants, having abandoned the taking of life, abstain from the taking of life; with rod laid down, with weapon laid down, they dwell conscientious, compassionate, and for the welfare and benefit of all living beings. Today, for this night and this day, I too, having abandoned the taking of life, abstain from the taking of life; with rod laid down, with weapon laid down, I dwell conscientious, compassionate, and for the welfare and benefit of all living beings. By this factor I emulate the Arahants, and my Uposatha will have been observed.' He is endowed with this first factor…"

“‘Yāvajīvaṃ arahanto uccāsayanamahāsayanaṃ pahāya uccāsayanamahāsayanā paṭiviratā nīcaseyyaṃ kappenti – mañcake vā tiṇasanthārake vā. Ahaṃ pajja imañca rattiṃ imañca divasaṃ uccāsayanamahāsayanaṃ pahāya uccāsayanamahāsayanā paṭivirato nīcaseyyaṃ kappemi – mañcake vā tiṇasanthārake vā. Imināpaṅgena arahataṃ anukaromi, uposatho ca me upavuttho bhavissatī’ti. Iminā aṭṭhamena aṅgena samannāgato hoti. Evaṃ upavuttho kho, visākhe, aṭṭhaṅgasamannāgato uposatho mahapphalo hoti mahānisaṃso mahājutiko mahāvipphāro.

"'For as long as they live, the Arahants, having abandoned high and luxurious beds, abstain from high and luxurious beds; they make use of a low sleeping place—either a cot or a grass-mat. Today, for this night and this day, I too, having abandoned high and luxurious beds, abstain from high and luxurious beds; I make use of a low sleeping place—either a cot or a grass-mat. By this factor I emulate the Arahants, and my Uposatha will have been observed.' He is endowed with this eighth factor. Thus, Visākhā, the Uposatha endowed with its eight factors, having been observed, is of great fruit, of great benefit, of great splendor, and of great pervasiveness."

“Kīvamahapphalo hoti, kīvamahānisaṃso, kīvamahājutiko, kīvamahāvipphāro? Seyyathāpi, visākhe, yo imesaṃ soḷasannaṃ mahājanapadānaṃ pahūtarattaratanānaṃ issariyādhipaccaṃ rajjaṃ kāreyya, seyyathidaṃ – aṅgānaṃ magadhānaṃ kāsīnaṃ kosalānaṃ vajjīnaṃ mallānaṃ cetīnaṃ vaṅgānaṃ kurūnaṃ pañcālānaṃ macchānaṃ sūrasenānaṃ [T 4.0262] assakānaṃ avantīnaṃ gandhārānaṃ kambojānaṃ, aṭṭhaṅgasamannāgatassa uposathassa etaṃ kalaṃ nāgghati soḷasiṃ. Taṃ kissa hetu? Kapaṇaṃ, visākhe, mānusakaṃ rajjaṃ dibbaṃ sukhaṃ upanidhāya.

"How great is its fruit, how great its benefit, how great its splendor, how great its pervasiveness? Suppose, Visākhā, one were to exercise sovereignty and dominion over these sixteen great countries abounding in many precious treasures—that is, Aṅga, Magadha, Kāsī, Kosala, Vajjī, Mallā, Cetī, Vaṅga, Kuru, Pañcāla, Maccha, Sūrasena, Assaka, Avanti, Gandhāra, and Kamboja—this would not be worth a sixteenth part of the Uposatha endowed with its eight factors. For what reason? Paltry, Visākhā, is human kingship when compared to divine happiness."

“Yāni, visākhe, mānusakāni paññāsa vassāni, cātumahārājikānaṃ devānaṃ eso eko rattindivo. Tāya rattiyā tiṃsarattiyo māso. Tena māsena dvādasamāsiyo saṃvaccharo. Tena saṃvaccharena dibbāni pañca vassasatāni cātumahārājikānaṃ devānaṃ āyuppamāṇaṃ. Ṭhānaṃ kho panetaṃ, visākhe, vijjati yaṃ idhekacco itthī vā puriso vā aṭṭhaṅgasamannāgataṃ uposathaṃ upavasitvā kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā cātumahārājikānaṃ devānaṃ sahabyataṃ upapajjeyya[P 4.0257]. Idaṃ kho panetaṃ, visākhe, sandhāya bhāsitaṃ – ‘kapaṇaṃ mānusakaṃ rajjaṃ dibbaṃ sukhaṃ upanidhāya’”.

“Visākhā, what are fifty human years is one night-and-day for the devas of the realm of the Four Great Kings. Thirty such nights make a month. Twelve such months make a year. Five hundred such divine years is the lifespan of the devas of the realm of the Four Great Kings. Now, Visākhā, this possibility exists: that some woman or man here, having observed the Uposatha endowed with its eight factors, on the breakup of the body, after death, might be reborn into the companionship of the devas of the realm of the Four Great Kings. It was with reference to this, Visākhā, that it was said: ‘Paltry is human kingship when compared to divine happiness.’”

“Yaṃ[V 3.0087], visākhe, mānusakaṃ vassasataṃ, tāvatiṃsānaṃ devānaṃ eso eko rattindivo. Tāya rattiyā tiṃsarattiyo māso. Tena māsena dvādasamāsiyo saṃvaccharo. Tena saṃvaccharena vassasahassaṃ tāvatiṃsānaṃ devānaṃ āyuppamāṇaṃ. Ṭhānaṃ kho panetaṃ, visākhe, vijjati [M 3.0086] yaṃ idhekacco itthī vā puriso vā aṭṭhaṅgasamannāgataṃ uposathaṃ upavasitvā kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā tāvatiṃsānaṃ devānaṃ sahabyataṃ upapajjeyya. Idaṃ kho panetaṃ, visākhe, sandhāya bhāsitaṃ – ‘kapaṇaṃ mānusakaṃ rajjaṃ dibbaṃ sukhaṃ upanidhāya’”.

“Visākhā, what is one hundred human years is one night-and-day for the devas of the Tāvatiṃsa realm. Thirty such nights make a month. Twelve such months make a year. One thousand divine years is the lifespan of the devas of the Tāvatiṃsa realm. Now, Visākhā, this possibility exists: that some woman or man here, having observed the Uposatha endowed with its eight factors, on the breakup of the body, after death, might be reborn into the companionship of the devas of the Tāvatiṃsa realm. It was with reference to this, Visākhā, that it was said: ‘Paltry is human kingship when compared to divine happiness.’”

“Yāni, visākhe, mānusakāni dve vassasatāni…pe… cattāri vassasatāni…pe… aṭṭha vassasatāni…pe… soḷasa vassasatāni paranimmitavasavattīnaṃ devānaṃ eso eko rattindivo[T 4.0263]. Tāya rattiyā tiṃsarattiyo māso. Tena māsena dvādasamāsiyo saṃvaccharo. Tena saṃvaccharena dibbāni soḷasa vassasahassāni paranimmitavasavattīnaṃ devānaṃ āyuppamāṇaṃ. Ṭhānaṃ kho panetaṃ, visākhe, vijjati yaṃ idhekacco itthī vā puriso vā aṭṭhaṅgasamannāgataṃ uposathaṃ upavasitvā kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā paranimmitavasavattīnaṃ devānaṃ sahabyataṃ upapajjeyya. Idaṃ kho panetaṃ, visākhe, sandhāya bhāsitaṃ – ‘kapaṇaṃ mānusakaṃ rajjaṃ dibbaṃ sukhaṃ upanidhāyā’”ti.

“Visākhā, what are two hundred human years… four hundred human years… eight hundred human years… sixteen hundred human years is one night-and-day for the devas of the Paranimmitavasavattī realm. Thirty such nights make a month. Twelve such months make a year. Sixteen thousand divine years is the lifespan of the devas of the Paranimmitavasavattī realm. Now, Visākhā, this possibility exists: that some woman or man here, having observed the Uposatha endowed with its eight factors, on the breakup of the body, after death, might be reborn into the companionship of the devas of the Paranimmitavasavattī realm. It was with reference to this, Visākhā, that it was said: ‘Paltry is human kingship when compared to divine happiness.’”

“Pāṇaṃ na haññe na cadinnamādiye,

Musā na bhāse na ca majjapo siyā;

Abrahmacariyā virameyya methunā,

Rattiṃ na bhuñjeyya vikālabhojanaṃ.

“One should not kill a living being, nor take what is not given; one should not speak falsely, nor be a drinker of intoxicants; one should refrain from unchaste conduct, from sexual intercourse; at night one should not eat food at the wrong time.

“Mālaṃ na dhāre na ca gandhamācare,

Mañce [P 4.0258] chamāyaṃ va sayetha santhate;

Etañhi aṭṭhaṅgikamāhuposathaṃ,

Buddhena dukkhantagunā pakāsitaṃ.

“One should not wear garlands nor use perfumes; one should sleep on a cot, or on the ground upon a mat. This, indeed, is the eight-factored Uposatha, they say, proclaimed by the Buddha, who has made an end of suffering.

“Cando ca suriyo ca ubho sudassanā,

Obhāsayaṃ anupariyanti yāvatā;

Tamonudā [V 3.0088] te pana antalikkhagā,

Nabhe pabhāsanti disāvirocanā.

“The moon and the sun, both beautiful to behold, radiate light as far as they travel; they dispel darkness, these sky-goers, shining in the sky, illuminating the directions.

“Etasmiṃ yaṃ vijjati antare dhanaṃ,

Muttā [T 4.0264] maṇi veḷuriyañca bhaddakaṃ;

Siṅgīsuvaṇṇaṃ atha vāpi kañcanaṃ,

Yaṃ jātarūpaṃ haṭakanti vuccati.

“Whatever wealth is found herein: pearls, gems, and excellent beryl; river gold, or also natural gold, that which is called ‘jātarūpa’ or ‘haṭaka’.

“Aṭṭhaṅgupetassa [M 3.0087] uposathassa,

Kalampi te nānubhavanti soḷasiṃ;

Candappabhā tāragaṇā ca sabbe.

“All that wealth, together with the radiance of the moon and all the hosts of stars, does not amount to the sixteenth part of the Uposatha endowed with eight factors.

“Tasmā hi nārī ca naro ca sīlavā,

Aṭṭhaṅgupetaṃ upavassuposathaṃ;

Puññāni katvāna sukhudrayāni,

Aninditā saggamupenti ṭhāna”nti. tatiyaṃ;

“Therefore, indeed, a virtuous woman and man should observe the Uposatha endowed with eight factors. Having performed meritorious deeds that yield happiness, being blameless, they attain a heavenly state.” The Third Sutta.

4. Vāseṭṭhasuttaṃ

4. The Vāseṭṭha Sutta

44. Ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā vesāliyaṃ viharati mahāvane kūṭāgārasālāyaṃ. Atha kho vāseṭṭho upāsako yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinnaṃ kho vāseṭṭhaṃ upāsakaṃ bhagavā etadavoca – “aṭṭhaṅgasamannāgato, vāseṭṭha, uposatho upavuttho mahapphalo hoti…pe… aninditā saggamupenti ṭhāna”nti.

44. On one occasion the Blessed One was dwelling at Vesālī in the Great Wood, at the Hall with the Peaked Roof. Then the lay follower Vāseṭṭha approached the Blessed One; having approached, he paid homage to the Blessed One and sat down to one side. To the lay follower Vāseṭṭha, so seated, the Blessed One said this: “Vāseṭṭha, the Uposatha endowed with eight factors, when observed, is of great fruit... being blameless, they attain a heavenly state.”

Evaṃ vutte vāseṭṭho upāsako bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “piyā [P 4.0259] me, bhante, ñātisālohitā aṭṭhaṅgasamannāgataṃ uposathaṃ upavaseyyuṃ, piyānampi me assa ñātisālohitānaṃ dīgharattaṃ hitāya sukhāya. Sabbe cepi, bhante, khattiyā aṭṭhaṅgasamannāgataṃ uposathaṃ upavaseyyuṃ, sabbesampissa [T 4.0265] khattiyānaṃ dīgharattaṃ hitāya sukhāya. Sabbe cepi, bhante, brāhmaṇā…pe… vessā [V 3.0089]… suddā aṭṭhaṅgasamannāgataṃ uposathaṃ upavaseyyuṃ, sabbesampissa suddānaṃ dīgharattaṃ hitāya sukhāyā”ti.

When this was said, the lay follower Vāseṭṭha said to the Blessed One: “Venerable sir, may my dear relatives and kinsmen observe the Uposatha endowed with eight factors; it would be for the long-term welfare and happiness of my dear relatives and kinsmen. Venerable sir, if all the khattiyas were to observe the Uposatha endowed with eight factors, it would be for the long-term welfare and happiness of all the khattiyas. Venerable sir, if all the brahmins… vessas… suddas were to observe the Uposatha endowed with eight factors, it would be for the long-term welfare and happiness of all the suddas.”

“Evametaṃ, vāseṭṭha, evametaṃ, vāseṭṭha! Sabbe cepi, vāseṭṭha, khattiyā aṭṭhaṅgasamannāgataṃ uposathaṃ upavaseyyuṃ, sabbesampissa khattiyānaṃ dīgharattaṃ hitāya sukhāya. Sabbe cepi, vāseṭṭha, brāhmaṇā…pe… vessā… suddā aṭṭhaṅgasamannāgataṃ uposathaṃ upavaseyyuṃ, sabbesampissa suddānaṃ dīgharattaṃ hitāya sukhāya. Sadevako cepi, vāseṭṭha, loko samārako sabrahmako sassamaṇabrāhmaṇī pajā sadevamanussā aṭṭhaṅgasamannāgataṃ uposathaṃ upavaseyyuṃ upavaseyya (?), sadevakassapissa sadevakassa (sabbattha) a. ni. 4.193; ma. ni. 3.64 passitabbaṃ lokassa samārakassa [M 3.0088] sabrahmakassa sassamaṇabrāhmaṇiyā pajāya sadevamanussāya dīgharattaṃ hitāya sukhāya. Ime cepi, vāseṭṭha, mahāsālā aṭṭhaṅgasamannāgataṃ uposathaṃ upavaseyyuṃ, imesampissa mahāsālānaṃ dīgharattaṃ hitāya sukhāya ( ) (sace ceteyyuṃ) katthaci atthi. a. ni. 4.193 passitabbaṃ. Ko pana vādo manussabhūtassā”ti! Catutthaṃ.

“So it is, Vāseṭṭha, so it is! Vāseṭṭha, if all the khattiyas were to observe the Uposatha endowed with eight factors, it would be for the long-term welfare and happiness of all the khattiyas. If all the brahmins… vessas… suddas were to observe the Uposatha endowed with eight factors, it would be for the long-term welfare and happiness of all the suddas. Vāseṭṭha, if this world with its devas, Māra, and Brahmā, and this generation with its ascetics, brahmins, kings, and humans, were to observe the Uposatha endowed with eight factors, it would be for the long-term welfare and happiness of this world with its devas, Māra, and Brahmā, and of this generation with its ascetics, brahmins, kings, and humans. Vāseṭṭha, if these great sāla trees were to observe the Uposatha endowed with eight factors, it would be for their long-term welfare and happiness. What then is to be said of a human being!” The Fourth Sutta.

5. Bojjhasuttaṃ

5. The Bojjha Sutta

45. Ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme. Atha kho bojjhā upāsikā yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinnaṃ kho bojjhaṃ upāsikaṃ bhagavā etadavoca –

45. On one occasion the Blessed One was dwelling at Sāvatthī in Jeta's Grove, Anāthapiṇḍika's Park. Then the laywoman Bojjhā approached the Blessed One, paid homage to him, and sat down to one side. The Blessed One said this to the laywoman Bojjhā as she was sitting to one side:

“Aṭṭhaṅgasamannāgato, bojjhe, uposatho upavuttho mahapphalo hoti mahānisaṃso mahājutiko mahāvipphāro. Kathaṃ [P 4.0260] upavuttho ca, bojjhe, aṭṭhaṅgasamannāgato uposatho mahapphalo hoti mahānisaṃso mahājutiko mahāvipphāro? Idha[T 4.0266], bojjhe, ariyasāvako iti paṭisañcikkhati – ‘yāvajīvaṃ arahanto pāṇātipātaṃ pahāya pāṇātipātā paṭiviratā nihitadaṇḍā nihitasatthā lajjī dayāpannā, sabbapāṇabhūtahitānukampino viharanti. Ahaṃ pajja imañca rattiṃ imañca divasaṃ pāṇātipātaṃ pahāya pāṇātipātā paṭivirato nihitadaṇḍo nihitasattho lajjī dayāpanno, sabbapāṇabhūtahitānukampī viharāmi. Imināpaṅgena arahataṃ anukaromi, uposatho ca me upavuttho bhavissatī’ti. Iminā paṭhamena aṅgena samannāgato hoti…pe….

“Bojjhā, when the Uposatha endowed with eight factors is observed, it is of great fruit, of great benefit, of great splendor, and of great pervasiveness. And how, Bojjhā, is the Uposatha endowed with eight factors observed so that it is of great fruit, of great benefit, of great splendor, and of great pervasiveness? Here, Bojjhā, a noble disciple reflects thus: ‘For as long as they live, the Arahants, having abandoned the destruction of life, refrain from the destruction of life; with rod laid down, with weapon laid down, they are conscientious, compassionate, and dwell compassionate for the welfare of all living beings and creatures. Today, for this night and this day, I too, having abandoned the destruction of life, refrain from the destruction of life; with rod laid down, with weapon laid down, I am conscientious, compassionate, and dwell compassionate for the welfare of all living beings and creatures. By this factor, I emulate the Arahants, and my Uposatha will have been observed.’ One is endowed with this first factor… and so on….

“‘Yāvajīvaṃ [V 3.0090] arahanto uccāsayanamahāsayanaṃ pahāya uccāsayanamahāsayanā paṭiviratā nīcaseyyaṃ kappenti – mañcake vā tiṇasanthārake vā. Ahaṃ pajja imañca rattiṃ imañca divasaṃ uccāsayanamahāsayanaṃ pahāya uccāsayanamahāsayanā paṭivirato nīcaseyyaṃ kappemi – mañcake vā tiṇasanthārake vā. Imināpaṅgena arahataṃ anukaromi, uposatho ca me upavuttho bhavissatī’ti. Iminā aṭṭhamena aṅgena samannāgato hoti. Evaṃ upavuttho kho, bojjhe, aṭṭhaṅgasamannāgato uposatho mahapphalo hoti mahānisaṃso mahājutiko mahāvipphāro.

“‘For as long as they live, the Arahants, having abandoned high and great seats, refrain from high and great seats; they make use of a low sleeping place—either on a small couch or on a grass mat. Today, for this night and this day, I too, having abandoned high and great seats, refrain from high and great seats; I make use of a low sleeping place—either on a small couch or on a grass mat. By this factor, I emulate the Arahants, and my Uposatha will have been observed.’ One is endowed with this eighth factor. Thus observed, Bojjhā, the Uposatha endowed with eight factors is of great fruit, of great benefit, of great splendor, and of great pervasiveness.

“Kīvamahapphalo [M 3.0089] hoti, kīvamahānisaṃso, kīvamahājutiko, kīvamahāvipphāro? Seyyathāpi, bojjhe, yo imesaṃ soḷasannaṃ mahājanapadānaṃ pahūtarattaratanānaṃ issariyādhipaccaṃ rajjaṃ kāreyya, seyyathidaṃ – aṅgānaṃ magadhānaṃ kāsīnaṃ kosalānaṃ vajjīnaṃ mallānaṃ cetīnaṃ vaṅgānaṃ kurūnaṃ pañcālānaṃ macchānaṃ sūrasenānaṃ assakānaṃ [P 4.0261] avantīnaṃ gandhārānaṃ kambojānaṃ, aṭṭhaṅgasamannāgatassa [T 4.0267] uposathassa etaṃ kalaṃ nāgghati soḷasiṃ. Taṃ kissa hetu? Kapaṇaṃ, bojjhe, mānusakaṃ rajjaṃ dibbaṃ sukhaṃ upanidhāya.

“To what extent is it of great fruit, of great benefit, of great splendor, of great pervasiveness? Suppose, Bojjhā, one were to exercise the sovereignty and dominion of kingship over these sixteen great lands, which are abundant in many pleasing treasures—that is, Aṅga, Magadha, Kāsī, Kosala, Vajjī, Mallā, Ceti, Vaṅga, Kuru, Pañcāla, Maccha, Sūrasena, Assaka, Avanti, Gandhāra, and Kamboja—this is not worth a sixteenth part of the Uposatha endowed with eight factors. For what reason? Because, Bojjhā, human kingship is wretched when compared with divine happiness.

“Yāni, bojjhe, mānusakāni paññāsa vassāni, cātumahārājikānaṃ devānaṃ eso eko rattindivo. Tāya rattiyā tiṃsarattiyo māso. Tena māsena dvādasamāsiyo saṃvaccharo. Tena saṃvaccharena dibbāni pañca vassasatāni cātumahārājikānaṃ devānaṃ āyuppamāṇaṃ. Ṭhānaṃ kho panetaṃ, bojjhe, vijjati yaṃ idhekacco itthī vā puriso vā aṭṭhaṅgasamannāgataṃ uposathaṃ upavasitvā kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā cātumahārājikānaṃ devānaṃ sahabyataṃ upapajjeyya. Idaṃ kho panetaṃ, bojjhe, sandhāya bhāsitaṃ – ‘kapaṇaṃ mānusakaṃ rajjaṃ dibbaṃ sukhaṃ upanidhāya’”.

“Bojjhā, what are fifty human years is one night and day for the devas of the Cātumahārājika realm. By that night, thirty nights are a month. By that month, twelve months are a year. By that year, five hundred divine years is the lifespan of the devas of the Cātumahārājika realm. Now, Bojjhā, this is a possibility: that some woman or man here, having observed the Uposatha endowed with eight factors, with the breakup of the body, after death, might be reborn into the company of the devas of the Cātumahārājika realm. It was in reference to this, Bojjhā, that it was said: ‘Human kingship is wretched when compared with divine happiness.’”

“Yaṃ, bojjhe, mānusakaṃ vassasataṃ…pe… tāni, bojjhe, mānusakāni dve vassasatāni…pe… cattāri vassasatāni…pe… aṭṭha vassasatāni…pe… soḷasa vassasatāni paranimmitavasavattīnaṃ devānaṃ eso eko rattindivo. Tāya rattiyā tiṃsarattiyo māso. Tena māsena dvādasamāsiyo saṃvaccharo. Tena saṃvaccharena dibbāni soḷasa vassasahassāni paranimmitavasavattīnaṃ devānaṃ āyuppamāṇaṃ. Ṭhānaṃ kho panetaṃ, bojjhe, vijjati yaṃ idhekacco itthī vā puriso vā aṭṭhaṅgasamannāgataṃ uposathaṃ upavasitvā kāyassa bhedā [V 3.0091] paraṃ maraṇā paranimmitavasavattīnaṃ devānaṃ sahabyataṃ upapajjeyya. Idaṃ kho panetaṃ, bojjhe, sandhāya bhāsitaṃ – ‘kapaṇaṃ mānusakaṃ rajjaṃ dibbaṃ sukhaṃ upanidhāyā’”ti.

“Bojjhā, what are one hundred human years… and so on… what are two hundred human years… and so on… four hundred human years… and so on… eight hundred human years… and so on… what are sixteen hundred human years is one night and day for the devas of the Paranimmitavasavattī realm. By that night, thirty nights are a month. By that month, twelve months are a year. By that year, sixteen thousand divine years is the lifespan of the devas of the Paranimmitavasavattī realm. Now, Bojjhā, this is a possibility: that some woman or man here, having observed the Uposatha endowed with eight factors, with the breakup of the body, after death, might be reborn into the company of the devas of the Paranimmitavasavattī realm. It was in reference to this, Bojjhā, that it was said: ‘Human kingship is wretched when compared with divine happiness.’”

“Pāṇaṃ [T 4.0268] na haññe na cadinnamādiye,

Musā na bhāse na ca majjapo siyā;

Abrahmacariyā [P 4.0262] virameyya methunā,

Rattiṃ na bhuñjeyya vikālabhojanaṃ.

“One should not kill a living being, nor take what is not given; one should not speak falsely, nor be a drinker of intoxicants. One should refrain from unchastity, from sexual intercourse; one should not eat at night, nor at the wrong time.

“Mālaṃ [M 3.0090] na dhāre na ca gandhamācare,

Mañce chamāyaṃ va sayetha santhate;

Etañhi aṭṭhaṅgikamāhuposathaṃ,

Buddhena dukkhantagunā pakāsitaṃ.

“One should not wear garlands nor use perfumes; one should sleep on a couch or on a spread on the ground. This, they say, is the eight-factored Uposatha, declared by the Buddha who has gone to the end of suffering.

“Cando ca suriyo ca ubho sudassanā,

Obhāsayaṃ anupariyanti yāvatā;

Tamonudā te pana antalikkhagā,

Nabhe pabhāsanti disāvirocanā.

“The moon and the sun, both fair to see, travel around illuminating as far as their range extends. These dispellers of darkness, traversing the sky, shine in the heavens, making the directions radiant.

“Etasmiṃ yaṃ vijjati antare dhanaṃ,

Muttā maṇi veḷuriyañca bhaddakaṃ;

Siṅgīsuvaṇṇaṃ atha vāpi kañcanaṃ,

Yaṃ jātarūpaṃ haṭakanti vuccati.

“Whatever wealth is found within this space—pearls, gems, and fine beryl; river-gold or also bullion, which is called native gold and refined gold—

“Aṭṭhaṅgupetassa uposathassa,

Kalampi te nānubhavanti soḷasiṃ;

Candappabhā tāragaṇā ca sabbe.

“All that wealth, and the light of the moon, and all the hosts of stars, do not equal even a sixteenth part of the Uposatha endowed with eight factors.

“Tasmā [V 3.0092] hi nārī ca naro ca sīlavā,

Aṭṭhaṅgupetaṃ upavassuposathaṃ;

Puññāni katvāna sukhudrayāni,

Aninditā saggamupenti ṭhāna”nti. pañcamaṃ;

“Therefore, a virtuous woman and man should observe the Uposatha endowed with eight factors. Having done meritorious deeds that result in happiness, irreproachable, they go to a heavenly state.” Fifth.

6. Anuruddhasuttaṃ

6. The Discourse with Anuruddha

46. Ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā kosambiyaṃ viharati ghositārāme. Tena kho pana samayena āyasmā anuruddho divāvihāraṃ gato hoti paṭisallīno. Atha kho sambahulā manāpakāyikā devatā yenāyasmā anuruddho tenupasaṅkamiṃsu; upasaṅkamitvā āyasmantaṃ anuruddhaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ aṭṭhaṃsu. Ekamantaṃ ṭhitā kho tā devatā āyasmantaṃ anuruddhaṃ etadavocuṃ – “mayaṃ, bhante [P 4.0263] anuruddha, manāpakāyikā nāma devatā tīsu ṭhānesu issariyaṃ kārema vasaṃ vattema. Mayaṃ, bhante anuruddha, yādisakaṃ vaṇṇaṃ ākaṅkhāma tādisakaṃ vaṇṇaṃ ṭhānaso paṭilabhāma; yādisakaṃ saraṃ ākaṅkhāma [M 3.0091] tādisakaṃ saraṃ ṭhānaso paṭilabhāma; yādisakaṃ sukhaṃ ākaṅkhāma tādisakaṃ sukhaṃ ṭhānaso paṭilabhāma. Mayaṃ, bhante anuruddha, manāpakāyikā nāma devatā imesu tīsu ṭhānesu issariyaṃ kārema vasaṃ vattemā”ti.

46. On one occasion the Blessed One was dwelling at Kosambi in Ghosita's Park. At that time, the Venerable Anuruddha had gone for his day's abiding in seclusion. Then a number of devatās of the Manāpakāyika group approached the Venerable Anuruddha; having approached, they paid homage to him and stood to one side. Standing to one side, those devatās said to the Venerable Anuruddha: "Venerable Anuruddha, we are devatās named Manāpakāyika, who exercise sovereignty and wield power in three matters. Venerable Anuruddha, whatever kind of appearance we desire, we obtain that kind of appearance in an instant; whatever kind of sound we desire, we obtain that kind of sound in an instant; whatever kind of pleasure we desire, we obtain that kind of pleasure in an instant. Venerable Anuruddha, we are devatās named Manāpakāyika who exercise sovereignty and wield power in these three matters."

Atha kho āyasmato anuruddhassa etadahosi – “aho vatimā devatā sabbāva nīlā assu nīlavaṇṇā nīlavatthā nīlālaṅkārā”ti. Atha kho tā devatā āyasmato anuruddhassa cittamaññāya sabbāva nīlā ahesuṃ nīlavaṇṇā nīlavatthā nīlālaṅkārā.

Then it occurred to the Venerable Anuruddha: "Oh, that all these devatās would be blue—of blue appearance, with blue garments, and blue adornments!" Then those devatās, knowing the Venerable Anuruddha's thought, all became blue—of blue appearance, with blue garments, and blue adornments.

Atha kho āyasmato anuruddhassa etadahosi – “aho vatimā devatā sabbāva pītā assu…pe… sabbāva lohitakā assu… sabbāva odātā [T 4.0270] assu odātavaṇṇā odātavatthā odātālaṅkārā”ti. Atha kho tā devatā āyasmato anuruddhassa cittamaññāya sabbāva odātā ahesuṃ odātavaṇṇā odātavatthā odātālaṅkārā.

Then it occurred to the Venerable Anuruddha: "Oh, that all these devatās might be yellow... that all these devatās might be red... that all these devatās might be white—of white appearance, with white garments, and white adornments!" Then those devatās, knowing the Venerable Anuruddha's thought, all became white—of white appearance, with white garments, and white adornments.

Atha kho tā devatā ekā ca ko (sī.), ekāva (syā. pī.) gāyi ekā ca ekā pana (sī.), ekāva (syā. pī.) nacci ekā ca ekā (sī.), ekāva (syā. pī.) accharaṃ vādesi. Seyyathāpi nāma pañcaṅgikassa tūriyassa turiyassa (sī. syā. pī.) suvinītassa suppaṭipatāḷitassa kusalehi susamannāhatassa [V 3.0093] saddo hoti vaggu ca rajanīyo ca kamanīyo ca pemanīyo ca madanīyo ca; evamevaṃ [P 4.0264] tāsaṃ devatānaṃ alaṅkārānaṃ saddo hoti vaggu ca rajanīyo ca kamanīyo ca pemanīyo ca madanīyo ca. Atha kho āyasmā anuruddho indriyāni okkhipi.

Then one of those devatās sang, one danced, and one clapped her hands. Just as the sound of a five-part musical ensemble, well-arranged, well-struck, and well-played by the skillful, is lovely, enchanting, desirable, endearing, and intoxicating; even so was the sound of those devatās' ornaments lovely, enchanting, desirable, endearing, and intoxicating. Then the Venerable Anuruddha lowered his faculties.

Atha kho tā devatā “na khvayyo anuruddho sādiyatī”ti sādayatīti (saddanītidhātumālā) tatthevantaradhāyiṃsu. Atha kho āyasmā anuruddho sāyanhasamayaṃ paṭisallānā vuṭṭhito yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinno kho āyasmā anuruddho bhagavantaṃ etadavoca –

Then those devatās, thinking, "The noble Anuruddha is not pleased," vanished right there. Then, in the evening, the Venerable Anuruddha arose from seclusion and approached the Blessed One. Having approached, he paid homage to the Blessed One and sat down to one side. Seated to one side, the Venerable Anuruddha said this to the Blessed One:

“Idhāhaṃ, bhante, divāvihāraṃ gato homi paṭisallīno. Atha kho, bhante, sambahulā manāpakāyikā devatā yenāhaṃ tenupasaṅkamiṃsu; upasaṅkamitvā maṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ aṭṭhaṃsu. Ekamantaṃ ṭhitā kho, bhante, tā [M 3.0092] devatā maṃ etadavocuṃ – ‘mayaṃ, bhante anuruddha, manāpakāyikā nāma devatā tīsu ṭhānesu issariyaṃ kārema vasaṃ vattema. Mayaṃ, bhante anuruddha, yādisakaṃ vaṇṇaṃ ākaṅkhāma tādisakaṃ vaṇṇaṃ ṭhānaso paṭilabhāma; yādisakaṃ saraṃ ākaṅkhāma tādisakaṃ saraṃ ṭhānaso [T 4.0271] paṭilabhāma; yādisakaṃ sukhaṃ ākaṅkhāma tādisakaṃ sukhaṃ ṭhānaso paṭilabhāma. Mayaṃ, bhante anuruddha, manāpakāyikā nāma devatā imesu tīsu ṭhānesu issariyaṃ kārema vasaṃ vattemā’ti. Tassa mayhaṃ, bhante, etadahosi – ‘aho vatimā devatā sabbāva nīlā assu nīlavaṇṇā nīlavatthā nīlālaṅkārā’ti. Atha kho, bhante, tā devatā mama cittamaññāya sabbāva nīlā ahesuṃ nīlavaṇṇā nīlavatthā nīlālaṅkārā.

“Here, venerable sir, I had gone for the day's abiding in seclusion. Then, venerable sir, a number of devatās of the Manāpakāyika group approached me. Having approached, they paid homage to me and stood to one side. Standing to one side, venerable sir, those devatās said this to me: ‘Venerable Anuruddha, we are devatās named Manāpakāyika, who exercise sovereignty and wield power in three matters. Venerable Anuruddha, whatever kind of appearance we desire, we obtain that kind of appearance in an instant; whatever kind of sound we desire, we obtain that kind of sound in an instant; whatever kind of pleasure we desire, we obtain that kind of pleasure in an instant. Venerable Anuruddha, we are devatās named Manāpakāyika who exercise sovereignty and wield power in these three matters.’ Then, venerable sir, it occurred to me: ‘Oh, that all these devatās would be blue—of blue appearance, with blue garments, and blue adornments!’ Then, venerable sir, those devatās, knowing my thought, all became blue—of blue appearance, with blue garments, and blue adornments.

“Tassa mayhaṃ, bhante, etadahosi – ‘aho vatimā devatā sabbāva pītā assu…pe… sabbāva lohitakā assu…pe… sabbāva odātā assu odātavaṇṇā odātavatthā odātālaṅkārā’ti[P 4.0265]. Atha kho, bhante, tā devatā mama cittamaññāya sabbāva odātā ahesuṃ odātavaṇṇā odātavatthā odātālaṅkārā.

“Then, venerable sir, it occurred to me: ‘Oh, that all these devatās might be yellow... that all these devatās might be red... that all these devatās might be white—of white appearance, with white garments, and white adornments!’ Then, venerable sir, those devatās, knowing my thought, all became white—of white appearance, with white garments, and white adornments.

“Atha kho, bhante, tā devatā ekā ca gāyi ekā ca nacci ekā ca accharaṃ vādesi. Seyyathāpi nāma pañcaṅgikassa tūriyassa suvinītassa suppaṭipatāḷitassa kusalehi susamannāhatassa saddo hoti vaggu ca rajanīyo ca kamanīyo ca pemanīyo ca madanīyo ca; evamevaṃ [V 3.0094] tāsaṃ devatānaṃ alaṅkārānaṃ saddo hoti vaggu ca rajanīyo ca kamanīyo ca pemanīyo ca madanīyo ca. Atha khvāhaṃ, bhante, indriyāni okkhipiṃ.

“Then, venerable sir, one of those devatās sang, one danced, and one clapped her hands. Just as the sound of a five-part musical ensemble, well-arranged, well-struck, and well-played by the skillful, is lovely, enchanting, desirable, endearing, and intoxicating; even so was the sound of those devatās' ornaments lovely, enchanting, desirable, endearing, and intoxicating. Then, venerable sir, I lowered my faculties.

“Atha kho, bhante, tā devatā ‘na khvayyo anuruddho sādiyatī’ti tatthevantaradhāyiṃsu. Katihi nu kho, bhante, dhammehi samannāgato mātugāmo kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā manāpakāyikānaṃ devānaṃ sahabyataṃ upapajjatī”ti?

“Then, venerable sir, those devatās, thinking, ‘The noble Anuruddha is not pleased,’ vanished right there. Now, venerable sir, endowed with how many qualities does a woman, with the breakup of the body, after death, reappear in the company of the Manāpakāyika devas?”

“Aṭṭhahi [T 4.0272] kho, anuruddha, dhammehi samannāgato mātugāmo kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā manāpakāyikānaṃ devānaṃ sahabyataṃ upapajjati. Katamehi aṭṭhahi? Idha, anuruddha, mātugāmo yassa mātāpitaro bhattuno denti atthakāmā hitesino anukampakā anukampaṃ upādāya tassa [M 3.0093] hoti pubbuṭṭhāyinī pacchānipātinī kiṅkārapaṭissāvinī manāpacārinī piyavādinī.

Anuruddha, indeed, a woman endowed with eight qualities, on the dissolution of the body, after death, is reborn into the companionship of the devas with delightful bodies. With which eight? Here, Anuruddha, a woman—to which husband her parents give her, desiring her benefit, seeking her welfare, being compassionate, out of compassion—for that husband, she is one who rises before him, lies down after him, obediently awaits his instructions, conducts herself pleasingly, and speaks endearing words.

“Ye te bhattu garuno guruno (ka.) honti – mātāti vā pitāti vā samaṇabrāhmaṇāti vā – te sakkaroti, garuṃ karoti garukaroti (sī. syā. pī.), māneti, pūjeti, abbhāgate ca āsanodakena paṭipūjeti.

Those who are revered by her husband—whether mother, or father, or ascetics and brahmins—she respects, reveres, esteems, and honors them. And when they arrive as guests, she honors them with a seat and water.

“Ye te bhattu abbhantarā kammantā – uṇṇāti vā kappāsāti vā – tattha dakkhā hoti analasā tatrupāyāya tatrūpāyāya (sī.), a. ni. 4.35; 11.14 vīmaṃsāya samannāgatā alaṃ kātuṃ alaṃ saṃvidhātuṃ.

Whatever domestic duties of her husband there are—whether with wool or with cotton—in that, she is skillful and not lazy, endowed with the wisdom to investigate the means for that work; she is capable of doing it herself and of directing others.

“Yo so bhattu abbhantaro antojano [P 4.0266] – dāsāti vā pessāti vā kammakarāti vā – tesaṃ katañca katato jānāti akatañca akatato jānāti, gilānakānañca balābalaṃ jānāti khādanīyaṃ bhojanīyañcassa paccaṃsena paccayena (syā.), paccattaṃsena (ka.) a. ni. 5.33 saṃvibhajati.

Whatever domestic folk of her husband there are—whether slaves, or messengers, or workers—she knows what has been done by them as done, and what has not been done as not done. Of the sick, she knows their strength and weakness. She distributes hard and soft foods to them according to each one's share.

“Yaṃ bhattu āharati dhanaṃ vā dhaññaṃ vā jātarūpaṃ vā taṃ ārakkhena guttiyā sampādeti, tattha ca hoti adhuttī athenī asoṇḍī avināsikā.

Whatever wealth, or grain, or gold the husband brings, she manages it with protection and security. And in that matter, she is not one given to sensual excess, not a thief, not a drunkard, and not one who causes ruin.

“Upāsikā kho pana hoti buddhaṃ saraṇaṃ gatā dhammaṃ saraṇaṃ gatā saṅghaṃ saraṇaṃ gatā.

Furthermore, she is a female lay follower who has gone for refuge to the Buddha, gone for refuge to the Dhamma, and gone for refuge to the Sangha.

“Sīlavatī [V 3.0095] kho pana hoti – pāṇātipātā paṭiviratā, adinnādānā paṭiviratā, kāmesumicchācārā paṭiviratā, musāvādā paṭiviratā, surāmerayamajjapamādaṭṭhānā paṭiviratā.

Furthermore, she is virtuous: she abstains from the destruction of life, abstains from taking what is not given, abstains from misconduct in sensual pleasures, abstains from false speech, and abstains from liquor, wine, and intoxicants which are a cause for heedlessness.

“Cāgavatī kho pana hoti. Vigatamalamaccherena cetasā agāraṃ ajjhāvasati muttacāgā muttacāgī (syā.) payatapāṇinī [T 4.0273] payatapāṇi (sī.), payatapāṇī (syā. pī. ka.) vossaggaratā yācayogā dānasaṃvibhāgaratā.

Furthermore, she is generous. She dwells managing the household with a mind devoid of the stain of stinginess, being freely generous, open-handed, delighting in relinquishment, fit to be asked from, and delighting in giving and sharing.

“Imehi kho, anuruddha, aṭṭhahi dhammehi samannāgato mātugāmo kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā manāpakāyikānaṃ devānaṃ sahabyataṃ upapajjatī”ti.

Anuruddha, indeed, a woman endowed with these eight qualities, on the dissolution of the body, after death, is reborn into the companionship of the devas with delightful bodies.

“Yo [M 3.0094] naṃ bharati sabbadā, niccaṃ ātāpi ussuko;

Taṃ sabbakāmadaṃ taṃ sabbakāmaharaṃ (sī. syā. pī.) sabbakāmaharaṃ (a. ni. 5.33 posaṃ, bhattāraṃ nātimaññati.

“She does not despise that husband—the man who provides all her desires, who always and constantly supports her, being ardent and diligent.

“Na cāpi sotthi bhattāraṃ, issāvādena rosaye;

Bhattu ca garuno sabbe, paṭipūjeti paṇḍitā.

“A virtuous woman should not provoke her husband with jealous words; the wise one honors all those revered by her husband.

“Uṭṭhāhikā uṭṭhāyikā (ka.) analasā, saṅgahitaparijjanā;

Bhattu manāpaṃ carati, sambhataṃ anurakkhati.

“Energetic and not lazy, with a well-cared-for retinue, she acts in a way that is pleasing to her husband and protects what he has acquired.

“Yā evaṃ vattati nārī, bhattu chandavasānugā;

Manāpā nāma te manāpakāyikā (sī. ka.) devā, yattha sā upapajjatī”ti. chaṭṭhaṃ;

“The woman who conducts herself thus, compliant with her husband's will, is reborn in the realm of the devas called 'the Delightful'.” Sixth.

7. Dutiyavisākhāsuttaṃ

7. The Second Discourse to Visākhā

47. Ekaṃ [P 4.0267] samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati pubbārāme migāramātupāsāde. Atha kho visākhā migāramātā…pe… ekamantaṃ nisinnaṃ kho visākhaṃ migāramātaraṃ bhagavā etadavoca –

47. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling in Sāvatthī, in the Eastern Park, the mansion of Migāra's mother. Then Visākhā, Migāra's mother... and so on... The Blessed One said this to Visākhā, Migāra's mother, as she was sitting to one side:

“Aṭṭhahi kho, visākhe, dhammehi samannāgato mātugāmo kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā manāpakāyikānaṃ [V 3.0096] devānaṃ sahabyataṃ upapajjati. Katamehi aṭṭhahi? Idha, visākhe, mātugāmo yassa mātāpitaro bhattuno denti atthakāmā hitesino anukampakā anukampaṃ upādāya tassa hoti pubbuṭṭhāyinī pacchānipātinī kiṅkārapaṭissāvinī manāpacārinī piyavādinī…pe….

“Visākhā, a woman endowed with eight qualities, with the breakup of the body, after death, is reborn in the company of the devas with delightful bodies. With what eight? Here, Visākhā, a woman whose parents give her to a husband—desiring her benefit, seeking her welfare, and compassionate, out of compassion—for that husband, she is one who rises before him, lies down after him, is attentive to his wishes, is agreeable in conduct, and speaks endearingly... and so on...

“Cāgavatī kho [T 4.0274] pana hoti. Vigatamalamaccherena cetasā agāraṃ ajjhāvasati muttacāgā payatapāṇinī vossaggaratā yācayogā dānasaṃvibhāgaratā. Imehi kho, visākhe, aṭṭhahi dhammehi samannāgato mātugāmo kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā manāpakāyikānaṃ devānaṃ sahabyataṃ upapajjatī”ti.

“Furthermore, she is generous. She dwells at home with a mind devoid of the stain of stinginess, freely generous, with open hands, delighting in relinquishment, approachable for requests, delighting in giving and sharing. Visākhā, a woman endowed with these eight qualities, with the breakup of the body, after death, is reborn in the company of the devas with delightful bodies.”

“Yo naṃ bharati sabbadā, niccaṃ ātāpi ussuko;

Taṃ sabbakāmadaṃ posaṃ, bhattāraṃ nātimaññati.

“She does not despise that husband—the man who provides all her desires, who always and constantly supports her, being ardent and diligent.

“Na [M 3.0095] cāpi sotthi bhattāraṃ, issāvādena rosaye;

Bhattu ca garuno sabbe, paṭipūjeti paṇḍitā.

“A virtuous woman should not provoke her husband with jealous words; the wise one honors all those revered by her husband.

“Uṭṭhāhikā analasā, saṅgahitaparijjanā;

Bhattu manāpaṃ carati, sambhataṃ anurakkhati.

“Energetic and not lazy, with a well-cared-for retinue, she acts in a way that is pleasing to her husband and protects what he has acquired.

“Yā evaṃ vattati nārī, bhattu chandavasānugā;

Manāpā nāma te manāpakāyikā (sī. ka.) devā, yattha sā upapajjatī”ti. sattamaṃ;

“The woman who conducts herself thus, compliant with her husband's will, is reborn in the realm of the devas called 'the Delightful'.” Seventh.

8. Nakulamātāsuttaṃ

8. The Discourse to Nakula’s Mother

48. Ekaṃ [P 4.0268] samayaṃ bhagavā bhaggesu viharati suṃsumāragire suṃsumāragire (sī. syā. pī.) bhesakaḷāvane migadāye. Atha kho nakulamātā gahapatānī yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā…pe…. Ekamantaṃ nisinnaṃ kho nakulamātaraṃ gahapatāniṃ bhagavā etadavoca –

48. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling among the Bhaggas at Suṃsumāragira, in the Bhesakaḷā Forest, the deer sanctuary. Then the householder Nakula’s mother went to the Blessed One... and so on... The Blessed One said this to the householder Nakula’s mother as she was sitting to one side:

“Aṭṭhahi kho, nakulamāte, dhammehi samannāgato mātugāmo kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā manāpakāyikānaṃ [V 3.0097] devānaṃ sahabyataṃ upapajjati. Katamehi aṭṭhahi? Idha, nakulamāte, mātugāmo yassa mātāpitaro bhattuno denti atthakāmā hitesino anukampakā anukampaṃ upādāya tassa hoti pubbuṭṭhāyinī pacchānipātinī kiṅkārapaṭissāvinī [T 4.0275] manāpacārinī piyavādinī.

“Nakula’s mother, a woman endowed with eight qualities, with the breakup of the body, after death, is reborn in the company of the devas with delightful bodies. With what eight? Here, Nakula’s mother, a woman whose parents give her to a husband—desiring her benefit, seeking her welfare, and compassionate, out of compassion—for that husband, she is one who rises before him, lies down after him, is attentive to his wishes, is agreeable in conduct, and speaks endearingly.”

“Ye te bhattu garuno honti – mātāti vā pitāti vā samaṇabrāhmaṇāti vā – te sakkaroti garuṃ karoti māneti pūjeti, abbhāgate ca āsanodakena paṭipūjeti.

“Those who are revered by her husband—whether his mother, father, ascetics, or brahmins—she respects, reveres, esteems, and honors them. And when they arrive as guests, she honors them with a seat and water.”

“Ye te bhattu abbhantarā kammantā – uṇṇāti vā kappāsāti vā – tattha dakkhā hoti analasā tatrupāyāya vīmaṃsāya samannāgatā alaṃ kātuṃ alaṃ saṃvidhātuṃ.

“Whatever domestic duties of her husband there are—whether with wool or with cotton—in that, she is skillful and not lazy, endowed with the wisdom to investigate the means for that work; she is capable of doing it herself and of directing others.”

“Yo so bhattu abbhantaro antojano – dāsāti vā pessāti vā kammakarāti vā – tesaṃ katañca katato jānāti akatañca akatato jānāti, gilānakānañca balābalaṃ jānāti khādanīyaṃ bhojanīyañcassa paccaṃsena saṃvibhajati.

Whatever domestic folk her husband has—whether slaves, messengers, or workers—she knows of their work what is done as done and what is undone as undone. She knows the strength and weakness of the sick among them, and she distributes hard and soft food to them according to each one's portion.

“Yaṃ [M 3.0096] bhattā āharati dhanaṃ vā dhaññaṃ vā rajataṃ vā jātarūpaṃ vā taṃ ārakkhena guttiyā sampādeti, tattha ca hoti adhuttī athenī asoṇḍī avināsikā.

Whatever wealth her husband brings—whether riches, grain, silver, or gold—she secures and protects it. And in that regard, she is not a profligate, not a thief, not a drunkard, and not wasteful.

“Upāsikā kho pana hoti buddhaṃ saraṇaṃ gatā dhammaṃ saraṇaṃ gatā saṅghaṃ saraṇaṃ gatā.

Moreover, she is a female lay disciple who has gone for refuge to the Buddha, gone for refuge to the Dhamma, and gone for refuge to the Saṅgha.

“Sīlavatī kho pana hoti – pāṇātipātā paṭiviratā…pe… surāmerayamajjapamādaṭṭhānā paṭiviratā…pe….

Moreover, she is virtuous: she abstains from the destruction of life... and so on... she abstains from spirits, liquor, and intoxicants, which are a basis for heedlessness.

“Cāgavatī kho pana hoti vigatamalamaccherena cetasā [P 4.0269] agāraṃ ajjhāvasati muttacāgā payatapāṇinī vossaggaratā yācayogā dānasaṃvibhāgaratā.

Moreover, she is generous. She dwells at home with a mind devoid of the stain of stinginess, freely generous, with open hands, delighting in relinquishment, approachable for requests, delighting in giving and sharing.

“Imehi kho, nakulamāte, aṭṭhahi dhammehi samannāgato mātugāmo kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā manāpakāyikānaṃ devānaṃ sahabyataṃ upapajjatī”ti.

Nakula’s mother, a woman endowed with these eight qualities, with the breakup of the body, after death, is reborn in the company of the devas with delightful bodies.

“Yo [V 3.0098] naṃ bharati sabbadā, niccaṃ ātāpi ussuko;

Taṃ sabbakāmadaṃ posaṃ, bhattāraṃ nātimaññati.

She does not despise her husband who always supports her, ever ardent and zealous, that man who provides all she desires.

“Na cāpi sotthi bhattāraṃ, issāvādena rosaye;

Bhattu ca garuno sabbe, paṭipūjeti paṇḍitā.

A good woman should not provoke her husband with jealous words; the wise woman honors all those respected by her husband.

“Uṭṭhāhikā analasā, saṅgahitaparijjanā;

Bhattu manāpaṃ carati, sambhataṃ anurakkhati.

Diligent, not lazy, with a well-cared-for retinue, she acts in ways pleasing to her husband and guards the wealth he has acquired.

“Yā evaṃ vattati nārī, bhattu chandavasānugā;

Manāpā nāma te manāpakāyikā (sī.) devā, yattha sā upapajjatī”ti. aṭṭhamaṃ;

The woman who conducts herself thus, compliant with her husband's will, is reborn in that realm where there are the devas called 'the Delightful.' Eighth.

9. Paṭhamaidhalokikasuttaṃ

9. The First Sutta on This World

49. Ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati pubbārāme migāramātupāsāde. Atha kho visākhā migāramātā yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami…pe…. Ekamantaṃ nisinnaṃ kho visākhaṃ migāramātaraṃ bhagavā etadavoca –

49. On one occasion the Blessed One was dwelling at Sāvatthī in the Eastern Park, in the palace of Migāra’s Mother. Then Visākhā, Migāra’s mother, approached the Blessed One… Having sat down to one side, the Blessed One said this to Visākhā, Migāra’s mother:

“Catūhi [M 3.0097] kho, visākhe, dhammehi samannāgato mātugāmo idhalokavijayāya paṭipanno hoti, ayaṃsa loko āraddho hoti. Katamehi catūhi? Idha, visākhe, mātugāmo susaṃvihitakammanto hoti, saṅgahitaparijano, bhattu manāpaṃ carati, sambhataṃ anurakkhati.

Visākhā, a woman endowed with four qualities is practicing for victory in this world; this world is successfully undertaken by her. With which four? Here, Visākhā, a woman is one who manages her work well, has well-cared-for attendants, acts in a way that is pleasing to her husband, and protects the wealth he has acquired.

“Kathañca[T 4.0276], visākhe, mātugāmo susaṃvihitakammanto hoti? Idha, visākhe, mātugāmo ye te bhattu abbhantarā kammantā – uṇṇāti vā kappāsāti vā – tattha dakkhā hoti analasā tatrupāyāya vīmaṃsāya samannāgatā alaṃ kātuṃ alaṃ saṃvidhātuṃ. Evaṃ kho, visākhe, mātugāmo susaṃvihitakammanto [P 4.0270] hoti.

And how, Visākhā, is a woman one who manages her work well? Here, Visākhā, in whatever internal work of her husband there is—such as with wool or cotton—she is skilled and not lazy, endowed with the investigative wisdom suitable for it, able to do it and able to manage it. In this way, Visākhā, a woman is one who manages her work well.

“Kathañca, visākhe, mātugāmo saṅgahitaparijano hoti? Idha, visākhe, mātugāmo yo so [V 3.0099] bhattu abbhantaro antojano – dāsāti vā pessāti vā kammakarāti vā – tesaṃ katañca katato jānāti akatañca akatato jānāti, gilānakānañca balābalaṃ jānāti khādanīyaṃ bhojanīyañcassa paccaṃsena saṃvibhajati. Evaṃ kho, visākhe, mātugāmo saṅgahitaparijano hoti.

And how, Visākhā, is a woman one who has well-cared-for attendants? Here, Visākhā, regarding the household folk of her husband—be they slaves, messengers, or workers—she knows what they have done as done and what they have not done as not done. She knows the strength and weakness of the sick. She distributes hard and soft food to them, each according to their share. In this way, Visākhā, a woman is one who has well-cared-for attendants.

“Kathañca, visākhe, mātugāmo bhattu manāpaṃ carati? Idha, visākhe, mātugāmo yaṃ bhattu amanāpasaṅkhātaṃ taṃ jīvitahetupi na ajjhācarati. Evaṃ kho, visākhe, mātugāmo bhattu manāpaṃ carati.

And how, Visākhā, does a woman act in a way that is pleasing to her husband? Here, Visākhā, a woman does not transgress in any way that is considered displeasing by her husband, even for the sake of her own life. In this way, Visākhā, a woman acts in a way that is pleasing to her husband.

“Kathañca, visākhe, mātugāmo sambhataṃ anurakkhati? Idha, visākhe, mātugāmo yaṃ bhattā āharati dhanaṃ vā dhaññaṃ vā rajataṃ vā jātarūpaṃ vā taṃ ārakkhena guttiyā sampādeti, tattha ca hoti adhuttī athenī asoṇḍī avināsikā. Evaṃ kho, visākhe, mātugāmo sambhataṃ anurakkhati. Imehi kho, visākhe, catūhi dhammehi samannāgato mātugāmo idhalokavijayāya paṭipanno hoti, ayaṃsa loko āraddho hoti.

And how, Visākhā, does a woman protect the wealth he has acquired? Here, Visākhā, whatever wealth her husband brings—be it wealth, grain, silver, or gold—she secures it with protection and guardianship. And in this, she is not a profligate, not a thief, not a drunkard, and not one who destroys it. In this way, Visākhā, a woman protects the wealth he has acquired. Visākhā, a woman endowed with these four qualities is practicing for victory in this world; this world is successfully undertaken by her.

“Catūhi kho[T 4.0277], visākhe, dhammehi samannāgato mātugāmo paralokavijayāya paṭipanno hoti, paraloko āraddho hoti. Katamehi catūhi? Idha, visākhe, mātugāmo saddhāsampanno hoti, sīlasampanno hoti, cāgasampanno hoti, paññāsampanno hoti.

Visākhā, a woman endowed with four qualities is practicing for victory in the next world; the next world is successfully undertaken by her. With which four? Here, Visākhā, a woman is endowed with faith, endowed with virtue, endowed with generosity, and endowed with wisdom.

“Kathañca[M 3.0098], visākhe, mātugāmo saddhāsampanno hoti? Idha, visākhe, mātugāmo saddho hoti, saddahati tathāgatassa bodhiṃ – ‘itipi so bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho vijjācaraṇasampanno sugato lokavidū anuttaro purisadammasārathi satthā devamanussānaṃ buddho bhagavā’ti. Evaṃ kho, visākhe, mātugāmo saddhāsampanno hoti.

And how, Visākhā, is a woman endowed with faith? Here, Visākhā, a woman has faith; she has faith in the enlightenment of the Tathāgata: ‘For this reason, the Blessed One is an Arahant, a Perfectly Enlightened One, accomplished in knowledge and conduct, a Sublime One, a Knower of the World, an Unsurpassed Trainer of persons to be tamed, a Teacher of gods and humans, the Buddha, the Blessed One.’ In this way, Visākhā, a woman is endowed with faith.

“Kathañca, visākhe, mātugāmo sīlasampanno hoti? Idha[P 4.0271], visākhe, mātugāmo pāṇātipātā paṭivirato hoti…pe… surāmerayamajjapamādaṭṭhānā paṭivirato hoti. Evaṃ kho, visākhe, mātugāmo sīlasampanno hoti.

And how, Visākhā, is a woman endowed with virtue? Here, Visākhā, a woman abstains from the destruction of life... and so on... abstains from distilled and fermented intoxicants which are the basis for heedlessness. In this way, Visākhā, a woman is endowed with virtue.

“Kathañca[V 3.0100], visākhe, mātugāmo cāgasampanno hoti? Idha, visākhe, mātugāmo vigatamalamaccherena cetasā agāraṃ ajjhāvasati muttacāgā payatapāṇinī vossaggaratā yācayogā dānasaṃvibhāgaratā. Evaṃ kho, visākhe, mātugāmo cāgasampanno hoti.

And how, Visākhā, is a woman endowed with generosity? Here, Visākhā, a woman dwells at home with a mind free from the stain of stinginess, is freely generous, open-handed, delights in relinquishment, is amenable to being asked, and delights in giving and sharing. In this way, Visākhā, a woman is endowed with generosity.

“Kathañca, visākhe, mātugāmo paññāsampanno hoti? Idha, visākhe, mātugāmo paññavā hoti…pe… evaṃ kho, visākhe, mātugāmo paññāsampanno hoti. Imehi kho, visākhe, catūhi [T 4.0278] dhammehi samannāgato mātugāmo paralokavijayāya paṭipanno hoti, paraloko āraddho hotī”ti.

“And how, Visākhā, is a woman endowed with wisdom? Here, Visākhā, a woman is wise… and so on… In this way, Visākhā, a woman is endowed with wisdom. Visākhā, a woman endowed with these four qualities is practicing for victory in the next world; the next world is successfully undertaken by her.”

“Susaṃvihitakammantā, saṅgahitaparijjanā;

Bhattu manāpaṃ carati, sambhataṃ anurakkhati.

“She manages her work well and has well-cared-for attendants; she acts in a way that is pleasing to her husband and protects the wealth he has acquired.

“Saddhā sīlena sampannā, vadaññū vītamaccharā;

Niccaṃ maggaṃ visodheti, sotthānaṃ samparāyikaṃ.

“Endowed with faith and virtue, generous and free from stinginess, she constantly purifies the path that leads to well-being in the future.

“Iccete aṭṭha dhammā ca, yassā vijjanti nāriyā;

Tampi sīlavatiṃ āhu, dhammaṭṭhaṃ saccavādiniṃ.

“The woman in whom these eight qualities are found, her they call virtuous, established in Dhamma, and a speaker of truth.

“Soḷasākārasampannā, aṭṭhaṅgasusamāgatā;

Tādisī sīlavatī upāsikā;

Upapajjati devalokaṃ manāpa”nti. navamaṃ;

“Endowed with sixteen aspects and possessed of the eight factors, such a virtuous lay female disciple is reborn in a pleasing heavenly world.” Ninth.

10. Dutiyaidhalokikasuttaṃ

10. The Second Discourse on This World

50. “Catūhi[M 3.0099], bhikkhave, dhammehi samannāgato mātugāmo idhalokavijayāya paṭipanno hoti, ayaṃsa loko āraddho hoti. Katamehi catūhi? Idha[P 4.0272], bhikkhave, mātugāmo susaṃvihitakammanto hoti, saṅgahitaparijano, bhattu manāpaṃ carati, sambhataṃ anurakkhati.

50. “Monks, a woman endowed with four qualities is practicing for the conquest of this world, and she has this world successfully undertaken. What are the four? Here, monks, a woman is well-organized in her work, has a well-cared-for retinue, acts in a way that is pleasing to her husband, and guards the wealth he has acquired.”

“Kathañca, bhikkhave, mātugāmo susaṃvihitakammanto hoti? Idha, bhikkhave, mātugāmo ye [V 3.0101] te bhattu abbhantarā kammantā…pe… evaṃ kho, bhikkhave, mātugāmo susaṃvihitakammanto hoti.

“And how, monks, is a woman well-organized in her work? Here, monks, a woman is skilled and diligent in whatever are her husband’s internal duties... and so on... Thus, monks, a woman is well-organized in her work.”

“Kathañca, bhikkhave, mātugāmo saṅgahitaparijano hoti? Idha, bhikkhave, mātugāmo yo so bhattu abbhantaro antojano…pe… evaṃ kho, bhikkhave, mātugāmo saṅgahitaparijano hoti.

“And how, monks, does a woman have a well-cared-for retinue? Here, monks, a woman knows how to treat the people within her husband's household... and so on... Thus, monks, a woman has a well-cared-for retinue.”

“Kathañca[T 4.0279], bhikkhave, mātugāmo bhattu manāpaṃ carati? Idha, bhikkhave, mātugāmo yaṃ bhattu amanāpasaṅkhātaṃ taṃ jīvitahetupi na ajjhācarati. Evaṃ kho, bhikkhave, mātugāmo bhattu manāpaṃ carati.

“And how, monks, does a woman act in a way that is pleasing to her husband? Here, monks, a woman does not transgress in what is considered displeasing to her husband, even for the sake of her life. Thus, monks, a woman acts in a way that is pleasing to her husband.”

“Kathañca, bhikkhave, mātugāmo sambhataṃ anurakkhati? Idha, bhikkhave, mātugāmo yaṃ bhattā āharati…pe… evaṃ kho, bhikkhave, mātugāmo sambhataṃ anurakkhati. Imehi kho, bhikkhave, catūhi dhammehi samannāgato mātugāmo idhalokavijayāya paṭipanno hoti, ayaṃsa loko āraddho hoti.

“And how, monks, does a woman guard the wealth he has acquired? Here, monks, whatever wealth her husband brings... and so on... she protects and guards it. Thus, monks, a woman guards the wealth he has acquired. Endowed with these four qualities, monks, a woman is practicing for the conquest of this world, and she has this world successfully undertaken.”

“Catūhi, bhikkhave, dhammehi samannāgato mātugāmo paralokavijayāya paṭipanno hoti, paraloko āraddho hoti. Katamehi catūhi? Idha, bhikkhave, mātugāmo saddhāsampanno hoti, sīlasampanno hoti, cāgasampanno hoti, paññāsampanno hoti.

“Monks, a woman endowed with four qualities is practicing for the conquest of the next world, and she has the next world successfully undertaken. What are the four? Here, monks, a woman is endowed with faith, endowed with virtue, endowed with generosity, and endowed with wisdom.”

“Kathañca, bhikkhave, mātugāmo saddhāsampanno hoti? Idha, bhikkhave, mātugāmo saddho hoti…pe… evaṃ [P 4.0273] kho, bhikkhave, mātugāmo saddhāsampanno hoti.

“And how, monks, is a woman endowed with faith? Here, monks, a woman has faith... and so on… Thus, monks, a woman is endowed with faith.”

“Kathañca[M 3.0100], bhikkhave, mātugāmo sīlasampanno hoti? Idha, bhikkhave, mātugāmo pāṇātipātā paṭivirato hoti…pe… surāmerayamajjapamādaṭṭhānā paṭivirato hoti. Evaṃ kho, bhikkhave, mātugāmo sīlasampanno hoti.

“And how, monks, is a woman endowed with virtue? Here, monks, a woman abstains from the destruction of life... and so on… she abstains from distilled and fermented intoxicants which are the basis for heedlessness. Thus, monks, a woman is endowed with virtue.”

“Kathañca, bhikkhave, mātugāmo cāgasampanno hoti? Idha, bhikkhave, mātugāmo vigatamalamaccherena [V 3.0102] cetasā agāraṃ ajjhāvasati…pe… evaṃ kho[T 4.0280], bhikkhave, mātugāmo cāgasampanno hoti.

“And how, monks, is a woman endowed with generosity? Here, monks, a woman dwells at home with a mind free from the stain of stinginess... and so on… Thus, monks, a woman is endowed with generosity.”

“Kathañca, bhikkhave, mātugāmo paññāsampanno hoti? Idha, bhikkhave, mātugāmo paññavā hoti…pe… evaṃ kho, bhikkhave, mātugāmo paññāsampanno hoti. Imehi kho, bhikkhave, catūhi dhammehi samannāgato mātugāmo paralokavijayāya paṭipanno hoti, paraloko āraddho hotī”ti.

“And how, monks, is a woman endowed with wisdom? Here, monks, a woman is wise... and so on… Thus, monks, a woman is endowed with wisdom. Endowed with these four qualities, monks, a woman is practicing for the conquest of the next world, and she has the next world successfully undertaken.”

“Susaṃvihitakammantā, saṅgahitaparijjanā;

Bhattu manāpaṃ carati, sambhataṃ anurakkhati.

“Well-organized in her work and with a well-cared-for retinue, she acts in a way that is pleasing to her husband and guards the wealth he has acquired.

“Saddhā sīlena sampannā, vadaññū vītamaccharā;

Niccaṃ maggaṃ visodheti, sotthānaṃ samparāyikaṃ.

Endowed with faith and virtue, generous, and free from stinginess, she always purifies the path that leads to well-being in the future.

“Iccete aṭṭha dhammā ca, yassā vijjanti nāriyā;

Tampi sīlavatiṃ āhu, dhammaṭṭhaṃ saccavādiniṃ.

The woman in whom these eight qualities are found, her they call virtuous, established in Dhamma, and a speaker of truth.

“Soḷasākārasampannā, aṭṭhaṅgasusamāgatā;

Tādisī sīlavatī upāsikā, upapajjati devalokaṃ manāpa”nti. dasamaṃ;

Endowed with sixteen aspects and perfectly possessed of the eight factors, such a virtuous female lay follower is reborn in a delightful heavenly world.” Tenth.

Uposathavaggo pañcamo.

The Uposatha Chapter, the Fifth.

Tassuddānaṃ –

The summary verse for this:

Saṃkhitte vitthate visākhe, vāseṭṭho bojjhāya pañcamaṃ;

Anuruddhaṃ puna visākhe, nakulā idhalokikā dveti.

In Brief, In Detail, and Visākhā; Vāseṭṭha, and to Bojjhā as the fifth; Anuruddha, again Visākhā; Nakula's Mother, and the two on This World.

Paṭhamapaṇṇāsakaṃ samattaṃ.

The First Fifty is concluded.

2. Dutiyapaṇṇāsakaṃ

2. The Second Fifty

(6) 1. Gotamīvaggo

(6) 1. The Gotamī Chapter

1. Gotamīsuttaṃ

The Discourse concerning Gotamī

51. Ekaṃ [T 4.0281][V 3.0103][M 3.0101][P 4.0274] samayaṃ bhagavā sakkesu viharati kapilavatthusmiṃ nigrodhārāme. Atha kho mahāpajāpatī mahāpajāpati (syā.) cūḷava. 402 gotamī yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ aṭṭhāsi. Ekamantaṃ ṭhitā kho mahāpajāpatī gotamī bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “sādhu, bhante, labheyya mātugāmo tathāgatappavedite dhammavinaye agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajja”nti. “Alaṃ, gotami! Mā te rucci mātugāmassa tathāgatappavedite dhammavinaye agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajjā”ti.

1. At one time, the Blessed One was dwelling among the Sakyans in Kapilavatthu at the Nigrodha monastery. Then Mahāpajāpatī Gotamī approached the Blessed One; having approached, she paid homage to the Blessed One and stood to one side. Standing to one side, Mahāpajāpatī Gotamī said this to the Blessed One: 'Venerable Sir, it would be good if a woman might obtain the going forth from the household life into homelessness in the Dhamma and Vinaya proclaimed by the Tathāgata.' 'Let it not be so, Gotamī! Do not approve of the going forth of a woman from the household life into homelessness in the Dhamma and Vinaya proclaimed by the Tathāgata.'

Dutiyampi kho mahāpajāpatī gotamī bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “sādhu, bhante, labheyya mātugāmo tathāgatappavedite dhammavinaye agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajja”nti. “Alaṃ, gotami! Mā te rucci mātugāmassa tathāgatappavedite dhammavinaye agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajjā”ti. “Tatiyampi kho mahāpajāpatī gotamī bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “sādhu bhante, labheyya mātugāmo tathāgatappavedite dhammavinaye agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajja”nti. “Alaṃ, gotami! Mā te rucci mātugāmassa tathāgatappavedite dhammavinaye agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajjā”ti.

For a second time, Mahāpajāpatī Gotamī said this to the Blessed One: 'Venerable Sir, it would be good if a woman might obtain the going forth from the household life into homelessness in the Dhamma and Vinaya proclaimed by the Tathāgata.' 'Enough, Gotamī! Do not approve of the going forth of a woman from the household life into homelessness in the Dhamma and Vinaya proclaimed by the Tathāgata.' For a third time, Mahāpajāpatī Gotamī said this to the Blessed One: 'Venerable Sir, it would be good if a woman might obtain the going forth from the household life into homelessness in the Dhamma and Vinaya proclaimed by the Tathāgata.' 'Enough, Gotamī! Do not approve of the going forth of a woman from the household life into homelessness in the Dhamma and Vinaya proclaimed by the Tathāgata.'

Atha kho mahāpajāpatī gotamī “na bhagavā anujānāti mātugāmassa tathāgatappavedite [T 4.0282] dhammavinaye agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajja”nti dukkhī dummanā assumukhī rudamānā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā padakkhiṇaṃ katvā pakkāmi.
