Then Mahāpajāpatī Gotamī, understanding, 'The Blessed One does not permit the going forth of a woman from the household life into homelessness in the Dhamma and Vinaya proclaimed by the Tathāgata,' being sorrowful, dejected, with a tearful face, and weeping, paid homage to the Blessed One, circumambulated him keeping him to her right, and departed.

Atha kho bhagavā kapilavatthusmiṃ yathābhirantaṃ viharitvā yena vesālī tena cārikaṃ pakkāmi[V 3.0104]. Anupubbena cārikaṃ caramāno yena vesālī tadavasari. Tatra sudaṃ bhagavā vesāliyaṃ viharati mahāvane kūṭāgārasālāyaṃ[M 3.0102]. Atha kho mahāpajāpatī gotamī kese chedāpetvā kāsāyāni vatthāni acchādetvā sambahulāhi sākiyānīhi saddhiṃ yena vesālī [P 4.0275] tena pakkāmi. Anupubbena yena vesālī mahāvanaṃ kūṭāgārasālā tenupasaṅkami. Atha kho mahāpajāpatī gotamī sūnehi pādehi rajokiṇṇena gattena dukkhī dummanā assumukhī rudamānā bahidvārakoṭṭhake aṭṭhāsi.

Then the Blessed One, having dwelt in Kapilavatthu for as long as he wished, set out on a journey towards Vesālī. Wandering on tour by stages, he arrived at Vesālī. There, in Vesālī, the Blessed One dwelt in the Great Wood, in the hall with the gabled roof. Then Mahāpajāpatī Gotamī, having had her hair cut off and having put on saffron-colored robes, set out for Vesālī together with many Sakyan ladies. By stages, she approached the hall with the gabled roof in the Great Wood at Vesālī. Then Mahāpajāpatī Gotamī, with swollen feet and a dust-covered body, being sorrowful, dejected, with a tearful face, and weeping, stood outside the gate-chamber.

Addasā kho āyasmā ānando mahāpajāpatiṃ gotamiṃ sūnehi pādehi rajokiṇṇena gattena dukkhiṃ dummanaṃ assumukhiṃ rudamānaṃ bahidvārakoṭṭhake ṭhitaṃ. Disvāna mahāpajāpatiṃ gotamiṃ etadavoca – “kiṃ nu tvaṃ, gotami, sūnehi pādehi rajokiṇṇena gattena dukkhī dummanā assumukhī rudamānā bahidvārakoṭṭhake ṭhitā”ti? “Tathā hi pana, bhante ānanda, na bhagavā anujānāti mātugāmassa tathāgatappavedite dhammavinaye agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajja”nti. “Tena hi tvaṃ, gotami, muhuttaṃ idheva tāva hohi, yāvāhaṃ bhagavantaṃ yācāmi mātugāmassa tathāgatappavedite dhammavinaye agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajja”nti.

The Venerable Ānanda saw Mahāpajāpatī Gotamī standing outside the gate-chamber with swollen feet and a dust-covered body, being sorrowful, dejected, with a tearful face, and weeping. Having seen her, he said this to Mahāpajāpatī Gotamī: 'For what reason, Gotamī, are you standing outside the gate-chamber with swollen feet and a dust-covered body, being sorrowful, dejected, with a tearful face, and weeping?' 'It is because, Venerable Ānanda, the Blessed One does not permit the going forth of a woman from the household life into homelessness in the Dhamma and Vinaya proclaimed by the Tathāgata.' 'In that case, Gotamī, please wait right here for a moment, while I ask the Blessed One for the going forth of a woman from the household life into homelessness in the Dhamma and Vinaya proclaimed by the Tathāgata.'

Atha [T 4.0283] kho āyasmā ānando yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinno kho āyasmā ānando bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “esā, bhante, mahāpajāpatī gotamī sūnehi pādehi rajokiṇṇena gattena dukkhī dummanā assumukhī rudamānā bahidvārakoṭṭhake ṭhitā – ‘na bhagavā anujānāti mātugāmassa tathāgatappavedite dhammavinaye agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajja’nti. Sādhu, bhante, labheyya mātugāmo tathāgatappavedite dhammavinaye agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajja”nti. “Alaṃ, ānanda! Mā te rucci mātugāmassa tathāgatappavedite dhammavinaye agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajjā”ti.

Then the Venerable Ānanda approached the Blessed One; having approached, he paid homage to the Blessed One and sat down to one side. Seated to one side, the Venerable Ānanda said this to the Blessed One: 'Venerable Sir, here is Mahāpajāpatī Gotamī standing outside the gate-chamber with swollen feet and a dust-covered body, being sorrowful, dejected, with a tearful face, and weeping, because 'the Blessed One does not permit the going forth of a woman from the household life into homelessness in the Dhamma and Vinaya proclaimed by the Tathāgata.' Venerable Sir, it would be good if a woman might obtain the going forth from the household life into homelessness in the Dhamma and Vinaya proclaimed by the Tathāgata.' 'Enough, Ānanda! Do not approve of the going forth of a woman from the household life into homelessness in the Dhamma and Vinaya proclaimed by the Tathāgata.'

Dutiyampi kho…pe… tatiyampi kho āyasmā ānando bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “sādhu, bhante, labheyya mātugāmo tathāgatappavedite dhammavinaye agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajja”nti. “Alaṃ, ānanda! Mā te rucci mātugāmassa tathāgatappavedite dhammavinaye agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajjā”ti.

For a second time… and for a third time, the Venerable Ānanda said this to the Blessed One: 'Venerable Sir, it would be good if a woman might obtain the going forth from the household life into homelessness in the Dhamma and Vinaya proclaimed by the Tathāgata.' 'Enough, Ānanda! Do not approve of the going forth of a woman from the household life into homelessness in the Dhamma and Vinaya proclaimed by the Tathāgata.'

Atha [V 3.0105][M 3.0103][P 4.0276] kho āyasmato ānandassa etadahosi – “na bhagavā anujānāti mātugāmassa tathāgatappavedite dhammavinaye agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajjaṃ. Yaṃnūnāhaṃ aññenapi pariyāyena bhagavantaṃ yāceyyaṃ mātugāmassa tathāgatappavedite dhammavinaye agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajja”nti. Atha kho āyasmā ānando bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “bhabbo nu kho, bhante, mātugāmo tathāgatappavedite dhammavinaye agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajitvā sotāpattiphalaṃ vā [T 4.0284] sakadāgāmiphalaṃ vā anāgāmiphalaṃ vā arahattaphalaṃ vā sacchikātu”nti? “Bhabbo, ānanda, mātugāmo tathāgatappavedite dhammavinaye agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajitvā sotāpattiphalampi sakadāgāmiphalampi anāgāmiphalampi arahattaphalampi sacchikātu”nti. “Sace, bhante, bhabbo mātugāmo tathāgatappavedite dhammavinaye agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajitvā sotāpattiphalampi…pe… arahattaphalampi sacchikātuṃ, bahukārā, bhante, mahāpajāpatī gotamī bhagavato mātucchā āpādikā posikā khīrassa dāyikā; bhagavantaṃ janettiyā kālaṅkatāya thaññaṃ pāyesi. Sādhu, bhante, labheyya mātugāmo tathāgatappavedite dhammavinaye agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajja”nti.

Then this thought occurred to the Venerable Ānanda: "The Blessed One does not permit a woman's going forth from the household life into homelessness in the Dhamma and Vinaya proclaimed by the Tathāgata. What if I were to ask the Blessed One by another means for a woman's going forth from the household life into homelessness in the Dhamma and Vinaya proclaimed by the Tathāgata?" Then the Venerable Ānanda said this to the Blessed One: "Venerable Sir, is a woman, having gone forth from the household life into homelessness in the Dhamma and Vinaya proclaimed by the Tathāgata, able to realize the fruit of stream-entry, or the fruit of once-returning, or the fruit of non-returning, or the fruit of arahantship?" "She is able, Ānanda. A woman, having gone forth from the household life into homelessness in the Dhamma and Vinaya proclaimed by the Tathāgata, is able to realize the fruit of stream-entry, and the fruit of once-returning, and the fruit of non-returning, and the fruit of arahantship." "If, Venerable Sir, a woman is able, having gone forth from the household life into homelessness in the Dhamma and Vinaya proclaimed by the Tathāgata, to realize the fruit of stream-entry... and the fruit of arahantship, then, Venerable Sir, Mahāpajāpatī Gotamī has been of great service to the Blessed One. She was his maternal aunt, his foster-mother, his nurse; when the Blessed One's mother passed away, she gave him her milk to drink. It would be good, Venerable Sir, if a woman might obtain the going forth from the household life into homelessness in the Dhamma and Vinaya proclaimed by the Tathāgata."

“Sace, ānanda, mahāpajāpatī gotamī aṭṭha garudhamme paṭiggaṇhāti, sāvassā hotu upasampadā –

“Ānanda, if Mahāpajāpatī Gotamī accepts the eight weighty rules, let that itself be her higher ordination.”

pāci. 149; cūḷava. 403 “Vassasatūpasampannāya bhikkhuniyā tadahūpasampannassa bhikkhuno abhivādanaṃ paccuṭṭhānaṃ añjalikammaṃ sāmīcikammaṃ kattabbaṃ. Ayampi dhammo sakkatvā garuṃ katvā garukatvā (sī. syā. pī.) mānetvā pūjetvā yāvajīvaṃ anatikkamanīyo.

A bhikkhunī who has been ordained for one hundred years is to pay homage, rise up, perform the añjali gesture, and show proper respect to a bhikkhu ordained that very day. This principle, too, is to be respected, revered, cherished, and honored, and is not to be transgressed for as long as one lives.

“Na bhikkhuniyā abhikkhuke āvāse vassaṃ upagantabbaṃ. Ayampi dhammo sakkatvā garuṃ katvā mānetvā pūjetvā yāvajīvaṃ anatikkamanīyo.

A bhikkhunī is not to enter upon the rains-residence in a dwelling where there is no bhikkhu. This principle, too, is to be respected, revered, cherished, and honored, and is not to be transgressed for as long as one lives.

“Anvaḍḍhamāsaṃ bhikkhuniyā bhikkhusaṅghato dve dhammā paccāsīsitabbā paccāsiṃsitabbā (sī. syā. pī.) – uposathapucchakañca, ovādūpasaṅkamanañca[P 4.0277]. Ayampi dhammo sakkatvā garuṃ katvā mānetvā pūjetvā yāvajīvaṃ anatikkamanīyo.

Every half-month, a bhikkhunī is to expect two things from the Bhikkhu Saṅgha: the asking of the Uposatha day, and the approaching for an exhortation. This principle, too, is to be respected, revered, cherished, and honored, and is not to be transgressed for as long as one lives.

“Vassaṃvuṭṭhāya [V 3.0106] bhikkhuniyā ubhatosaṅghe tīhi ṭhānehi pavāretabbaṃ – diṭṭhena vā sutena vā parisaṅkāya vā. Ayampi dhammo sakkatvā garuṃ katvā mānetvā pūjetvā [T 4.0285] yāvajīvaṃ anatikkamanīyo.

After completing the rains-residence, a bhikkhunī is to invite criticism before both Saṅghas on three grounds: regarding what has been seen, what has been heard, or what is suspected. This principle, too, is to be respected, revered, cherished, and honored, and is not to be transgressed for as long as one lives.

“Garudhammaṃ [M 3.0104] ajjhāpannāya bhikkhuniyā ubhatosaṅghe pakkhamānattaṃ caritabbaṃ. Ayampi dhammo sakkatvā garuṃ katvā mānetvā pūjetvā yāvajīvaṃ anatikkamanīyo.

A bhikkhunī who has transgressed a grave rule must undergo mānatta probation for half a month before both Saṅghas. This principle, too, is to be respected, revered, cherished, and honored, and is not to be transgressed for as long as one lives.

“Dve vassāni chasu dhammesu sikkhitasikkhāya sikkhamānāya ubhatosaṅghe upasampadā pariyesitabbā. Ayampi dhammo sakkatvā garuṃ katvā mānetvā pūjetvā yāvajīvaṃ anatikkamanīyo.

A probationer who has trained for two years in the six rules is to seek higher ordination from both Saṅghas. This principle, too, is to be respected, revered, cherished, and honored, and is not to be transgressed for as long as one lives.

“Na kenaci pariyāyena bhikkhuniyā bhikkhu akkositabbo paribhāsitabbo. Ayampi dhammo sakkatvā garuṃ katvā mānetvā pūjetvā yāvajīvaṃ anatikkamanīyo.

In no way is a bhikkhu to be reviled or disparaged by a bhikkhunī. This principle, too, is to be respected, revered, cherished, and honored, and is not to be transgressed for as long as one lives.

“Ajjatagge ovaṭo bhikkhunīnaṃ bhikkhūsu vacanapatho, anovaṭo bhikkhūnaṃ bhikkhunīsu vacanapatho. Ayampi dhammo sakkatvā garuṃ katvā mānetvā pūjetvā yāvajīvaṃ anatikkamanīyo.

From today onwards, the path of speech of bhikkhunīs towards bhikkhus is closed; the path of speech of bhikkhus towards bhikkhunīs is not closed. This principle, too, is to be respected, revered, cherished, and honored, and is not to be transgressed for as long as one lives.

“Sace, ānanda, mahāpajāpatī gotamī ime aṭṭha garudhamme paṭiggaṇhāti, sāvassā hotu upasampadā”ti.

“Ānanda, if Mahāpajāpatī Gotamī accepts these eight weighty principles, let that itself be her higher ordination.”

Atha kho āyasmā ānando bhagavato santike ime aṭṭha garudhamme uggahetvā yena mahāpajāpatī gotamī tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā mahāpajāpatiṃ gotamiṃ etadavoca –

Then the Venerable Ānanda, having learned these eight weighty principles in the presence of the Blessed One, approached Mahāpajāpatī Gotamī. Having approached, he said this to Mahāpajāpatī Gotamī:

“Sace kho tvaṃ, gotami, aṭṭha garudhamme paṭiggaṇheyyāsi, sāva te bhavissati upasampadā –

Gotamī, if you accept these eight weighty principles, that itself will be your higher ordination:

“Vassasatūpasampannāya bhikkhuniyā tadahūpasampannassa bhikkhuno abhivādanaṃ paccuṭṭhānaṃ añjalikammaṃ sāmīcikammaṃ kattabbaṃ. Ayampi dhammo sakkatvā garuṃ katvā mānetvā pūjetvā yāvajīvaṃ anatikkamanīyo…pe….

A bhikkhunī who has been ordained for one hundred years is to pay homage, rise up, perform the añjali gesture, and show proper respect to a bhikkhu ordained that very day. This principle, too, is to be respected, revered, cherished, and honored, and is not to be transgressed for as long as one lives… and so on…

“Ajjatagge [V 3.0107] ovaṭo bhikkhunīnaṃ bhikkhūsu vacanapatho, anovaṭo [P 4.0278] bhikkhūnaṃ bhikkhunīsu vacanapatho. Ayampi dhammo [T 4.0286] sakkatvā garuṃ katvā mānetvā pūjetvā yāvajīvaṃ anatikkamanīyo. Sace kho tvaṃ, gotami, ime aṭṭha garudhamme paṭiggaṇheyyāsi, sāva te bhavissati upasampadā”ti.

From today onwards, the path of speech of bhikkhunīs towards bhikkhus is closed; the path of speech of bhikkhus towards bhikkhunīs is not closed. This principle, too, is to be respected, revered, cherished, and honored, and is not to be transgressed for as long as one lives. Gotamī, if you accept these eight weighty principles, that itself will be your higher ordination.

“Seyyathāpi[M 3.0105], bhante ānanda, itthī vā puriso vā daharo yuvā maṇḍanakajātiko maṇḍanakajātiyo (sī. pī.) sīsaṃnhāto sīsaṃnahāto (sī. pī.), sīsanahāto (syā.) uppalamālaṃ vā vassikamālaṃ vā adhimuttakamālaṃ atimuttakamālaṃ (sī.) vā labhitvā ubhohi hatthehi paṭiggahetvā uttamaṅge sirasmiṃ patiṭṭhāpeyya; evamevaṃ kho ahaṃ, bhante ānanda, ime aṭṭha garudhamme paṭiggaṇhāmi yāvajīvaṃ anatikkamanīye”ti.

Venerable Ānanda, just as a woman or a man—young, youthful, and fond of adornment—having bathed their head, would receive a garland of lotuses, or jasmine, or atimuttaka flowers with both hands and place it on their head; even so, Venerable Ānanda, I accept these eight weighty principles, not to be transgressed for as long as I live.

Atha kho āyasmā ānando yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinno kho āyasmā ānando bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “paṭiggahitā, bhante, mahāpajāpatiyā gotamiyā aṭṭha garudhammā yāvajīvaṃ anatikkamanīyā”ti.

Then the Venerable Ānanda approached the Blessed One, paid homage to him, and sat down to one side. Seated to one side, the Venerable Ānanda said this to the Blessed One: “Venerable sir, the eight weighty principles, not to be transgressed for as long as she lives, have been accepted by Mahāpajāpatī Gotamī.”

“Sace, ānanda, nālabhissa mātugāmo tathāgatappavedite dhammavinaye agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajjaṃ, ciraṭṭhitikaṃ, ānanda, brahmacariyaṃ abhavissa, vassasahassameva saddhammo tiṭṭheyya. Yato ca kho, ānanda, mātugāmo tathāgatappavedite dhammavinaye agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajito, na dāni, ānanda, brahmacariyaṃ ciraṭṭhitikaṃ bhavissati. Pañceva dāni, ānanda, vassasatāni saddhammo ṭhassati.

Ānanda, if women had not obtained the going forth from the household life into homelessness in the Dhamma and Vinaya proclaimed by the Tathāgata, the holy life would have been long-lasting, Ānanda; the true Dhamma would have endured for a thousand years. But since, Ānanda, women have gone forth from the household life into homelessness in the Dhamma and Vinaya proclaimed by the Tathāgata, the holy life will not now be long-lasting. Now, Ānanda, the true Dhamma will endure for only five hundred years.

“Seyyathāpi, ānanda, yāni kānici kulāni bahutthikāni bahukitthikāni (sī. pī.), bahuitthikāni (syā.) appapurisakāni, tāni suppadhaṃsiyāni honti corehi kumbhatthenakehi; evamevaṃ kho, ānanda, yasmiṃ dhammavinaye labhati mātugāmo agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajjaṃ, na taṃ brahmacariyaṃ ciraṭṭhitikaṃ hoti.

Ānanda, just as those families with many women and few men are easily plundered by thieves, by burglars; even so, Ānanda, in whatever Dhamma and Vinaya women obtain the going forth from the household life into homelessness, that holy life is not long-lasting.

“Seyyathāpi[T 4.0287], ānanda, sampanne sālikkhette setaṭṭhikā [P 4.0279] nāma rogajāti nipatati, evaṃ taṃ sālikkhettaṃ na ciraṭṭhitikaṃ hoti; evamevaṃ kho, ānanda, yasmiṃ dhammavinaye labhati mātugāmo agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajjaṃ, na taṃ brahmacariyaṃ ciraṭṭhitikaṃ hoti.

Ānanda, just as when a type of blight called ‘white-bone’ falls upon a thriving rice field, that rice field is not long-lasting; even so, Ānanda, in whatever Dhamma and Vinaya women obtain the going forth from the household life into homelessness, that holy life is not long-lasting.

“Seyyathāpi[V 3.0108], ānanda, sampanne ucchukkhette mañjiṭṭhikā mañjeṭṭhikā (sī. syā.) nāma rogajāti nipatati, evaṃ taṃ ucchukkhettaṃ na ciraṭṭhitikaṃ hoti; evamevaṃ kho, ānanda, yasmiṃ dhammavinaye labhati mātugāmo agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajjaṃ, na taṃ brahmacariyaṃ ciraṭṭhitikaṃ hoti.

Ānanda, just as when a type of blight called ‘red-rust’ falls upon a thriving sugarcane field, that sugarcane field is not long-lasting; even so, Ānanda, in whatever Dhamma and Vinaya women obtain the going forth from the household life into homelessness, that holy life is not long-lasting.

“Seyyathāpi[M 3.0106], ānanda, puriso mahato taḷākassa paṭikacceva paṭigacceva (sī. pī.) āḷiṃ bandheyya yāvadeva udakassa anatikkamanāya; evamevaṃ kho, ānanda, mayā paṭikacceva bhikkhunīnaṃ aṭṭha garudhammā paññattā yāvajīvaṃ anatikkamanīyā”ti. Paṭhamaṃ.

“Ānanda, just as a man would build an embankment for a great reservoir beforehand, precisely to prevent the water from overflowing; even so, Ānanda, I have laid down beforehand for the bhikkhunīs these eight weighty rules, not to be transgressed for as long as life lasts.” First.

2. Ovādasuttaṃ

2. The Discourse on Exhortation

52. Ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā vesāliyaṃ viharati mahāvane kūṭāgārasālāyaṃ. Atha kho āyasmā ānando yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinno kho āyasmā ānando bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “katihi nu kho, bhante, dhammehi samannāgato bhikkhu bhikkhunovādako sammannitabbo”ti?

52. On one occasion the Blessed One was dwelling at Vesālī, in the Great Wood, at the Hall with the Peaked Roof. Then the Venerable Ānanda approached the Blessed One; having approached, he paid homage to the Blessed One and sat down to one side. Seated to one side, the Venerable Ānanda said this to the Blessed One: “Venerable Sir, endowed with how many qualities should a bhikkhu be designated as an instructor of bhikkhunīs?”

pāci. 147 “Aṭṭhahi kho, ānanda, dhammehi samannāgato bhikkhu bhikkhunovādako sammannitabbo. Katamehi aṭṭhahi? Idhānanda, bhikkhu sīlavā hoti…pe… samādāya sikkhati sikkhāpadesu; bahussuto hoti…pe… diṭṭhiyā suppaṭividdhā; ubhayāni kho panassa pātimokkhāni vitthārena svāgatāni [T 4.0288] honti suvibhattāni suppavattīni suvinicchitāni suttaso anubyañjanaso; kalyāṇavāco hoti kalyāṇavākkaraṇo, poriyā vācāya samannāgato vissaṭṭhāya visaṭṭhāya (ka.) anelagaḷāya aneḷagaḷāya (sī. ka.) atthassa viññāpaniyā; paṭibalo hoti [P 4.0280] bhikkhunisaṅghassa dhammiyā kathāya sandassetuṃ samādapetuṃ samuttejetuṃ sampahaṃsetuṃ; yebhuyyena bhikkhunīnaṃ piyo hoti manāpo; na kho panetaṃ bhagavantaṃ uddissa pabbajitāya kāsāyavatthanivasanāya garudhammaṃ ajjhāpannapubbo hoti; vīsativasso vā hoti atirekavīsativasso vā. Imehi kho, ānanda, aṭṭhahi dhammehi samannāgato bhikkhu bhikkhunovādako sammannitabbo”ti. Dutiyaṃ.

“Ānanda, a bhikkhu endowed with eight qualities should be designated as an instructor of bhikkhunīs. What eight? Here, Ānanda, a bhikkhu is virtuous… he trains himself, having undertaken the training rules; he is learned… his view is well-penetrated; both Pātimokkhas are well-mastered by him in detail, well-analyzed, well-established, and well-determined according to the rules and their details; he has a good voice and good delivery, possessing urbane speech that is clear, articulate, and makes the meaning known; he is capable of instructing, establishing, rousing, and gladdening the Saṅgha of bhikkhunīs with a talk on the Dhamma; he is, for the most part, dear and agreeable to the bhikkhunīs; he has never before fallen into a grave offense with regard to a bhikkhunī who has gone forth in dedication to the Blessed One and wears the ochre-dyed robe; he is of twenty years' standing, or more than twenty years' standing. Ānanda, a bhikkhu endowed with these eight qualities should be designated as an instructor of bhikkhunīs.” Second.

3. Saṃkhittasuttaṃ

3. The Concise Discourse

53. cūḷava. 406 Ekaṃ [V 3.0109] samayaṃ bhagavā vesāliyaṃ viharati mahāvane kūṭāgārasālāyaṃ. Atha kho mahāpajāpatī gotamī yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ aṭṭhāsi. Ekamantaṃ ṭhitā kho sā mahāpajāpatī gotamī bhagavantaṃ etadavoca –

53. On one occasion the Blessed One was dwelling at Vesālī, in the Great Wood, at the Hall with the Peaked Roof. Then Mahāpajāpatī Gotamī approached the Blessed One; having approached and paid homage to the Blessed One, she stood to one side. Standing to one side, Mahāpajāpatī Gotamī said this to the Blessed One:

“Sādhu [M 3.0107] me, bhante, bhagavā saṃkhittena dhammaṃ desetu, yamahaṃ bhagavato dhammaṃ sutvā ekā vūpakaṭṭhā appamattā ātāpinī pahitattā vihareyya”nti. “Ye kho tvaṃ, gotami, dhamme jāneyyāsi – ‘ime dhammā sarāgāya saṃvattanti, no virāgāya; saṃyogāya saṃvattanti, no visaṃyogāya; ācayāya saṃvattanti, no apacayāya; mahicchatāya saṃvattanti, no appicchatāya; asantuṭṭhiyā saṃvattanti, no santuṭṭhiyā; saṅgaṇikāya saṃvattanti, no pavivekāya; kosajjāya saṃvattanti, no vīriyārambhāya; dubbharatāya saṃvattanti, no subharatāyā’ti, ekaṃsena, gotami, dhāreyyāsi – ‘neso dhammo, neso vinayo, netaṃ satthusāsana’”nti[T 4.0289].

“Venerable Sir, it would be good if the Blessed One would teach me the Dhamma in brief, so that I, having heard the Dhamma from the Blessed One, might dwell alone, secluded, heedful, ardent, and with a mind resolved on Nibbāna.” “Gotamī, of those things you might know: ‘These things conduce to passion, not to dispassion; they conduce to being yoked, not to being unyoked; they conduce to accumulation, not to diminution; they conduce to great desire, not to fewness of desire; they conduce to discontent, not to contentment; they conduce to association, not to seclusion; they conduce to laziness, not to the arousal of energy; they conduce to being difficult to support, not to being easy to support’—you should hold it definitively, Gotamī: ‘This is not the Dhamma, this is not the Vinaya, this is not the Teacher’s instruction.’”

“Ye ca kho tvaṃ, gotami, dhamme jāneyyāsi – ‘ime dhammā virāgāya saṃvattanti, no sarāgāya; visaṃyogāya saṃvattanti, no saṃyogāya; apacayāya saṃvattanti, no ācayāya; appicchatāya saṃvattanti, no mahicchatāya; santuṭṭhiyā saṃvattanti, no asantuṭṭhiyā; pavivekāya saṃvattanti, no saṅgaṇikāya[P 4.0281]; vīriyārambhāya saṃvattanti, no kosajjāya; subharatāya saṃvattanti, no dubbharatāyā’ti, ekaṃsena, gotami, dhāreyyāsi – ‘eso dhammo, eso vinayo, etaṃ satthusāsana’”nti. Tatiyaṃ.

“And of those things you might know, Gotamī: ‘These things conduce to dispassion, not to passion; they conduce to being unyoked, not to being yoked; they conduce to diminution, not to accumulation; they conduce to fewness of desire, not to great desire; they conduce to contentment, not to discontent; they conduce to seclusion, not to association; they conduce to the arousal of energy, not to laziness; they conduce to being easy to support, not to being difficult to support’—you should hold it definitively, Gotamī: ‘This is the Dhamma, this is the Vinaya, this is the Teacher’s instruction.’” Third.

4. Dīghajāṇusuttaṃ

4. The Dīghajāṇu Discourse

54. Ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā koliyesu viharati kakkarapattaṃ nāma koliyānaṃ nigamo. Atha kho dīghajāṇu koliyaputto yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinno kho dīghajāṇu koliyaputto bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “mayaṃ, bhante, gihī kāmabhogino kāmabhogī (sī. syā. pī.) puttasambādhasayanaṃ ajjhāvasāma, kāsikacandanaṃ paccanubhoma[V 3.0110], mālāgandhavilepanaṃ dhārayāma, jātarūparajataṃ sādayāma. Tesaṃ no, bhante, bhagavā amhākaṃ tathā dhammaṃ desetu ye amhākaṃ assu dhammā diṭṭhadhammahitāya diṭṭhadhammasukhāya, samparāyahitāya samparāyasukhāyā”ti.

54. On one occasion the Blessed One was dwelling among the Koliyans in a market town of the Koliyans named Kakkarapatta. Then the Koliyan son Dīghajāṇu approached the Blessed One; having approached and paid homage to the Blessed One, he sat down to one side. Seated to one side, the Koliyan son Dīghajāṇu said this to the Blessed One: “Venerable Sir, we are laypeople, enjoyers of sensual pleasures; we dwell ruling over a home crowded with children; we use sandalwood from Kāsī; we wear garlands, perfumes, and ointments; we delight in gold and silver. For us such as these, Venerable Sir, may the Blessed One teach the Dhamma in such a way that it will be for our welfare and happiness in this present life, and for our welfare and happiness in the life to come.”

“Cattārome, byagghapajja, dhammā kulaputtassa diṭṭhadhammahitāya saṃvattanti diṭṭhadhammasukhāya. Katame cattāro? Uṭṭhānasampadā, ārakkhasampadā, kalyāṇamittatā, samajīvitā samajīvikatā (sī.) a. ni. 8.75. Katamā [T 4.0290] ca, byagghapajja, uṭṭhānasampadā? Idha, byagghapajja, kulaputto [M 3.0108] yena kammaṭṭhānena jīvikaṃ jīvitaṃ (ka.) kappeti – yadi kasiyā, yadi vaṇijjāya, yadi gorakkhena, yadi issattena issatthena (sī. syā. pī.), yadi rājaporisena, yadi sippaññatarena – tattha dakkho hoti analaso, tatrupāyāya vīmaṃsāya samannāgato, alaṃ kātuṃ alaṃ saṃvidhātuṃ. Ayaṃ vuccati, byagghapajja, uṭṭhānasampadā.

“Byagghapajja, these four things conduce to the welfare and happiness of a clansman in this present life. What four? Accomplishment in exertion, accomplishment in protection, good friendship, and balanced livelihood. And what, Byagghapajja, is accomplishment in exertion? Here, Byagghapajja, a clansman makes his living by whatever occupation—whether by farming, by trade, by cattle-herding, by archery, by service to the king, or by some other craft. In that he is skilled and not lazy, endowed with an investigative wisdom into the means thereto, capable of doing and arranging. This, Byagghapajja, is called accomplishment in exertion.”

“Katamā ca, byagghapajja, ārakkhasampadā? Idha, byagghapajja, kulaputtassa bhogā honti uṭṭhānavīriyādhigatā [P 4.0282] bāhābalaparicitā, sedāvakkhittā, dhammikā dhammaladdhā. Te ārakkhena guttiyā sampādeti – ‘kinti me ime bhoge neva rājāno hareyyuṃ, na corā hareyyuṃ, na aggi ḍaheyya, na udakaṃ vaheyya, na appiyā dāyādā hareyyu’nti! Ayaṃ vuccati, byagghapajja, ārakkhasampadā.

“And what, Byagghapajja, is accomplishment in protection? Here, Byagghapajja, a clansman has wealth acquired by initiative and energy, gathered by the strength of his arms, earned by the dripping of sweat, righteous, and righteously obtained. He furnishes it with protection and security, thinking: ‘How might it be that neither kings nor thieves might take this wealth of mine, nor fire burn it, nor water carry it away, nor unloved heirs take it?’ This, Byagghapajja, is called accomplishment in protection.”

“Katamā ca, byagghapajja, kalyāṇamittatā? Idha, byagghapajja, kulaputto yasmiṃ gāme vā nigame vā paṭivasati, tattha ye te honti – gahapatī vā gahapatiputtā vā daharā vā vuddhasīlino, vuddhā vā vuddhasīlino, saddhāsampannā, sīlasampannā, cāgasampannā, paññāsampannā – tehi saddhiṃ santiṭṭhati sallapati sākacchaṃ samāpajjati; yathārūpānaṃ saddhāsampannānaṃ saddhāsampadaṃ anusikkhati, yathārūpānaṃ sīlasampannānaṃ sīlasampadaṃ anusikkhati, yathārūpānaṃ cāgasampannānaṃ cāgasampadaṃ anusikkhati, yathārūpānaṃ paññāsampannānaṃ paññāsampadaṃ anusikkhati. Ayaṃ vuccati, byagghapajja, kalyāṇamittatā.

“And what, Byagghapajja, is good friendship? Here, Byagghapajja, in whatever village or market town a clansman dwells, he associates with, converses with, and engages in discussion with those who are householders or sons of householders, whether young and mature in virtue, or old and mature in virtue, who are accomplished in faith, accomplished in virtue, accomplished in generosity, and accomplished in wisdom. He trains in emulation of the accomplishment in faith of those accomplished in faith; he trains in emulation of the accomplishment in virtue of those accomplished in virtue; he trains in emulation of the accomplishment in generosity of those accomplished in generosity; he trains in emulation of the accomplishment in wisdom of those accomplished in wisdom. This, Byagghapajja, is called good friendship.”

“Katamā [T 4.0291] ca, byagghapajja, samajīvitā? Idha, byagghapajja, kulaputto āyañca bhogānaṃ viditvā, vayañca bhogānaṃ viditvā, samaṃ jīvikaṃ samajīvikaṃ (syā.), samajīvitaṃ (ka.) kappeti nāccogāḷhaṃ nātihīnaṃ – ‘evaṃ me āyo vayaṃ pariyādāya ṭhassati, na ca me vayo āyaṃ pariyādāya ṭhassatī’ti. Seyyathāpi[V 3.0111], byagghapajja, tulādhāro vā tulādhārantevāsī vā tulaṃ paggahetvā jānāti – ‘ettakena vā onataṃ oṇataṃ (ka.), ettakena vā unnata’nti uṇṇatanti (ka.); evamevaṃ kho, byagghapajja, kulaputto āyañca bhogānaṃ viditvā, vayañca bhogānaṃ viditvā, samaṃ jīvikaṃ kappeti nāccogāḷhaṃ nātihīnaṃ – ‘evaṃ me āyo vayaṃ pariyādāya ṭhassati[P 4.0283], na ca me vayo āyaṃ pariyādāya ṭhassatī’ti. Sacāyaṃ, byagghapajja, kulaputto appāyo [M 3.0109] samāno uḷāraṃ jīvikaṃ jīvitaṃ (ka.) kappeti, tassa bhavanti vattāro – ‘udumbarakhādīvāyaṃ udumbarakhādikaṃ vāyaṃ (sī. pī.), udumbarakhādakaṃ cāyaṃ (syā.) kulaputto bhoge khādatī’ti. Sace panāyaṃ, byagghapajja, kulaputto mahāyo samāno kasiraṃ jīvikaṃ jīvitaṃ (ka.) kappeti, tassa bhavanti vattāro – ‘ajeṭṭhamaraṇaṃvāyaṃ ajaddhumārikaṃ vāyaṃ (sī. pī.), addhamārakaṃ cāyaṃ (syā.), ettha jaddhūti asanaṃ = bhattabhuñjanaṃ, tasmā ajaddhumārikanti anasanamaraṇanti vuttaṃ hoti. ma. ni. 1.379 adholipiyā “ajaddhuka”nti padaṃ dassitaṃ kulaputto marissatī’ti. Yato ca khoyaṃ, byagghapajja, kulaputto āyañca bhogānaṃ viditvā, vayañca bhogānaṃ viditvā, samaṃ jīvikaṃ kappeti nāccogāḷhaṃ nātihīnaṃ – ‘evaṃ me āyo vayaṃ pariyādāya ṭhassati, na ca me vayo āyaṃ pariyādāya ṭhassatī’ti. Ayaṃ vuccati, byagghapajja, samajīvitā.

“And what, Byagghapajja, is a balanced livelihood? Here, Byagghapajja, a clansman, knowing his income and expenditures, lives a balanced life, neither extravagant nor miserly, thinking: ‘Thus my income will exceed my expenditures, and my expenditures will not exceed my income.’ Just as, Byagghapajja, a scale-holder or his apprentice, holding up the scales, knows: ‘It has dipped down by so much, or it has lifted up by so much’; so too, Byagghapajja, a clansman, knowing his income and expenditures, lives a balanced life, neither extravagant nor miserly, thinking: ‘Thus my income will exceed my expenditures, and my expenditures will not exceed my income.’ If, Byagghapajja, a clansman with little income lives extravagantly, people will say of him: ‘This clansman consumes his wealth like one who eats udumbara fruit.’ But if, Byagghapajja, a clansman with great income lives a meager life, people will say of him: ‘This clansman will die a pauper's death.’ But when, Byagghapajja, a clansman, knowing his income and expenditures, lives a balanced life, neither extravagant nor miserly, thinking: ‘Thus my income will exceed my expenditures, and my expenditures will not exceed my income,’ this, Byagghapajja, is called a balanced livelihood.”

“Evaṃ samuppannānaṃ, byagghapajja, bhogānaṃ cattāri apāyamukhāni honti – itthidhutto, surādhutto, akkhadhutto, pāpamitto pāpasahāyo pāpasampavaṅko. Seyyathāpi, byagghapajja, mahato taḷākassa cattāri ceva [T 4.0292] āyamukhāni, cattāri ca apāyamukhāni. Tassa puriso yāni ceva āyamukhāni tāni pidaheyya, yāni ca apāyamukhāni tāni vivareyya; devo ca na sammā dhāraṃ anuppaveccheyya. Evañhi tassa, byagghapajja, mahato taḷākassa parihāniyeva pāṭikaṅkhā, no vuddhi; evamevaṃ, byagghapajja, evaṃ samuppannānaṃ bhogānaṃ cattāri apāyamukhāni honti – itthidhutto, surādhutto, akkhadhutto, pāpamitto pāpasahāyo pāpasampavaṅko.

“For wealth thus arisen, Byagghapajja, there are four avenues of dissipation: womanizing, drunkenness, gambling, and having bad friends, bad companions, and bad associates. Just as, Byagghapajja, for a great reservoir that has four inlets and four outlets, if a man were to close the inlets and open the outlets, and the sky were not to send down sufficient rain, then indeed, Byagghapajja, for that great reservoir only a decrease is to be expected, not an increase; just so, Byagghapajja, for wealth thus arisen, there are four avenues of dissipation: womanizing, drunkenness, gambling, and having bad friends, bad companions, and bad associates.”

“Evaṃ samuppannānaṃ, byagghapajja, bhogānaṃ cattāri āyamukhāni honti – na itthidhutto, na surādhutto, na akkhadhutto[P 4.0284], kalyāṇamitto kalyāṇasahāyo kalyāṇasampavaṅko. Seyyathāpi, byagghapajja, mahato taḷākassa cattāri ceva āyamukhāni, cattāri ca apāyamukhāni. Tassa puriso yāni ceva āyamukhāni tāni vivareyya, yāni ca apāyamukhāni tāni pidaheyya; devo ca sammā dhāraṃ anuppaveccheyya. Evañhi tassa, byagghapajja, mahato taḷākassa vuddhiyeva pāṭikaṅkhā, no parihāni; evamevaṃ kho, byagghapajja, evaṃ samuppannānaṃ bhogānaṃ cattāri āyamukhāni honti – na itthidhutto[M 3.0110], na surādhutto, na akkhadhutto, kalyāṇamitto kalyāṇasahāyo [V 3.0112] kalyāṇasampavaṅko. Ime kho, byagghapajja, cattāro dhammā kulaputtassa diṭṭhadhammahitāya saṃvattanti diṭṭhadhammasukhāya.

“For wealth thus arisen, Byagghapajja, there are four avenues of increase: not being a womanizer, not being a drunkard, not being a gambler, and having good friends, good companions, and good associates. Just as, Byagghapajja, for a great reservoir that has four inlets and four outlets, if a man were to open the inlets and close the outlets, and the sky were to send down sufficient rain, then indeed, Byagghapajja, for that great reservoir an increase is to be expected, not a decrease. Even so, Byagghapajja, for wealth that has thus arisen, there are four avenues of increase: not being a womanizer, not being a drunkard, not being a gambler, and having good friends, good companions, and good associates. These four qualities, Byagghapajja, conduce to a clansman’s welfare and happiness in this very life.”

“Cattārome, byagghapajja, dhammā kulaputtassa samparāyahitāya saṃvattanti samparāyasukhāya. Katame cattāro? Saddhāsampadā, sīlasampadā[T 4.0293], cāgasampadā, paññāsampadā. Katamā ca, byagghapajja, saddhāsampadā? Idha, byagghapajja, kulaputto saddho hoti, saddahati tathāgatassa bodhiṃ – ‘itipi so bhagavā…pe… satthā devamanussānaṃ buddho bhagavā’ti. Ayaṃ vuccati, byagghapajja, saddhāsampadā.

“These four qualities, Byagghapajja, conduce to a clansman’s welfare and happiness in the life to come. What four? Accomplishment in faith, accomplishment in virtue, accomplishment in generosity, and accomplishment in wisdom. And what, Byagghapajja, is accomplishment in faith? Here, Byagghapajja, a clansman is endowed with faith; he places faith in the Tathāgata’s enlightenment, thus: ‘Indeed, the Blessed One is… the Teacher of gods and humans, the Buddha, the Blessed One.’ This, Byagghapajja, is called accomplishment in faith.”

“Katamā ca, byagghapajja, sīlasampadā? Idha, byagghapajja, kulaputto pāṇātipātā paṭivirato hoti…pe… surāmerayamajjapamādaṭṭhānā paṭivirato hoti. Ayaṃ vuccati, byagghapajja, sīlasampadā.

“And what, Byagghapajja, is accomplishment in virtue? Here, Byagghapajja, a clansman abstains from the destruction of life… he abstains from fermented and distilled liquors, which are the basis for heedlessness. This, Byagghapajja, is called accomplishment in virtue.”

“Katamā ca, byagghapajja, cāgasampadā? Idha, byagghapajja, kulaputto vigatamalamaccherena cetasā agāraṃ ajjhāvasati muttacāgo payatapāṇi vossaggarato yācayogo dānasaṃvibhāgarato. Ayaṃ vuccati, byagghapajja, cāgasampadā.

“And what, Byagghapajja, is accomplishment in generosity? Here, Byagghapajja, a clansman dwells at home with a mind rid of the stain of stinginess, freely generous, open-handed, delighting in relinquishment, responsive to requests, delighting in giving and sharing. This, Byagghapajja, is called accomplishment in generosity.”

“Katamā ca, byagghapajja, paññāsampadā? Idha[P 4.0285], byagghapajja, kulaputto paññavā hoti, udayatthagāminiyā paññāya samannāgato ariyāya nibbedhikāya sammā dukkhakkhayagāminiyā. Ayaṃ vuccati, byagghapajja, paññāsampadā. Ime kho, byagghapajja, cattāro dhammā kulaputtassa samparāyahitāya saṃvattanti samparāyasukhāyā”ti.

“And what, Byagghapajja, is accomplishment in wisdom? Here, Byagghapajja, a clansman is wise, endowed with wisdom that discerns arising and passing away, which is noble and penetrative, leading to the complete destruction of suffering. This, Byagghapajja, is called accomplishment in wisdom. These four qualities, Byagghapajja, conduce to a clansman’s welfare and happiness in the life to come.”

“Uṭṭhātā kammadheyyesu, appamatto vidhānavā;

Samaṃ kappeti jīvikaṃ jīvitaṃ (ka.), sambhataṃ anurakkhati.

“Energetic in his undertakings, heedful and methodical, he maintains a balanced livelihood and guards what he has acquired.

“Saddho sīlena sampanno, vadaññū vītamaccharo;

Niccaṃ [T 4.0294] maggaṃ visodheti, sotthānaṃ samparāyikaṃ.

“Endowed with faith and virtue, generous, free from stinginess, he constantly purifies the path to well-being in the life to come.

“Iccete [V 3.0113] aṭṭha dhammā ca, saddhassa gharamesino;

Akkhātā saccanāmena, ubhayattha sukhāvahā.

“These eight qualities, indeed, for a faithful householder, declared by the one truly named, bring happiness in both respects.

“Diṭṭhadhammahitatthāya, samparāyasukhāya ca;

Evametaṃ gahaṭṭhānaṃ, cāgo puññaṃ pavaḍḍhatī”ti. catutthaṃ;

“For welfare in this very life, and for happiness in the life to come, thus for householders, generosity and merit increase.”

5. Ujjayasuttaṃ

5. The Ujjaya Sutta

55. Atha [M 3.0111] kho ujjayo brāhmaṇo yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavatā saddhiṃ sammodi. Sammodanīyaṃ kathaṃ sāraṇīyaṃ vītisāretvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinno kho ujjayo brāhmaṇo bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “mayaṃ, bho gotama, pavāsaṃ gantukāmā. Tesaṃ no bhavaṃ gotamo amhākaṃ tathā dhammaṃ desetu – ye amhākaṃ assu dhammā diṭṭhadhammahitāya, diṭṭhadhammasukhāya, samparāyahitāya, samparāyasukhāyā”ti.

55. Then the brahmin Ujjaya approached the Blessed One. Having approached, he exchanged greetings with the Blessed One. When they had concluded their courteous and memorable talk, he sat down to one side. Sitting to one side, the brahmin Ujjaya said to the Blessed One: “We, Master Gotama, desire to go abroad. May Master Gotama teach us the Dhamma in such a way that those qualities will be for our welfare and happiness in this very life, and for our welfare and happiness in the life to come.”

“Cattārome, brāhmaṇa, dhammā kulaputtassa diṭṭhadhammahitāya saṃvattanti, diṭṭhadhammasukhāya. Katame cattāro? Uṭṭhānasampadā[P 4.0286], ārakkhasampadā, kalyāṇamittatā, samajīvitā. Katamā ca, brāhmaṇa, uṭṭhānasampadā? Idha, brāhmaṇa, kulaputto yena kammaṭṭhānena jīvikaṃ kappeti – yadi kasiyā, yadi vaṇijjāya, yadi gorakkhena, yadi issattena, yadi rājaporisena, yadi sippaññatarena – tattha dakkho hoti analaso, tatrupāyāya vīmaṃsāya samannāgato, alaṃ kātuṃ alaṃ saṃvidhātuṃ. Ayaṃ vuccati, brāhmaṇa, uṭṭhānasampadā.

“These four qualities, brahmin, conduce to a clansman’s welfare and happiness in this very life. What four? Accomplishment in diligence, accomplishment in protection, good friendship, and balanced living. And what, brahmin, is accomplishment in diligence? Here, brahmin, a clansman earns his living by whatever occupation—whether by farming, by trade, by cattle-rearing, by archery, by royal service, or by some other craft. In that, he is skilled and not lazy, endowed with investigative wisdom into the means for it, able to do it and able to get it done. This, brahmin, is called accomplishment in diligence.”

“Katamā ca, brāhmaṇa, ārakkhasampadā? Idha, brāhmaṇa, kulaputtassa [T 4.0295] bhogā honti uṭṭhānavīriyādhigatā, bāhābalaparicitā, sedāvakkhittā, dhammikā dhammaladdhā. Te ārakkhena guttiyā sampādeti – ‘kinti me ime bhoge neva rājāno hareyyuṃ, na corā hareyyuṃ, na aggi ḍaheyya, na udakaṃ vaheyya, na appiyā dāyādā hareyyu’nti. Ayaṃ vuccati, brāhmaṇa, ārakkhasampadā.

“And what, brahmin, is accomplishment in protection? Here, brahmin, a clansman has wealth acquired by diligence and effort, amassed by the strength of his arms, earned by the sweat of his brow, which is righteous and righteously obtained. He furnishes it with protection and security, thinking: ‘How might it be that kings would not take away this wealth of mine, nor thieves take it away, nor fire burn it, nor water sweep it away, nor unloved heirs take it away?’ This, brahmin, is called accomplishment in protection.”

“Katamā ca, brāhmaṇa, kalyāṇamittatā? Idha, brāhmaṇa, kulaputto yasmiṃ gāme vā nigame [V 3.0114] vā paṭivasati tatra ye te honti – gahapatī vā gahapatiputtā vā daharā vā vuddhasīlino, vuddhā vā vuddhasīlino, saddhāsampannā, sīlasampannā, cāgasampannā, paññāsampannā – tehi saddhiṃ santiṭṭhati sallapati sākacchaṃ samāpajjati; yathārūpānaṃ saddhāsampannānaṃ saddhāsampadaṃ anusikkhati, yathārūpānaṃ sīlasampannānaṃ sīlasampadaṃ anusikkhati, yathārūpānaṃ cāgasampannānaṃ cāgasampadaṃ anusikkhati, yathārūpānaṃ paññāsampannānaṃ paññāsampadaṃ anusikkhati. Ayaṃ vuccati, brāhmaṇa, kalyāṇamittatā.

“And what, brahmin, is good friendship? Here, brahmin, a clansman dwells in whatever village or town. There, those householders or householders’ sons, whether young but mature in virtue or old and mature in virtue, who are accomplished in faith, accomplished in virtue, accomplished in generosity, and accomplished in wisdom—with them he associates, converses, and engages in discussion. He trains in emulation of the accomplishment in faith of those accomplished in faith; he trains in emulation of the accomplishment in virtue of those accomplished in virtue; he trains in emulation of the accomplishment in generosity of those accomplished in generosity; he trains in emulation of the accomplishment in wisdom of those accomplished in wisdom. This, brahmin, is called good friendship.”

“Katamā [M 3.0112] ca, brāhmaṇa, samajīvitā? Idha, brāhmaṇa, kulaputto āyañca bhogānaṃ viditvā vayañca bhogānaṃ viditvā samaṃ jīvikaṃ kappeti nāccogāḷhaṃ [P 4.0287] nātihīnaṃ – ‘evaṃ me āyo vayaṃ pariyādāya ṭhassati, na ca me vayo āyaṃ pariyādāya ṭhassatī’ti. Seyyathāpi, brāhmaṇa, tulādhāro vā tulādhārantevāsī vā tulaṃ paggahetvā jānāti – ‘ettakena vā onataṃ, ettakena vā unnata’nti; evamevaṃ kho, brāhmaṇa, kulaputto āyañca [T 4.0296] bhogānaṃ viditvā vayañca bhogānaṃ viditvā samaṃ jīvikaṃ kappeti nāccogāḷhaṃ nātihīnaṃ – ‘evaṃ me āyo vayaṃ pariyādāya ṭhassati, na ca me vayo āyaṃ pariyādāya ṭhassatī’ti. Sacāyaṃ, brāhmaṇa, kulaputto appāyo samāno uḷāraṃ jīvikaṃ kappeti, tassa bhavanti vattāro – ‘udumbarakhādīvāyaṃ kulaputto bhoge khādatī’ti. Sace panāyaṃ, brāhmaṇa, kulaputto mahāyo samāno kasiraṃ jīvikaṃ kappeti, tassa bhavanti vattāro – ‘ajeṭṭhamaraṇaṃvāyaṃ kulaputto marissatī’ti. Yato ca khoyaṃ, brāhmaṇa, kulaputto āyañca bhogānaṃ viditvā vayañca bhogānaṃ viditvā samaṃ jīvikaṃ kappeti nāccogāḷhaṃ nātihīnaṃ – ‘evaṃ me āyo vayaṃ pariyādāya ṭhassati, na ca me vayo āyaṃ pariyādāya ṭhassatī’ti, ayaṃ vuccati, brāhmaṇa, samajīvitā.

“And what, brahmin, is balanced living? Here, brahmin, a clansman, knowing his income and expenditure, leads a balanced life, neither too extravagant nor too meager, thinking: ‘In this way, my income will exceed my expenditure, and my expenditure will not exceed my income.’ Just as, brahmin, a weigher or his apprentice, holding up the scales, knows: ‘By this much it is lowered, by this much it is raised’; even so, brahmin, a clansman, knowing his income and expenditure, leads a balanced life, neither too extravagant nor too meager, thinking: ‘In this way, my income will exceed my expenditure, and my expenditure will not exceed my income.’ If, brahmin, this clansman, having a small income, leads an extravagant life, people will say of him: ‘This clansman consumes his wealth like a fig-eater.’ If, however, this clansman, having a large income, leads a wretched life, people will say of him: ‘This clansman will die a wretched death.’ But when, brahmin, a clansman, knowing his income and expenditure, leads a balanced life, neither too extravagant nor too meager, thinking: ‘In this way, my income will exceed my expenditure, and my expenditure will not exceed my income,’ this, brahmin, is called balanced living.”

“Evaṃ samuppannānaṃ, brāhmaṇa, bhogānaṃ cattāri apāyamukhāni honti – itthidhutto, surādhutto, akkhadhutto, pāpamitto pāpasahāyo pāpasampavaṅko. Seyyathāpi, brāhmaṇa, mahato taḷākassa cattāri ceva āyamukhāni, cattāri ca apāyamukhāni. Tassa puriso yāni ceva āyamukhāni tāni pidaheyya, yāni ca apāyamukhāni tāni vivareyya; devo ca na sammā dhāraṃ anuppaveccheyya. Evañhi tassa brāhmaṇa[P 4.0288], mahato taḷākassa parihāniyeva pāṭikaṅkhā, no vuddhi[V 3.0115]; evamevaṃ kho, brāhmaṇa, evaṃ samuppannānaṃ bhogānaṃ cattāri apāyamukhāni honti – itthidhutto, surādhutto, akkhadhutto, pāpamitto pāpasahāyo pāpasampavaṅko.

“For wealth that has thus arisen, brahmin, there are four avenues of ruin: womanizing, drunkenness, gambling, and having evil friends, evil companions, and evil associates. Just as, brahmin, for a great reservoir there might be four inlets and four outlets, and a person were to close the inlets and open the outlets, and the heavens were not to send down sufficient rain; for that great reservoir, brahmin, only a decline is to be expected, not an increase. In the same way, brahmin, for wealth that has thus arisen, there are four avenues of ruin: womanizing, drunkenness, gambling, and having evil friends, evil companions, and evil associates.”

“Evaṃ [T 4.0297] samuppannānaṃ, brāhmaṇa, bhogānaṃ cattāri āyamukhāni honti – na itthidhutto, na surādhutto, na akkhadhutto, kalyāṇamitto kalyāṇasahāyo kalyāṇasampavaṅko. Seyyathāpi, brāhmaṇa, mahato taḷākassa cattāri ceva āyamukhāni cattāri ca apāyamukhāni. Tassa puriso yāni ceva āyamukhāni tāni vivareyya, yāni ca apāyamukhāni [M 3.0113] tāni pidaheyya; devo ca sammā dhāraṃ anuppaveccheyya. Evañhi tassa, brāhmaṇa, mahato taḷākassa vuddhiyeva pāṭikaṅkhā, no parihāni; evamevaṃ kho, brāhmaṇa, evaṃ samuppannānaṃ bhogānaṃ cattāri āyamukhāni honti – na itthidhutto…pe… kalyāṇasampavaṅko. Ime kho, brāhmaṇa, cattāro dhammā kulaputtassa diṭṭhadhammahitāya saṃvattanti diṭṭhadhammasukhāya.

“For wealth that has arisen in this way, brahmin, there are four avenues of increase: not being a womanizer, not being a drunkard, not being a gambler, and having good friends, good companions, and good associates. Just as, brahmin, for a great reservoir there might be four inlets and four outlets, and a person were to open the inlets and close the outlets, and the heavens were to send down rain properly; for that great reservoir, brahmin, only growth is to be expected, not decline. In the same way, brahmin, for wealth that has arisen in this way, there are four avenues of increase: not being a womanizer… and so on… having good associates. These four qualities, brahmin, conduce to a clansman’s welfare and happiness in this present life.”

“Cattārome, brāhmaṇa, kulaputtassa dhammā samparāyahitāya saṃvattanti samparāyasukhāya. Katame cattāro? Saddhāsampadā, sīlasampadā, cāgasampadā, paññāsampadā. Katamā ca, brāhmaṇa, saddhāsampadā? Idha, brāhmaṇa, kulaputto saddho hoti, saddahati tathāgatassa bodhiṃ – ‘itipi so bhagavā…pe… satthā devamanussānaṃ buddho bhagavā’ti. Ayaṃ vuccati, brāhmaṇa, saddhāsampadā.

“These four qualities, brahmin, conduce to a clansman’s welfare and happiness in the life to come. What four? Accomplishment in faith, accomplishment in virtue, accomplishment in generosity, and accomplishment in wisdom. And what, brahmin, is accomplishment in faith? Here, brahmin, a clansman is faithful; he places faith in the Tathāgata’s enlightenment: ‘Thus indeed is the Blessed One… teacher of gods and humans, the Buddha, the Blessed One.’ This, brahmin, is called accomplishment in faith.”

“Katamā ca, brāhmaṇa, sīlasampadā? Idha, brāhmaṇa, kulaputto pāṇātipātā paṭivirato hoti…pe… surāmerayamajjapamādaṭṭhānā paṭivirato hoti. Ayaṃ vuccati, brāhmaṇa, sīlasampadā.

“And what, brahmin, is accomplishment in virtue? Here, brahmin, a clansman abstains from the destruction of life… and so on… he abstains from liquor, wine, and intoxicants, which are a cause for heedlessness. This, brahmin, is called accomplishment in virtue.”

“Katamā [T 4.0298] ca, brāhmaṇa, cāgasampadā? Idha[P 4.0289], brāhmaṇa, kulaputto vigatamalamaccherena cetasā agāraṃ ajjhāvasati muttacāgo payatapāṇi vossaggarato yācayogo dānasaṃvibhāgarato. Ayaṃ vuccati, brāhmaṇa, cāgasampadā.

“And what, brahmin, is accomplishment in generosity? Here, brahmin, a clansman dwells at home with a mind devoid of the stain of stinginess, freely generous, open-handed, delighting in relinquishing, one who can be asked, delighting in giving and sharing. This, brahmin, is called accomplishment in generosity.”

“Katamā ca, brāhmaṇa, paññāsampadā? Idha, brāhmaṇa, kulaputto paññavā hoti…pe… sammā [V 3.0116] dukkhakkhayagāminiyā. Ayaṃ vuccati, brāhmaṇa, paññāsampadā. Ime kho, brāhmaṇa, cattāro dhammā kulaputtassa samparāyahitāya saṃvattanti samparāyasukhāyā”ti.

“And what, brahmin, is accomplishment in wisdom? Here, brahmin, a clansman is wise… and so on… endowed with wisdom that leads rightly to the complete destruction of suffering. This, brahmin, is called accomplishment in wisdom. These four qualities, brahmin, conduce to a clansman’s welfare and happiness in the life to come.”

“Uṭṭhātā kammadheyyesu, appamatto vidhānavā;

Samaṃ kappeti jīvikaṃ, sambhataṃ anurakkhati.

“Energetic in his undertakings, heedful and well-organized, he maintains a balanced livelihood and guards his accumulated wealth.

“Saddho sīlena sampanno, vadaññū vītamaccharo;

Niccaṃ maggaṃ visodheti, sotthānaṃ samparāyikaṃ.

“Faithful and accomplished in virtue, generous and free from stinginess, he ever purifies the path to welfare in the life to come.

“Iccete aṭṭha dhammā ca, saddhassa gharamesino;

Akkhātā saccanāmena, ubhayattha sukhāvahā.

“Thus these eight qualities of a faithful householder have been declared by the one whose name is Truth; they bring happiness in both worlds.

“Diṭṭhadhammahitatthāya, samparāyasukhāya ca;

Evametaṃ gahaṭṭhānaṃ, cāgo puññaṃ pavaḍḍhatī”ti. pañcamaṃ;

“For welfare in this present life and for happiness in the life to come—thus for householders, generosity and merit increase.” The fifth sutta.

6. Bhayasuttaṃ

6. The Discourse on Fear

56. “‘Bhaya’nti [M 3.0114] cūḷani. khaggavisāṇasuttaniddesa 137, bhikkhave, kāmānametaṃ adhivacanaṃ. ‘Dukkha’nti, bhikkhave, kāmānametaṃ adhivacanaṃ. ‘Rogo’ti, bhikkhave, kāmānametaṃ adhivacanaṃ. ‘Gaṇḍo’ti, bhikkhave, kāmānametaṃ adhivacanaṃ. ‘Salla’nti, bhikkhave, kāmānametaṃ adhivacanaṃ. ‘Saṅgo’ti, bhikkhave, kāmānametaṃ adhivacanaṃ. ‘Paṅko’ti, bhikkhave, kāmānametaṃ adhivacanaṃ[T 4.0299]. ‘Gabbho’ti, bhikkhave, kāmānametaṃ adhivacanaṃ. Kasmā ca, bhikkhave, ‘bhaya’nti kāmānametaṃ adhivacanaṃ? Yasmā ca kāmarāgarattāyaṃ, bhikkhave, chandarāgavinibaddho diṭṭhadhammikāpi bhayā na parimuccati, samparāyikāpi [P 4.0290] bhayā na parimuccati, tasmā ‘bhaya’nti kāmānametaṃ adhivacanaṃ. Kasmā ca, bhikkhave, ‘dukkha’nti…pe… ‘rogo’ti… ‘gaṇḍo’ti… ‘salla’nti… ‘saṅgo’ti… ‘paṅko’ti… ‘gabbho’ti kāmānametaṃ adhivacanaṃ? Yasmā ca kāmarāgarattāyaṃ, bhikkhave, chandarāgavinibaddho diṭṭhadhammikāpi gabbhā na parimuccati, samparāyikāpi gabbhā na parimuccati, tasmā ‘gabbho’ti kāmānametaṃ adhivacanaṃ”.

56. “‘Fear,’ Bhikkhus, is a designation for sensual pleasures. ‘Suffering,’ Bhikkhus, is a designation for sensual pleasures. ‘Disease,’ Bhikkhus, is a designation for sensual pleasures. ‘Tumor,’ Bhikkhus, is a designation for sensual pleasures. ‘Dart,’ Bhikkhus, is a designation for sensual pleasures. ‘Attachment,’ Bhikkhus, is a designation for sensual pleasures. ‘Mire,’ Bhikkhus, is a designation for sensual pleasures. ‘Womb,’ Bhikkhus, is a designation for sensual pleasures. And why, Bhikkhus, is ‘fear’ a designation for sensual pleasures? Because, Bhikkhus, this being, who is impassioned with sensual lust, bound by desire and lust, is not freed from fear in the present life, nor is he freed from fear in the future life; therefore, ‘fear’ is a designation for sensual pleasures. And why, Bhikkhus, is ‘suffering’…pe… ‘disease’… ‘tumor’… ‘dart’… ‘attachment’… ‘mire’… ‘womb’ a designation for sensual pleasures? Because, Bhikkhus, this being, who is impassioned with sensual lust, bound by desire and lust, is not freed from the womb in the present life, nor is he freed from the womb in the future life; therefore, ‘womb’ is a designation for sensual pleasures.”

“Bhayaṃ [V 3.0117] dukkhañca rogo ca, gaṇḍo sallañca saṅgo ca;

Paṅko gabbho ca ubhayaṃ, ete kāmā pavuccanti;

Yattha satto puthujjano.

“Fear, suffering, and disease, a tumor, a dart, and attachment, mire and the womb—these are called sensual pleasures, wherein the ordinary person is attached.

“Otiṇṇo sātarūpena, puna gabbhāya gacchati;

Yato ca bhikkhu ātāpī, sampajaññaṃ sampajañño (syā. ka.) saṃ. ni. 4.251 passitabbaṃ na riccati.

“Overwhelmed by its agreeable aspect, one goes again to the womb. But when a bhikkhu is ardent and does not abandon clear comprehension,

“So imaṃ palipathaṃ duggaṃ, atikkamma tathāvidho;

Pajaṃ jātijarūpetaṃ, phandamānaṃ avekkhatī”ti. chaṭṭhaṃ;

“then such a one, having crossed over this mire, this difficult path, beholds this generation, endowed with birth and old age, trembling.” Sixth.

7. Paṭhamaāhuneyyasuttaṃ

7. The First Discourse on Those Worthy of Offerings

57. “Aṭṭhahi, bhikkhave, dhammehi samannāgato bhikkhu āhuneyyo hoti pāhuneyyo dakkhiṇeyyo añjalikaraṇīyo anuttaraṃ puññakkhettaṃ lokassa. Katamehi aṭṭhahi? Idha, bhikkhave, bhikkhu sīlavā hoti [T 4.0300] …pe… samādāya sikkhati sikkhāpadesu; bahussuto hoti…pe… diṭṭhiyā suppaṭividdhā; kalyāṇamitto hoti kalyāṇasahāyo kalyāṇasampavaṅko[M 3.0115]; sammādiṭṭhiko hoti, sammādassanena [P 4.0291] samannāgato; catunnaṃ jhānānaṃ ābhicetasikānaṃ diṭṭhadhammasukhavihārānaṃ nikāmalābhī hoti akicchalābhī akasiralābhī; anekavihitaṃ pubbenivāsaṃ anussarati, seyyathidaṃ – ekampi jātiṃ dvepi jātiyo…pe… iti sākāraṃ sauddesaṃ anekavihitaṃ pubbenivāsaṃ anussarati; dibbena cakkhunā visuddhena atikkantamānusakena…pe… yathākammūpage satte pajānāti; āsavānaṃ khayā…pe… sacchikatvā upasampajja viharati. Imehi kho, bhikkhave, aṭṭhahi dhammehi samannāgato bhikkhu āhuneyyo hoti…pe… anuttaraṃ puññakkhettaṃ lokassā”ti. Sattamaṃ.

57. “Bhikkhus, a bhikkhu endowed with eight qualities is worthy of offerings, worthy of hospitality, worthy of gifts, and worthy of reverential salutation—an unsurpassed field of merit for the world. What eight? Here, bhikkhus, a bhikkhu is virtuous…pe… he trains in the precepts, undertaking them; he is learned…pe… has penetrated well with wisdom; he has good friends, good companions, and is devoted to good friends; he possesses right view and is endowed with right vision; he attains at will, without difficulty, without hardship, the four jhānas that are of the higher mind, pleasant abidings here and now; he recollects his manifold past lives, that is—one birth, two births…pe… thus with their aspects and details he recollects his manifold past lives; with the divine eye, purified and surpassing the human…pe… he understands beings passing on according to their kamma; with the destruction of the taints…pe… he realizes it for himself with direct knowledge, and having entered upon it, he dwells in it. Bhikkhus, a bhikkhu endowed with these eight qualities is worthy of offerings…pe… an unsurpassed field of merit for the world.” Seventh.

8. Dutiyaāhuneyyasuttaṃ

8. The Second Discourse on Those Worthy of Offerings

58. “Aṭṭhahi, bhikkhave, dhammehi samannāgato bhikkhu āhuneyyo hoti…pe… anuttaraṃ puññakkhettaṃ lokassa. Katamehi aṭṭhahi? Idha, bhikkhave, bhikkhu sīlavā hoti [V 3.0118] …pe… samādāya sikkhati sikkhāpadesu; bahussuto hoti…pe… diṭṭhiyā suppaṭividdhā; āraddhavīriyo viharati thāmavā daḷhaparakkamo anikkhittadhuro kusalesu dhammesu; āraññiko hoti pantasenāsano; aratiratisaho hoti, uppannaṃ aratiṃ abhibhuyya abhibhuyya viharati; bhayabheravasaho hoti, uppannaṃ bhayabheravaṃ abhibhuyya abhibhuyya viharati[P 4.0292]; catunnaṃ jhānānaṃ ābhicetasikānaṃ diṭṭhadhammasukhavihārānaṃ nikāmalābhī hoti akicchalābhī akasiralābhī; āsavānaṃ khayā…pe… sacchikatvā [T 4.0301] upasampajja viharati. Imehi kho, bhikkhave, aṭṭhahi dhammehi samannāgato bhikkhu āhuneyyo…pe… anuttaraṃ puññakkhettaṃ lokassā”ti. Aṭṭhamaṃ.

58. “Bhikkhus, a bhikkhu endowed with eight qualities is worthy of offerings…pe… an unsurpassed field of merit for the world. With which eight? Here, bhikkhus, a bhikkhu is virtuous…pe… having undertaken them, he trains in the training rules; he is learned…pe… and has well-penetrated the truths with wisdom; he dwells with energy aroused, possessing strength, firm in exertion, not having laid down the burden regarding wholesome qualities; he is a forest-dweller, one who resorts to remote lodgings; he endures discontent and delight, and dwells having repeatedly overcome arisen discontent; he endures fear and dread, and dwells having repeatedly overcome arisen fear and dread; he is an obtainer at will, without difficulty, without trouble, of the four jhānas which pertain to the higher mind and are pleasant dwellings in this very life; through the destruction of the taints…pe… he realizes it for himself with direct knowledge, and having entered upon it, he dwells in it. Bhikkhus, a bhikkhu endowed with these eight qualities is worthy of offerings…pe… an unsurpassed field of merit for the world.” Eighth.

9. Paṭhamapuggalasuttaṃ

9. The First Discourse on Individuals

59. “Aṭṭhime bhikkhave, puggalā āhuneyyā pāhuneyyā dakkhiṇeyyā añjalikaraṇīyā anuttaraṃ puññakkhettaṃ lokassa? Katame aṭṭha? Sotāpanno, sotāpattiphalasacchikiriyāya paṭipanno, sakadāgāmī, sakadāgāmiphalasacchikiriyāya paṭipanno, anāgāmī, anāgāmiphalasacchikiriyāya paṭipanno, arahā, arahattāya paṭipanno. Ime kho, bhikkhave, aṭṭha puggalā āhuneyyā…pe… anuttaraṃ puññakkhettaṃ lokassā”ti.

59. “Bhikkhus, these eight individuals are worthy of offerings, worthy of hospitality, worthy of gifts, and worthy of reverential salutation—an unsurpassed field of merit for the world. Which eight? The stream-enterer, one practicing for the realization of the fruit of stream-entry; the once-returner, one practicing for the realization of the fruit of once-returning; the non-returner, one practicing for the realization of the fruit of non-returning; the Arahant, one practicing for Arahantship. These, bhikkhus, are the eight individuals who are worthy of offerings…pe… an unsurpassed field of merit for the world.”

“Cattāro [M 3.0116] ca paṭipannā, cattāro ca phale ṭhitā;

Esa saṅgho ujubhūto, paññāsīlasamāhito.

“Four who are practicing, and four established in the fruit; this Saṅgha is upright, established in wisdom and virtue.

“Yajamānānaṃ manussānaṃ, puññapekkhāna pāṇinaṃ;

Karotaṃ opadhikaṃ puññaṃ, saṅghe dinnaṃ mahapphala”nti. navamaṃ;

“For humans who make offerings, for beings who seek merit, performing merit connected with the substrata of existence, what is given to the Saṅgha is of great fruit.” Ninth.

10. Dutiyapuggalasuttaṃ

10. The Second Discourse on Individuals

60. “Aṭṭhime, bhikkhave, puggalā āhuneyyā…pe… anuttaraṃ puññakkhettaṃ lokassa. Katame aṭṭha? Sotāpanno[P 4.0293], sotāpattiphalasacchikiriyāya paṭipanno…pe… arahā, arahattāya [V 3.0119] paṭipanno. Ime kho, bhikkhave, aṭṭha puggalā āhuneyyā…pe… anuttaraṃ puññakkhettaṃ lokassā”ti.

60. “Bhikkhus, these eight individuals are worthy of offerings…pe… an unsurpassed field of merit for the world. Which eight? The stream-enterer; one practicing for the realization of the fruit of stream-entry…pe… the Arahant; one practicing for Arahantship. These, bhikkhus, are the eight individuals worthy of offerings…pe… an unsurpassed field of merit for the world.”

“Cattāro ca paṭipannā, cattāro ca phale ṭhitā;

Esa [T 4.0302] saṅgho samukkaṭṭho, sattānaṃ aṭṭha puggalā.

“Four who are practicing, and four established in the fruit; this Saṅgha is supreme—these eight persons among beings.

“Yajamānānaṃ manussānaṃ, puññapekkhāna pāṇinaṃ;

Karotaṃ opadhikaṃ puññaṃ, ettha dinnaṃ mahapphala”nti. dasamaṃ;

“For humans who make offerings, for beings who seek merit, performing merit connected with the substrata of existence, what is given to these is of great fruit.” Tenth.

Gotamīvaggo paṭhamo.

The First Chapter on Gotamī.

Tassuddānaṃ –

The Summary Verse:

Gotamī ovādaṃ saṃkhittaṃ, dīghajāṇu ca ujjayo;

Bhayā dve āhuneyyā ca, dve ca aṭṭha puggalāti.

Gotamī, Advice, Brief, Dīghajāṇu, and Ujjaya; Fear, two on the Worthy of Offerings, and two on the Eight Individuals.

(7) 2. Bhūmicālavaggo

(7) 2. The Chapter on Earthquakes

1. Icchāsuttaṃ

1. 1. The Discourse on Desire

61. a. ni. 8.77 “Aṭṭhime[V 3.0120], bhikkhave, puggalā santo saṃvijjamānā lokasmiṃ. Katame aṭṭha? Idha, bhikkhave, bhikkhuno pavivittassa viharato nirāyattavuttino icchā uppajjati lābhāya. So uṭṭhahati ghaṭati vāyamati lābhāya. Tassa uṭṭhahato ghaṭato vāyamato lābhāya lābho nuppajjati. So tena alābhena socati kilamati paridevati, urattāḷiṃ kandati, sammohaṃ āpajjati. Ayaṃ vuccati, bhikkhave – ‘bhikkhu iccho viharati lābhāya[M 3.0117], uṭṭhahati [P 4.0294] ghaṭati vāyamati lābhāya, na ca lābhī, socī ca paridevī ca, cuto ca saddhammā’”.

61. “Bhikkhus, these eight individuals exist, are found in the world. Which eight? Here, bhikkhus, for a bhikkhu dwelling secluded, whose conduct is not dependent, a desire arises for gain. He strives, endeavors, and exerts himself for gain. For him who strives, endeavors, and exerts himself for gain, gain does not arise. Due to that lack of gain, he sorrows, becomes weary, laments, beats his breast and cries out, and falls into confusion. This, bhikkhus, is called: ‘a bhikkhu who dwells desiring gain, strives, endeavors, and exerts himself for gain, but is not a recipient of gain; he is sorrowful and lamenting, and has fallen away from the true Dhamma.’”

“Idha pana, bhikkhave, bhikkhuno pavivittassa viharato nirāyattavuttino icchā uppajjati lābhāya. So uṭṭhahati ghaṭati vāyamati lābhāya. Tassa uṭṭhahato ghaṭato vāyamato lābhāya lābho uppajjati. So tena lābhena majjati pamajjati pamādamāpajjati. Ayaṃ [T 4.0303] vuccati, bhikkhave – ‘bhikkhu iccho viharati lābhāya, uṭṭhahati ghaṭati vāyamati lābhāya, lābhī ca madī ca pamādī ca, cuto ca saddhammā’”.

“Furthermore, bhikkhus, here, while a bhikkhu is dwelling secluded and living independently, a desire for gain arises in him. For the sake of gain, he rouses himself, makes an effort, and strives. As he rouses himself, makes an effort, and strives for gain, gain arises for him. Because of that gain, he becomes intoxicated, becomes negligent, and falls into heedlessness. This, bhikkhus, is called: ‘A bhikkhu dwells full of desire for gain; for the sake of gain he rouses himself, makes an effort, and strives; he gains possessions, becomes intoxicated and heedless, and falls away from the true Dhamma.’”

“Idha pana, bhikkhave, bhikkhuno pavivittassa viharato nirāyattavuttino icchā uppajjati lābhāya. So na uṭṭhahati na ghaṭati na vāyamati lābhāya. Tassa anuṭṭhahato aghaṭato [V 3.0121] avāyamato lābhāya lābho nuppajjati. So tena alābhena socati, kilamati, paridevati, urattāḷiṃ kandati, sammohaṃ āpajjati. Ayaṃ vuccati, bhikkhave – ‘bhikkhu iccho viharati lābhāya, na uṭṭhahati na ghaṭati na vāyamati lābhāya, na ca lābhī, socī ca paridevī ca, cuto ca saddhammā’”.

“Furthermore, bhikkhus, here, for a bhikkhu dwelling secluded, whose conduct is not dependent, a desire arises for gain. He does not strive, does not endeavor, and does not exert himself for gain. For him who does not strive, does not endeavor, and does not exert himself for gain, gain does not arise. Due to that lack of gain, he sorrows, becomes weary, laments, beats his breast and cries out, and falls into confusion. This, bhikkhus, is called: ‘a bhikkhu who dwells desiring gain, does not strive, does not endeavor, does not exert himself for gain, and is not a recipient of gain; he is sorrowful and lamenting, and has fallen away from the true Dhamma.’”

“Idha pana, bhikkhave, bhikkhuno pavivittassa viharato nirāyattavuttino icchā uppajjati lābhāya. So na uṭṭhahati, na ghaṭati, na vāyamati lābhāya. Tassa anuṭṭhahato, aghaṭato, avāyamato lābhāya lābho uppajjati. So tena lābhena majjati, pamajjati, pamādamāpajjati. Ayaṃ vuccati, bhikkhave – ‘bhikkhu iccho viharati lābhāya, na uṭṭhahati na ghaṭati na vāyamati lābhāya, lābhī ca madī ca, pamādī ca, cuto ca saddhammā’”.

“Furthermore, bhikkhus, here, for a bhikkhu dwelling secluded, whose conduct is not dependent, a desire arises for gain. He does not strive, does not endeavor, and does not exert himself for gain. For him who does not strive, does not endeavor, and does not exert himself for gain, gain arises. Due to that gain, he becomes intoxicated, becomes negligent, and falls into heedlessness. This, bhikkhus, is called: ‘a bhikkhu who dwells desiring gain, does not strive, does not endeavor, does not exert himself for gain, is a recipient of gain, is intoxicated and negligent, and has fallen away from the true Dhamma.’”

“Idha pana, bhikkhave, bhikkhuno pavivittassa viharato nirāyattavuttino icchā uppajjati lābhāya. So uṭṭhahati ghaṭati vāyamati lābhāya. Tassa uṭṭhahato ghaṭato vāyamato [P 4.0295] lābhāya lābho nuppajjati. So tena alābhena na socati na kilamati na paridevati, na urattāḷiṃ kandati, na sammohaṃ āpajjati. Ayaṃ vuccati, bhikkhave – ‘bhikkhu iccho viharati lābhāya, uṭṭhahati ghaṭati vāyamati lābhāya, na [T 4.0304] ca lābhī, na ca socī na ca paridevī, accuto ca saddhammā’”.

“Furthermore, bhikkhus, here, for a bhikkhu dwelling secluded, whose conduct is not dependent, a desire arises for gain. He strives, endeavors, and exerts himself for gain. For him who strives, endeavors, and exerts himself for gain, gain does not arise. Due to that lack of gain, he does not sorrow, does not become weary, does not lament, does not beat his breast and cry out, and does not fall into confusion. This, bhikkhus, is called: ‘a bhikkhu who dwells desiring gain, strives, endeavors, and exerts himself for gain, but is not a recipient of gain; he is not sorrowful, not lamenting, and has not fallen away from the true Dhamma.’”

“Idha pana, bhikkhave, bhikkhuno pavivittassa viharato nirāyattavuttino icchā uppajjati lābhāya. So uṭṭhahati ghaṭati vāyamati lābhāya. Tassa [M 3.0118] uṭṭhahato ghaṭato vāyamato lābhāya lābho uppajjati. So tena lābhena na majjati, na pamajjati, na pamādamāpajjati. Ayaṃ vuccati, bhikkhave – ‘bhikkhu iccho viharati lābhāya, uṭṭhahati ghaṭati vāyamati lābhāya, lābhī ca, na ca madī na ca pamādī, accuto ca saddhammā’”.

“Furthermore, bhikkhus, here, for a bhikkhu dwelling secluded, whose conduct is not dependent, a desire arises for gain. He strives, endeavors, and exerts himself for gain. For him who strives, endeavors, and exerts himself for gain, gain arises. Due to that gain, he does not become intoxicated, does not become negligent, and does not fall into heedlessness. This, bhikkhus, is called: ‘a bhikkhu who dwells desiring gain, strives, endeavors, and exerts himself for gain, is a recipient of gain, is not intoxicated, not negligent, and has not fallen away from the true Dhamma.’”

“Idha pana, bhikkhave, bhikkhuno pavivittassa viharato nirāyattavuttino icchā uppajjati lābhāya. So na uṭṭhahati, na ghaṭati, na vāyamati lābhāya. Tassa anuṭṭhahato, aghaṭato, avāyamato lābhāya lābho nuppajjati. So tena alābhena na socati, na kilamati, na paridevati, na urattāḷiṃ kandati, na sammohaṃ āpajjati. Ayaṃ vuccati, bhikkhave – ‘bhikkhu iccho viharati lābhāya, na uṭṭhahati, na ghaṭati, na vāyamati lābhāya[V 3.0122], na ca lābhī, na ca socī na ca paridevī, accuto ca saddhammā’”.

“Furthermore, bhikkhus, here, for a bhikkhu dwelling secluded, whose conduct is not dependent, a desire arises for gain. He does not strive, does not endeavor, and does not exert himself for gain. For him who does not strive, does not endeavor, and does not exert himself for gain, gain does not arise. Due to that lack of gain, he does not sorrow, does not become weary, does not lament, does not beat his breast and cry out, and does not fall into confusion. This, bhikkhus, is called: ‘a bhikkhu who dwells desiring gain, does not strive, does not endeavor, does not exert himself for gain, is not a recipient of gain, is not sorrowful, not lamenting, and has not fallen away from the true Dhamma.’”

“Idha pana, bhikkhave, bhikkhuno pavivittassa viharato nirāyattavuttino icchā uppajjati lābhāya. So na uṭṭhahati, na ghaṭati, na vāyamati lābhāya. Tassa anuṭṭhahato aghaṭato avāyamato lābhāya lābho uppajjati. So tena lābhena na majjati, na pamajjati, na pamādamāpajjati. Ayaṃ vuccati, bhikkhave – ‘bhikkhu iccho viharati lābhāya, na uṭṭhahati, na ghaṭati, na vāyamati lābhāya, lābhī ca, na ca madī na ca pamādī, accuto ca saddhammā [T 4.0305]’. Ime kho, bhikkhave, aṭṭha puggalā santo saṃvijjamānā lokasmi”nti. Paṭhamaṃ.

“Furthermore, bhikkhus, here, for a bhikkhu dwelling secluded, whose conduct is not dependent, a desire arises for gain. He does not strive, does not endeavor, and does not exert himself for gain. For him who does not strive, does not endeavor, and does not exert himself for gain, gain arises. Due to that gain, he does not become intoxicated, does not become negligent, and does not fall into heedlessness. This, bhikkhus, is called: ‘a bhikkhu who dwells desiring gain, does not strive, does not endeavor, does not exert himself for gain, is a recipient of gain, is not intoxicated, not negligent, and has not fallen away from the true Dhamma.’ Indeed, bhikkhus, these eight individuals exist, are found in the world.” First.

2. Alaṃsuttaṃ

2. 2. The Discourse on Competence

62. “Chahi[P 4.0296], bhikkhave, dhammehi samannāgato bhikkhu alaṃ attano alaṃ paresaṃ. Katamehi chahi? Idha, bhikkhave, bhikkhu khippanisanti ca hoti kusalesu dhammesu; sutānañca dhammānaṃ dhāraṇajātiko dhārakajātiko (sī. syā. pī.) a. ni. 8.78 hoti; dhātānañca dhatānañca (sī. syā. pī.) dhammānaṃ atthūpaparikkhitā atthūpavarikkhī (sī. syā. pī.) hoti; atthamaññāya dhammamaññāya dhammānudhammappaṭipanno ca hoti; kalyāṇavāco ca hoti kalyāṇavākkaraṇo, poriyā vācāya samannāgato vissaṭṭhāya anelagaḷāya atthassa viññāpaniyā; sandassako ca hoti samādapako samādāpako (?) samuttejako [M 3.0119] sampahaṃsako sabrahmacārīnaṃ. Imehi kho, bhikkhave, chahi dhammehi samannāgato bhikkhu alaṃ attano alaṃ paresaṃ.

62. “Bhikkhus, a bhikkhu endowed with six qualities is competent for his own welfare and competent for the welfare of others. What six? Here, bhikkhus, a bhikkhu is quick in comprehension regarding wholesome qualities; he is of a nature to retain the teachings he has heard; he is one who examines the meaning of the teachings he has retained; understanding the meaning and the Dhamma, he practices in accordance with the Dhamma; he is of good speech and good utterance, endowed with a refined, clear, articulate voice that makes the meaning known; and he is one who instructs, encourages, arouses, and gladdens his fellow monks in the holy life. Bhikkhus, a bhikkhu endowed with these six qualities is competent for his own welfare and competent for the welfare of others.”

“Pañcahi, bhikkhave, dhammehi samannāgato bhikkhu alaṃ attano alaṃ paresaṃ. Katamehi pañcahi? Idha, bhikkhave, bhikkhu na heva kho khippanisanti ca hoti kusalesu dhammesu; sutānañca dhammānaṃ dhāraṇajātiko hoti; dhātānañca dhammānaṃ atthūpaparikkhitā hoti; atthamaññāya dhammamaññāya dhammānudhammappaṭipanno ca hoti; kalyāṇavāco ca hoti…pe… atthassa viññāpaniyā; sandassako ca hoti samādapako samuttejako sampahaṃsako sabrahmacārīnaṃ. Imehi kho, bhikkhave, pañcahi dhammehi samannāgato bhikkhu alaṃ attano alaṃ paresaṃ.

“Bhikkhus, a bhikkhu endowed with five qualities is competent for his own welfare and competent for the welfare of others. What five? Here, bhikkhus, a bhikkhu is not quick in comprehension regarding wholesome qualities; yet he is of a nature to retain the teachings he has heard; he is one who examines the meaning of the teachings he has retained; understanding the meaning and the Dhamma, he practices in accordance with the Dhamma; he is of good speech... (etc.) ...that makes the meaning known; and he is one who instructs, encourages, arouses, and gladdens his fellow monks in the holy life. Bhikkhus, a bhikkhu endowed with these five qualities is competent for his own welfare and competent for the welfare of others.”

“Catūhi[T 4.0306], bhikkhave, dhammehi samannāgato bhikkhu alaṃ attano nālaṃ paresaṃ. Katamehi catūhi? Idha, bhikkhave, bhikkhu khippanisanti ca hoti kusalesu dhammesu; sutānañca dhammānaṃ dhāraṇajātiko hoti[P 4.0297]; dhātānañca dhammānaṃ atthūpaparikkhitā hoti; atthamaññāya dhammamaññāya dhammānudhammappaṭipanno ca hoti; no ca kalyāṇavāco hoti kalyāṇavākkaraṇo, poriyā vācāya samannāgato vissaṭṭhāya anelagaḷāya atthassa viññāpaniyā; no ca sandassako hoti samādapako [V 3.0123] samuttejako sampahaṃsako sabrahmacārīnaṃ. Imehi kho, bhikkhave, catūhi dhammehi samannāgato bhikkhu alaṃ attano nālaṃ paresaṃ.

“Bhikkhus, a bhikkhu endowed with four qualities is competent for his own welfare, but not competent for the welfare of others. What four? Here, bhikkhus, a bhikkhu is quick in comprehension regarding wholesome qualities; he is of a nature to retain the teachings he has heard; he is one who examines the meaning of the teachings he has retained; understanding the meaning and the Dhamma, he practices in accordance with the Dhamma; but he is not of good speech and good utterance, endowed with a refined, clear, articulate voice that makes the meaning known; nor is he one who instructs, encourages, arouses, and gladdens his fellow monks in the holy life. Bhikkhus, a bhikkhu endowed with these four qualities is competent for his own welfare, but not competent for the welfare of others.”

“Catūhi, bhikkhave, dhammehi samannāgato bhikkhu alaṃ paresaṃ nālaṃ attano. Katamehi catūhi? Idha, bhikkhave, bhikkhu khippanisanti ca hoti kusalesu dhammesu; sutānañca dhammānaṃ dhāraṇajātiko hoti; no ca dhātānaṃ dhammānaṃ atthūpaparikkhitā hoti; na ca atthamaññāya dhammamaññāya dhammānudhammappaṭipanno hoti; kalyāṇavāco ca hoti kalyāṇavākkaraṇo…pe… atthassa viññāpaniyā; sandassako ca hoti…pe… sabrahmacārīnaṃ. Imehi kho, bhikkhave, catūhi dhammehi samannāgato bhikkhu alaṃ paresaṃ, nālaṃ attano.

“Bhikkhus, a bhikkhu endowed with four qualities is competent for the welfare of others, but not competent for his own welfare. What four? Here, bhikkhus, a bhikkhu is quick in comprehension regarding wholesome qualities; he is of a nature to retain the teachings he has heard; but he does not examine the meaning of the teachings he has retained; nor does he practice in accordance with the Dhamma after understanding the meaning and the Dhamma; yet he is of good speech and good utterance... (etc.) ...that makes the meaning known; and he is one who instructs... (etc.) ...his fellow monks in the holy life. Bhikkhus, a bhikkhu endowed with these four qualities is competent for the welfare of others, but not competent for his own welfare.”

“Tīhi, bhikkhave, dhammehi samannāgato bhikkhu alaṃ attano nālaṃ paresaṃ. Katamehi tīhi? Idha, bhikkhave, bhikkhu na heva kho khippanisanti ca hoti kusalesu dhammesu; sutānañca dhammānaṃ dhāraṇajātiko hoti; dhātānañca [T 4.0307][M 3.0120] dhammānaṃ atthūpaparikkhitā hoti; atthamaññāya dhammamaññāya dhammānudhammappaṭipanno ca hoti; no ca kalyāṇavāco hoti kalyāṇavākkaraṇo, poriyā vācāya samannāgato vissaṭṭhāya anelagaḷāya [P 4.0298] atthassa viññāpaniyā; no ca sandassako hoti samādapako samuttejako sampahaṃsako sabrahmacārīnaṃ. Imehi kho, bhikkhave, tīhi dhammehi samannāgato bhikkhu alaṃ attano, nālaṃ paresaṃ.

“Bhikkhus, a bhikkhu endowed with three qualities is competent for his own welfare, but not competent for the welfare of others. What three? Here, bhikkhus, a bhikkhu is not quick in comprehension regarding wholesome qualities; yet he is of a nature to retain the teachings he has heard; he is one who examines the meaning of the teachings he has retained; understanding the meaning and the Dhamma, he practices in accordance with the Dhamma; but he is not of good speech and good utterance, endowed with a refined, clear, articulate voice that makes the meaning known; nor is he one who instructs, encourages, arouses, and gladdens his fellow monks in the holy life. Bhikkhus, a bhikkhu endowed with these three qualities is competent for his own welfare, but not competent for the welfare of others.”

“Tīhi, bhikkhave, dhammehi samannāgato bhikkhu alaṃ paresaṃ, nālaṃ attano. Katamehi tīhi? Idha, bhikkhave, bhikkhu na heva kho khippanisanti ca hoti kusalesu dhammesu; sutānañca dhammānaṃ dhāraṇajātiko hoti; no ca dhātānaṃ dhammānaṃ atthūpaparikkhitā hoti; no ca atthamaññāya dhammamaññāya dhammānudhammappaṭipanno hoti; kalyāṇavāco ca hoti…pe… atthassa viññāpaniyā; sandassako ca hoti samādapako samuttejako sampahaṃsako sabrahmacārīnaṃ. Imehi kho, bhikkhave, tīhi dhammehi samannāgato bhikkhu alaṃ paresaṃ, nālaṃ attano.

“Bhikkhus, a bhikkhu endowed with three qualities is competent for the welfare of others, but not competent for his own welfare. What three? Here, bhikkhus, a bhikkhu is not quick in comprehension regarding wholesome qualities; yet he is of a nature to retain the teachings he has heard; but he does not examine the meaning of the teachings he has retained; nor does he practice in accordance with the Dhamma after understanding the meaning and the Dhamma; yet he is of good speech... (etc.) ...that makes the meaning known; and he is one who instructs, encourages, arouses, and gladdens his fellow monks in the holy life. Bhikkhus, a bhikkhu endowed with these three qualities is competent for the welfare of others, but not competent for his own welfare.”

“Dvīhi, bhikkhave, dhammehi samannāgato bhikkhu alaṃ attano, nālaṃ paresaṃ. Katamehi dvīhi? Idha, bhikkhave, bhikkhu na heva kho khippanisanti ca hoti kusalesu dhammesu; no ca [V 3.0124] sutānaṃ dhammānaṃ dhāraṇajātiko hoti; dhātānañca dhammānaṃ atthūpaparikkhitā hoti; atthamaññāya dhammamaññāya dhammānudhammappaṭipanno ca hoti; no ca kalyāṇavāco hoti…pe… atthassa viññāpaniyā; no ca sandassako hoti…pe… sabrahmacārīnaṃ[T 4.0308]. Imehi kho, bhikkhave, dvīhi dhammehi samannāgato bhikkhu alaṃ attano, nālaṃ paresaṃ.

“Bhikkhus, a bhikkhu endowed with two qualities is competent for his own welfare, but not competent for the welfare of others. What two? Here, bhikkhus, a bhikkhu is not quick in comprehension regarding wholesome qualities, nor is he of a nature to retain the teachings he has heard; yet he is one who examines the meaning of the teachings he has retained, and understanding the meaning and the Dhamma, he practices in accordance with the Dhamma; but he is not of good speech... (etc.) ...that makes the meaning known; nor is he one who instructs... (etc.) ...his fellow monks in the holy life. Bhikkhus, a bhikkhu endowed with these two qualities is competent for his own welfare, but not competent for the welfare of others.”

“Dvīhi, bhikkhave, dhammehi samannāgato bhikkhu alaṃ paresaṃ, nālaṃ attano. Katamehi dvīhi?
