Idha, bhikkhave, bhikkhu na heva kho khippanisanti ca hoti kusalesu dhammesu; no ca sutānaṃ dhammānaṃ dhāraṇajātiko hoti; no ca dhātānaṃ dhammānaṃ atthūpaparikkhitā [P 4.0299] hoti; no ca atthamaññāya dhammamaññāya dhammānudhammappaṭipanno hoti; kalyāṇavāco ca hoti kalyāṇavākkaraṇo, poriyā vācāya samannāgato vissaṭṭhāya anelagaḷāya atthassa viññāpaniyā; sandassako ca hoti samādapako samuttejako sampahaṃsako sabrahmacārīnaṃ. Imehi kho, bhikkhave[M 3.0121], dvīhi dhammehi samannāgato bhikkhu alaṃ paresaṃ, nālaṃ attano”ti. Dutiyaṃ.

“Bhikkhus, a bhikkhu endowed with two qualities is capable for others, but not capable for himself. What two? Here, bhikkhus, a bhikkhu is not quick to investigate wholesome qualities; nor is he one who retains the teachings he has heard; nor does he investigate the meaning of the teachings he has retained; nor, having understood the meaning and the Dhamma, does he practice in accordance with the Dhamma. However, he has a pleasant voice and speaks well, being endowed with refined speech that is clear, articulate, and makes the meaning known; and he is one who instructs, encourages, rouses, and gladdens his fellow practitioners of the holy life. Bhikkhus, a bhikkhu endowed with these two qualities is capable for others, but not capable for himself.” Second.

3. Saṃkhittasuttaṃ

3. The Brief Discourse

63. Atha kho aññataro bhikkhu yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami…pe… ekamantaṃ nisinno kho so bhikkhu bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “sādhu me, bhante, bhagavā saṃkhittena dhammaṃ desetu, yamahaṃ bhagavato dhammaṃ sutvā eko vūpakaṭṭho appamatto ātāpī pahitatto vihareyya”nti. “Evamevaṃ panidhekacce moghapurisā mamaññeva ajjhesanti. Dhamme ca bhāsite mamaññeva anubandhitabbaṃ maññantī”ti. “Desetu me, bhante, bhagavā saṃkhittena dhammaṃ, desetu sugato saṃkhittena dhammaṃ. Appeva nāmāhaṃ bhagavato bhāsitassa atthaṃ ājāneyyaṃ, appeva nāmāhaṃ bhagavato bhāsitassa dāyādo assa”nti. “Tasmātiha [T 4.0309] te, bhikkhu evaṃ sikkhitabbaṃ – ‘ajjhattaṃ me cittaṃ ṭhitaṃ bhavissati susaṇṭhitaṃ, na ca uppannā pāpakā akusalā dhammā cittaṃ pariyādāya ṭhassantī’ti. Evañhi te, bhikkhu, sikkhitabbaṃ”.

63. Then a certain bhikkhu approached the Blessed One… and sat down to one side. Seated there, that bhikkhu said to the Blessed One: “It would be good, venerable sir, if the Blessed One would teach me the Dhamma in brief, so that, having heard the Dhamma from the Blessed One, I might dwell alone, withdrawn, heedful, ardent, and resolute.” “Even so, some worthless men here only implore me. And when the Dhamma is spoken, they think they should just follow me.” “Let the Blessed One teach me the Dhamma in brief! Let the Sugata teach the Dhamma in brief! Perhaps I may understand the meaning of the Blessed One’s words; perhaps I may become an heir to the Blessed One’s teaching.” “Therefore, bhikkhu, you should train yourself thus: ‘My mind will be inwardly steady and well-established, and evil, unwholesome states that have arisen will not remain, having overcome my mind.’ That is how you should train yourself, bhikkhu.”

“Yato kho te, bhikkhu, ajjhattaṃ cittaṃ ṭhitaṃ hoti susaṇṭhitaṃ, na ca uppannā pāpakā akusalā dhammā cittaṃ pariyādāya tiṭṭhanti, tato te, bhikkhu, evaṃ sikkhitabbaṃ – ‘mettā [V 3.0125][P 4.0300] me cetovimutti bhāvitā bhavissati bahulīkatā yānīkatā vatthukatā anuṭṭhitā paricitā susamāraddhā’ti. Evañhi te, bhikkhu, sikkhitabbaṃ.

“When, bhikkhu, your mind is inwardly steady and well-established, and evil, unwholesome states that have arisen do not remain, having overcome the mind, then you should train yourself thus: ‘My liberation of mind through loving-kindness will be developed, cultivated, made a vehicle, made a basis, established, familiarized, and well-undertaken.’ That is how you should train yourself, bhikkhu.

“Yato kho te, bhikkhu, ayaṃ samādhi evaṃ bhāvito hoti bahulīkato, tato tvaṃ, bhikkhu, imaṃ samādhiṃ savitakkampi savicāraṃ savitakkasavicārampi (ka.) bhāveyyāsi, avitakkampi vicāramattaṃ avitakkavicāramattampi (ka.) visuddhi. 1.271 passitabbaṃ bhāveyyāsi, avitakkampi avicāraṃ avitakkaavicārampi (ka.) bhāveyyāsi, sappītikampi bhāveyyāsi, nippītikampi bhāveyyāsi, sātasahagatampi bhāveyyāsi, upekkhāsahagatampi bhāveyyāsi.

“When, bhikkhu, this concentration of yours has been thus developed and cultivated, then you, bhikkhu, should develop this concentration with initial application and sustained application; you should develop it without initial application but with sustained application only; you should develop it without initial application and without sustained application; you should develop it with rapture; you should develop it without rapture; you should develop it accompanied by happiness; you should develop it accompanied by equanimity.

“Yato kho, te bhikkhu, ayaṃ samādhi evaṃ bhāvito hoti subhāvito, tato te, bhikkhu, evaṃ sikkhitabbaṃ – ‘karuṇā me cetovimutti… muditā me cetovimutti… upekkhā me cetovimutti bhāvitā bhavissati [M 3.0122] bahulīkatā yānīkatā vatthukatā anuṭṭhitā paricitā susamāraddhā’ti. Evañhi te, bhikkhu, sikkhitabbaṃ.

“When, bhikkhu, this concentration has been thus developed and well-developed, then you, bhikkhu, should train yourself thus: ‘My liberation of mind through compassion… my liberation of mind through sympathetic joy… my liberation of mind through equanimity will be developed, cultivated, made a vehicle, made a basis, established, familiarized, and well-undertaken.’ That is how you should train yourself, bhikkhu.

“Yato kho te, bhikkhu, ayaṃ samādhi evaṃ bhāvito hoti subhāvito, tato tvaṃ, bhikkhu, imaṃ samādhiṃ savitakkasavicārampi bhāveyyāsi, avitakkavicāramattampi bhāveyyāsi, avitakkaavicārampi bhāveyyāsi, sappītikampi bhāveyyāsi, nippītikampi bhāveyyāsi, sātasahagatampi bhāveyyāsi, upekkhāsahagatampi bhāveyyāsi.

“When, bhikkhu, this concentration of yours has been thus developed and well-developed, then you, bhikkhu, should develop this concentration with initial application and sustained application; you should develop it without initial application but with sustained application only; you should develop it without initial application and without sustained application; you should develop it with rapture; you should develop it without rapture; you should develop it accompanied by happiness; you should develop it accompanied by equanimity.

“Yato kho te, bhikkhu, ayaṃ samādhi evaṃ bhāvito hoti subhāvito, tato te, bhikkhu, evaṃ sikkhitabbaṃ – ‘kāye kāyānupassī viharissāmi ātāpī sampajāno satimā, vineyya loke abhijjhādomanassa’nti. Evañhi te, bhikkhu, sikkhitabbaṃ.

“When, bhikkhu, this concentration has been thus developed and well-developed, then you, bhikkhu, should train yourself thus: ‘I will dwell contemplating the body in the body, ardent, clearly comprehending, and mindful, having removed covetousness and displeasure in the world.’ That is how you should train yourself, bhikkhu.

“Yato kho te, bhikkhu, ayaṃ samādhi evaṃ bhāvito hoti bahulīkato, tato tvaṃ, bhikkhu, imaṃ samādhiṃ savitakkasavicārampi bhāveyyāsi, avitakkavicāramattampi [P 4.0301] bhāveyyāsi, avitakkaavicārampi bhāveyyāsi, sappītikampi bhāveyyāsi, nippītikampi bhāveyyāsi, sātasahagatampi bhāveyyāsi[T 4.0310], upekkhāsahagatampi bhāveyyāsi.

“When, bhikkhu, this concentration of yours has been thus developed and cultivated, then you, bhikkhu, should develop this concentration with initial application and sustained application; you should develop it without initial application but with sustained application only; you should develop it without initial application and without sustained application; you should develop it with rapture; you should develop it without rapture; you should develop it accompanied by happiness; you should develop it accompanied by equanimity.

“Yato kho te, bhikkhu, ayaṃ samādhi evaṃ bhāvito hoti subhāvito, tato te, bhikkhu[V 3.0126], evaṃ sikkhitabbaṃ – ‘vedanāsu vedanānupassī viharissāmi ātāpī sampajāno satimā, vineyya loke abhijjhādomanassa’nti; citte cittānupassī viharissāmi ātāpī sampajāno satimā, vineyya loke abhijjhādomanassa’nti; dhammesu dhammānupassī viharissāmi ātāpī sampajāno satimā, vineyya loke abhijjhādomanassa’nti. Evañhi te, bhikkhu, sikkhitabbaṃ.

“When, bhikkhu, this concentration has been thus developed and well-developed, then you, bhikkhu, should train yourself thus: ‘I will dwell contemplating feelings in feelings, ardent, clearly comprehending, and mindful, having removed covetousness and displeasure in the world’; ‘I will dwell contemplating mind in mind, ardent, clearly comprehending, and mindful, having removed covetousness and displeasure in the world’; ‘I will dwell contemplating phenomena in phenomena, ardent, clearly comprehending, and mindful, having removed covetousness and displeasure in the world.’ That is how you should train yourself, bhikkhu.

“Yato kho te, bhikkhu, ayaṃ samādhi evaṃ bhāvito hoti bahulīkato, tato tvaṃ, bhikkhu, imaṃ samādhiṃ savitakkasavicārampi bhāveyyāsi, avitakkavicāramattampi bhāveyyāsi, avitakkaavicārampi bhāveyyāsi, sappītikampi bhāveyyāsi, nippītikampi bhāveyyāsi, sātasahagatampi bhāveyyāsi, upekkhāsahagatampi bhāveyyāsi.

“When, bhikkhu, this concentration of yours has been thus developed and cultivated, then you, bhikkhu, should develop this concentration with initial application and sustained application; you should develop it without initial application but with sustained application only; you should develop it without initial application and without sustained application; you should develop it with rapture; you should develop it without rapture; you should develop it accompanied by happiness; you should develop it accompanied by equanimity.

“Yato kho te, bhikkhu, ayaṃ samādhi evaṃ bhāvito hoti subhāvito, tato tvaṃ, bhikkhu, yena yeneva gagghasi phāsuṃyeva gagghasi, yattha yattha ṭhassasi phāsuṃyeva ṭhassasi, yattha yattha nisīdissasi phāsuṃyeva nisīdissasi, yattha yattha seyyaṃ kappessasi [T 4.0311] phāsuṃyeva seyyaṃ kappessasī”ti.

“Bhikkhu, when this concentration has been thus developed and well-developed by you, then, bhikkhu, wherever you go, you will go at ease; wherever you stand, you will stand at ease; wherever you sit, you will sit at ease; wherever you make your bed, you will make your bed at ease.”

Atha [M 3.0123] kho so bhikkhu bhagavatā iminā ovādena ovadito uṭṭhāyāsanā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā padakkhiṇaṃ katvā pakkāmi. Atha kho so bhikkhu eko vūpakaṭṭho appamatto ātāpī pahitatto viharanto nacirasseva – yassatthāya kulaputtā sammadeva agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajanti, tadanuttaraṃ – brahmacariyapariyosānaṃ diṭṭheva dhamme sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja vihāsi. “Khīṇā [P 4.0302] jāti, vusitaṃ brahmacariyaṃ, kataṃ karaṇīyaṃ, nāparaṃ itthattāyā”ti abbhaññāsi. Aññataro ca pana so bhikkhu arahataṃ ahosīti. Tatiyaṃ.

Then that bhikkhu, having been exhorted by the Blessed One with this instruction, rose from his seat, paid homage to the Blessed One, circumambulated him respectfully, and departed. Then that bhikkhu, dwelling alone, secluded, heedful, ardent, and resolute, before long realized for himself with direct knowledge in this very life that unsurpassed culmination of the holy life for the sake of which clansmen rightly go forth from the household life into homelessness, and having attained it, he dwelled therein. He knew directly: “Birth is destroyed, the holy life has been lived, what had to be done has been done, there is no more for this state of being.” And that bhikkhu became one of the Arahants. The Third.

4. Gayāsīsasuttaṃ

4. The Discourse at Gayāsīsa

64. Ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā gayāyaṃ viharati gayāsīse. Tatra kho bhagavā bhikkhū āmantesi…pe… “pubbāhaṃ, bhikkhave, sambodhā anabhisambuddho bodhisattova samāno obhāsaññeva kho sañjānāmi, no ca rūpāni passāmi”.

64. On one occasion the Blessed One was dwelling at Gayā, at Gayāsīsa. There the Blessed One addressed the bhikkhus… “Bhikkhus, before my enlightenment, when I was an unenlightened Bodhisatta, I perceived only light, but I did not see forms.”

“Tassa [V 3.0127] mayhaṃ, bhikkhave, etadahosi – ‘sace kho ahaṃ obhāsañceva sañjāneyyaṃ rūpāni ca passeyyaṃ; evaṃ me idaṃ ñāṇadassanaṃ parisuddhataraṃ assā’”ti.

“Then, bhikkhus, this thought occurred to me: ‘If I were to perceive light and also see forms, my knowledge and vision would thus become purer.’”

“So kho ahaṃ, bhikkhave, aparena samayena appamatto ātāpī pahitatto viharanto obhāsañceva sañjānāmi, rūpāni ca passāmi; no ca kho tāhi devatāhi saddhiṃ santiṭṭhāmi sallapāmi sākacchaṃ samāpajjāmi.

“Then, bhikkhus, at a later time, dwelling heedful, ardent, and resolute, I perceived light and saw forms; but I did not stand with those deities, converse with them, or enter into discussion with them.”

“Tassa mayhaṃ, bhikkhave, etadahosi – ‘sace kho ahaṃ obhāsañceva sañjāneyyaṃ, rūpāni ca passeyyaṃ, tāhi ca devatāhi saddhiṃ santiṭṭheyyaṃ sallapeyyaṃ [T 4.0312] sākacchaṃ samāpajjeyyaṃ; evaṃ me idaṃ ñāṇadassanaṃ parisuddhataraṃ assā’”ti.

“Then, bhikkhus, this thought occurred to me: ‘If I were to perceive light and see forms, and also to stand with those deities, converse with them, and enter into discussion with them, my knowledge and vision would thus become purer.’”

“So kho ahaṃ, bhikkhave, aparena samayena appamatto ātāpī pahitatto viharanto obhāsañceva sañjānāmi, rūpāni ca passāmi, tāhi ca devatāhi saddhiṃ santiṭṭhāmi sallapāmi sākacchaṃ samāpajjāmi; no ca kho tā devatā jānāmi – imā devatā amukamhā vā amukamhā vā devanikāyāti.

“Then, bhikkhus, at a later time, dwelling heedful, ardent, and resolute, I perceived light and saw forms; I stood with those deities, conversed with them, and entered into discussion with them; but I did not know of those deities: ‘These deities are from this or that group of deities.’”

“Tassa [M 3.0124][P 4.0303] mayhaṃ, bhikkhave, etadahosi – ‘sace kho ahaṃ obhāsañceva sañjāneyyaṃ, rūpāni ca passeyyaṃ, tāhi ca devatāhi saddhiṃ santiṭṭheyyaṃ sallapeyyaṃ sākacchaṃ samāpajjeyyaṃ, tā ca devatā jāneyyaṃ – imā devatā amukamhā vā amukamhā vā devanikāyā’ti; evaṃ me idaṃ ñāṇadassanaṃ parisuddhataraṃ assā’”ti.

“Then, bhikkhus, this thought occurred to me: ‘If I were to perceive light and see forms, to stand with those deities, converse with them, and enter into discussion with them, and also to know of those deities: “These deities are from this or that group of deities,” my knowledge and vision would thus become purer.’”

“So kho ahaṃ, bhikkhave, aparena samayena appamatto ātāpī pahitatto viharanto obhāsañceva sañjānāmi, rūpāni ca passāmi, tāhi ca devatāhi saddhiṃ santiṭṭhāmi sallapāmi sākacchaṃ samāpajjāmi, tā ca devatā jānāmi – ‘imā devatā amukamhā vā amukamhā vā devanikāyā’ti; no ca kho tā devatā jānāmi – ‘imā devatā imassa kammassa vipākena ito cutā tattha upapannā’ti…pe… tā ca devatā jānāmi – ‘imā devatā imassa kammassa vipākena ito cutā tattha upapannā’ti; no ca kho tā devatā jānāmi – ‘imā devatā imassa kammassa vipākena evamāhārā evaṃsukhadukkhappaṭisaṃvediniyo’ti [V 3.0128] …pe… tā ca devatā jānāmi – ‘imā devatā imassa kammassa vipākena evamāhārā evaṃsukhadukkhappaṭisaṃvediniyo’ti; no ca kho tā devatā jānāmi – ‘imā devatā evaṃdīghāyukā [T 4.0313] evaṃciraṭṭhitikā’ti…pe… tā ca devatā jānāmi – ‘imā devatā evaṃdīghāyukā evaṃciraṭṭhitikā’ti; no ca kho tā devatā jānāmi yadi vā me imāhi devatāhi saddhiṃ sannivutthapubbaṃ yadi vā na sannivutthapubbanti.

“Then, bhikkhus, at a later time, dwelling heedful, ardent, and resolute, I perceived light and saw forms; I stood with those deities, conversed with them, and entered into discussion with them; and I knew of those deities: ‘These deities are from this or that group of deities.’ But I did not know of those deities: ‘These deities, as a result of this kamma, passed away from here and have arisen there’… I knew of those deities: ‘These deities, as a result of this kamma, passed away from here and have arisen there.’ But I did not know of those deities: ‘These deities, as a result of this kamma, have such nourishment and experience such pleasure and pain’… I knew of those deities: ‘These deities, as a result of this kamma, have such nourishment and experience such pleasure and pain.’ But I did not know of those deities: ‘These deities have such a long lifespan and are of such long standing’… I knew of those deities: ‘These deities have such a long lifespan and are of such long standing.’ But I did not know whether or not I had previously lived together with these deities.”

“Tassa mayhaṃ, bhikkhave, etadahosi – ‘sace kho ahaṃ obhāsañceva sañjāneyyaṃ, rūpāni ca passeyyaṃ, tāhi ca devatāhi saddhiṃ santiṭṭheyyaṃ sallapeyyaṃ sākacchaṃ samāpajjeyyaṃ[P 4.0304], tā ca devatā jāneyyaṃ – ‘imā devatā amukamhā vā amukamhā vā devanikāyā’ti, tā ca devatā jāneyyaṃ – ‘imā devatā imassa kammassa vipākena ito cutā tattha upapannā’ti, tā ca devatā jāneyyaṃ – ‘imā devatā evamāhārā evaṃsukhadukkhappaṭisaṃvediniyo’ti, tā ca devatā jāneyyaṃ – ‘imā devatā evaṃdīghāyukā evaṃciraṭṭhitikā’ti, tā ca devatā jāneyyaṃ yadi vā me imāhi devatāhi saddhiṃ sannivutthapubbaṃ yadi vā na sannivutthapubbanti; evaṃ me idaṃ ñāṇadassanaṃ parisuddhataraṃ assā’”ti.

“Then, bhikkhus, this thought occurred to me: ‘If I were to perceive light and see forms; to stand with those deities, converse with them, and enter into discussion with them; and to know of those deities: “These deities are from this or that group of deities”; and to know of those deities: “These deities, as a result of this kamma, passed away from here and have arisen there”; and to know of those deities: “These deities have such nourishment and experience such pleasure and pain”; and to know of those deities: “These deities have such a long lifespan and are of such long standing”; and to know whether or not I had previously lived together with these deities; my knowledge and vision would thus become purer.’”

“So [M 3.0125] kho ahaṃ, bhikkhave, aparena samayena appamatto ātāpī pahitatto viharanto obhāsañceva sañjānāmi, rūpāni ca passāmi, tāhi ca devatāhi saddhiṃ santiṭṭhāmi sallapāmi sākacchaṃ samāpajjāmi, tā ca devatā jānāmi – ‘imā devatā amukamhā vā amukamhā vā devanikāyā’ti, tā ca devatā jānāmi – ‘imā devatā imassa kammassa vipākena ito cutā tattha upapannā’ti, tā ca devatā jānāmi – ‘imā devatā evamāhārā evaṃsukhadukkhappaṭisaṃvediniyo’ti[T 4.0314], tā ca devatā jānāmi – ‘imā devatā evaṃdīghāyukā evaṃciraṭṭhitikā’ti, tā ca devatā jānāmi yadi vā me devatāhi saddhiṃ sannivutthapubbaṃ yadi vā na sannivutthapubbanti.

Indeed, Bhikkhus, at another time, while dwelling diligent, ardent, and with a mind directed towards Nibbāna, I both perceived light and saw forms. I stood together with those deities, conversed with them, and entered into discussion with them. And I knew those deities: ‘These deities are from this or that company of devas.’ And I knew those deities: ‘These deities, as a result of this kamma, having passed away from here, have arisen there.’ And I knew those deities: ‘These deities have such-and-such food and experience such-and-such pleasure and pain.’ And I knew those deities: ‘These deities have such a long lifespan and are of such long-standing duration.’ And I knew, concerning those deities, whether I had previously dwelt together with them or had not previously dwelt together with them.

“Yāvakīvañca me, bhikkhave, evaṃ aṭṭhaparivaṭṭaṃ adhidevañāṇadassanaṃ na suvisuddhaṃ ahosi, neva tāvāhaṃ, bhikkhave, ‘sadevake loke samārake sabrahmake sassamaṇabrāhmaṇiyā pajāya sadevamanussāya anuttaraṃ sammāsambodhiṃ abhisambuddho’ti abhisambuddho (sī. syā. pī.) paccaññāsiṃ. Yato ca kho me[V 3.0129], bhikkhave, evaṃ aṭṭhaparivaṭṭaṃ adhidevañāṇadassanaṃ suvisuddhaṃ ahosi, athāhaṃ, bhikkhave, ‘sadevake [P 4.0305] loke samārake sabrahmake sassamaṇabrāhmaṇiyā pajāya sadevamanussāya anuttaraṃ sammāsambodhiṃ abhisambuddho’ti paccaññāsiṃ; ñāṇañca pana me dassanaṃ udapādi; akuppā me cetovimutti vimutti (ka. sī. ka.); ayamantimā jāti natthi dāni punabbhavo”ti. Catutthaṃ.

Bhikkhus, for as long as this knowledge and vision concerning the higher devas with its eight aspects was not exceedingly pure for me, for so long, Bhikkhus, I did not claim to have fully awakened to the unsurpassed, perfect enlightenment in this world with its devas, its Māras, and its Brahmās, among this generation with its ascetics and brahmins, with its devas and humans. But indeed, Bhikkhus, when this knowledge and vision concerning the higher devas with its eight aspects became exceedingly pure for me, then, Bhikkhus, I claimed to have fully awakened to the unsurpassed, perfect enlightenment in this world with its devas, its Māras, and its Brahmās, among this generation with its ascetics and brahmins, with its devas and humans. And knowledge and vision arose in me: ‘My liberation of mind is unshakeable. This is the final birth. Now there is no more renewed existence.’ Fourth.

5. Abhibhāyatanasuttaṃ

5. The Discourse on the Spheres of Mastery

65. dī. ni. 3.338, 358; a. ni. 10.29 “Aṭṭhimāni, bhikkhave, abhibhāyatanāni. Katamāni aṭṭha? Ajjhattaṃ rūpasaññī eko bahiddhā rūpāni passati parittāni suvaṇṇadubbaṇṇāni. ‘Tāni abhibhuyya jānāmi passāmī’ti, evaṃsaññī hoti. Idaṃ paṭhamaṃ abhibhāyatanaṃ.

65. Bhikkhus, there are these eight spheres of mastery. What eight? One, being percipient of form internally, sees forms externally that are limited, of good and bad color. He becomes one with the perception: ‘Having overcome them, I know, I see.’ This is the first sphere of mastery.

“Ajjhattaṃ rūpasaññī eko bahiddhā rūpāni passati appamāṇāni suvaṇṇadubbaṇṇāni. ‘Tāni abhibhuyya jānāmi passāmī’ti, evaṃsaññī hoti. Idaṃ dutiyaṃ abhibhāyatanaṃ.

One, being percipient of form internally, sees forms externally that are immeasurable, of good and bad color. He becomes one with the perception: ‘Having overcome them, I know, I see.’ This is the second sphere of mastery.

“Ajjhattaṃ [M 3.0126] arūpasaññī eko bahiddhā rūpāni passati parittāni suvaṇṇadubbaṇṇāni. ‘Tāni abhibhuyya jānāmi passāmī’ti, evaṃsaññī hoti. Idaṃ tatiyaṃ [T 4.0315] abhibhāyatanaṃ.

One, not being percipient of form internally, sees forms externally that are limited, of good and bad color. He becomes one with the perception: ‘Having overcome them, I know, I see.’ This is the third sphere of mastery.

“Ajjhattaṃ arūpasaññī eko bahiddhā rūpāni passati appamāṇāni suvaṇṇadubbaṇṇāni. ‘Tāni abhibhuyya jānāmi passāmī’ti, evaṃsaññī hoti. Idaṃ catutthaṃ abhibhāyatanaṃ.

One, not being percipient of form internally, sees forms externally that are immeasurable, of good and bad color. He becomes one with the perception: ‘Having overcome them, I know, I see.’ This is the fourth sphere of mastery.

“Ajjhattaṃ arūpasaññī eko bahiddhā rūpāni passati nīlāni nīlavaṇṇāni nīlanidassanāni nīlanibhāsāni. ‘Tāni abhibhuyya jānāmi passāmī’ti, evaṃsaññī hoti. Idaṃ pañcamaṃ abhibhāyatanaṃ.

One, not being percipient of form internally, sees forms externally that are blue, of blue color, appearing as blue, and of blue radiance. He becomes one with the perception: ‘Having overcome them, I know, I see.’ This is the fifth sphere of mastery.

“Ajjhattaṃ arūpasaññī eko bahiddhā rūpāni passati pītāni pītavaṇṇāni pītanidassanāni pītanibhāsāni. ‘Tāni abhibhuyya jānāmi passāmī’ti, evaṃsaññī hoti. Idaṃ chaṭṭhaṃ abhibhāyatanaṃ.

One, not being percipient of form internally, sees forms externally that are yellow, of yellow color, appearing as yellow, and of yellow radiance. He becomes one with the perception: ‘Having overcome them, I know, I see.’ This is the sixth sphere of mastery.

“Ajjhattaṃ [V 3.0130][P 4.0306] arūpasaññī eko bahiddhā rūpāni passati lohitakāni lohitakavaṇṇāni lohitakanidassanāni lohitakanibhāsāni. ‘Tāni abhibhuyya jānāmi passāmī’ti, evaṃsaññī hoti. Idaṃ sattamaṃ abhibhāyatanaṃ.

One, not being percipient of form internally, sees forms externally that are red, of red color, appearing as red, and of red radiance. He becomes one with the perception: ‘Having overcome them, I know, I see.’ This is the seventh sphere of mastery.

“Ajjhattaṃ arūpasaññī eko bahiddhā rūpāni passati odātāni odātavaṇṇāni odātanidassanāni odātanibhāsāni. ‘Tāni abhibhuyya jānāmi passāmī’ti, evaṃsaññī hoti. Idaṃ aṭṭhamaṃ abhibhāyatanaṃ. Imāni kho, bhikkhave, aṭṭha abhibhāyatanānī”ti. Pañcamaṃ.

One, not being percipient of form internally, sees forms externally that are white, of white color, appearing as white, and of white radiance. He becomes one with the perception: ‘Having overcome them, I know, I see.’ This is the eighth sphere of mastery. Indeed, Bhikkhus, these are the eight spheres of mastery. Fifth.

6. Vimokkhasuttaṃ

6. The Discourse on Liberations

66. “Aṭṭhime, bhikkhave, vimokkhā. Katame aṭṭha? Rūpī rūpāni passati. Ayaṃ paṭhamo vimokkho.

66. Bhikkhus, there are these eight liberations. What eight? Possessed of form, one sees forms. This is the first liberation.

“Ajjhattaṃ arūpasaññī, bahiddhā arūpasaññī eko bahiddhā (syā. pī. ka.) dī. ni. 2.129; dī. ni. 3.338, 358; a. ni. 8.119; ma. ni. 2.248 passitabbaṃ rūpāni passati. Ayaṃ dutiyo vimokkho.

Not percipient of form internally, one sees forms externally. This is the second liberation.

“Subhanteva adhimutto hoti. Ayaṃ tatiyo vimokkho.

One is intent only upon 'It is beautiful.' This is the third liberation.

“Sabbaso [M 3.0127] rūpasaññānaṃ samatikkamā paṭighasaññānaṃ atthaṅgamā nānattasaññānaṃ amanasikārā ‘ananto [T 4.0316] ākāso’ti ākāsānañcāyatanaṃ upasampajja viharati. Ayaṃ catuttho vimokkho.

By completely transcending perceptions of form, with the cessation of perceptions of resistance, and by not attending to perceptions of diversity, thinking ‘space is infinite,’ one attains and abides in the base of infinite space. This is the fourth liberation.

“Sabbaso ākāsānañcāyatanaṃ samatikkamma ‘anantaṃ viññāṇa’nti viññāṇañcāyatanaṃ upasampajja viharati. Ayaṃ pañcamo vimokkho.

By completely transcending the base of infinite space, thinking ‘consciousness is infinite,’ one attains and abides in the base of infinite consciousness. This is the fifth liberation.

“Sabbaso viññāṇañcāyatanaṃ samatikkamma ‘natthi kiñcī’ti ākiñcaññāyatanaṃ upasampajja viharati. Ayaṃ chaṭṭho vimokkho.

By completely transcending the base of infinite consciousness, thinking ‘there is nothing,’ one attains and abides in the base of nothingness. This is the sixth liberation.

“Sabbaso [V 3.0131] ākiñcaññāyatanaṃ samatikkamma nevasaññānāsaññāyatanaṃ upasampajja viharati. Ayaṃ sattamo vimokkho.

By completely transcending the base of nothingness, one attains and abides in the base of neither-perception-nor-non-perception. This is the seventh liberation.

“Sabbaso nevasaññānāsaññāyatanaṃ samatikkamma saññāvedayitanirodhaṃ upasampajja viharati. Ayaṃ aṭṭhamo vimokkho. Ime kho, bhikkhave, aṭṭha vimokkhā”ti. Chaṭṭhaṃ.

By completely transcending the base of neither-perception-nor-non-perception, one attains and abides in the cessation of perception and feeling. This is the eighth liberation. Indeed, Bhikkhus, these are the eight liberations. Sixth.

7. Anariyavohārasuttaṃ

7. The Discourse on Ignoble Expressions

67. “Aṭṭhime[P 4.0307], bhikkhave, anariyavohārā. Katame aṭṭha? Adiṭṭhe diṭṭhavāditā, asute sutavāditā, amute mutavāditā, aviññāte viññātavāditā, diṭṭhe adiṭṭhavāditā, sute asutavāditā, mute amutavāditā, viññāte aviññātavāditā. Ime kho, bhikkhave, aṭṭha anariyavohārā”ti. Sattamaṃ.

67. Bhikkhus, there are these eight ignoble expressions. What eight? Declaring as seen what was not seen; declaring as heard what was not heard; declaring as sensed what was not sensed; declaring as known what was not known; declaring as unseen what was seen; declaring as unheard what was heard; declaring as unsensed what was sensed; declaring as unknown what was known. Indeed, Bhikkhus, these are the eight ignoble expressions. Seventh.

8. Ariyavohārasuttaṃ

8. The Discourse on Noble Expressions

68. “Aṭṭhime, bhikkhave, ariyavohārā. Katame aṭṭha? Adiṭṭhe adiṭṭhavāditā, asute asutavāditā, amute amutavāditā, aviññāte aviññātavāditā, diṭṭhe diṭṭhavāditā, sute sutavāditā, mute mutavāditā, viññāte viññātavāditā. Ime kho, bhikkhave, aṭṭha ariyavohārā”ti. Aṭṭhamaṃ.

68. Monks, there are these eight noble expressions. What eight? Speaking of what is unseen as unseen; speaking of what is unheard as unheard; speaking of what is unsensed as unsensed; speaking of what is uncognized as uncognized; speaking of what is seen as seen; speaking of what is heard as heard; speaking of what is sensed as sensed; and speaking of what is cognized as cognized. Indeed, monks, these are the eight noble expressions.

9. Parisāsuttaṃ

9. The Discourse on Assemblies

69. “Aṭṭhimā, bhikkhave, parisā. Katamā aṭṭha? Khattiyaparisā[T 4.0317], brāhmaṇaparisā, gahapatiparisā, samaṇaparisā, cātumahārājikaparisā, tāvatiṃsaparisā, māraparisā, brahmaparisā. Abhijānāmi kho panāhaṃ, bhikkhave, anekasataṃ [M 3.0128] khattiyaparisaṃ upasaṅkamitā. Tatrapi mayā sannisinnapubbañceva sallapitapubbañca sākacchā ca samāpannapubbā. Tattha yādisako tesaṃ vaṇṇo hoti tādisako mayhaṃ vaṇṇo hoti, yādisako tesaṃ saro hoti tādisako mayhaṃ saro hoti. Dhammiyā ca kathāya sandassemi samādapemi samuttejemi sampahaṃsemi[V 3.0132]. Bhāsamānañca maṃ na jānanti – ‘ko nu kho ayaṃ bhāsati devo vā manusso vā’ti. Dhammiyā kathāya sandassetvā samādapetvā samuttejetvā sampahaṃsetvā antaradhāyāmi. Antarahitañca maṃ na jānanti – ‘ko nu kho ayaṃ antarahito devo vā manusso vā’”ti.

69. Monks, there are these eight assemblies. What eight? The assembly of nobles, the assembly of brahmins, the assembly of householders, the assembly of ascetics, the assembly of the devas of the Four Great Kings, the assembly of the Tāvatiṃsa devas, the assembly of Māra, and the assembly of Brahmā. I recall, monks, having approached many hundreds of assemblies of nobles. There, I have sat with them, conversed with them, and engaged in discussion with them before. Whatever their appearance was, such was my appearance; whatever their voice was, such was my voice. I instructed, roused, inspired, and gladdened them with a talk on the Dhamma. While I was speaking, they did not know me: ‘Who is this that speaks—a deva or a human?’ Having instructed, roused, inspired, and gladdened them with a talk on the Dhamma, I would vanish. And when I had vanished, they did not know me: ‘Who is this that has vanished—a deva or a human?’

“Abhijānāmi [P 4.0308] kho panāhaṃ, bhikkhave, anekasataṃ brāhmaṇaparisaṃ…pe… gahapatiparisaṃ… samaṇaparisaṃ… cātumahārājikaparisaṃ… tāvatiṃsaparisaṃ… māraparisaṃ… brahmaparisaṃ upasaṅkamitā. Tatrapi mayā sannisinnapubbañceva sallapitapubbañca sākacchā ca samāpannapubbā. Tattha yādisako tesaṃ vaṇṇo hoti tādisako mayhaṃ vaṇṇo hoti, yādisako tesaṃ saro hoti tādisako mayhaṃ saro hoti. Dhammiyā ca kathāya sandassemi samādapemi samuttejemi sampahaṃsemi. Bhāsamānañca maṃ na jānanti – ‘ko nu kho ayaṃ bhāsati devo vā manusso vā’ti. Dhammiyā kathāya sandassetvā samādapetvā samuttejetvā sampahaṃsetvā antaradhāyāmi. Antarahitañca maṃ na jānanti – ‘ko nu [T 4.0318] kho ayaṃ antarahito devo vā manusso vā’ti. Imā kho, bhikkhave, aṭṭha parisā”ti. Navamaṃ.

I recall, monks, having approached many hundreds of brahmin assemblies... householder assemblies... ascetic assemblies... assemblies of the devas of the Four Great Kings... assemblies of the Tāvatiṃsa devas... assemblies of Māra... and assemblies of Brahmā. There, I have sat with them, conversed with them, and engaged in discussion with them before. Whatever their appearance was, such was my appearance; whatever their voice was, such was my voice. I instructed, roused, inspired, and gladdened them with a talk on the Dhamma. While I was speaking, they did not know me: ‘Who is this that speaks—a deva or a human?’ Having instructed, roused, inspired, and gladdened them with a talk on the Dhamma, I would vanish. And when I had vanished, they did not know me: ‘Who is this that has vanished—a deva or a human?’ These, monks, are the eight assemblies.

10. Bhūmicālasuttaṃ

10. The Discourse on the Earthquake

70. Ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā vesāliyaṃ viharati mahāvane kūṭāgārasālāyaṃ. Atha kho bhagavā pubbaṇhasamayaṃ nivāsetvā pattacīvaramādāya vesāliṃ piṇḍāya pāvisi. Vesāliyaṃ piṇḍāya caritvā pacchābhattaṃ piṇḍapātapaṭikkanto āyasmantaṃ ānandaṃ āmantesi – “gaṇhāhi, ānanda, nisīdanaṃ. Yena cāpālaṃ cetiyaṃ pāvālacetiyaṃ (syā.), cāpālacetiyaṃ (pī. ka.) tenupasaṅkamissāma divāvihārāyā”ti. “Evaṃ, bhante”ti kho āyasmā ānando bhagavato paṭissutvā nisīdanaṃ ādāya bhagavantaṃ piṭṭhito piṭṭhito anubandhi.

70. On one occasion the Blessed One was dwelling at Vesālī in the Great Wood, at the Hall with the Peaked Roof. Then the Blessed One, having dressed in the morning, took his bowl and robe and entered Vesālī for alms. Having gone for alms in Vesālī, after the meal, on returning from the alms-round, he addressed the Venerable Ānanda: 'Take the sitting cloth, Ānanda. We shall approach the Cāpāla Shrine for the daytime abiding.' 'Yes, venerable sir,' the Venerable Ānanda replied to the Blessed One, and taking the sitting cloth, he followed closely behind the Blessed One.

Atha [M 3.0129] kho bhagavā yena cāpālaṃ cetiyaṃ tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā paññatte āsane nisīdi. Nisajja kho bhagavā āyasmantaṃ ānandaṃ āmantesi – “ramaṇīyā[P 4.0309], ānanda, vesālī, ramaṇīyaṃ udenaṃ cetiyaṃ, ramaṇīyaṃ gotamakaṃ cetiyaṃ, ramaṇīyaṃ sattambaṃ cetiyaṃ, ramaṇīyaṃ bahuputtakaṃ cetiyaṃ; ramaṇīyaṃ sārandadaṃ cetiyaṃ, ramaṇīyaṃ cāpālaṃ cetiyaṃ. Yassa kassaci[V 3.0133], ānanda, cattāro iddhipādā bhāvitā bahulīkatā yānīkatā vatthukatā anuṭṭhitā paricitā susamāraddhā, ākaṅkhamāno so, ānanda, kappaṃ vā tiṭṭheyya kappāvasesaṃ vā. Tathāgatassa kho, ānanda, cattāro iddhipādā bhāvitā bahulīkatā yānīkatā vatthukatā anuṭṭhitā paricitā susamāraddhā. Ākaṅkhamāno, ānanda, tathāgato kappaṃ vā tiṭṭheyya kappāvasesaṃ vā”ti. Evampi kho āyasmā ānando bhagavatā oḷārike nimitte kayiramāne oḷārike [T 4.0319] obhāse kayiramāne nāsakkhi paṭivijjhituṃ; na bhagavantaṃ yāci – “tiṭṭhatu, bhante, bhagavā kappaṃ, tiṭṭhatu sugato kappaṃ bahujanahitāya bahujanasukhāya lokānukampāya atthāya hitāya sukhāya devamanussāna”nti, yathā taṃ mārena pariyuṭṭhitacitto.

Then the Blessed One approached the Cāpāla Shrine; having approached, he sat down on the prepared seat. Having sat down, the Blessed One addressed the Venerable Ānanda: 'Delightful, Ānanda, is Vesālī; delightful is the Udena Shrine; delightful is the Gotamaka Shrine; delightful is the Sattamba Shrine; delightful is the Bahuputta Shrine; delightful is the Sārandada Shrine; delightful is the Cāpāla Shrine. For whosoever, Ānanda, the four bases of spiritual power have been developed, made much of, made a vehicle, made a foundation, established, mastered, and well undertaken, he, Ānanda, if he so desires, could remain for an aeon or for the remainder of an aeon. For the Tathāgata, Ānanda, the four bases of spiritual power have been developed, made much of, made a vehicle, made a foundation, established, mastered, and well undertaken. If he so desires, Ānanda, the Tathāgata could remain for an aeon or for the remainder of an aeon.' Even so, while this gross sign was being made by the Blessed One, while this gross hint was being made, the Venerable Ānanda was not able to penetrate it; he did not request the Blessed One: 'Venerable sir, may the Blessed One remain for an aeon, may the Sugata remain for an aeon, for the welfare of the many, for the happiness of the many, out of compassion for the world, for the good, welfare, and happiness of gods and humans,' just as one whose mind is overcome by Māra.

Dutiyampi kho bhagavā…pe… tatiyampi kho bhagavā āyasmantaṃ ānandaṃ āmantesi – “ramaṇīyā, ānanda, vesālī, ramaṇīyaṃ udenaṃ cetiyaṃ, ramaṇīyaṃ gotamakaṃ cetiyaṃ, ramaṇīyaṃ sattambaṃ cetiyaṃ, ramaṇīyaṃ bahuputtakaṃ cetiyaṃ, ramaṇīyaṃ sārandadaṃ cetiyaṃ, ramaṇīyaṃ cāpālaṃ cetiyaṃ. Yassa kassaci, ānanda, cattāro iddhipādā bhāvitā bahulīkatā yānīkatā vatthukatā anuṭṭhitā paricitā susamāraddhā, ākaṅkhamāno so, ānanda, kappaṃ vā tiṭṭheyya kappāvasesaṃ vā. Tathāgatassa kho, ānanda, cattāro iddhipādā bhāvitā…pe… ākaṅkhamāno, ānanda, tathāgato kappaṃ vā tiṭṭheyya kappāvasesaṃ vā”ti. Evampi kho āyasmā ānando bhagavatā [P 4.0310] oḷārike nimitte kayiramāne oḷārike obhāse kayiramāne nāsakkhi paṭivijjhituṃ; na bhagavantaṃ yāci – “tiṭṭhatu, bhante, bhagavā kappaṃ, tiṭṭhatu sugato kappaṃ bahujanahitāya bahujanasukhāya lokānukampāya atthāya hitāya sukhāya devamanussāna”nti, yathā taṃ mārena pariyuṭṭhitacitto.

For a second time… and for a third time, the Blessed One addressed the Venerable Ānanda: ‘Delightful, Ānanda, is Vesālī; delightful is the Udena Shrine; delightful is the Gotamaka Shrine; delightful is the Sattamba Shrine; delightful is the Bahuputta Shrine; delightful is the Sārandada Shrine; delightful is the Cāpāla Shrine. For whosoever, Ānanda, the four bases of spiritual power have been developed, made much of, made a vehicle, made a foundation, established, mastered, and well undertaken, he, Ānanda, if he so desires, could remain for an aeon or for the remainder of an aeon. For the Tathāgata, Ānanda, the four bases of spiritual power have been developed… If he so desires, Ānanda, the Tathāgata could remain for an aeon or for the remainder of an aeon.’ Even so, while this gross sign was being made by the Blessed One, while this gross hint was being given, the Venerable Ānanda was not able to penetrate it; he did not request the Blessed One: ‘Venerable sir, may the Blessed One remain for an aeon, may the Sugata remain for an aeon, for the welfare of the many, for the happiness of the many, out of compassion for the world, for the good, welfare, and happiness of gods and humans,’ as his mind was overcome by Māra.

Atha [M 3.0130] kho bhagavā āyasmantaṃ ānandaṃ āmantesi – “gaccha tvaṃ gaccha kho tvaṃ (saṃ. ni. 5.822) udā. 51 passitabbaṃ, ānanda, yassa dāni kālaṃ maññasī”ti. “Evaṃ, bhante”ti kho āyasmā ānando bhagavato paṭissutvā uṭṭhāyāsanā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā padakkhiṇaṃ katvā bhagavato avidūre aññatarasmiṃ rukkhamūle nisīdi. Atha kho māro pāpimā acirapakkante āyasmante ānande bhagavantaṃ [T 4.0320] etadavoca –

Then the Blessed One addressed the Venerable Ānanda: ‘You may go now, Ānanda, at the time you deem fit.’ ‘Yes, venerable sir,’ the Venerable Ānanda replied to the Blessed One, and having risen from his seat, paid homage to the Blessed One, circumambulated him keeping him to the right, and sat down at the root of a certain tree not far from the Blessed One. Then, not long after the Venerable Ānanda had departed, Māra the evil one said this to the Blessed One:

“Parinibbātu dāni, bhante, bhagavā, parinibbātu sugato. Parinibbānakālo dāni, bhante[V 3.0134], bhagavato. Bhāsitā kho panesā, bhante, bhagavatā vācā – ‘na tāvāhaṃ, pāpima, parinibbāyissāmi yāva me bhikkhū na sāvakā bhavissanti viyattā vinītā visāradā pattayogakkhemā idaṃ padaṃ dī. ni. 2.168 ca saṃ. ni. 5.822 ca na dissati bahussutā dhammadharā dhammānudhammappaṭipannā sāmīcippaṭipannā anudhammacārino, sakaṃ ācariyakaṃ uggahetvā ācikkhissanti desessanti paññapessanti paṭṭhapessanti vivarissanti vibhajissanti uttānīkarissanti uppannaṃ parappavādaṃ sahadhammena suniggahitaṃ niggahetvā sappāṭihāriyaṃ dhammaṃ desessantī’ti. Etarahi, bhante, bhikkhū bhagavato sāvakā viyattā vinītā visāradā pattayogakkhemā bahussutā dhammadharā dhammānudhammappaṭipannā sāmīcippaṭipannā anudhammacārino, sakaṃ ācariyakaṃ uggahetvā ācikkhanti desenti paññapenti paṭṭhapenti vivaranti vibhajanti uttānīkaronti uppannaṃ parappavādaṃ sahadhammena suniggahitaṃ niggahetvā sappāṭihāriyaṃ dhammaṃ desenti.

“Venerable sir, let the Blessed One now attain final Nibbāna, let the Sugata attain final Nibbāna. Now, venerable sir, is the time for the Blessed One's final Nibbāna. For, venerable sir, this word was spoken by the Blessed One: ‘I will not attain final Nibbāna, evil one, so long as my bhikkhu disciples are not accomplished, disciplined, confident, secure from bondage, learned, upholders of the Dhamma, practicing in accordance with the Dhamma, practicing fittingly, living in accordance with the Dhamma; so long as they have not, after learning their own teacher's doctrine, declared it, taught it, proclaimed it, established it, revealed it, analyzed it, and made it clear; and so long as they have not, after having well refuted with principle an arisen opposing doctrine, taught the Dhamma with its wonders.’ But now, venerable sir, the Blessed One's bhikkhu disciples are accomplished, disciplined, confident, secure from bondage, learned, upholders of the Dhamma, practicing in accordance with the Dhamma, practicing fittingly, living in accordance with the Dhamma; they have, after learning their own teacher's doctrine, declared it, taught it, proclaimed it, established it, revealed it, analyzed it, and made it clear; and they have, after having well refuted with principle an arisen opposing doctrine, taught the Dhamma with its wonders.”

“Parinibbātu dāni, bhante, bhagavā, parinibbātu sugato. Parinibbānakālo dāni, bhante, bhagavato. Bhāsitā kho panesā, bhante, bhagavatā vācā – ‘na tāvāhaṃ, pāpima, parinibbāyissāmi yāva me bhikkhuniyo na sāvikā bhavissanti…pe… yāva me upāsakā na sāvakā [P 4.0311] bhavissanti…pe… yāva me upāsikā na sāvikā bhavissanti viyattā vinītā visāradā pattayogakkhemā bahussutā dhammadharā dhammānudhammappaṭipannā sāmīcippaṭipannā anudhammacāriniyo, sakaṃ ācariyakaṃ uggahetvā [T 4.0321] ācikkhissanti desessanti paññapessanti paṭṭhapessanti vivarissanti vibhajissanti uttānīkarissanti, uppannaṃ parappavādaṃ sahadhammena suniggahitaṃ niggahetvā sappāṭihāriyaṃ dhammaṃ desessantī’ti. Etarahi, bhante, upāsikā bhagavato sāvikā viyattā vinītā visāradā pattayogakkhemā bahussutā [M 3.0131] dhammadharā dhammānudhammappaṭipannā sāmīcippaṭipannā anudhammacāriniyo, sakaṃ ācariyakaṃ uggahetvā ācikkhanti desenti paññapenti paṭṭhapenti vivaranti vibhajanti uttānīkaronti, uppannaṃ parappavādaṃ sahadhammena suniggahitaṃ niggahetvā sappāṭihāriyaṃ dhammaṃ desenti.

“Venerable sir, let the Blessed One now attain final Nibbāna; let the Sugata attain final Nibbāna. Now, venerable sir, is the time for the Blessed One’s final Nibbāna. For these words were spoken by the Blessed One: ‘I will not attain final Nibbāna yet, Evil One, so long as my bhikkhunī disciples have not become accomplished… and so on… so long as my upāsaka disciples have not become accomplished… and so on… so long as my upāsikā disciples have not become accomplished, disciplined, confident, assured, having attained security from bondage, learned, bearers of the Dhamma, practicing in accordance with the Dhamma, practicing properly, and living in conformity with the Dhamma; so long as, having learned their own teacher’s teaching, they do not declare it, teach it, make it known, establish it, disclose it, analyze it, and make it clear; and so long as, having thoroughly refuted with the Dhamma any opposing doctrine that has arisen, they do not teach the Dhamma with convincing power.’ But now, venerable sir, the Blessed One’s upāsikā disciples are accomplished, disciplined, confident, assured, having attained security from bondage, learned, bearers of the Dhamma, practicing in accordance with the Dhamma, practicing properly, and living in conformity with the Dhamma. Having learned their own teacher’s teaching, they declare it, teach it, make it known, establish it, disclose it, analyze it, and make it clear; and having thoroughly refuted with the Dhamma any opposing doctrine that has arisen, they teach the Dhamma with convincing power.”

“Parinibbātu dāni, bhante, bhagavā, parinibbātu sugato. Parinibbānakālo dāni, bhante, bhagavato. Bhāsitā kho panesā, bhante, bhagavatā vācā – ‘na tāvāhaṃ, pāpima, parinibbāyissāmi yāva me idaṃ brahmacariyaṃ na iddhañceva bhavissati phītañca vitthārikaṃ bāhujaññaṃ [V 3.0135] puthubhūtaṃ, yāva devamanussehi suppakāsita’nti. Etarahi, bhante, bhagavato brahmacariyaṃ iddhañceva phītañca vitthārikaṃ bāhujaññaṃ puthubhūtaṃ, yāva devamanussehi suppakāsitaṃ.

“Venerable sir, let the Blessed One now attain final Nibbāna, let the Sugata attain final Nibbāna. Now, venerable sir, is the time for the Blessed One’s final Nibbāna. For this saying was spoken by the Blessed One: ‘I will not attain final Nibbāna, evil one, as long as this holy life of mine is not successful, flourishing, widespread, widely known, extensive, and well-proclaimed among gods and humans.’ But now, venerable sir, the Blessed One’s holy life is successful, flourishing, widespread, widely known, extensive, and well-proclaimed among gods and humans.”

“Parinibbātu dāni, bhante, bhagavā, parinibbātu sugato. Parinibbānakālo dāni, bhante, bhagavato”ti. “Appossukko tvaṃ, pāpima, hohi. Naciraṃ tathāgatassa parinibbānaṃ bhavissati. Ito tiṇṇaṃ māsānaṃ accayena tathāgato parinibbāyissatī”ti.

“Venerable sir, let the Blessed One now attain final Nibbāna, let the Sugata attain final Nibbāna. Now is the time, venerable sir, for the Blessed One’s final Nibbāna.” “Be unconcerned, evil one. The Tathāgata’s final Nibbāna will not be long. In three months from now the Tathāgata will attain final Nibbāna.”

Atha kho bhagavā cāpāle cetiye sato sampajāno āyusaṅkhāraṃ ossaji. Ossaṭṭhe ca bhagavatā āyusaṅkhāre [T 4.0322] mahābhūmicālo ahosi bhiṃsanako salomahaṃso, devadundubhiyo ca phaliṃsu. Atha kho bhagavā etamatthaṃ viditvā tāyaṃ velāyaṃ imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –

Then the Blessed One, at the Cāpāla Shrine, mindful and clearly comprehending, relinquished the life-conditioning force. And when the Blessed One had relinquished the life-conditioning force, there was a great earthquake, terrifying and causing the hair to stand on end, and the celestial drums resounded. Then the Blessed One, understanding this matter, on that occasion uttered this inspired utterance:

“Tulamatulañca [P 4.0312] sambhavaṃ, bhavasaṅkhāramavassaji muni;

Ajjhattarato samāhito, abhindi kavacamivattasambhava”nti.

“The Sage relinquished the measurable and the immeasurable, the cause of existence, the formations of existence. Delighting inwardly, composed, he shattered what is born of self, like a coat of mail.”

Atha kho āyasmato ānandassa etadahosi – “mahā vatāyaṃ bhūmicālo; sumahā vatāyaṃ bhūmicālo bhiṃsanako salomahaṃso, devadundubhiyo ca phaliṃsu. Ko nu kho hetu, ko paccayo mahato bhūmicālassa pātubhāvāyā”ti?

Then it occurred to the Venerable Ānanda: “How great, indeed, is this earthquake! How very great is this earthquake, terrifying and causing the hair to stand on end, and the celestial drums resounded. What, now, is the cause, what is the condition for the appearance of a great earthquake?”

Atha kho āyasmā ānando yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinno kho āyasmā ānando bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “mahā vatāyaṃ, bhante, bhūmicālo[M 3.0132]; sumahā vatāyaṃ, bhante, bhūmicālo bhiṃsanako salomahaṃso, devadundubhiyo ca phaliṃsu. Ko nu kho, bhante, hetu, ko paccayo mahato bhūmicālassa pātubhāvāyā”ti?

Then the Venerable Ānanda approached the Blessed One; having approached, he paid homage to the Blessed One and sat down to one side. Sitting to one side, the Venerable Ānanda said to the Blessed One: “How great, venerable sir, is this earthquake! How very great, venerable sir, is this earthquake—terrifying, causing the hair to stand on end, and the celestial drums resounded. What, venerable sir, is the cause, what is the condition for the appearance of a great earthquake?”

“Aṭṭhime, ānanda, hetū, aṭṭha paccayā mahato bhūmicālassa pātubhāvāya. Katame aṭṭha? Ayaṃ, ānanda, mahāpathavī udake patiṭṭhitā; udakaṃ vāte patiṭṭhitaṃ; vāto ākāsaṭṭho hoti. So, ānanda, samayo yaṃ mahāvātā vāyanti; mahāvātā vāyantā udakaṃ kampenti; udakaṃ [V 3.0136] kampitaṃ pathaviṃ kampeti. Ayaṃ, ānanda, paṭhamo hetu, paṭhamo paccayo mahato bhūmicālassa pātubhāvāya.

“Ānanda, there are these eight causes, eight conditions for the appearance of a great earthquake. What are the eight? This great earth, Ānanda, is established on water; the water is established on wind; the wind is established in space. There comes a time, Ānanda, when great winds blow; the great winds, blowing, shake the water; the shaken water shakes the earth. This, Ānanda, is the first cause, the first condition for the appearance of a great earthquake.

“Puna caparaṃ, ānanda, samaṇo vā brāhmaṇo [T 4.0323] vā iddhimā cetovasippatto devatā vā mahiddhikā mahānubhāvā. Tassa parittā pathavīsaññā bhāvitā hoti, appamāṇā āposaññā. So imaṃ pathaviṃ kampeti saṅkampeti sampakampeti sampavedheti. Ayaṃ, ānanda, dutiyo hetu, dutiyo paccayo mahato bhūmicālassa pātubhāvāya.

“Furthermore, Ānanda, a recluse or a brahmin who is powerful and has attained mastery of mind, or a deity of great power and might, has cultivated a limited perception of earth and an immeasurable perception of water. He makes this earth shake, quake, tremble intensely, and quiver violently. This, Ānanda, is the second cause, the second condition for the appearance of a great earthquake.

“Puna caparaṃ, ānanda, yadā bodhisatto tusitā kāyā cavitvā sato sampajāno mātukucchiṃ okkamati, tadāyaṃ pathavī [P 4.0313] kampati saṅkampati sampakampati sampavedhati. Ayaṃ, ānanda, tatiyo hetu; tatiyo paccayo mahato bhūmicālassa pātubhāvāya.

“Furthermore, Ānanda, when the Bodhisatta passes away from the Tusita realm and descends into his mother’s womb, mindful and clearly comprehending, then this earth shakes, quakes, trembles intensely, and quivers violently. This, Ānanda, is the third cause, the third condition for the appearance of a great earthquake.

“Puna caparaṃ, ānanda, yadā bodhisatto sato sampajāno mātukucchismā nikkhamati, tadāyaṃ pathavī kampati saṅkampati sampakampati sampavedhati. Ayaṃ, ānanda, catuttho hetu, catuttho paccayo mahato bhūmicālassa pātubhāvāya.

“Furthermore, Ānanda, when the Bodhisatta, mindful and clearly comprehending, emerges from his mother’s womb, then this earth shakes, quakes, trembles intensely, and quivers violently. This, Ānanda, is the fourth cause, the fourth condition for the appearance of a great earthquake.

“Puna caparaṃ, ānanda, yadā tathāgato anuttaraṃ sammāsambodhiṃ abhisambujjhati, tadāyaṃ pathavī kampati saṅkampati sampakampati sampavedhati. Ayaṃ, ānanda, pañcamo hetu, pañcamo paccayo mahato bhūmicālassa pātubhāvāya.

“Furthermore, Ānanda, when the Tathāgata awakens to the unsurpassed perfect enlightenment, then this earth shakes, quakes, trembles intensely, and quivers violently. This, Ānanda, is the fifth cause, the fifth condition for the appearance of a great earthquake.

“Puna caparaṃ, ānanda, yadā tathāgato anuttaraṃ dhammacakkaṃ pavatteti, tadāyaṃ pathavī kampati saṅkampati sampakampati sampavedhati. Ayaṃ, ānanda, chaṭṭho hetu, chaṭṭho paccayo mahato bhūmicālassa pātubhāvāya.

“Furthermore, Ānanda, when the Tathāgata sets in motion the unsurpassed Wheel of Dhamma, then this earth shakes, quakes, trembles intensely, and quivers violently. This, Ānanda, is the sixth cause, the sixth condition for the appearance of a great earthquake.

“Puna [M 3.0133] caparaṃ, ānanda, yadā tathāgato sato sampajāno āyusaṅkhāraṃ ossajjati, tadāyaṃ pathavī kampati saṅkampati sampakampati sampavedhati. Ayaṃ, ānanda, sattamo hetu, sattamo paccayo mahato bhūmicālassa pātubhāvāya.

“Furthermore, Ānanda, when the Tathāgata, mindful and clearly comprehending, relinquishes the life-conditioning force, then this earth shakes, quakes, trembles intensely, and quivers violently. This, Ānanda, is the seventh cause, the seventh condition for the appearance of a great earthquake.

“Puna [V 3.0137] caparaṃ, ānanda, yadā tathāgato anupādisesāya nibbānadhātuyā [T 4.0324] parinibbāyati, tadāyaṃ pathavī kampati saṅkampati sampakampati sampavedhati. Ayaṃ, ānanda, aṭṭhamo hetu, aṭṭhamo paccayo mahato bhūmicālassa pātubhāvāya. Ime kho, ānanda, aṭṭha hetū, aṭṭha paccayā mahato bhūmicālassa pātubhāvāyā”ti. Dasamaṃ.

“Furthermore, Ānanda, when the Tathāgata attains final Nibbāna with the Nibbāna-element without remainder, then this earth shakes, quakes, trembles intensely, and quivers violently. This, Ānanda, is the eighth cause, the eighth condition for the appearance of a great earthquake. These, Ānanda, are the eight causes, the eight conditions for the appearance of a great earthquake.”

Bhūmicālavaggo dutiyo.

The Chapter on Earthquakes, the Second.

Tassuddānaṃ –

The summary verse for this chapter:

Icchā [P 4.0314] alañca saṃkhittaṃ, gayā abhibhunā saha;

Vimokkho dve ca vohārā, parisā bhūmicālenāti.

Desire, Enough, and Concise, Gayā along with Abhibhu; Liberation, the Two Expressions, the Assembly, and with the Earthquake.

(8) 3. Yamakavaggo

(8) 3. The Chapter of Pairs

1. Paṭhamasaddhāsuttaṃ

1. 1. The First Discourse on Faith

71. “Saddho [V 3.0138] ca saddho (syā.) ettheva. a. ni. 9.4, bhikkhave, bhikkhu hoti, no ca no (syā.) evamuparipi “no”tveva dissati sīlavā. Evaṃ so tenaṅgena aparipūro hoti. Tena taṃ aṅgaṃ paripūretabbaṃ – ‘kintāhaṃ saddho ca assaṃ sīlavā cā’ti. Yato ca kho, bhikkhave, bhikkhu saddho ca hoti sīlavā ca, evaṃ so tenaṅgena paripūro hoti.

71. “Bhikkhus, a bhikkhu might be faithful, but not virtuous. In that case, he is incomplete in that factor. That factor should be fulfilled by him, thinking: ‘How might I be both faithful and virtuous?’ When, bhikkhus, a bhikkhu is both faithful and virtuous, in that case, he is complete in that factor.

“Saddho ca, bhikkhave, bhikkhu hoti sīlavā ca, no ca bahussuto. Evaṃ so tenaṅgena aparipūro hoti. Tena taṃ aṅgaṃ paripūretabbaṃ – ‘kintāhaṃ saddho ca assaṃ, sīlavā ca, bahussuto cā’ti. Yato ca kho, bhikkhave, bhikkhu saddho ca hoti sīlavā ca bahussuto ca, evaṃ so tenaṅgena paripūro hoti.

“Bhikkhus, a bhikkhu might be faithful and virtuous, but not learned. In that case, he is incomplete in that factor. That factor should be fulfilled by him, thinking: ‘How might I be faithful, virtuous, and learned?’ When, bhikkhus, a bhikkhu is faithful, virtuous, and learned, in that case, he is complete in that factor.

“Saddho [M 3.0134] ca, bhikkhave, bhikkhu hoti sīlavā ca bahussuto ca, no ca dhammakathiko…pe… dhammakathiko ca, no ca parisāvacaro…pe… parisāvacaro ca, no ca visārado parisāya dhammaṃ deseti…pe… visārado ca parisāya dhammaṃ deseti, no ca catunnaṃ [T 4.0325] jhānānaṃ ābhicetasikānaṃ diṭṭhadhammasukhavihārānaṃ nikāmalābhī hoti akicchalābhī akasiralābhī…pe… catunnaṃ jhānānaṃ ābhicetasikānaṃ diṭṭhadhammasukhavihārānaṃ nikāmalābhī hoti akicchalābhī akasiralābhī, no ca āsavānaṃ khayā anāsavaṃ cetovimuttiṃ paññāvimuttiṃ diṭṭheva dhamme sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja viharati; evaṃ so tenaṅgena aparipūro hoti. Tena taṃ aṅgaṃ paripūretabbaṃ [P 4.0315] – ‘kintāhaṃ saddho ca assaṃ, sīlavā ca, bahussuto ca, dhammakathiko ca, parisāvacaro ca, visārado ca parisāya dhammaṃ deseyyaṃ, catunnañca jhānānaṃ ābhicetasikānaṃ diṭṭhadhammasukhavihārānaṃ nikāmalābhī assaṃ akicchalābhī akasiralābhī, āsavānañca khayā anāsavaṃ cetovimuttiṃ paññāvimuttiṃ diṭṭheva dhamme sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja vihareyya’”nti.

“Bhikkhus, a bhikkhu is faithful, virtuous, and learned, but is not a Dhamma-speaker… is a Dhamma-speaker, but does not frequent assemblies… frequents assemblies, but does not teach the Dhamma to an assembly with confidence… teaches the Dhamma to an assembly with confidence, but is not an obtainer at will, without trouble, and without difficulty of the four jhānas which are of the higher mind and are pleasant dwellings in this very life… is an obtainer of the four jhānas, but does not, through the destruction of the taints, realize for himself with direct knowledge, in this very life, the taintless liberation of mind, liberation by wisdom, and abide having attained it; in that case, he is incomplete in that factor. That factor should be fulfilled by him, thinking: ‘How might I be faithful, virtuous, learned, a Dhamma-speaker, one who frequents assemblies, one who teaches the Dhamma to an assembly with confidence, an obtainer at will, without trouble, and without difficulty of the four jhānas which are of the higher mind and are pleasant dwellings in this very life, and one who, through the destruction of the taints, realizes for himself with direct knowledge, in this very life, the taintless liberation of mind, liberation by wisdom, and abides having attained it?’”

“Yato [V 3.0139] ca kho, bhikkhave, bhikkhu saddho ca hoti, sīlavā ca, bahussuto ca, dhammakathiko ca, parisāvacaro ca, visārado ca parisāya dhammaṃ deseti, catunnañca jhānānaṃ ābhicetasikānaṃ diṭṭhadhammasukhavihārānaṃ nikāmalābhī hoti akicchalābhī akasiralābhī, āsavānañca khayā anāsavaṃ cetovimuttiṃ paññāvimuttiṃ diṭṭheva dhamme sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja viharati; evaṃ so tenaṅgena paripūro hoti. Imehi, kho, bhikkhave, aṭṭhahi dhammehi samannāgato bhikkhu samantapāsādiko ca hoti sabbākāraparipūro cā”ti. Paṭhamaṃ.

“When, bhikkhus, a bhikkhu is faithful, virtuous, learned, a Dhamma-speaker, one who frequents assemblies, one who teaches the Dhamma to an assembly with confidence, an obtainer at will, without trouble, and without difficulty of the four jhānas which are of the higher mind and are pleasant dwellings in this very life, and who, through the destruction of the taints, realizes for himself with direct knowledge, in this very life, the taintless liberation of mind, liberation by wisdom, and abides having attained it; in that case, he is complete in that factor. A bhikkhu who is endowed with these eight qualities, bhikkhus, is one who inspires confidence all around and is complete in all aspects.” The first.

2. Dutiyasaddhāsuttaṃ

2. The Second Discourse on Faith

72. “Saddho ca, bhikkhave, bhikkhu hoti, no ca sīlavā. Evaṃ so tenaṅgena [T 4.0326] aparipūro hoti. Tena taṃ aṅgaṃ paripūretabbaṃ – ‘kintāhaṃ saddho ca assaṃ sīlavā cā’ti. Yato ca kho, bhikkhave, bhikkhu saddho ca hoti sīlavā ca, evaṃ so tenaṅgena paripūro hoti.

72. “Bhikkhus, a bhikkhu might be faithful, but not virtuous. Thus he is incomplete in that factor. That factor should be fulfilled by him, thinking: ‘How might I be faithful and virtuous?’ When, bhikkhus, a bhikkhu is faithful and virtuous, thus he is complete in that factor.

“Saddho ca, bhikkhave, bhikkhu hoti sīlavā ca, no ca bahussuto…pe… bahussuto ca, no ca dhammakathiko…pe… dhammakathiko ca, no ca parisāvacaro…pe… parisāvacaro ca, no ca visārado parisāya dhammaṃ deseti [M 3.0135] …pe… [P 4.0316] visārado ca parisāya dhammaṃ deseti, no ca ye te santā vimokkhā atikkamma rūpe āruppā te kāyena phusitvā viharati…pe… ye te santā vimokkhā atikkamma rūpe āruppā te kāyena phusitvā viharati, no ca āsavānaṃ khayā anāsavaṃ cetovimuttiṃ paññāvimuttiṃ diṭṭheva dhamme sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja viharati; evaṃ so tenaṅgena aparipūro hoti. Tena taṃ aṅgaṃ paripūretabbaṃ – ‘kintāhaṃ saddho ca assaṃ, sīlavā ca, bahussuto ca, dhammakathiko ca, parisāvacaro ca, visārado ca parisāya dhammaṃ deseyyaṃ, ye te santā vimokkhā atikkamma rūpe āruppā te kāyena phusitvā vihareyyaṃ, āsavānañca khayā anāsavaṃ cetovimuttiṃ paññāvimuttiṃ diṭṭheva dhamme sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja vihareyya’”nti.

“Bhikkhus, a bhikkhu might be faithful and virtuous, but not learned… learned but not a speaker of the Dhamma… a speaker of the Dhamma but not one who frequents assemblies… one who frequents assemblies but not one who confidently teaches the Dhamma in an assembly… one who confidently teaches the Dhamma in an assembly but not one who dwells having touched with his body those peaceful liberations that transcend form, the formless attainments… one who dwells having touched with his body those peaceful liberations that transcend form, the formless attainments, but not one who, with the destruction of the taints, realizes for himself with direct knowledge in this very life the taintless liberation of mind and liberation by wisdom, and having attained it, dwells therein; thus he is incomplete in that factor. That factor should be fulfilled by him, thinking: ‘How might I be faithful, virtuous, learned, a speaker of the Dhamma, one who frequents assemblies, one who would confidently teach the Dhamma in an assembly, one who would dwell having touched with his body those peaceful liberations that transcend form, the formless attainments, and one who, with the destruction of the taints, would realize for himself with direct knowledge in this very life the taintless liberation of mind and liberation by wisdom, and having attained it, would dwell therein?’”

“Yato ca kho, bhikkhave, bhikkhu saddho ca hoti, sīlavā ca, bahussuto ca, dhammakathiko ca, parisāvacaro ca, visārado ca parisāya dhammaṃ deseti. Ye te santā vimokkhā [V 3.0140] atikkamma rūpe āruppā te ca kāyena phusitvā viharati, āsavānañca khayā…pe… sacchikatvā upasampajja viharati; evaṃ so tenaṅgena paripūro hoti. Imehi kho, bhikkhave, aṭṭhahi dhammehi samannāgato bhikkhu [T 4.0327] samantapāsādiko ca hoti sabbākāraparipūro cā”ti. Dutiyaṃ.

“And when, bhikkhus, a bhikkhu is faithful, virtuous, learned, a speaker of the Dhamma, one who frequents assemblies, and one who confidently teaches the Dhamma in an assembly; and he dwells having touched with his body those peaceful liberations that transcend form, the formless attainments; and with the destruction of the taints… having realized for himself with direct knowledge, he attains and dwells in the taintless liberation of mind, liberation by wisdom, in this very life; thus he is complete in that factor. Indeed, bhikkhus, a bhikkhu possessed of these eight qualities inspires confidence all around and is complete in all respects.” The second.

3. Paṭhamamaraṇassatisuttaṃ

3. The First Discourse on Mindfulness of Death

73. Ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā nātike nādike (sī. syā.), nāṭike (pī.) a. ni. 6.19 viharati giñjakāvasathe. Tatra kho bhagavā bhikkhū āmantesi – “bhikkhavo”ti[P 4.0317]. “Bhadante”ti te bhikkhū bhagavato paccassosuṃ. Bhagavā etadavoca – “maraṇassati, bhikkhave, bhāvitā bahulīkatā mahapphalā hoti mahānisaṃsā amatogadhā amatapariyosānā. Bhāvetha no tumhe, bhikkhave, maraṇassati”nti.

73. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Nātika in the brick hall. There the Blessed One addressed the bhikkhus: “Bhikkhus!” “Venerable sir,” those bhikkhus replied to the Blessed One. The Blessed One said this: “Bhikkhus, mindfulness of death, when developed and cultivated, is of great fruit and great benefit. It plunges into the Deathless, and culminates in the Deathless. You should cultivate mindfulness of death, bhikkhus.”

Evaṃ vutte aññataro bhikkhu bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “ahaṃ kho, bhante, bhāvemi maraṇassati”nti. “Yathā kathaṃ pana tvaṃ, bhikkhu, bhāvesi maraṇassati”nti? “Idha mayhaṃ, bhante, evaṃ hoti – ‘aho vatāhaṃ rattindivaṃ jīveyyaṃ[M 3.0136], bhagavato sāsanaṃ manasi kareyyaṃ, bahu bahuṃ (sī. pī.) vata me kataṃ assā’ti. Evaṃ kho ahaṃ, bhante, bhāvemi maraṇassati”nti.

When this was said, a certain bhikkhu said to the Blessed One: “Venerable sir, I do indeed cultivate mindfulness of death.” “But in what way, bhikkhu, do you cultivate mindfulness of death?” “Here, venerable sir, it occurs to me thus: ‘Oh, that I might live for a day and a night! I would attend to the Blessed One’s teaching. Much indeed would be accomplished by me.’ Thus, venerable sir, I cultivate mindfulness of death.”

Aññataropi kho bhikkhu bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “ahampi kho, bhante, bhāvemi maraṇassati”nti. “Yathā kathaṃ pana tvaṃ, bhikkhu, bhāvesi maraṇassati”nti? “Idha mayhaṃ, bhante, evaṃ hoti – ‘aho vatāhaṃ divasaṃ jīveyyaṃ, bhagavato sāsanaṃ manasi kareyyaṃ, bahu vata me kataṃ assā’ti. Evaṃ kho ahaṃ, bhante, bhāvemi maraṇassati”nti.

Another monk also said to the Blessed One: “Venerable sir, I too cultivate mindfulness of death.” “But in what way, monk, do you cultivate mindfulness of death?” “Here, venerable sir, it occurs to me thus: ‘Oh, that I might live for a day! I would attend to the Blessed One’s teaching. Much indeed would be accomplished by me.’ Thus, venerable sir, I cultivate mindfulness of death.”

Aññataropi kho bhikkhu bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “ahampi kho, bhante, bhāvemi maraṇassati”nti. “Yathā kathaṃ pana tvaṃ, bhikkhu, bhāvesi maraṇassati”nti? “Idha mayhaṃ, bhante, evaṃ hoti – ‘aho vatāhaṃ upaḍḍhadivasaṃ jīveyyaṃ, bhagavato [T 4.0328] sāsanaṃ manasi kareyyaṃ, bahu vata me kataṃ assā’ti. Evaṃ kho ahaṃ, bhante, bhāvemi maraṇassati”nti.

Another monk also said to the Blessed One: “Venerable sir, I too cultivate mindfulness of death.” “But in what way, monk, do you cultivate mindfulness of death?” “Here, venerable sir, it occurs to me thus: ‘Oh, that I might live for half a day! I would attend to the Blessed One’s teaching. Much indeed would be accomplished by me.’ Thus, venerable sir, I cultivate mindfulness of death.”

Aññataropi kho bhikkhu bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “ahampi kho, bhante, bhāvemi maraṇassati”nti[V 3.0141]. “Yathā kathaṃ pana tvaṃ, bhikkhu, bhāvesi maraṇassati”nti? “Idha mayhaṃ, bhante, evaṃ hoti – ‘aho vatāhaṃ tadantaraṃ jīveyyaṃ yadantaraṃ ekapiṇḍapātaṃ bhuñjāmi, bhagavato sāsanaṃ manasi kareyyaṃ, bahu vata me kataṃ assā’ti. Evaṃ kho ahaṃ, bhante, bhāvemi maraṇassati”nti.

Another monk also said to the Blessed One: “Venerable sir, I too cultivate mindfulness of death.” “But in what way, monk, do you cultivate mindfulness of death?” “Here, venerable sir, it occurs to me thus: ‘Oh, that I might live for the time it takes to eat one alms-meal! I would attend to the Blessed One’s teaching. Much indeed would be accomplished by me.’ Thus, venerable sir, I cultivate mindfulness of death.”

Aññataropi kho bhikkhu bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “ahampi kho, bhante, bhāvemi maraṇassati”nti. “Yathā kathaṃ pana tvaṃ, bhikkhu, bhāvesi maraṇassati”nti? “Idha mayhaṃ[P 4.0318], bhante, evaṃ hoti – ‘aho vatāhaṃ tadantaraṃ jīveyyaṃ yadantaraṃ upaḍḍhapiṇḍapātaṃ bhuñjāmi, bhagavato sāsanaṃ manasi kareyyaṃ, bahu vata me kataṃ assā’ti. Evaṃ kho ahaṃ, bhante, bhāvemi maraṇassati”nti.

Another monk also said to the Blessed One: “Venerable sir, I too cultivate mindfulness of death.” “But in what way, monk, do you cultivate mindfulness of death?” “Here, venerable sir, it occurs to me thus: ‘Oh, that I might live for the time it takes to eat half an alms-meal! I would attend to the Blessed One’s teaching. Much indeed would be accomplished by me.’ Thus, venerable sir, I cultivate mindfulness of death.”

Aññataropi kho bhikkhu bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “ahampi kho, bhante, bhāvemi maraṇassati”nti. “Yathā kathaṃ pana tvaṃ, bhikkhu, bhāvesi maraṇassati”nti? “Idha mayhaṃ, bhante, evaṃ hoti – ‘aho vatāhaṃ tadantaraṃ jīveyyaṃ yadantaraṃ cattāro pañca ālope saṅkhāditvā saṅkharitvā (ka.) ajjhoharāmi, bhagavato [M 3.0137] sāsanaṃ manasi kareyyaṃ, bahu vata me kataṃ assā’ti. Evaṃ kho ahaṃ, bhante, bhāvemi maraṇassati”nti.

Another monk also said to the Blessed One: “Venerable sir, I too cultivate mindfulness of death.” “But in what way, monk, do you cultivate mindfulness of death?” “Here, venerable sir, it occurs to me thus: ‘Oh, that I might live for the time it takes to chew and swallow four or five morsels! I would attend to the Blessed One’s teaching. Much indeed would be accomplished by me.’ Thus, venerable sir, I cultivate mindfulness of death.”

Aññataropi kho bhikkhu bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “ahampi kho, bhante, bhāvemi [T 4.0329] maraṇassati”nti. “Yathā kathaṃ pana tvaṃ, bhikkhu, bhāvesi maraṇassati”nti? “Idha mayhaṃ, bhante, evaṃ hoti – ‘aho vatāhaṃ tadantaraṃ jīveyyaṃ yadantaraṃ ekaṃ ālopaṃ saṅkhāditvā saṅkharitvā (ka.) ajjhoharāmi, bhagavato sāsanaṃ manasi kareyyaṃ, bahu vata me kataṃ assā’ti. Evaṃ kho ahaṃ, bhante, bhāvemi maraṇassati”nti.

Another monk also said to the Blessed One: “Venerable sir, I too cultivate mindfulness of death.” “But in what way, monk, do you cultivate mindfulness of death?” “Here, venerable sir, it occurs to me thus: ‘Oh, that I might live for the time it takes to chew and swallow a single morsel! I would attend to the Blessed One’s teaching. Much indeed would be accomplished by me.’ Thus, venerable sir, I cultivate mindfulness of death.”

Aññataropi kho bhikkhu bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “ahampi kho, bhante, bhāvemi maraṇassati”nti. “Yathā kathaṃ pana tvaṃ, bhikkhu, bhāvesi maraṇassati”nti? “Idha mayhaṃ bhante, evaṃ hoti – ‘aho vatāhaṃ tadantaraṃ jīveyyaṃ yadantaraṃ assasitvā vā passasāmi, passasitvā vā assasāmi, bhagavato sāsanaṃ manasi kareyyaṃ, bahu vata me kataṃ assā’ti. Evaṃ kho ahaṃ, bhante bhāvemi maraṇassati”nti.

Another monk also said to the Blessed One: “Venerable sir, I too cultivate mindfulness of death.” “But in what way, monk, do you cultivate mindfulness of death?” “Here, venerable sir, it occurs to me thus: ‘Oh, that I might live for the time it takes to breathe out after breathing in, or to breathe in after breathing out! I would attend to the Blessed One’s teaching. Much indeed would be accomplished by me.’ Thus, venerable sir, I cultivate mindfulness of death.”

Evaṃ [V 3.0142] vutte bhagavā te bhikkhū etadavoca – “yvāyaṃ yo cāyaṃ (ka. sī.), bhikkhave, bhikkhu evaṃ maraṇassatiṃ bhāveti – ‘aho vatāhaṃ rattindivaṃ jīveyyaṃ, bhagavato sāsanaṃ manasi kareyyaṃ, bahu vata me kataṃ assā’ti. Yo cāyaṃ yo pāyaṃ (ka.) a. ni. 6.19 passitabbaṃ, bhikkhave, bhikkhu evaṃ maraṇassatiṃ bhāveti – ‘aho vatāhaṃ [P 4.0319] divasaṃ jīveyyaṃ, bhagavato sāsanaṃ manasi kareyyaṃ, bahu vata me kataṃ assā’ti; yo cāyaṃ yo pāyaṃ (ka.) a. ni. 6.19 passitabbaṃ, bhikkhave, bhikkhu evaṃ maraṇassatiṃ bhāveti – ‘aho vatāhaṃ upaḍḍhadivasaṃ jīveyyaṃ, bhagavato sāsanaṃ manasi kareyyaṃ, bahu vata me kataṃ assā’ti. Yo cāyaṃ yo pāyaṃ (ka.) a. ni. 6.19 passitabbaṃ, bhikkhave, bhikkhu evaṃ maraṇassatiṃ bhāveti – ‘aho vatāhaṃ tadantaraṃ jīveyyaṃ yadantaraṃ ekapiṇḍapātaṃ bhuñjāmi, bhagavato [T 4.0330] sāsanaṃ manasi kareyyaṃ, bahu vata me kataṃ assā’ti; yo cāyaṃ yo pāyaṃ (ka.) a. ni. 6.19 passitabbaṃ, bhikkhave, bhikkhu evaṃ maraṇassatiṃ bhāveti – ‘aho vatāhaṃ tadantaraṃ jīveyyaṃ yadantaraṃ upaḍḍhapiṇḍapātaṃ bhuñjāmi, bhagavato sāsanaṃ manasi kareyyaṃ, bahu vata me kataṃ assā’ti. Yo cāyaṃ yo pāyaṃ (ka.) a. ni. 6.19 passitabbaṃ, bhikkhave, bhikkhu evaṃ maraṇassatiṃ bhāveti – ‘aho vatāhaṃ tadantaraṃ [M 3.0138] jīveyyaṃ yadantaraṃ cattāro pañca ālope saṅkhāditvā ajjhoharāmi, bhagavato sāsanaṃ manasi kareyyaṃ, bahu vata me kataṃ assā’ti – ime vuccanti, bhikkhave, ‘bhikkhū pamattā viharanti, dandhaṃ maraṇassatiṃ bhāventi āsavānaṃ khayāya’”.

When this was said, the Blessed One said this to those monks: “Monks, that monk who develops mindfulness of death thus: ‘Oh, that I might live for a day and a night! I would attend to the Blessed One’s teaching. Much indeed would be accomplished by me!’; and that monk who develops mindfulness of death thus: ‘Oh, that I might live for a day! I would attend to the Blessed One’s teaching. Much indeed would be accomplished by me!’; and that monk who develops mindfulness of death thus: ‘Oh, that I might live for half a day! I would attend to the Blessed One’s teaching. Much indeed would be accomplished by me!’; and that monk who develops mindfulness of death thus: ‘Oh, that I might live for the time it takes to eat one alms-meal! I would attend to the Blessed One’s teaching. Much indeed would be accomplished by me!’; and that monk who develops mindfulness of death thus: ‘Oh, that I might live for the time it takes to eat half an alms-meal! I would attend to the Blessed One’s teaching. Much indeed would be accomplished by me!’; and that monk who develops mindfulness of death thus: ‘Oh, that I might live for the time it takes to chew and swallow four or five morsels! I would attend to the Blessed One’s teaching. Much indeed would be accomplished by me!’—these, monks, are called monks who dwell heedlessly, who slowly develop mindfulness of death for the destruction of the taints.”

“Yo ca khvāyaṃ yo cāyaṃ (syā.), yo ca kho yaṃ (ka.), bhikkhave, bhikkhu evaṃ maraṇassatiṃ bhāveti – ‘aho vatāhaṃ tadantaraṃ jīveyyaṃ yadantaraṃ ekaṃ ālopaṃ saṅkhāditvā ajjhoharāmi, bhagavato sāsanaṃ manasi kareyyaṃ, bahu vata me kataṃ assā’ti. Yo cāyaṃ yo pāyaṃ (ka.), bhikkhave, bhikkhu evaṃ maraṇassatiṃ bhāveti – ‘aho vatāhaṃ tadantaraṃ jīveyyaṃ yadantaraṃ assasitvā vā passasāmi, passasitvā vā assasāmi, bhagavato sāsanaṃ manasi kareyyaṃ, bahu vata me kataṃ assā’ti – ime vuccanti, bhikkhave, ‘bhikkhū appamattā viharanti, tikkhaṃ maraṇassatiṃ bhāventi āsavānaṃ khayāya’”.

“Bhikkhus, any monk who develops mindfulness of death thus: ‘Oh, that I might live for the time it takes to chew and swallow a single morsel! I would attend to the Blessed One’s teaching. Much indeed would be accomplished by me!’; and any monk who develops mindfulness of death thus: ‘Oh, that I might live for the time it takes to breathe in after breathing out, or to breathe out after breathing in! I would attend to the Blessed One’s teaching. Much indeed would be accomplished by me!’—these, Bhikkhus, are called monks who dwell heedfully, who keenly develop mindfulness of death for the destruction of the taints.”

“Tasmātiha, bhikkhave, evaṃ sikkhitabbaṃ – ‘appamattā viharissāma, tikkhaṃ maraṇassatiṃ bhāvayissāma āsavānaṃ khayāyā’ti. Evañhi vo, bhikkhave, sikkhitabba”nti. Tatiyaṃ.

“Therefore, Bhikkhus, you should train thus: ‘We will dwell heedfully; we will keenly develop mindfulness of death for the destruction of the taints.’ Thus indeed, Bhikkhus, should you train.”

4. Dutiyamaraṇassatisuttaṃ

4. The Second Discourse on Mindfulness of Death

74. Ekaṃ [P 4.0320] samayaṃ bhagavā nātike viharati giñjakāvasathe. Tatra [T 4.0331] kho bhagavā bhikkhū āmantesi [V 3.0143] …pe… maraṇassati, bhikkhave, bhāvitā bahulīkatā mahapphalā hoti mahānisaṃsā amatogadhā amatapariyosānā.

74. On one occasion the Blessed One was dwelling at Nātika in the Brick Hall. There the Blessed One addressed the bhikkhus… “Mindfulness of death, Bhikkhus, when developed and cultivated, is of great fruit and great benefit, it is grounded in the Deathless, and has the Deathless as its culmination.

“Kathaṃ bhāvitā ca, bhikkhave, maraṇassati kathaṃ bahulīkatā mahapphalā hoti mahānisaṃsā amatogadhā amatapariyosānā? Idha, bhikkhave, bhikkhu divase nikkhante rattiyā patihitāya paṭihitāya (pī.), (a. ni. 6.20 passitabbaṃ) iti paṭisañcikkhati – ‘bahukā kho me paccayā maraṇassa – ahi vā maṃ ḍaṃseyya, vicchiko vā maṃ ḍaṃseyya, satapadī vā maṃ ḍaṃseyya; tena me assa kālakiriyā. So mama assa mamassa (a. ni. 6.20) antarāyo. Upakkhalitvā vā papateyyaṃ, bhattaṃ vā me bhuttaṃ byāpajjeyya, pittaṃ vā me kuppeyya, semhaṃ vā me kuppeyya, satthakā vā me vātā kuppeyyuṃ[M 3.0139], manussā vā maṃ upakkameyyuṃ, amanussā vā maṃ upakkameyyuṃ; tena me assa kālakiriyā. So mama assa antarāyo’ti. Tena, bhikkhave, bhikkhunā iti paṭisañcikkhitabbaṃ – ‘atthi nu kho me pāpakā akusalā dhammā appahīnā ye me assu rattiṃ kālaṃ karontassa antarāyāyā’”ti.

“And how, Bhikkhus, is mindfulness of death developed and cultivated so that it is of great fruit and great benefit, is grounded in the Deathless, and has the Deathless as its culmination? Here, Bhikkhus, when the day has passed and night has arrived, a bhikkhu reflects thus: ‘The causes for my death are indeed many. A snake might bite me, a scorpion might bite me, or a centipede might bite me; through that, my death would occur. That would be an obstacle for me. Or I might stumble and fall; or the food I have eaten might cause affliction; or my bile might be provoked; or my phlegm might be provoked; or piercing winds within me might be provoked; or humans might attack me; or non-human beings might attack me; through that, my death would occur. That would be an obstacle for me.’ Then, Bhikkhus, that bhikkhu should reflect thus: ‘Are there any evil, unwholesome states in me that have not been abandoned, which might be an obstacle for me if I were to die tonight?’”

“Sace, bhikkhave, bhikkhu paccavekkhamāno evaṃ jānāti – ‘atthi me pāpakā akusalā dhammā appahīnā ye me assu rattiṃ kālaṃ karontassa antarāyāyā’ti, tena, bhikkhave, bhikkhunā tesaṃyeva pāpakānaṃ akusalānaṃ dhammānaṃ pahānāya adhimatto chando ca vāyāmo ca ussāho ca ussoḷhī ca appaṭivānī ca sati ca sampajaññañca karaṇīyaṃ.

“If, Bhikkhus, a bhikkhu, upon reviewing, knows thus: ‘There are evil, unwholesome states in me not yet abandoned, which would be an obstacle for me if I were to die at night,’ then, Bhikkhus, that bhikkhu should apply extraordinary desire, effort, exertion, zeal, persistence, mindfulness, and clear comprehension for the abandoning of those very evil, unwholesome states.

“Seyyathāpi, bhikkhave, ādittacelo vā ādittasīso vā tasseva celassa vā sīsassa vā nibbāpanāya adhimattaṃ chandañca vāyāmañca ussāhañca ussoḷhiñca [T 4.0332] appaṭivāniñca satiñca sampajaññañca kareyya; evamevaṃ kho, bhikkhave, tena bhikkhunā tesaṃyeva pāpakānaṃ akusalānaṃ [P 4.0321] dhammānaṃ pahānāya adhimatto chando ca vāyāmo ca ussāho ca ussoḷhī ca appaṭivānī ca sati ca sampajaññañca karaṇīyaṃ.

“Just as, Bhikkhus, a person whose robe or head was on fire would apply extraordinary desire, effort, exertion, zeal, persistence, mindfulness, and clear comprehension to extinguish that very robe or head; even so, Bhikkhus, that bhikkhu should apply extraordinary desire, effort, exertion, zeal, persistence, mindfulness, and clear comprehension to abandon those very evil, unwholesome states.

“Sace pana, bhikkhave, bhikkhu paccavekkhamāno evaṃ jānāti – ‘natthi me pāpakā akusalā dhammā appahīnā ye me assu rattiṃ kālaṃ karontassa antarāyāyā’ti[T 4.0333], tena, bhikkhave, bhikkhunā teneva pītipāmojjena vihātabbaṃ ahorattānusikkhinā kusalesu dhammesu.

“But if, Bhikkhus, a bhikkhu, upon reviewing, knows thus: ‘There are no evil, unwholesome states unabandoned in me which would be an obstacle for me if I were to die at night,’ then, Bhikkhus, that bhikkhu should abide in that very joy and gladness, training day and night in wholesome states.

“Idha [V 3.0144] pana, bhikkhave, bhikkhu rattiyā nikkhantāya divase patihite iti paṭisañcikkhati – ‘bahukā kho me paccayā maraṇassa – ahi vā maṃ ḍaṃseyya, vicchiko vā maṃ ḍaṃseyya, satapadī vā maṃ ḍaṃseyya; tena me assa kālakiriyā. So mama assa antarāyo. Upakkhalitvā vā papateyyaṃ, bhattaṃ vā me bhuttaṃ byāpajjeyya, pittaṃ vā me kuppeyya, semhaṃ vā me kuppeyya, satthakā vā me vātā kuppeyyuṃ, manussā vā maṃ upakkameyyuṃ, amanussā vā maṃ upakkameyyuṃ; tena me assa kālakiriyā. So mama assa antarāyo’ti. Tena, bhikkhave, bhikkhunā iti paṭisañcikkhitabbaṃ – ‘atthi nu kho me pāpakā akusalā dhammā appahīnā ye me assu divā kālaṃ karontassa antarāyāyā’”ti.

“Furthermore, Bhikkhus, here a bhikkhu, when the night has passed and the day has arrived, reflects thus: ‘The causes for my death are indeed many. A snake might bite me, a scorpion might bite me, or a centipede might bite me; through that, my death would occur. That would be an obstacle for me. Or I might stumble and fall; or the food I have eaten might cause affliction; or my bile might be provoked; or my phlegm might be provoked; or piercing winds within me might be provoked; or humans might attack me; or non-human beings might attack me; through that, my death would occur. That would be an obstacle for me.’ Then, Bhikkhus, that bhikkhu should reflect thus: ‘Are there any evil, unwholesome states in me that have not been abandoned, which would be an obstacle for me if I were to die during the day?’”

“Sace[M 3.0140], bhikkhave, bhikkhu paccavekkhamāno evaṃ jānāti – ‘atthi me pāpakā akusalā dhammā appahīnā ye me assu divā kālaṃ karontassa antarāyāyā’ti, tena, bhikkhave, bhikkhunā tesaṃyeva pāpakānaṃ akusalānaṃ dhammānaṃ pahānāya adhimatto chando ca vāyāmo ca ussāho ca ussoḷhī ca appaṭivānī ca sati ca sampajaññañca karaṇīyaṃ.

“If, Bhikkhus, a bhikkhu, upon reviewing, knows thus: ‘There are evil, unwholesome states in me not yet abandoned, which would be an obstacle for me if I were to die during the day,’ then, Bhikkhus, that bhikkhu should apply extraordinary desire, effort, exertion, zeal, persistence, mindfulness, and clear comprehension for the abandoning of those very evil, unwholesome states.
