“Seyyathāpi, bhikkhave, ādittacelo vā ādittasīso vā tasseva celassa vā sīsassa vā nibbāpanāya adhimattaṃ chandañca vāyāmañca ussāhañca ussoḷhiñca appaṭivāniñca satiñca sampajaññañca kareyya; evamevaṃ kho, bhikkhave, tena bhikkhunā tesaṃyeva pāpakānaṃ akusalānaṃ dhammānaṃ pahānāya adhimatto chando ca vāyāmo ca ussāho ca ussoḷhī ca appaṭivānī ca sati ca sampajaññañca karaṇīyaṃ.

“Just as, Bhikkhus, a person whose robe or head was on fire would apply extraordinary desire, effort, exertion, zeal, persistence, mindfulness, and clear comprehension to extinguish that very robe or head; even so, Bhikkhus, that bhikkhu should apply extraordinary desire, effort, exertion, zeal, persistence, mindfulness, and clear comprehension to abandon those very evil, unwholesome states.”

“Sace pana, bhikkhave, bhikkhu paccavekkhamāno evaṃ jānāti – ‘natthi me pāpakā akusalā dhammā appahīnā [P 4.0322] ye me assu divā kālaṃ karontassa antarāyāyā’ti, tena, bhikkhave, bhikkhunā teneva pītipāmojjena vihātabbaṃ ahorattānusikkhinā kusalesu dhammesu. Evaṃ bhāvitā kho, bhikkhave, maraṇassati evaṃ bahulīkatā mahapphalā hoti mahānisaṃsā amatogadhā amatapariyosānā”ti. Catutthaṃ.

“But if, Bhikkhus, a bhikkhu, upon reflection, knows thus: ‘There are no evil, unwholesome states unabandoned in me that would be an obstacle for me if I were to die during the day,’ then, Bhikkhus, that bhikkhu should abide in that very joy and gladness, training day and night in wholesome states. Thus developed, Bhikkhus, and thus cultivated, mindfulness of death is of great fruit, of great benefit, plunging into the Deathless, culminating in the Deathless.” Fourth.

5. Paṭhamasampadāsuttaṃ

5. The First Discourse on Accomplishments

75. “Aṭṭhimā[V 3.0145], bhikkhave, sampadā. Katamā aṭṭha? a. ni. 8.54 Uṭṭhānasampadā, ārakkhasampadā, kalyāṇamittatā, samajīvitā, saddhāsampadā, sīlasampadā, cāgasampadā, paññāsampadā – imā kho, bhikkhave, aṭṭha sampadā”ti.

75. “Bhikkhus, there are these eight accomplishments. What eight? The accomplishment of diligence, the accomplishment of protection, good friendship, a balanced livelihood, the accomplishment of faith, the accomplishment of virtue, the accomplishment of generosity, and the accomplishment of wisdom. These, Bhikkhus, are the eight accomplishments.”

“Uṭṭhātā kammadheyyesu, appamatto vidhānavā;

Samaṃ kappeti jīvikaṃ, sambhataṃ anurakkhati.

“One who is diligent in their work, heedful and methodical, maintains a balanced livelihood and guards the wealth that has been gathered.

“Saddho [T 4.0334] sīlena sampanno, vadaññū vītamaccharo;

Niccaṃ maggaṃ visodheti, sotthānaṃ samparāyikaṃ.

“Endowed with faith and virtue, generous and free from stinginess, one constantly purifies the path to well-being in the next life.

“Iccete aṭṭha dhammā ca, saddhassa gharamesino;

Akkhātā saccanāmena, ubhayattha sukhāvahā.

“Thus these eight qualities for a person of faith who seeks the household life have been declared by the one whose name is Truth; they bring happiness in both spheres.

“Diṭṭhadhammahitatthāya, samparāyasukhāya ca;

Evametaṃ gahaṭṭhānaṃ, cāgo puññaṃ pavaḍḍhatī”ti. pañcamaṃ;

“For one’s welfare and benefit in this present life and for happiness in the next, thus for householders, generosity and merit increase.” Fifth.

6. Dutiyasampadāsuttaṃ

6. The Second Discourse on Accomplishments

76. “Aṭṭhimā[M 3.0141], bhikkhave, sampadā. Katamā aṭṭha? Uṭṭhānasampadā, ārakkhasampadā, kalyāṇamittatā, samajīvitā, saddhāsampadā, sīlasampadā, cāgasampadā, paññāsampadā. Katamā ca, bhikkhave, uṭṭhānasampadā? Idha, bhikkhave, kulaputto yena kammaṭṭhānena jīvitaṃ kappeti – yadi kasiyā yadi vaṇijjāya yadi gorakkhena yadi issattena yadi rājaporisena yadi sippaññatarena – tattha [P 4.0323] dakkho hoti analaso, tatrupāyāya vīmaṃsāya samannāgato, alaṃ kātuṃ alaṃ saṃvidhātunti. Ayaṃ vuccati, bhikkhave, uṭṭhānasampadā.

76. “Bhikkhus, there are these eight accomplishments. What eight? The accomplishment of diligence, the accomplishment of protection, good friendship, a balanced livelihood, the accomplishment of faith, the accomplishment of virtue, the accomplishment of generosity, and the accomplishment of wisdom. And what, Bhikkhus, is the accomplishment of diligence? Here, Bhikkhus, a person of good family makes a living by some occupation—whether by agriculture, by trade, by cattle-herding, by archery, by royal service, or by some other craft. In that, they are skilled and not lazy, endowed with investigative wisdom into its methods, able to do it and to manage it. This, Bhikkhus, is called the accomplishment of diligence.

“Katamā ca, bhikkhave, ārakkhasampadā? Idha, bhikkhave, kulaputtassa bhogā honti uṭṭhānavīriyādhigatā [V 3.0146] bāhābalaparicitā sedāvakkhittā dhammikā dhammaladdhā te ārakkhena guttiyā sampādeti – ‘kinti me bhoge neva rājāno hareyyuṃ, na corā hareyyuṃ, na aggi ḍaheyya, na udakaṃ vaheyya, na appiyā dāyādā hareyyu’nti. Ayaṃ vuccati, bhikkhave, ārakkhasampadā.

“And what, Bhikkhus, is the accomplishment of protection? Here, Bhikkhus, a person of good family has wealth acquired through diligence and effort, amassed by the strength of their arms, earned by the dripping of sweat, righteous and righteously obtained. They bring it to accomplishment by guarding and protecting it, thinking: ‘How can I prevent my wealth from being taken by kings or thieves, from being burned by fire, from being carried away by water, or from being taken by unloved heirs?’ This, Bhikkhus, is called the accomplishment of protection.

“Katamā [T 4.0335] ca, bhikkhave, kalyāṇamittatā? Idha, bhikkhave, kulaputto yasmiṃ gāme vā nigame vā paṭivasati, tattha ye te honti gahapatī vā gahapatiputtā vā daharā vā vuddhasīlino vuddhā vā vuddhasīlino saddhāsampannā sīlasampannā cāgasampannā paññāsampannā, tehi saddhiṃ santiṭṭhati sallapati sākacchaṃ samāpajjati; yathārūpānaṃ saddhāsampannānaṃ saddhāsampadaṃ anusikkhati, yathārūpānaṃ sīlasampannānaṃ sīlasampadaṃ anusikkhati, yathārūpānaṃ cāgasampannānaṃ cāgasampadaṃ anusikkhati, yathārūpānaṃ paññāsampannānaṃ paññāsampadaṃ anusikkhati. Ayaṃ vuccati, bhikkhave, kalyāṇamittatā.

“And what, Bhikkhus, is good friendship? Here, Bhikkhus, a person of good family resides in a certain village or town. There, whatever householders or householders’ children there may be, whether young or old, who are mature in virtue, accomplished in faith, accomplished in virtue, accomplished in generosity, and accomplished in wisdom—one associates with them, converses with them, and engages in discussion with them. One trains in imitation of the accomplishment of faith of those accomplished in faith; one trains in imitation of the accomplishment of virtue of those accomplished in virtue; one trains in imitation of the accomplishment of generosity of those accomplished in generosity; one trains in imitation of the accomplishment of wisdom of those accomplished in wisdom. This, Bhikkhus, is called good friendship.

“Katamā ca, bhikkhave, samajīvitā? Idha, bhikkhave, kulaputto āyañca bhogānaṃ viditvā vayañca bhogānaṃ viditvā samaṃ jīvikaṃ kappeti nāccogāḷhaṃ nātihīnaṃ – ‘evaṃ me āyo vayaṃ pariyādāya ṭhassati, na ca me vayo āyaṃ pariyādāya ṭhassatī’ti. Seyyathāpi, bhikkhave, tulādhāro vā tulādhārantevāsī vā tulaṃ paggahetvā jānāti – ‘ettakena vā onataṃ, ettakena [P 4.0324] vā unnata’nti; evamevaṃ kho, bhikkhave, kulaputto āyañca bhogānaṃ viditvā vayañca bhogānaṃ viditvā samaṃ jīvikaṃ kappeti nāccogāḷhaṃ nātihīnaṃ – ‘evaṃ me āyo vayaṃ pariyādāya ṭhassati, na ca me vayo āyaṃ pariyādāya ṭhassatī’ti. Sacāyaṃ, bhikkhave, kulaputto [M 3.0142] appāyo samāno uḷāraṃ jīvikaṃ kappeti, tassa bhavanti vattāro ‘udumbarakhādī vāyaṃ kulaputto bhoge khādatī’ti. Sace panāyaṃ, bhikkhave, kulaputto [T 4.0336] mahāyo samāno kasiraṃ jīvikaṃ kappeti, tassa bhavanti vattāro – ‘ajeṭṭhamaraṇaṃ vāyaṃ kulaputto marissatī’ti. Yato ca khoyaṃ, bhikkhave, kulaputto āyañca bhogānaṃ viditvā vayañca bhogānaṃ viditvā samaṃ jīvikaṃ kappeti nāccogāḷhaṃ nātihīnaṃ – ‘evaṃ me āyo vayaṃ pariyādāya ṭhassati, na ca me vayo āyaṃ pariyādāya ṭhassatī’ti. Ayaṃ vuccati, bhikkhave, samajīvitā.

“And what, Bhikkhus, is a balanced livelihood? Here, Bhikkhus, a person of good family, knowing the income and expenditure of their wealth, maintains a balanced livelihood, neither too extravagant nor too meager, thinking: ‘Thus my income will cover my expenditure, and my expenditure will not exceed my income.’ Just as, Bhikkhus, a scale-holder or their apprentice, holding up the scale, knows, ‘It is lowered by this much,’ or ‘It is raised by this much’; in the same way, Bhikkhus, a person of good family, knowing the income and expenditure of their wealth, maintains a balanced livelihood, neither too extravagant nor too meager, thinking: ‘Thus my income will cover my expenditure, and my expenditure will not exceed my income.’ If, Bhikkhus, this person of good family, having little income, were to lead an extravagant life, there would be those who say of them: ‘This person of good family consumes their wealth like one eating figs from a cluster-fig tree.’ And if, Bhikkhus, this person of good family, having a large income, were to lead a wretched life, there would be those who say of them: ‘This person of good family will die a wretched death.’ But when, Bhikkhus, this person of good family, knowing the income and expenditure of their wealth, maintains a balanced livelihood, neither too extravagant nor too meager, thinking: ‘Thus my income will cover my expenditure, and my expenditure will not exceed my income,’ this, Bhikkhus, is called a balanced livelihood.

“Katamā [V 3.0147] ca bhikkhave, saddhāsampadā? Idha, bhikkhave, kulaputto saddho hoti, saddahati tathāgatassa bodhiṃ – ‘itipi so bhagavā…pe… satthā devamanussānaṃ buddho bhagavā’ti. Ayaṃ vuccati, bhikkhave, saddhāsampadā.

“And what, Bhikkhus, is the accomplishment of faith? Here, Bhikkhus, a person of good family has faith; they have confidence in the enlightenment of the Tathāgata: ‘For this reason, the Blessed One is... a teacher of gods and humans, the Buddha, the Blessed One.’ This, Bhikkhus, is called the accomplishment of faith.

“Katamā ca, bhikkhave, sīlasampadā? Idha, bhikkhave, kulaputto pāṇātipātā paṭivirato hoti…pe… surāmerayamajjapamādaṭṭhānā paṭivirato hoti. Ayaṃ vuccati, bhikkhave, sīlasampadā.

“And what, Bhikkhus, is the accomplishment of virtue? Here, Bhikkhus, a person of good family abstains from the destruction of life... abstains from intoxicating drinks and drugs which are the basis for heedlessness. This, Bhikkhus, is called the accomplishment of virtue.

“Katamā ca, bhikkhave, cāgasampadā? Idha, bhikkhave, kulaputto vigatamalamaccherena cetasā agāraṃ ajjhāvasati…pe… yācayogo dānasaṃvibhāgarato. Ayaṃ vuccati, bhikkhave, cāgasampadā.

“And what, bhikkhus, is the accomplishment of generosity? Here, bhikkhus, a person of good family dwells at home with a mind free from the stain of stinginess, responsive to requests, delighting in giving and sharing. This, bhikkhus, is called the accomplishment of generosity.

“Katamā ca, bhikkhave, paññāsampadā? Idha[P 4.0325], bhikkhave, kulaputto paññavā hoti…pe… sammā dukkhakkhayagāminiyā. Ayaṃ vuccati, bhikkhave, paññāsampadā. Imā kho, bhikkhave, aṭṭha sampadā”ti.

“And what, bhikkhus, is the accomplishment of wisdom? Here, bhikkhus, a person of good family is wise... endowed with the wisdom that rightly leads to the destruction of suffering. This, bhikkhus, is called the accomplishment of wisdom. These, bhikkhus, are the eight accomplishments.”

“Uṭṭhātā kammadheyyesu, appamatto vidhānavā;

Samaṃ kappeti jīvikaṃ, sambhataṃ anurakkhati.

“Energetic in his undertakings, heedful and well-organized, he maintains a balanced livelihood and guards what he has accumulated.

“Saddho [T 4.0337] sīlena sampanno, vadaññū vītamaccharo;

Niccaṃ maggaṃ visodheti, sotthānaṃ samparāyikaṃ.

“Faithful, endowed with virtue, generous, free from stinginess, he constantly purifies the path to welfare in the life to come.

“Iccete aṭṭha dhammā ca, saddhassa gharamesino;

Akkhātā saccanāmena, ubhayattha sukhāvahā.

“Thus these eight qualities, declared by the Truthful One for a faithful householder, are conducive to happiness in both worlds.

“Diṭṭhadhammahitatthāya, samparāyasukhāya ca;

Evametaṃ gahaṭṭhānaṃ, cāgo puññaṃ pavaḍḍhatī”ti. chaṭṭhaṃ;

“For the welfare of this present life and for happiness in the life to come—thus for householders, this generosity and merit increase.” Sixth.

7. Icchāsuttaṃ

7. The Discourse on Desire

77. Tatra [M 3.0143][V 3.0148] kho āyasmā sāriputto bhikkhū āmantesi – “āvuso bhikkhavo”ti! “Āvuso”ti kho te bhikkhū āyasmato sāriputtassa paccassosuṃ. Āyasmā sāriputto etadavoca –

77. Then the Venerable Sāriputta addressed the bhikkhus: “Friends, bhikkhus!” “Friend,” those bhikkhus replied to the Venerable Sāriputta. The Venerable Sāriputta then said this:

a. ni. 8.61 “Aṭṭhime, āvuso, puggalā santo saṃvijjamānā lokasmiṃ. Katame aṭṭha? Idhāvuso, bhikkhuno pavivittassa viharato nirāyattavuttino icchā uppajjati lābhāya. So uṭṭhahati, ghaṭati, vāyamati lābhāya. Tassa uṭṭhahato, ghaṭato, vāyamato lābhāya lābho nuppajjati. So tena alābhena socati kilamati paridevati, urattāḷiṃ kandati, sammohaṃ āpajjati. Ayaṃ vuccatāvuso, ‘bhikkhu iccho viharati lābhāya, uṭṭhahati, ghaṭati[P 4.0326], vāyamati lābhāya, na ca lābhī, socī ca paridevī ca, cuto ca saddhammā’”.

“Friends, these eight persons are found existing in the world. Which eight? Here, friends, for a bhikkhu dwelling in seclusion, with an independent way of life, a desire for gain arises. He strives, exerts himself, and makes an effort for gain. For him, striving, exerting himself, and making an effort for gain, gain does not arise. Because of that lack of gain, he sorrows, becomes weary, and laments; he beats his breast and wails, and falls into bewilderment. This, friends, is called: ‘a bhikkhu who dwells desiring gain, strives, exerts himself, and makes an effort for gain, but does not obtain gain, is sorrowful and lamenting, and has fallen away from the good Dhamma.’”

“Idha panāvuso, bhikkhuno pavivittassa viharato nirāyattavuttino icchā uppajjati lābhāya. So uṭṭhahati, ghaṭati, vāyamati lābhāya. Tassa uṭṭhahato ghaṭato vāyamato lābhāya lābho uppajjati. So tena [T 4.0338] lābhena majjati pamajjati pamādamāpajjati. Ayaṃ vuccatāvuso, ‘bhikkhu iccho viharati lābhāya, uṭṭhahati ghaṭati vāyamati lābhāya, lābhī ca, madī ca pamādī ca, cuto ca saddhammā’”.

“Furthermore, friends, for a bhikkhu dwelling in seclusion, with an independent way of life, a desire for gain arises. He strives, exerts himself, and makes an effort for gain. For him, striving, exerting himself, and making an effort for gain, gain arises. Because of that gain, he becomes intoxicated, becomes heedless, and falls into negligence. This, friends, is called: ‘a bhikkhu who dwells desiring gain, strives, exerts himself, and makes an effort for gain, obtains gain, is intoxicated and heedless, and has fallen away from the good Dhamma.’”

“Idha panāvuso, bhikkhuno pavivittassa viharato nirāyattavuttino icchā uppajjati lābhāya. So na uṭṭhahati, na ghaṭati, na vāyamati lābhāya. Tassa anuṭṭhahato, aghaṭato, avāyamato lābhāya lābho nuppajjati. So tena alābhena socati kilamati paridevati, urattāḷiṃ kandati, sammohaṃ āpajjati. Ayaṃ vuccatāvuso, ‘bhikkhu iccho viharati lābhāya, na uṭṭhahati, na ghaṭati, na vāyamati lābhāya, na ca lābhī, socī ca paridevī ca, cuto ca saddhammā’”.

“Furthermore, friends, for a bhikkhu dwelling in seclusion, with an independent way of life, a desire for gain arises. He does not strive, does not exert himself, and does not make an effort for gain. For him, not striving, not exerting himself, and not making an effort for gain, gain does not arise. Because of that lack of gain, he sorrows, becomes weary, and laments; he beats his breast and wails, and falls into bewilderment. This, friends, is called: ‘a bhikkhu who dwells desiring gain, does not strive, exert himself, or make an effort for gain, does not obtain gain, is sorrowful and lamenting, and has fallen away from the good Dhamma.’”

“Idha panāvuso, bhikkhuno pavivittassa viharato nirāyattavuttino icchā uppajjati lābhāya. So na uṭṭhahati, na ghaṭati, na vāyamati lābhāya. Tassa anuṭṭhahato, aghaṭato, avāyamato [V 3.0149] lābhāya lābho uppajjati. So tena lābhena majjati pamajjati pamādamāpajjati. Ayaṃ vuccatāvuso, ‘bhikkhu iccho viharati lābhāya, na uṭṭhahati na [M 3.0144] ghaṭati na vāyamati lābhāya, lābhī ca, madī ca pamādī ca, cuto ca saddhammā’”.

“Furthermore, friends, for a bhikkhu dwelling in seclusion, with an independent way of life, a desire for gain arises. He does not strive, does not exert himself, and does not make an effort for gain. For him, not striving, not exerting himself, and not making an effort for gain, gain arises. Because of that gain, he becomes intoxicated, becomes heedless, and falls into negligence. This, friends, is called: ‘a bhikkhu who dwells desiring gain, does not strive, exert himself, or make an effort for gain, obtains gain, is intoxicated and heedless, and has fallen away from the good Dhamma.’”

“Idha panāvuso, bhikkhuno pavivittassa viharato nirāyattavuttino icchā uppajjati lābhāya. So uṭṭhahati, ghaṭati, vāyamati lābhāya. Tassa uṭṭhahato, ghaṭato, vāyamato lābhāya, lābho nuppajjati. So tena alābhena na socati na kilamati [P 4.0327] na paridevati, na urattāḷiṃ kandati, na sammohaṃ [T 4.0339] āpajjati. Ayaṃ vuccatāvuso, ‘bhikkhu iccho viharati lābhāya, uṭṭhahati ghaṭati vāyamati lābhāya, na ca lābhī, na ca socī na ca paridevī, accuto ca saddhammā’”.

“Furthermore, friends, for a bhikkhu dwelling in seclusion, with an independent way of life, a desire for gain arises. He strives, exerts himself, and makes an effort for gain. For him, striving, exerting himself, and making an effort for gain, gain does not arise. Because of that lack of gain, he does not sorrow, does not become weary, and does not lament; he does not beat his breast and wail, and does not fall into bewilderment. This, friends, is called: ‘a bhikkhu who dwells desiring gain, strives, exerts himself, and makes an effort for gain, but does not obtain gain, is not sorrowful or lamenting, and has not fallen away from the good Dhamma.’”

“Idha panāvuso, bhikkhuno pavivittassa viharato nirāyattavuttino icchā uppajjati lābhāya. So uṭṭhahati, ghaṭati, vāyamati lābhāya. Tassa uṭṭhahato, ghaṭato, vāyamato lābhāya, lābho uppajjati. So tena lābhena na majjati na pamajjati na pamādamāpajjati. Ayaṃ vuccatāvuso, ‘bhikkhu iccho viharati lābhāya, uṭṭhahati, ghaṭati, vāyamati lābhāya, lābhī ca, na ca madī na ca pamādī, accuto ca saddhammā’”.

“Furthermore, friends, for a bhikkhu dwelling in seclusion, with an independent way of life, a desire for gain arises. He strives, exerts himself, and makes an effort for gain. For him, striving, exerting himself, and making an effort for gain, gain arises. Because of that gain, he is not intoxicated, not heedless, and does not fall into negligence. This, friends, is called: ‘a bhikkhu who dwells desiring gain, strives, exerts himself, and makes an effort for gain, obtains gain, is not intoxicated or heedless, and has not fallen away from the good Dhamma.’”

“Idha panāvuso, bhikkhuno pavivittassa viharato nirāyattavuttino icchā uppajjati lābhāya. So na uṭṭhahati, na ghaṭati, na vāyamati lābhāya. Tassa anuṭṭhahato, aghaṭato, avāyamato lābhāya, lābho nuppajjati. So tena alābhena na socati na kilamati na paridevati, na urattāḷiṃ kandati, na sammohaṃ āpajjati. Ayaṃ vuccatāvuso, ‘bhikkhu iccho viharati lābhāya, na uṭṭhahati, na ghaṭati, na vāyamati lābhāya, na ca lābhī, na ca socī na ca paridevī, accuto ca saddhammā’”.

“Furthermore, friends, for a bhikkhu dwelling in seclusion, with an independent way of life, a desire for gain arises. He does not strive, does not exert himself, and does not make an effort for gain. For him, not striving, not exerting himself, and not making an effort for gain, gain does not arise. Because of that lack of gain, he does not sorrow, does not become weary, and does not lament; he does not beat his breast and wail, and does not fall into bewilderment. This, friends, is called: ‘a bhikkhu who dwells desiring gain, does not strive, exert himself, or make an effort for gain, does not obtain gain, is not sorrowful or lamenting, and has not fallen away from the good Dhamma.’”

“Idha panāvuso, bhikkhuno pavivittassa viharato nirāyattavuttino icchā uppajjati lābhāya. So na uṭṭhahati, na ghaṭati, na vāyamati lābhāya. Tassa anuṭṭhahato, aghaṭato, avāyamato lābhāya, lābho uppajjati. So tena lābhena na majjati na pamajjati na pamādamāpajjati. Ayaṃ vuccatāvuso[T 4.0340], ‘bhikkhu iccho viharati lābhāya, na uṭṭhahati, na [P 4.0328] ghaṭati[V 3.0150], na vāyamati lābhāya, lābhī ca, na ca madī na ca pamādī, accuto ca saddhammā’. Ime kho, āvuso, aṭṭha puggalā santo saṃvijjamānā lokasmi”nti. Sattamaṃ.

Furthermore, friends, here a desire for gain arises for a bhikkhu dwelling in seclusion, whose conduct is unattached. He does not strive, exert himself, or make an effort for gain. For him, who does not strive, does not exert himself, and does not make an effort for gain, gain arises. On account of that gain, he is not intoxicated, is not heedless, and does not fall into heedlessness. This, friends, is called: ‘a bhikkhu who dwells desiring gain, but does not strive, exert himself, or make an effort for gain; he is one who obtains gain, yet is not intoxicated and not heedless, and has not fallen away from the good Dhamma.’ Indeed, friends, these are the eight individuals who are found existing in the world.” The seventh.

8. Alaṃsuttaṃ

8. 8. The Sutta on Sufficiency

78. a. ni. 8.62 Tatra [M 3.0145] kho āyasmā sāriputto bhikkhū āmantesi…pe… chahāvuso, dhammehi samannāgato bhikkhu alaṃ attano, alaṃ paresaṃ. Katamehi chahi? Idhāvuso, bhikkhu khippanisanti ca hoti kusalesu dhammesu; sutānañca dhammānaṃ dhāraṇajātiko hoti; dhātānañca dhammānaṃ atthūpaparikkhitā hoti; atthamaññāya dhammamaññāya dhammānudhammappaṭipanno ca hoti; kalyāṇavāco ca hoti kalyāṇavākkaraṇo, poriyā vācāya samannāgato vissaṭṭhāya anelagaḷāya atthassa viññāpaniyā; sandassako ca hoti samādapako samuttejako sampahaṃsako sabrahmacārīnaṃ. Imehi kho, āvuso, chahi dhammehi samannāgato bhikkhu alaṃ attano, alaṃ paresaṃ.

78. There, the Venerable Sāriputta addressed the bhikkhus… and so on… Friends, a bhikkhu endowed with six qualities is sufficient for himself and sufficient for others. With which six? Here, friends, a bhikkhu is quick to investigate wholesome qualities; he is of a nature to retain the teachings he has heard; he is an investigator of the meaning of the teachings he has retained; having understood the meaning and having understood the Dhamma, he practices in accordance with the Dhamma; he has good speech and good enunciation, is endowed with urbane speech that is clear, fluent, and makes the meaning known; and he is one who instructs, incites, rouses, and gladdens his companions in the holy life. Indeed, friends, a bhikkhu endowed with these six qualities is sufficient for himself and sufficient for others.

“Pañcahāvuso, dhammehi samannāgato bhikkhu alaṃ attano, alaṃ paresaṃ. Katamehi pañcahi? Idhāvuso, bhikkhu na heva kho khippanisanti ca hoti kusalesu dhammesu; sutānañca dhammānaṃ dhāraṇajātiko hoti; dhātānañca dhammānaṃ atthūpaparikkhitā hoti; atthamaññāya dhammamaññāya dhammānudhammappaṭipanno ca hoti; kalyāṇavāco ca hoti…pe… sandassako ca hoti…pe… sabrahmacārīnaṃ. Imehi [P 4.0329] kho, āvuso, pañcahi dhammehi samannāgato bhikkhu alaṃ attano, alaṃ paresaṃ.

Friends, a bhikkhu endowed with five qualities is sufficient for himself and sufficient for others. With which five? Here, friends, a bhikkhu is not quick to investigate wholesome qualities; but he is of a nature to retain the teachings he has heard; he is an investigator of the meaning of the teachings he has retained; having understood the meaning and having understood the Dhamma, he practices in accordance with the Dhamma; he has good speech… and so on… and he is one who instructs… and so on… his companions in the holy life. Indeed, friends, a bhikkhu endowed with these five qualities is sufficient for himself and sufficient for others.

“Catūhāvuso, dhammehi samannāgato bhikkhu alaṃ attano, nālaṃ paresaṃ[T 4.0341]. Katamehi catūhi? Idhāvuso, bhikkhu khippanisanti ca hoti kusalesu dhammesu; sutānañca dhammānaṃ dhāraṇajātiko hoti; dhātānañca dhammānaṃ atthūpaparikkhitā hoti; atthamaññāya dhammamaññāya dhammānudhammappaṭipanno ca hoti; no ca kalyāṇavāco hoti…pe… no ca sandassako hoti…pe… sabrahmacārīnaṃ. Imehi kho, āvuso, catūhi dhammehi samannāgato bhikkhu alaṃ attano, nālaṃ paresaṃ.

Friends, a bhikkhu endowed with four qualities is sufficient for himself, but not sufficient for others. With which four? Here, friends, a bhikkhu is quick to investigate wholesome qualities; he is of a nature to retain the teachings he has heard; he is an investigator of the meaning of the teachings he has retained; and having understood the meaning and having understood the Dhamma, he practices in accordance with the Dhamma; but he does not have good speech… and so on… and he is not one who instructs… and so on… his companions in the holy life. Indeed, friends, a bhikkhu endowed with these four qualities is sufficient for himself, but not sufficient for others.

“Catūhāvuso[V 3.0151], dhammehi samannāgato bhikkhu alaṃ paresaṃ, nālaṃ attano. Katamehi catūhi? Idhāvuso, bhikkhu khippanisanti ca hoti kusalesu dhammesu; sutānañca dhammānaṃ dhāraṇajātiko hoti; no ca dhātānaṃ dhammānaṃ atthūpaparikkhitā hoti; no ca atthamaññāya dhammamaññāya dhammānudhammappaṭipanno hoti; kalyāṇavāco ca hoti…pe… sandassako [M 3.0146] ca hoti…pe… sabrahmacārīnaṃ. Imehi kho, āvuso, catūhi dhammehi samannāgato bhikkhu alaṃ paresaṃ, nālaṃ attano.

Friends, a bhikkhu endowed with four qualities is sufficient for others, but not sufficient for himself. With which four? Here, friends, a bhikkhu is quick to investigate wholesome qualities; and he is of a nature to retain the teachings he has heard; but he is not an investigator of the meaning of the teachings he has retained; nor, having understood the meaning and having understood the Dhamma, does he practice in accordance with the Dhamma; but he has good speech… and so on… and he is one who instructs… and so on… his companions in the holy life. Indeed, friends, a bhikkhu endowed with these four qualities is sufficient for others, but not sufficient for himself.

“Tīhāvuso, dhammehi samannāgato bhikkhu alaṃ attano, nālaṃ paresaṃ. Katamehi tīhi? Idhāvuso, bhikkhu na heva kho khippanisanti ca hoti kusalesu dhammesu; sutānañca dhammānaṃ dhāraṇajātiko [P 4.0330] hoti; dhātānañca dhammānaṃ atthūpaparikkhitā hoti; atthamaññāya dhammamaññāya dhammānudhammappaṭipanno ca hoti; no ca kalyāṇavāco hoti…pe… no ca sandassako hoti…pe… sabrahmacārīnaṃ. Imehi kho, āvuso, tīhi dhammehi samannāgato bhikkhu alaṃ attano, nālaṃ paresaṃ.

Friends, a bhikkhu endowed with three qualities is sufficient for himself, but not sufficient for others. With which three? Here, friends, a bhikkhu is not quick to investigate wholesome qualities; but he is of a nature to retain the teachings he has heard; he is an investigator of the meaning of the teachings he has retained; and having understood the meaning and having understood the Dhamma, he practices in accordance with the Dhamma; but he does not have good speech… and so on… and he is not one who instructs… and so on… his companions in the holy life. Indeed, friends, a bhikkhu endowed with these three qualities is sufficient for himself, but not sufficient for others.

“Tīhāvuso[T 4.0342], dhammehi samannāgato bhikkhu alaṃ paresaṃ, nālaṃ attano. Katamehi tīhi? Idhāvuso, bhikkhu na heva kho khippanisanti ca hoti kusalesu dhammesu; sutānañca dhammānaṃ dhāraṇajātiko hoti; no ca dhātānaṃ dhammānaṃ atthūpaparikkhitā hoti; no ca atthamaññāya dhammamaññāya dhammānudhammappaṭipanno hoti; kalyāṇavāco ca hoti…pe… atthassa viññāpaniyā, sandassako ca hoti…pe… sabrahmacārīnaṃ. Imehi kho, āvuso, tīhi dhammehi samannāgato bhikkhu alaṃ paresaṃ, nālaṃ attano.

Friends, a bhikkhu endowed with three qualities is sufficient for others, but not sufficient for himself. With which three? Here, friends, a bhikkhu is not quick to investigate wholesome qualities; but he is of a nature to retain the teachings he has heard; however, he is not an investigator of the meaning of the teachings he has retained; nor, having understood the meaning and having understood the Dhamma, does he practice in accordance with the Dhamma; but he has good speech… that makes the meaning known; and he is one who instructs… and so on… his companions in the holy life. Indeed, friends, a bhikkhu endowed with these three qualities is sufficient for others, but not sufficient for himself.

“Dvīhāvuso, dhammehi samannāgato bhikkhu alaṃ attano, nālaṃ paresaṃ. Katamehi dvīhi? Idhāvuso, bhikkhu na heva kho khippanisanti ca hoti kusalesu dhammesu; no ca sutānaṃ dhammānaṃ dhāraṇajātiko hoti; dhātānañca dhammānaṃ atthūpaparikkhitā hoti; atthamaññāya dhammamaññāya dhammānudhammappaṭipanno ca hoti; no ca kalyāṇavāco hoti…pe… no ca sandassako hoti…pe… sabrahmacārīnaṃ. Imehi kho, āvuso, dvīhi dhammehi samannāgato bhikkhu alaṃ attano, nālaṃ paresaṃ.

Friends, a bhikkhu endowed with two qualities is sufficient for himself, but not sufficient for others. With which two? Here, friends, a bhikkhu is not quick to investigate wholesome qualities; nor is he of a nature to retain the teachings he has heard; but he is an investigator of the meaning of the teachings he has retained; and having understood the meaning and having understood the Dhamma, he practices in accordance with the Dhamma; but he does not have good speech… and so on… and he is not one who instructs… and so on… his companions in the holy life. Indeed, friends, a bhikkhu endowed with these two qualities is sufficient for himself, but not sufficient for others.

“Dvīhāvuso[V 3.0152][P 4.0331], dhammehi samannāgato bhikkhu alaṃ paresaṃ, nālaṃ attano. Katamehi dvīhi? Idhāvuso, bhikkhu na heva kho khippanisanti ca hoti kusalesu dhammesu; no ca sutānaṃ dhammānaṃ dhāraṇajātiko hoti; no ca dhātānaṃ dhammānaṃ atthūpaparikkhitā hoti; no ca atthamaññāya dhammamaññāya dhammānudhammappaṭipanno hoti; kalyāṇavāco ca hoti kalyāṇavākkaraṇo[M 3.0147], poriyā vācāya samannāgato vissaṭṭhāya anelagaḷāya atthassa [T 4.0343] viññāpaniyā; sandassako ca hoti samādapako samuttejako sampahaṃsako sabrahmacārīnaṃ. Imehi kho, āvuso, dvīhi dhammehi samannāgato bhikkhu alaṃ paresaṃ, nālaṃ attano”ti. Aṭṭhamaṃ.

“Friends, a bhikkhu endowed with two qualities is sufficient for others, but not for himself. What two? Here, friends, a bhikkhu is not quick in discerning wholesome qualities; nor is he one who retains the teachings he has heard; nor is he one who examines the meaning of the teachings he has retained; nor does he, having understood the meaning and the Dhamma, practice in accordance with the Dhamma; but he has good speech and good expression, is endowed with cultured speech that is clear, fluent, and capable of making the meaning understood; and he is one who explains, exhorts, incites, and gladdens his spiritual companions. A bhikkhu endowed with these two qualities, friends, is sufficient for others, but not for himself.” Eighth.

9. Parihānasuttaṃ

9. The Discourse on Decline

79. “Aṭṭhime, bhikkhave, dhammā sekhassa bhikkhuno parihānāya saṃvattanti. Katame aṭṭha? Kammārāmatā, bhassārāmatā, niddārāmatā, saṅgaṇikārāmatā, indriyesu aguttadvāratā, bhojane amattaññutā, saṃsaggārāmatā, papañcārāmatā – ime kho, bhikkhave, aṭṭha dhammā sekhassa bhikkhuno parihānāya saṃvattanti.

79. “Bhikkhus, these eight states lead to the decline of a trainee bhikkhu. Which eight? Delight in activity, delight in chatter, delight in sleep, delight in company, being unguarded in the sense faculties, immoderation in food, delight in association, and delight in proliferation. These, bhikkhus, are the eight states that lead to the decline of a trainee bhikkhu.”

“Aṭṭhime, bhikkhave, dhammā sekhassa bhikkhuno aparihānāya saṃvattanti. Katame aṭṭha? Na kammārāmatā, na bhassārāmatā, na niddārāmatā, na saṅgaṇikārāmatā, indriyesu guttadvāratā, bhojane mattaññutā, asaṃsaggārāmatā, nippapañcārāmatā – ime kho, bhikkhave, aṭṭha dhammā sekhassa bhikkhuno aparihānāya saṃvattantī”ti. Navamaṃ.

“Bhikkhus, these eight states lead to the non-decline of a trainee bhikkhu. Which eight? Not delighting in activity, not delighting in chatter, not delighting in sleep, not delighting in company, being guarded in the sense faculties, moderation in food, not delighting in association, and not delighting in proliferation. These, bhikkhus, are the eight states that lead to the non-decline of a trainee bhikkhu.” Ninth.

10. Kusītārambhavatthusuttaṃ

10. The Discourse on the Grounds for Laziness

80. di. ni. 3.334, 358 “Aṭṭhimāni[P 4.0332], bhikkhave, kusītavatthūni. Katamāni aṭṭha? Idha, bhikkhave, bhikkhunā kammaṃ kattabbaṃ hoti. Tassa evaṃ hoti – ‘kammaṃ kho me kattabbaṃ bhavissati. Kammaṃ kho pana me karontassa kāyo kilamissati. Handāhaṃ nipajjāmī’ti. So nipajjati, na vīriyaṃ ārabhati appattassa pattiyā anadhigatassa adhigamāya asacchikatassa sacchikiriyāya. Idaṃ, bhikkhave, paṭhamaṃ kusītavatthu.

80. “Bhikkhus, there are these eight grounds for laziness. What eight? Here, bhikkhus, a bhikkhu has work to do. He thinks: ‘I will have work to do. While doing this work, my body will become tired. Let me lie down.’ So he lies down and does not arouse energy for the attainment of the unattained, for the achievement of the unachieved, for the realization of the unrealized. This, bhikkhus, is the first ground for laziness.

“Puna [V 3.0153] caparaṃ, bhikkhave, bhikkhunā kammaṃ kataṃ hoti. Tassa evaṃ hoti – ‘ahaṃ [T 4.0344] kho kammaṃ akāsiṃ. Kammaṃ kho pana me karontassa kāyo kilanto. Handāhaṃ nipajjāmī’ti. So nipajjati, na vīriyaṃ ārabhati appattassa pattiyā anadhigatassa adhigamāya asacchikatassa sacchikiriyāya. Idaṃ, bhikkhave, dutiyaṃ kusītavatthu.

“Furthermore, bhikkhus, a bhikkhu has done work. He thinks: ‘I have done work. While doing the work, my body became tired. Let me lie down.’ So he lies down and does not arouse energy for the attainment of the unattained, for the achievement of the unachieved, for the realization of the unrealized. This, bhikkhus, is the second ground for laziness.

“Puna [M 3.0148] caparaṃ, bhikkhave, bhikkhunā maggo gantabbo hoti. Tassa evaṃ hoti – ‘maggo me gantabbo bhavissati. Maggaṃ kho pana me gacchantassa kāyo kilamissati. Handāhaṃ nipajjāmī’ti. So nipajjati, na vīriyaṃ ārabhati appattassa pattiyā anadhigatassa adhigamāya asacchikatassa sacchikiriyāya. Idaṃ, bhikkhave, tatiyaṃ kusītavatthu.

“Furthermore, bhikkhus, a bhikkhu has a journey to make. He thinks: ‘I will have a journey to make. While making the journey, my body will become tired. Let me lie down.’ So he lies down and does not arouse energy for the attainment of the unattained, for the achievement of the unachieved, for the realization of the unrealized. This, bhikkhus, is the third ground for laziness.”

“Puna caparaṃ, bhikkhave, bhikkhunā maggo gato hoti. Tassa evaṃ hoti – ‘ahaṃ kho maggaṃ agamāsiṃ. Maggaṃ kho pana me gacchantassa kāyo kilanto. Handāhaṃ nipajjāmī’ti. So nipajjati, na vīriyaṃ ārabhati appattassa pattiyā anadhigatassa adhigamāya asacchikatassa sacchikiriyāya. Idaṃ bhikkhave, catutthaṃ kusītavatthu.

“Furthermore, bhikkhus, a bhikkhu has made a journey. He thinks: ‘I have made a journey. My body is tired from the journey. Let me lie down.’ So he lies down and does not arouse energy for the attainment of the unattained, for the achievement of the unachieved, for the realization of the unrealized. This, bhikkhus, is the fourth ground for laziness.”

“Puna caparaṃ, bhikkhave, bhikkhu gāmaṃ vā nigamaṃ vā piṇḍāya caranto na labhati lūkhassa vā paṇītassa vā bhojanassa yāvadatthaṃ pāripūriṃ. Tassa evaṃ hoti [P 4.0333] – ‘ahaṃ kho gāmaṃ vā nigamaṃ vā piṇḍāya caranto nālatthaṃ lūkhassa vā paṇītassa vā bhojanassa yāvadatthaṃ pāripūriṃ. Tassa me kāyo kilanto akammañño. Handāhaṃ nipajjāmī’ti. So nipajjati, na vīriyaṃ ārabhati…pe… idaṃ, bhikkhave, pañcamaṃ kusītavatthu.

“Furthermore, bhikkhus, a bhikkhu, while wandering for alms in a village or town, does not obtain coarse or fine food to his full satisfaction. He thinks: ‘While wandering for alms in a village or town, I did not obtain coarse or fine food to my full satisfaction. My body is tired and unfit for work. Let me lie down.’ So he lies down and does not arouse energy for the attainment of the unattained, for the achievement of the unachieved, for the realization of the unrealized. This, bhikkhus, is the fifth ground for laziness.

“Puna [T 4.0345] caparaṃ, bhikkhave, bhikkhu gāmaṃ vā nigamaṃ vā piṇḍāya caranto labhati lūkhassa vā paṇītassa vā bhojanassa yāvadatthaṃ pāripūriṃ. Tassa evaṃ hoti – ‘ahaṃ kho gāmaṃ vā nigamaṃ vā piṇḍāya caranto alatthaṃ lūkhassa vā paṇītassa vā bhojanassa yāvadatthaṃ pāripūriṃ. Tassa me kāyo garuko akammañño māsācitaṃ maññe. Handāhaṃ nipajjāmī’ti. So nipajjati, na vīriyaṃ ārabhati…pe… idaṃ, bhikkhave, chaṭṭhaṃ kusītavatthu.

“Furthermore, bhikkhus, a bhikkhu, while wandering for alms in a village or town, obtains coarse or fine food to his full satisfaction. He thinks: ‘While wandering for alms in a village or town, I obtained coarse or fine food to my full satisfaction. My body is heavy and unfit for work, as if filled with beans. Let me lie down.’ So he lies down and does not arouse energy for the attainment of the unattained, for the achievement of the unachieved, for the realization of the unrealized. This, bhikkhus, is the sixth ground for laziness.

“Puna caparaṃ, bhikkhave, bhikkhuno uppanno hoti appamattako ābādho. Tassa evaṃ hoti [V 3.0154] – ‘uppanno kho me ayaṃ appamattako ābādho atthi kappo nipajjituṃ. Handāhaṃ nipajjāmī’ti. So nipajjati, na vīriyaṃ ārabhati…pe… idaṃ, bhikkhave, sattamaṃ kusītavatthu.

“Furthermore, bhikkhus, a slight illness has arisen in a bhikkhu. He thinks: ‘This slight illness has arisen in me. It is suitable to lie down. Let me lie down.’ So he lies down and does not arouse energy for the attainment of the unattained, for the achievement of the unachieved, for the realization of the unrealized. This, bhikkhus, is the seventh ground for laziness.”

“Puna caparaṃ, bhikkhave, bhikkhu gilānā vuṭṭhito a. ni. 6.16 suttavaṇṇanā ṭīkā oloketabbā hoti aciravuṭṭhito gelaññā. Tassa evaṃ hoti – ‘ahaṃ kho gilānā vuṭṭhito aciravuṭṭhito gelaññā. Tassa me kāyo dubbalo akammañño. Handāhaṃ nipajjāmī’ti[M 3.0149]. So nipajjati, na vīriyaṃ ārabhati appattassa pattiyā anadhigatassa adhigamāya asacchikatassa sacchikiriyāya. Idaṃ, bhikkhave, aṭṭhamaṃ kusītavatthu. Imāni kho, bhikkhave, aṭṭha kusītavatthūni.

“Furthermore, monks, a monk has recovered from illness, having recently recovered from sickness. It occurs to him: ‘I have recovered from illness, having recently recovered from sickness. My body is weak and unfit for work. Let me lie down.’ So he lies down and does not arouse energy for the attainment of the unattained, for the acquisition of the unacquired, for the realization of the unrealized. This, monks, is the eighth ground for laziness. These, monks, are the eight grounds for laziness.”

dī. ni. 3.335, 358 “Aṭṭhimāni[P 4.0334], bhikkhave, ārambhavatthūni. Katamāni aṭṭha? Idha, bhikkhave, bhikkhunā kammaṃ kattabbaṃ hoti. Tassa evaṃ hoti – ‘kammaṃ kho me kattabbaṃ bhavissati. Kammaṃ kho mayā karontena na sukaraṃ buddhānaṃ sāsanaṃ manasi kātuṃ. Handāhaṃ paṭikacceva vīriyaṃ ārabhāmi appattassa [T 4.0346] pattiyā anadhigatassa adhigamāya asacchikatassa sacchikiriyāyā’ti. So vīriyaṃ ārabhati appattassa pattiyā anadhigatassa adhigamāya asacchikatassa sacchikiriyāya. Idaṃ, bhikkhave, paṭhamaṃ ārambhavatthu.

“Monks, there are these eight grounds for arousing energy. What eight? Here, monks, a monk has work to be done. It occurs to him: ‘I will have work to do. While doing the work, it will not be easy for me to bear in mind the teaching of the Buddhas. Let me arouse energy beforehand for the attainment of the unattained, for the acquisition of the unacquired, for the realization of the unrealized.’ So he arouses energy for the attainment of the unattained, for the acquisition of the unacquired, for the realization of the unrealized. This, monks, is the first ground for arousing energy.”

“Puna caparaṃ, bhikkhave, bhikkhunā kammaṃ kataṃ hoti. Tassa evaṃ hoti – ‘ahaṃ kho kammaṃ akāsiṃ. Kammaṃ kho panāhaṃ karonto nāsakkhiṃ buddhānaṃ sāsanaṃ manasi kātuṃ. Handāhaṃ vīriyaṃ ārabhāmi appattassa pattiyā anadhigatassa adhigamāya asacchikatassa sacchikiriyāyā’ti. So vīriyaṃ ārabhati. Idaṃ, bhikkhave, dutiyaṃ ārambhavatthu.

“Furthermore, monks, a monk has done work. It occurs to him: ‘I have done work. While doing that work, I was unable to bear in mind the teaching of the Buddhas. Let me arouse energy for the attainment of the unattained, for the acquisition of the unacquired, for the realization of the unrealized.’ So he arouses energy for the attainment of the unattained, for the acquisition of the unacquired, for the realization of the unrealized. This, monks, is the second ground for arousing energy.”

“Puna caparaṃ, bhikkhave, bhikkhunā maggo gantabbo hoti. Tassa evaṃ hoti – maggo kho me gantabbo bhavissati. Maggaṃ kho pana me gacchantena na sukaraṃ buddhānaṃ sāsanaṃ manasi kātuṃ. Handāhaṃ vīriyaṃ…pe… idaṃ, bhikkhave, tatiyaṃ ārambhavatthu.

“Furthermore, monks, a monk has a journey to undertake. It occurs to him: ‘I will have a journey to undertake. While undertaking the journey, it will not be easy for me to bear in mind the teaching of the Buddhas. Let me arouse energy beforehand for the attainment of the unattained, for the acquisition of the unacquired, for the realization of the unrealized.’ So he arouses energy for the attainment of the unattained, for the acquisition of the unacquired, for the realization of the unrealized. This, monks, is the third ground for arousing energy.”

“Puna caparaṃ, bhikkhave, bhikkhunā maggo gato hoti. Tassa evaṃ hoti – ahaṃ kho maggaṃ [V 3.0155] agamāsiṃ. Maggaṃ kho panāhaṃ gacchanto nāsakkhiṃ buddhānaṃ sāsanaṃ manasi kātuṃ. Handāhaṃ vīriyaṃ ārabhāmi…pe… idaṃ, bhikkhave, catutthaṃ ārambhavatthu.

“Furthermore, monks, a monk has undertaken a journey. It occurs to him: ‘I have undertaken a journey. While undertaking the journey, I was unable to bear in mind the teaching of the Buddhas. Let me arouse energy for the attainment of the unattained, for the acquisition of the unacquired, for the realization of the unrealized.’ So he arouses energy for the attainment of the unattained, for the acquisition of the unacquired, for the realization of the unrealized. This, monks, is the fourth ground for arousing energy.”

“Puna [P 4.0335] caparaṃ, bhikkhave, bhikkhu gāmaṃ vā nigamaṃ vā piṇḍāya caranto na labhati lūkhassa vā paṇītassa vā bhojanassa yāvadatthaṃ pāripūriṃ. Tassa evaṃ hoti – ahaṃ kho gāmaṃ vā nigamaṃ vā piṇḍāya caranto nālatthaṃ lūkhassa vā paṇītassa vā bhojanassa yāvadatthaṃ pāripūriṃ. Tassa me [T 4.0347] kāyo lahuko kammañño. Handāhaṃ vīriyaṃ ārabhāmi…pe… idaṃ, bhikkhave, pañcamaṃ ārambhavatthu.

“Furthermore, monks, a monk, while wandering for alms in a village or a town, does not obtain coarse or fine food to his full satisfaction. It occurs to him: ‘While wandering for alms in a village or a town, I did not obtain coarse or fine food to my full satisfaction. My body is light and fit for work. Let me arouse energy for the attainment of the unattained, for the acquisition of the unacquired, for the realization of the unrealized.’ So he arouses energy for the attainment of the unattained, for the acquisition of the unacquired, for the realization of the unrealized. This, monks, is the fifth ground for arousing energy.”

“Puna [M 3.0150] caparaṃ, bhikkhave, bhikkhu gāmaṃ vā nigamaṃ vā piṇḍāya caranto labhati lūkhassa vā paṇītassa vā bhojanassa yāvadatthaṃ pāripūriṃ. Tassa evaṃ hoti – ahaṃ kho gāmaṃ vā nigamaṃ vā piṇḍāya caranto alatthaṃ lūkhassa vā paṇītassa vā bhojanassa yāvadatthaṃ pāripūriṃ. Tassa me kāyo balavā kammañño. Handāhaṃ vīriyaṃ ārabhāmi…pe… idaṃ, bhikkhave, chaṭṭhaṃ ārambhavatthu.

“Furthermore, monks, a monk, while wandering for alms in a village or a town, obtains coarse or fine food to his full satisfaction. It occurs to him: ‘While wandering for alms in a village or a town, I obtained coarse or fine food to my full satisfaction. My body is strong and fit for work. Let me arouse energy for the attainment of the unattained, for the acquisition of the unacquired, for the realization of the unrealized.’ So he arouses energy for the attainment of the unattained, for the acquisition of the unacquired, for the realization of the unrealized. This, monks, is the sixth ground for arousing energy.”

“Puna caparaṃ, bhikkhave, bhikkhuno uppanno hoti appamattako ābādho. Tassa evaṃ hoti – uppanno kho me ayaṃ appamattako ābādho. Ṭhānaṃ kho panetaṃ vijjati yaṃ me ābādho pavaḍḍheyya. Handāhaṃ paṭikacceva vīriyaṃ ārabhāmi…pe… idaṃ, bhikkhave, sattamaṃ ārambhavatthu.

“Furthermore, monks, a minor illness has arisen in a monk. It occurs to him: ‘This minor illness has arisen in me. It is possible that my illness might worsen. Let me arouse energy beforehand for the attainment of the unattained, for the acquisition of the unacquired, for the realization of the unrealized.’ So he arouses energy for the attainment of the unattained, for the acquisition of the unacquired, for the realization of the unrealized. This, monks, is the seventh ground for arousing energy.”

“Puna caparaṃ, bhikkhave, bhikkhu gilānā vuṭṭhito hoti aciravuṭṭhito gelaññā. Tassa evaṃ hoti – ‘ahaṃ kho gilānā vuṭṭhito aciravuṭṭhito gelaññā. Ṭhānaṃ kho panetaṃ vijjati yaṃ me ābādho paccudāvatteyya. Handāhaṃ paṭikacceva vīriyaṃ ārabhāmi appattassa pattiyā anadhigatassa adhigamāya asacchikatassa sacchikiriyāyā’ti. So vīriyaṃ ārabhati appattassa pattiyā anadhigatassa adhigamāya asacchikatassa sacchikiriyāya. Idaṃ, bhikkhave, aṭṭhamaṃ ārambhavatthu. Imāni kho, bhikkhave, aṭṭha ārambhavatthūnī”ti. Dasamaṃ.

“Furthermore, monks, a monk has recovered from illness, having recently recovered from sickness. It occurs to him: ‘I have recovered from illness, having recently recovered from sickness. It is possible that my illness might return. Let me arouse energy beforehand for the attainment of the unattained, for the acquisition of the unacquired, for the realization of the unrealized.’ So he arouses energy for the attainment of the unattained, for the acquisition of the unacquired, for the realization of the unrealized. This, monks, is the eighth ground for arousing energy. These, monks, are the eight grounds for arousing energy.”

Yamakavaggo tatiyo.

The Third Chapter on Pairs.

Tassuddānaṃ [V 3.0156] –

The summary verse for this is:

Dve [T 4.0348][P 4.0336] saddhā dve maraṇassatī, dve sampadā athāpare;

Icchā alaṃ parihānaṃ, kusītārambhavatthūnīti.

Two on faith, two on mindfulness of death, two on accomplishments, desire, sufficiency, decline, and the grounds for laziness and for arousing energy.

(9) 4. Sativaggo

(9) 4. The Chapter on Mindfulness

1. Satisampajaññasuttaṃ

1. 1. The Discourse on Mindfulness and Clear Comprehension

81. “Satisampajaññe[V 3.0157], bhikkhave, asati satisampajaññavipannassa hatūpanisaṃ hoti hirottappaṃ. Hirottappe asati hirottappavipannassa hatūpaniso hoti indriyasaṃvaro. Indriyasaṃvare asati indriyasaṃvaravipannassa hatūpanisaṃ hoti [M 3.0151] sīlaṃ. Sīle asati sīlavipannassa hatūpaniso hoti sammāsamādhi. Sammāsamādhimhi asati sammāsamādhivipannassa hatūpanisaṃ hoti yathābhūtañāṇadassanaṃ. Yathābhūtañāṇadassane asati yathābhūtañāṇadassanavipannassa hatūpaniso hoti nibbidāvirāgo. Nibbidāvirāge asati nibbidāvirāgavipannassa hatūpanisaṃ hoti vimuttiñāṇadassanaṃ. Seyyathāpi, bhikkhave, rukkho sākhāpalāsavipanno. Tassa papaṭikāpi na pāripūriṃ gacchati, tacopi… pheggupi… sāropi na pāripūriṃ gacchati. Evamevaṃ kho, bhikkhave, satisampajaññe asati satisampajaññavipannassa hatūpanisaṃ hoti hirottappaṃ; hirottappe asati hirottappavipannassa hatūpaniso hoti…pe… vimuttiñāṇadassanaṃ.

81. “Bhikkhus, when mindfulness and clear comprehension are absent, for one deficient in mindfulness and clear comprehension, the supporting condition for moral shame and moral dread is destroyed. When moral shame and moral dread are absent, for one deficient in moral shame and moral dread, the supporting condition for restraint of the senses is destroyed. When restraint of the senses is absent, for one deficient in restraint of the senses, the supporting condition for virtue is destroyed. When virtue is absent, for one deficient in virtue, the supporting condition for right concentration is destroyed. When right concentration is absent, for one deficient in right concentration, the supporting condition for the knowledge and vision of things as they truly are is destroyed. When the knowledge and vision of things as they truly are is absent, for one deficient in the knowledge and vision of things as they truly are, the supporting condition for disenchantment and dispassion is destroyed. When disenchantment and dispassion are absent, for one deficient in disenchantment and dispassion, the supporting condition for the knowledge and vision of liberation is destroyed. Just as, bhikkhus, when a tree is deficient in branches and foliage, its outer bark does not reach fullness, nor its inner bark, nor its sapwood, nor its heartwood reaches fullness; in the same way, bhikkhus, when mindfulness and clear comprehension are absent, for one deficient in mindfulness and clear comprehension, the supporting condition for moral shame and moral dread is destroyed. When moral shame and moral dread are absent, for one deficient in moral shame and moral dread… the knowledge and vision of liberation has its supporting condition destroyed.”

“Satisampajaññe, bhikkhave, sati satisampajaññasampannassa upanisasampannaṃ hoti hirottappaṃ. Hirottappe sati hirottappasampannassa upanisasampanno hoti indriyasaṃvaro. Indriyasaṃvare sati indriyasaṃvarasampannassa [T 4.0349] upanisasampannaṃ hoti sīlaṃ. Sīle sati sīlasampannassa upanisasampanno hoti sammāsamādhi. Sammāsamādhimhi sati sammāsamādhisampannassa upanisasampannaṃ hoti yathābhūtañāṇadassanaṃ. Yathābhūtañāṇadassane sati yathābhūtañāṇadassanasampannassa upanisasampanno hoti nibbidāvirāgo[P 4.0337]. Nibbidāvirāge sati nibbidāvirāgasampannassa upanisasampannaṃ hoti vimuttiñāṇadassanaṃ. Seyyathāpi, bhikkhave, rukkho sākhāpalāsasampanno. Tassa papaṭikāpi pāripūriṃ gacchati, tacopi… pheggupi… sāropi pāripūriṃ gacchati. Evamevaṃ kho, bhikkhave, satisampajaññe sati satisampajaññasampannassa upanisasampannaṃ hoti hirottappaṃ; hirottappe sati hirottappasampannassa upanisasampanno hoti…pe… vimuttiñāṇadassana”nti. Paṭhamaṃ.

“Bhikkhus, when mindfulness and clear comprehension are present, for one endowed with mindfulness and clear comprehension, the supporting condition for moral shame and moral dread is fulfilled. When moral shame and moral dread are present, for one endowed with moral shame and moral dread, the supporting condition for restraint of the senses is fulfilled. When restraint of the senses is present, for one endowed with restraint of the senses, the supporting condition for virtue is fulfilled. When virtue is present, for one endowed with virtue, the supporting condition for right concentration is fulfilled. When right concentration is present, for one endowed with right concentration, the supporting condition for the knowledge and vision of things as they truly are is fulfilled. When the knowledge and vision of things as they truly are is present, for one endowed with the knowledge and vision of things as they truly are, the supporting condition for disenchantment and dispassion is fulfilled. When disenchantment and dispassion are present, for one endowed with disenchantment and dispassion, the supporting condition for the knowledge and vision of liberation is fulfilled. Just as, bhikkhus, when a tree is endowed with branches and foliage, its outer bark reaches fullness, and its inner bark… its sapwood… its heartwood also reaches fullness; in the same way, bhikkhus, when mindfulness and clear comprehension are present, for one endowed with mindfulness and clear comprehension, the supporting condition for moral shame and moral dread is fulfilled. When moral shame and moral dread are present, for one endowed with moral shame and moral dread… the knowledge and vision of liberation has its supporting condition fulfilled.” The First.

2. Puṇṇiyasuttaṃ

2. The Discourse to Puṇṇiya

82. Atha [V 3.0158] kho āyasmā puṇṇiyo yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinno kho āyasmā puṇṇiyo bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “ko nu kho, bhante, hetu ko paccayo yena appekadā tathāgataṃ dhammadesanā paṭibhāti, appekadā na paṭibhātī”ti? “Saddho ca, puṇṇiya, bhikkhu hoti, no cupasaṅkamitā; neva tathāgataṃ dhammadesanā paṭibhāti. Yato ca kho[M 3.0152], puṇṇiya, bhikkhu saddho ca hoti, upasaṅkamitā ca; evaṃ tathāgataṃ dhammadesanā paṭibhāti. Saddho ca, puṇṇiya, bhikkhu hoti, upasaṅkamitā ca, no ca payirupāsitā…pe… payirupāsitā ca, no ca paripucchitā… paripucchitā [T 4.0350] ca, no ca ohitasoto dhammaṃ suṇāti… ohitasoto ca dhammaṃ suṇāti, no ca sutvā dhammaṃ dhāreti… sutvā ca dhammaṃ dhāreti, no ca dhātānaṃ dhammānaṃ atthaṃ upaparikkhati… dhātānañca dhammānaṃ atthaṃ upaparikkhati, no ca atthamaññāya dhammamaññāya dhammānudhammappaṭipanno hoti. Neva tāva tathāgataṃ dhammadesanā paṭibhāti.

82. Then the Venerable Puṇṇiya approached the Blessed One, paid homage to him, and sat down to one side. Seated thus, the Venerable Puṇṇiya said to the Blessed One: “Venerable sir, what is the cause, what is the reason why at times a teaching of the Dhamma occurs to the Tathāgata, and at other times it does not?” “Puṇṇiya, a bhikkhu may have faith, but he does not approach the Tathāgata; in that case, a teaching of the Dhamma does not occur to the Tathāgata. But when, Puṇṇiya, a bhikkhu has faith and also approaches the Tathāgata, a teaching of the Dhamma occurs to him. A bhikkhu may have faith and approach the Tathāgata, but he does not attend upon him… he attends upon him, but does not ask questions… he asks questions, but does not listen to the Dhamma with an attentive ear… he listens with an attentive ear, but having heard the Dhamma, he does not retain it… having heard the Dhamma, he retains it, but does not investigate the meaning of the teachings he has retained… he investigates the meaning of the teachings he has retained, but having understood the meaning and the teaching, he does not practice in accordance with the Dhamma. In that case, a teaching of the Dhamma does not occur to the Tathāgata.

“Yato ca kho, puṇṇiya, bhikkhu saddho ca [P 4.0338] hoti, upasaṅkamitā ca, payirupāsitā ca, paripucchitā ca, ohitasoto ca dhammaṃ suṇāti, sutvā ca dhammaṃ dhāreti, dhātānañca dhammānaṃ atthaṃ upaparikkhati, atthamaññāya dhammamaññāya dhammānudhammappaṭipanno ca hoti; evaṃ tathāgataṃ dhammadesanā paṭibhāti. Imehi kho, puṇṇiya, aṭṭhahi dhammehi samannāgatā samannāgato (sī. pī.), samannāgataṃ (syā. ka.) ekantapaṭibhānā ekantapaṭibhānaṃ (sabbattha) a. ni. 10.83 pana passitabbaṃ tathāgataṃ dhammadesanā hotī”ti. Dutiyaṃ.

“But when, Puṇṇiya, a bhikkhu has faith, approaches the Tathāgata, attends upon him, asks questions, listens to the Dhamma with an attentive ear, retains the Dhamma after hearing it, investigates the meaning of the teachings he has retained, and having understood the meaning and the teaching, practices in accordance with the Dhamma—in that case, a teaching of the Dhamma occurs to the Tathāgata. Puṇṇiya, for one endowed with these eight qualities, a teaching of the Dhamma occurs to the Tathāgata with unerring clarity.” The Second.

3. Mūlakasuttaṃ

3. The Discourse on the Root

83. a. ni. 10.58 passitabbaṃ “Sace, bhikkhave, aññatitthiyā paribbājakā evaṃ puccheyyuṃ – ‘kiṃmūlakā, āvuso, sabbe dhammā, kiṃsambhavā sabbe dhammā, kiṃsamudayā sabbe dhammā, kiṃsamosaraṇā sabbe dhammā, kiṃpamukhā sabbe dhammā, kiṃadhipateyyā sabbe dhammā, kiṃuttarā sabbe dhammā, kiṃsārā sabbe dhammā’ti, evaṃ puṭṭhā tumhe, bhikkhave, tesaṃ aññatitthiyānaṃ paribbājakānaṃ kinti byākareyyāthā”ti? “Bhagavaṃmūlakā no, bhante, dhammā, bhagavaṃnettikā bhagavaṃpaṭisaraṇā. Sādhu, bhante[V 3.0159], bhagavantaṃyeva paṭibhātu etassa bhāsitassa attho. Bhagavato sutvā bhikkhū dhāressantī”ti.

83. “Bhikkhus, if wanderers of other sects should ask you: ‘Friends, what is the root of all things? What is their origin? What is their source? What is their convergence? What is their chief? What is their sovereign? What is their superior? What is their essence?’—if asked thus, bhikkhus, how would you answer those wanderers of other sects?” “Venerable sir, our teachings have the Blessed One as their root, the Blessed One as their guide, and the Blessed One as their refuge. It would be good, venerable sir, if the meaning of this statement would be illuminated by the Blessed One himself. Having heard it from the Blessed One, the bhikkhus will retain it.”

“Tena hi, bhikkhave, desessāmi. Taṃ suṇātha, sādhukaṃ manasi karotha; bhāsissāmī”ti[T 4.0351]. “Evaṃ, bhante”ti kho te bhikkhū bhagavato paccassosuṃ. Bhagavā etadavoca – “sace, bhikkhave, aññatitthiyā paribbājakā evaṃ puccheyyuṃ – ‘kiṃmūlakā, āvuso, sabbe dhammā, kiṃsambhavā sabbe dhammā, kiṃsamudayā sabbe dhammā, kiṃsamosaraṇā sabbe dhammā, kiṃpamukhā sabbe dhammā[M 3.0153], kiṃadhipateyyā [P 4.0339] sabbe dhammā, kiṃuttarā sabbe dhammā, kiṃsārā sabbe dhammā’ti, evaṃ puṭṭhā tumhe, bhikkhave, tesaṃ aññatitthiyānaṃ paribbājakānaṃ evaṃ byākareyyātha – ‘chandamūlakā, āvuso, sabbe dhammā, manasikārasambhavā sabbe dhammā, phassasamudayā sabbe dhammā, vedanāsamosaraṇā sabbe dhammā, samādhippamukhā sabbe dhammā, satādhipateyyā sabbe dhammā, paññuttarā sabbe dhammā, vimuttisārā sabbe dhammā’ti, evaṃ puṭṭhā tumhe, bhikkhave, tesaṃ aññatitthiyānaṃ paribbājakānaṃ evaṃ byākareyyāthā”ti. Tatiyaṃ.

“In that case, monks, I will teach. Listen to it, apply your minds well; I will speak.” “Yes, venerable sir,” those monks replied to the Blessed One. The Blessed One said this: “Monks, if wanderers of other sects should ask you: ‘Friends, what is the root of all phenomena? What is their origin? What is their source? What is their convergence? What is their chief? What is their sovereign? What is their superior? What is their essence?’—if asked thus, monks, you should answer those wanderers of other sects in this way: ‘Friends, all phenomena have desire as their root. All phenomena have attention as their origin. All phenomena have contact as their source. All phenomena have feeling as their convergence. All phenomena have concentration as their chief. All phenomena have mindfulness as their sovereign. All phenomena have wisdom as their superior. All phenomena have liberation as their essence.’ If asked thus, monks, you should answer those wanderers of other sects in this way.” The third.

4. Corasuttaṃ

4. The Discourse on the Thief

84. “Aṭṭhahi, bhikkhave, aṅgehi samannāgato mahācoro khippaṃ pariyāpajjati, na ciraṭṭhitiko hoti. Katamehi aṭṭhahi? Appaharantassa paharati, anavasesaṃ ādiyati, itthiṃ hanati, kumāriṃ dūseti, pabbajitaṃ vilumpati, rājakosaṃ vilumpati, accāsanne kammaṃ karoti, na ca nidhānakusalo hoti. Imehi kho, bhikkhave, aṭṭhahaṅgehi samannāgato mahācoro khippaṃ pariyāpajjati, na ciraṭṭhitiko hoti.

84. “Monks, a great thief endowed with eight factors is quickly captured and does not last long. With which eight? He strikes one who is not striking; he takes everything, leaving no remainder; he kills a woman; he violates a maiden; he robs one who has gone forth; he plunders the royal treasury; he operates in the immediate vicinity; and he is not skilled in concealing what has been acquired. Monks, a great thief endowed with these eight factors is quickly captured and does not last long.”

“Aṭṭhahi, bhikkhave, aṅgehi samannāgato mahācoro na khippaṃ pariyāpajjati, ciraṭṭhitiko hoti. Katamehi aṭṭhahi? Na appaharantassa paharati[T 4.0352], na anavasesaṃ ādiyati, na itthiṃ hanati, na kumāriṃ dūseti, na pabbajitaṃ vilumpati, na rājakosaṃ vilumpati, na accāsanne kammaṃ karoti, nidhānakusalo ca hoti. Imehi kho, bhikkhave, aṭṭhahaṅgehi samannāgato mahācoro na khippaṃ pariyāpajjati, ciraṭṭhitiko hotī”ti. Catutthaṃ.

“Monks, a great thief endowed with eight factors is not quickly captured and lasts a long time. With which eight? He does not strike one who is not striking; he does not take everything, leaving no remainder; he does not kill a woman; he does not violate a maiden; he does not rob one who has gone forth; he does not plunder the royal treasury; he does not operate in the immediate vicinity; and he is skilled in concealing what has been acquired. Monks, a great thief endowed with these eight factors is not quickly captured and lasts a long time.” The Fourth.

5. Samaṇasuttaṃ

5. The Discourse on the Ascetic

85. “‘Samaṇo’ti[V 3.0160][P 4.0340], bhikkhave, tathāgatassetaṃ adhivacanaṃ arahato sammāsambuddhassa. ‘Brāhmaṇo’ti, bhikkhave, tathāgatassetaṃ adhivacanaṃ arahato sammāsambuddhassa. ‘Vedagū’ti, bhikkhave, tathāgatassetaṃ adhivacanaṃ arahato sammāsambuddhassa. ‘Bhisakko’ti, bhikkhave, tathāgatassetaṃ adhivacanaṃ arahato sammāsambuddhassa. ‘Nimmalo’ti, bhikkhave, tathāgatassetaṃ adhivacanaṃ arahato sammāsambuddhassa. ‘Vimalo’ti, bhikkhave, tathāgatassetaṃ adhivacanaṃ arahato sammāsambuddhassa. ‘Ñāṇī’ti, bhikkhave, tathāgatassetaṃ adhivacanaṃ arahato [M 3.0154] sammāsambuddhassa. ‘Vimutto’ti, bhikkhave, tathāgatassetaṃ adhivacanaṃ arahato sammāsambuddhassā”ti.

85. “Monks, ‘Ascetic’ is a designation for the Tathāgata, the Arahant, the Perfectly Enlightened One. Monks, ‘Brahmin’ is a designation for the Tathāgata, the Arahant, the Perfectly Enlightened One. Monks, ‘One who has gone to the end of knowledge’ is a designation for the Tathāgata, the Arahant, the Perfectly Enlightened One. Monks, ‘Physician’ is a designation for the Tathāgata, the Arahant, the Perfectly Enlightened One. Monks, ‘The Stainless’ is a designation for the Tathāgata, the Arahant, the Perfectly Enlightened One. Monks, ‘The Pure’ is a designation for the Tathāgata, the Arahant, the Perfectly Enlightened One. Monks, ‘The Knower’ is a designation for the Tathāgata, the Arahant, the Perfectly Enlightened One. Monks, ‘The Liberated’ is a designation for the Tathāgata, the Arahant, the Perfectly Enlightened One.”

“Yaṃ samaṇena pattabbaṃ, brāhmaṇena vusīmatā;

Yaṃ vedagunā pattabbaṃ, bhisakkena anuttaraṃ.

“That which is to be attained by an ascetic, by a brahmin who has completed the holy life; that which is to be attained by one who has gone to the end of knowledge, by the unsurpassed physician.

“Yaṃ nimmalena pattabbaṃ, vimalena sucīmatā;

Yaṃ ñāṇinā ca pattabbaṃ, vimuttena anuttaraṃ.

That which is to be attained by the stainless one, by the spotless pure one; that which is to be attained by the Knower, and by the unsurpassed liberated one.

“Sohaṃ vijitasaṅgāmo, mutto mocemi bandhanā;

Nāgomhi paramadanto, asekho parinibbuto”ti. pañcamaṃ;

I am one victorious in battle; being free, I free others from bondage. I am a Nāga, supremely tamed, one beyond training, fully extinguished.” The Fifth.

6. Yasasuttaṃ

6. The Discourse on Fame

86. Ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā kosalesu cārikaṃ caramāno mahatā bhikkhusaṅghena [T 4.0353] saddhiṃ yena icchānaṅgalaṃ nāma kosalānaṃ brāhmaṇagāmo tadavasari. Tatra sudaṃ bhagavā icchānaṅgale viharati icchānaṅgalavanasaṇḍe. Assosuṃ kho icchānaṅgalakā brāhmaṇagahapatikā – “samaṇo khalu bho gotamo sakyaputto sakyakulā pabbajito icchānaṅgalaṃ anuppatto icchānaṅgale viharati icchānaṅgalavanasaṇḍe[P 4.0341]. Taṃ kho pana bhavantaṃ gotamaṃ evaṃ kalyāṇo kittisaddo abbhuggato – ‘itipi [V 3.0161] so bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho…pe… sādhu kho pana tathārūpānaṃ arahataṃ dassanaṃ hotī’”ti.

86. On one occasion the Blessed One, while wandering on tour in the Kosalan country with a large Sangha of monks, arrived at a Kosalan brahmin village named Icchānaṅgala. There the Blessed One stayed in the Icchānaṅgala woodland grove. The brahmin householders of Icchānaṅgala heard: “Indeed, the ascetic Gotama, the son of the Sakyans who went forth from the Sakyan clan, has arrived at Icchānaṅgala and is staying in the Icchānaṅgala woodland grove. And a good report about that venerable Gotama has spread thus: ‘That Blessed One is an Arahant, a Perfectly Enlightened One... It is indeed good to see such Arahants.’”

Atha kho icchānaṅgalakā brāhmaṇagahapatikā tassā rattiyā accayena pahutaṃ khādanīyaṃ bhojanīyaṃ ādāya yena icchānaṅgalavanasaṇḍo tenupasaṅkamiṃsu; upasaṅkamitvā bahidvārakoṭṭhake aṭṭhaṃsu uccāsaddā mahāsaddā. Tena kho pana samayena āyasmā nāgito bhagavato upaṭṭhāko hoti. Atha kho bhagavā āyasmantaṃ nāgitaṃ āmantesi – “ke pana te, nāgita, uccāsaddā mahāsaddā kevaṭṭā maññe macchavilope”ti? “Ete, bhante, icchānaṅgalakā brāhmaṇagahapatikā pahutaṃ khādanīyaṃ bhojanīyaṃ ādāya bahidvārakoṭṭhake ṭhitā bhagavantaṃyeva uddissa bhikkhusaṅghañcā”ti. “Māhaṃ, nāgita, yasena samāgamaṃ, mā ca mayā yaso. Yo kho, nāgita, nayimassa nekkhammasukhassa pavivekasukhassa upasamasukhassa sambodhasukhassa nikāmalābhī assa akicchalābhī akasiralābhī. Yassāhaṃ nekkhammasukhassa pavivekasukhassa upasamasukhassa sambodhasukhassa [M 3.0155] nikāmalābhī nikāmalābhī assaṃ (bahūsu) a. ni. 5.30 passitabbaṃ. tattha hi ayaṃ pāṭhabhedā natthi akicchalābhī akasiralābhī, so [T 4.0354] taṃ mīḷhasukhaṃ middhasukhaṃ lābhasakkārasilokasukhaṃ sādiyeyyā”ti.

Then, when the night had passed, the brahmin householders of Icchānaṅgala, taking abundant hard and soft food, approached the Icchānaṅgala woodland grove. Having approached, they stood outside the gate-house, making a loud and great noise. At that time, the Venerable Nāgita was the Blessed One’s attendant. Then the Blessed One addressed the Venerable Nāgita: “Nāgita, what is that loud and great noise? I imagine it is fishermen quarreling over their catch.” “Venerable sir, these are the brahmin householders of Icchānaṅgala who, having taken abundant hard and soft food, are standing outside the gate-house for the sake of the Blessed One and the Saṅgha of bhikkhus.” “Nāgita, may I not have association with fame, and may fame not have association with me. Let him who is not an obtainer at will, without difficulty, and without trouble of this bliss of renunciation, this bliss of seclusion, this bliss of peace, and this bliss of awakening—of which I am an obtainer at will, without difficulty, and without trouble—relish that filthy pleasure, that drowsy pleasure, and that pleasure of gain, honor, and renown.”

“Adhivāsetu dāni, bhante, bhagavā. Adhivāsetu sugato. Adhivāsanakālo dāni, bhante, bhagavato. Yena yeneva dāni, bhante, bhagavā gamissati tanninnāva bhavissanti brāhmaṇagahapatikā negamā ceva jānapadā ca. Seyyathāpi[P 4.0342], bhante, thullaphusitake deve vassante yathāninnaṃ udakāni pavattanti; evamevaṃ kho, bhante, yena yeneva dāni bhagavā gamissati tanninnāva bhavissanti brāhmaṇagahapatikā negamā ceva jānapadā ca. Taṃ kissa hetu? Tathā hi, bhante, bhagavato sīlapaññāṇa”nti.

“Venerable sir, may the Blessed One now consent. May the Sugata consent. Now, venerable sir, is the time for the Blessed One’s consent. For wherever the Blessed One now goes, the brahmin householders of the towns and the countryside will be inclined towards that very place. Just as, venerable sir, when the sky rains with heavy drops, the waters flow towards the low-lying ground, even so, venerable sir, wherever the Blessed One now goes, the brahmin householders of the towns and the countryside will be inclined towards that very place. For what reason? Because, venerable sir, of the Blessed One’s virtue and wisdom.”

“Māhaṃ, nāgita, yasena samāgamaṃ, mā ca mayā yaso. Yo kho, nāgita, nayimassa nekkhammasukhassa pavivekasukhassa upasamasukhassa sambodhasukhassa nikāmalābhī assa akicchalābhī akasiralābhī. Yassāhaṃ nekkhammasukhassa pavivekasukhassa upasamasukhassa sambodhasukhassa nikāmalābhī akicchalābhī akasiralābhī, so taṃ mīḷhasukhaṃ middhasukhaṃ lābhasakkārasilokasukhaṃ sādiyeyya.

“Nāgita, may I not have association with fame, and may fame not have association with me. Let him who is not an obtainer at will, without difficulty, and without trouble of this bliss of renunciation, this bliss of seclusion, this bliss of peace, and this bliss of awakening—of which I am an obtainer at will, without difficulty, and without trouble—relish that filthy pleasure, that drowsy pleasure, and that pleasure of gain, honor, and renown.”

“Devatāpi [V 3.0162] kho, nāgita, ekaccā nayimassa ekaccā imassa (?) nekkhammasukhassa pavivekasukhassa upasamasukhassa sambodhasukhassa nikāmalābhiniyo assu idaṃ padaṃ katthaci natthi akicchalābhiniyo nikāmalābhiniyo akicchalābhiniyo (?) akasiralābhiniyo, yassāhaṃ nekkhammasukhassa pavivekasukhassa upasamasukhassa sambodhasukhassa nikāmalābhī akicchalābhī akasiralābhī. Tumhākampi tāsampi (?) kho, nāgita, saṅgamma samāgamma saṅgaṇikavihāraṃ anuyuttānaṃ viharataṃ anuyutte viharante disvā (?) evaṃ hoti – ‘na hi nūname na hanūname (sī. syā. pī.) āyasmanto imassa nekkhammasukhassa pavivekasukhassa upasamasukhassa sambodhasukhassa nikāmalābhino assu idaṃ padaṃ katthaci natthi akicchalābhino akasiralābhino. Yassāhaṃ nekkhammasukhassa pavivekasukhassa upasamasukhassa sambodhasukhassa nikāmalābhī akicchalābhī akasiralābhī. Tathā hi [M 3.0156] pana me āyasmanto [P 4.0343] saṅgamma samāgamma saṅgaṇikavihāraṃ anuyuttā viharanti’”.

“Indeed, Nāgita, even some deities are not obtainers at will, without difficulty, and without trouble of this bliss of renunciation, this bliss of seclusion, this bliss of peace, and this bliss of awakening—of which I am an obtainer at will, without difficulty, and without trouble. For you too, Nāgita, while dwelling devoted to coming together, assembling, and living in company, does this thought not occur: ‘Surely these venerables are not obtainers at will, without difficulty, and without trouble of this bliss of renunciation, this bliss of seclusion, this bliss of peace, and this bliss of awakening—of which I am an obtainer at will, without difficulty, and without trouble. That must be why these venerables dwell devoted to coming together, assembling, and living in company.’?”

“Idhāhaṃ[T 4.0355], nāgita, bhikkhū passāmi aññamaññaṃ aṅgulipatodakena aṅgulipatodakehi (sī. pī. ka.) sañjagghante saṅkīḷante. Tassa mayhaṃ, nāgita, evaṃ hoti – ‘na hi nūname āyasmanto imassa nekkhammasukhassa pavivekasukhassa upasamasukhassa sambodhasukhassa nikāmalābhino assu akicchalābhino akasiralābhino. Yassāhaṃ nekkhammasukhassa pavivekasukhassa upasamasukhassa sambodhasukhassa nikāmalābhī akicchalābhī akasiralābhī. Tathā hi pana me āyasmanto aññamaññaṃ aṅgulipatodakena sañjagghanti saṅkīḷanti’”.

“Here, Nāgita, I see bhikkhus laughing loudly and playing, poking one another with their fingers. To me, Nāgita, this thought occurs: ‘Surely these venerables are not obtainers at will, without difficulty, and without trouble of this bliss of renunciation, this bliss of seclusion, this bliss of peace, and this bliss of awakening—of which I am an obtainer at will, without difficulty, and without trouble. That must be why these venerables laugh loudly and play, poking one another with their fingers.’”

“Idha panāhaṃ idhāhaṃ (sī. pī. ka.), nāgita, bhikkhū passāmi yāvadatthaṃ udarāvadehakaṃ bhuñjitvā seyyasukhaṃ passasukhaṃ middhasukhaṃ anuyutte viharante. Tassa mayhaṃ, nāgita, evaṃ hoti – ‘na hi nūname āyasmanto imassa nekkhammasukhassa pavivekasukhassa upasamasukhassa sambodhasukhassa nikāmalābhino assu akicchalābhino akasiralābhino. Yassāhaṃ nekkhammasukhassa pavivekasukhassa upasamasukhassa sambodhasukhassa nikāmalābhī akicchalābhī akasiralābhī. Tathā hi pana me āyasmanto yāvadatthaṃ udarāvadehakaṃ bhuñjitvā seyyasukhaṃ passasukhaṃ middhasukhaṃ anuyuttā viharanti’”.

“Furthermore, Nāgita, I see bhikkhus who, having eaten as they wished to their belly's fill, dwell devoted to the pleasure of sleeping, the pleasure of lying on their side, and the pleasure of drowsiness. To me, Nāgita, this thought occurs: ‘Surely these venerables are not obtainers at will, without difficulty, and without trouble of this bliss of renunciation, this bliss of seclusion, this bliss of peace, and this bliss of awakening—of which I am an obtainer at will, without difficulty, and without trouble. That must be why these venerables, having eaten as they wished to their belly's fill, dwell devoted to the pleasure of sleeping, the pleasure of lying on their side, and the pleasure of drowsiness.’”

“Idhāhaṃ idha panāhaṃ (?), nāgita, bhikkhuṃ passāmi gāmantavihāriṃ samāhitaṃ nisinnaṃ. Tassa mayhaṃ, nāgita, evaṃ hoti – ‘idāni imaṃ idānimaṃ (katthaci) a. ni. 6.42 āyasmantaṃ ārāmiko vā upaṭṭhahissati paccessati (sī. pī.), upaṭṭhahati (ka.) samaṇuddeso vā[V 3.0163]. Taṃ tamhā so tamhā (ka. sī.), so taṃ tamhā (?) samādhimhā cāvessatī’ti. Tenāhaṃ[P 4.0344], nāgita, tassa bhikkhuno na attamano homi gāmantavihārena.

“Here, Nāgita, I see a bhikkhu dwelling near a village, sitting composed. To me, Nāgita, this thought occurs: ‘Now a monastery attendant or a novice will attend to this venerable one and dislodge him from that concentration.’ Therefore, Nāgita, I am not pleased with that bhikkhu’s dwelling near a village.”

“Idha panāhaṃ, nāgita, bhikkhuṃ passāmi āraññikaṃ araññe pacalāyamānaṃ nisinnaṃ. Tassa mayhaṃ, nāgita, evaṃ hoti – ‘idāni ayamāyasmā imaṃ niddākilamathaṃ paṭivinodetvā araññasaññaṃyeva manasi karissati ekatta’nti[T 4.0356]. Tenāhaṃ[M 3.0157], nāgita, tassa bhikkhuno attamano homi araññavihārena.

Here, Nāgita, I see a forest-dwelling monk sitting in the forest, dozing off. It occurs to me, Nāgita: 'Now this venerable one will dispel this weariness from drowsiness and attend exclusively to the unified perception of the forest.' Therefore, Nāgita, I am pleased with that monk's dwelling in the forest.

“Idha panāhaṃ, nāgita, bhikkhuṃ passāmi āraññikaṃ araññe asamāhitaṃ nisinnaṃ. Tassa mayhaṃ, nāgita, evaṃ hoti – ‘idāni ayamāyasmā asamāhitaṃ vā cittaṃ samādahissati samādahessati (katthaci), samāhitaṃ vā cittaṃ anurakkhissatī’ti. Tenāhaṃ, nāgita, tassa bhikkhuno attamano homi araññavihārena.

Here, Nāgita, I see a forest-dwelling monk sitting in the forest uncomposed. It occurs to me, Nāgita: ‘Now this venerable one will compose his uncomposed mind, or he will guard his composed mind.’ Therefore, Nāgita, I am pleased with that monk’s dwelling in the forest.

“Idha panāhaṃ, nāgita, bhikkhuṃ passāmi āraññikaṃ araññe samāhitaṃ nisinnaṃ. Tassa mayhaṃ, nāgita, evaṃ hoti – ‘idāni ayamāyasmā avimuttaṃ vā cittaṃ vimuccissati, vimuttaṃ vā cittaṃ anurakkhissatī’ti. Tenāhaṃ, nāgita, tassa bhikkhuno attamano homi araññavihārena.

Here, Nāgita, I see a forest-dwelling monk sitting composed in the forest. It occurs to me, Nāgita: ‘Now this venerable one will liberate his unliberated mind, or he will guard his liberated mind.’ Therefore, Nāgita, I am pleased with that monk’s dwelling in the forest.

“Idha panāhaṃ, nāgita, bhikkhuṃ passāmi gāmantavihāriṃ lābhiṃ cīvarapiṇḍapātasenāsanagilānapaccayabhesajjaparikkhārānaṃ. So taṃ lābhasakkārasilokaṃ nikāmayamāno riñcati paṭisallānaṃ, riñcati araññavanapatthāni pantāni senāsanāni; gāmanigamarājadhāniṃ osaritvā vāsaṃ kappeti. Tenāhaṃ, nāgita, tassa bhikkhuno na attamano homi gāmantavihārena.

Here, Nāgita, I see a monk dwelling near a village who obtains robes, almsfood, lodging, and medicinal requisites for the sick. Longing for gain, honor, and renown, he gives up seclusion and abandons remote forest and woodland dwellings; he resorts to a village, town, or royal city and takes up residence there. Therefore, Nāgita, I am not pleased with that monk’s dwelling near a village.

“Idha panāhaṃ, nāgita, bhikkhuṃ passāmi āraññikaṃ lābhiṃ cīvarapiṇḍapātasenāsanagilānapaccayabhesajjaparikkhārānaṃ. So taṃ lābhasakkārasilokaṃ paṭipaṇāmetvā na riñcati paṭisallānaṃ, na riñcati araññavanapatthāni pantāni senāsanāni. Tenāhaṃ, nāgita, tassa bhikkhuno attamano homi araññavihārena. [ ] etthantare pāṭho a. ni. 6.42 chakkanipāteyeva dissati, na ettha aṭṭhakanipāte

Here, Nāgita, I see a forest-dwelling monk who obtains robes, almsfood, lodging, and medicinal requisites for the sick. Having set aside that gain, honor, and renown, he does not relinquish seclusion, he does not abandon remote forest and woodland dwellings. Therefore, Nāgita, I am pleased with that monk’s dwelling in the forest.

“Yasmāhaṃ [T 4.0357][V 3.0164] yasmiṃhaṃ (katthaci), nāgita, samaye addhānamaggappaṭipanno na kañci passāmi purato vā pacchato vā, phāsu me, nāgita, tasmiṃ samaye hoti antamaso uccārapassāvakammāyā”ti. Chaṭṭhaṃ.

When, Nāgita, I am traveling on a long road and see no one ahead or behind me, at that time, Nāgita, I am at ease, even for the purpose of defecating and urinating.” The sixth.

7. Pattanikujjanasuttaṃ

7. The Discourse on Overturning the Bowl

87. cūḷava. 265 “Aṭṭhahi[M 3.0158], bhikkhave, aṅgehi samannāgatassa upāsakassa ākaṅkhamāno saṅgho pattaṃ nikkujjeyya nikujjeyya (ka.). Katamehi aṭṭhahi? Bhikkhūnaṃ [P 4.0345] alābhāya parisakkati, bhikkhūnaṃ anatthāya parisakkati, bhikkhūnaṃ avāsāya anāvāsāya (sī. syā.) parisakkati, bhikkhū akkosati paribhāsati, bhikkhū bhikkhūhi bhedeti vibhedeti (bahūsu), buddhassa avaṇṇaṃ bhāsati, dhammassa avaṇṇaṃ bhāsati, saṅghassa avaṇṇaṃ bhāsati. Imehi kho, bhikkhave, aṭṭhahaṅgehi samannāgatassa upāsakassa ākaṅkhamāno saṅgho pattaṃ nikkujjeyya.

87. “Bhikkhus, if the Saṅgha wishes, it may overturn the bowl for a lay follower who is endowed with eight qualities. With which eight? He strives for the non-acquisition of gains for bhikkhus, he strives for the disadvantage of bhikkhus, he strives for the non-residence of bhikkhus, he insults and reviles bhikkhus, he causes division between bhikkhus, he speaks in dispraise of the Buddha, he speaks in dispraise of the Dhamma, he speaks in dispraise of the Saṅgha. Bhikkhus, if the Saṅgha wishes, it may overturn the bowl for a lay follower who is endowed with these eight qualities.”

“Aṭṭhahi, bhikkhave, aṅgehi samannāgatassa upāsakassa ākaṅkhamāno saṅgho pattaṃ ukkujjeyya. Katamehi aṭṭhahi? Na bhikkhūnaṃ alābhāya parisakkati, na bhikkhūnaṃ anatthāya parisakkati, na bhikkhūnaṃ avāsāya parisakkati, na bhikkhū akkosati paribhāsati, na bhikkhū bhikkhūhi bhedeti, buddhassa vaṇṇaṃ bhāsati, dhammassa vaṇṇaṃ bhāsati, saṅghassa vaṇṇaṃ bhāsati. Imehi kho, bhikkhave, aṭṭhahaṅgehi samannāgatassa upāsakassa ākaṅkhamāno saṅgho pattaṃ ukkujjeyyā”ti. Sattamaṃ.

“Bhikkhus, if the Saṅgha wishes, it may turn the bowl upright for a lay follower who is endowed with eight qualities. With which eight? He does not strive for the non-acquisition of gains for bhikkhus, he does not strive for the disadvantage of bhikkhus, he does not strive for the non-residence of bhikkhus, he does not insult and revile bhikkhus, he does not cause division between bhikkhus, he speaks in praise of the Buddha, he speaks in praise of the Dhamma, he speaks in praise of the Saṅgha. Bhikkhus, if the Saṅgha wishes, it may turn the bowl upright for a lay follower who is endowed with these eight qualities.” Seventh.

8. Appasādapavedanīyasuttaṃ

8. The Discourse on Announcing Displeasure

88. “Aṭṭhahi, bhikkhave, dhammehi samannāgatassa bhikkhuno ākaṅkhamānā upāsakā appasādaṃ pavedeyyuṃ. Katamehi aṭṭhahi? Gihīnaṃ alābhāya parisakkati, gihīnaṃ anatthāya parisakkati, gihī akkosati paribhāsati, gihī gihīhi bhedeti, buddhassa avaṇṇaṃ bhāsati, dhammassa avaṇṇaṃ bhāsati, saṅghassa avaṇṇaṃ bhāsati, agocare ca naṃ passanti. Imehi [T 4.0358] kho, bhikkhave, aṭṭhahi dhammehi samannāgatassa bhikkhuno ākaṅkhamānā upāsakā appasādaṃ pavedeyyuṃ.

88. “Bhikkhus, if lay followers wish, they may announce their displeasure with a bhikkhu who is endowed with eight qualities. With which eight?
