He strives for the non-acquisition of gains for householders, he strives for the disadvantage of householders, he strives for the non-residence of householders, he insults and reviles householders, he causes division between householders, he speaks in dispraise of the Buddha, he speaks in dispraise of the Dhamma, he speaks in dispraise of the Saṅgha, and they see him in an improper resort. Bhikkhus, if lay followers wish, they may announce their displeasure with a bhikkhu who is endowed with these eight qualities.”

“Aṭṭhahi[V 3.0165][P 4.0346], bhikkhave, dhammehi samannāgatassa bhikkhuno ākaṅkhamānā upāsakā pasādaṃ pavedeyyuṃ. Katamehi aṭṭhahi? Na gihīnaṃ alābhāya parisakkati, na gihīnaṃ anatthāya parisakkati, na gihī akkosati paribhāsati, na gihī gihīhi bhedeti, buddhassa vaṇṇaṃ bhāsati, dhammassa vaṇṇaṃ bhāsati, saṅghassa vaṇṇaṃ bhāsati, gocare ca naṃ passanti. Imehi kho, bhikkhave[M 3.0159], aṭṭhahi dhammehi samannāgatassa bhikkhuno ākaṅkhamānā upāsakā pasādaṃ pavedeyyu”nti. Aṭṭhamaṃ.

“Bhikkhus, if lay followers wish, they may announce their pleasure with a bhikkhu who is endowed with eight qualities. With which eight? He does not strive for the non-acquisition of gains for householders, he does not strive for the disadvantage of householders, he does not strive for the non-residence of householders, he does not insult and revile householders, he does not cause division between householders, he speaks in praise of the Buddha, he speaks in praise of the Dhamma, he speaks in praise of the Saṅgha, and they see him in a proper resort. Bhikkhus, if lay followers wish, they may announce their pleasure with a bhikkhu who is endowed with these eight qualities.” Eighth.

9. Paṭisāraṇīyasuttaṃ

9. The Discourse on the Act of Reconciliation

89. cūḷava. 39 thokaṃ visadisaṃ “Aṭṭhahi, bhikkhave, dhammehi samannāgatassa bhikkhuno ākaṅkhamāno saṅgho paṭisāraṇīyakammaṃ kareyya. Katamehi aṭṭhahi? Gihīnaṃ alābhāya parisakkati, gihīnaṃ anatthāya parisakkati, gihī akkosati paribhāsati, gihī gihīhi bhedeti, buddhassa avaṇṇaṃ bhāsati, dhammassa avaṇṇaṃ bhāsati, saṅghassa avaṇṇaṃ bhāsati, dhammikañca gihipaṭissavaṃ na saccāpeti. Imehi kho, bhikkhave, aṭṭhahi dhammehi samannāgatassa bhikkhuno ākaṅkhamāno saṅgho paṭisāraṇīyaṃ kammaṃ kareyya.

89. “Bhikkhus, if it wishes, the Saṅgha may carry out an act of reconciliation against a bhikkhu who possesses eight qualities. What eight? He strives for the non-acquisition of gains by householders, he strives for the disadvantage of householders, he insults and reviles householders, he causes division between householders, he speaks in dispraise of the Buddha, he speaks in dispraise of the Dhamma, he speaks in dispraise of the Saṅgha, and he does not fulfill a lawful promise made to householders. Bhikkhus, if it wishes, the Saṅgha may carry out an act of reconciliation against a bhikkhu who possesses these eight qualities.”

“Aṭṭhahi, bhikkhave, dhammehi samannāgatassa bhikkhuno ākaṅkhamāno saṅgho paṭisāraṇīyakammaṃ paṭippassambheyya. Katamehi aṭṭhahi? Na gihīnaṃ alābhāya parisakkati, na gihīnaṃ anatthāya parisakkati, na gihī akkosati paribhāsati, na gihī gihīhi bhedeti, buddhassa vaṇṇaṃ bhāsati, dhammassa [P 4.0347] vaṇṇaṃ bhāsati, saṅghassa vaṇṇaṃ bhāsati, dhammikañca gihipaṭissavaṃ saccāpeti[T 4.0359]. Imehi kho, bhikkhave, aṭṭhahi dhammehi samannāgatassa bhikkhuno ākaṅkhamāno saṅgho paṭisāraṇīyakammaṃ paṭippassambheyyā”ti. Navamaṃ.

“Bhikkhus, if it wishes, the Saṅgha may revoke an act of reconciliation for a bhikkhu who possesses eight qualities. What eight? He does not strive for the non-acquisition of gains by householders, he does not strive for the disadvantage of householders, he does not insult and revile householders, he does not cause division between householders, he speaks in praise of the Buddha, he speaks in praise of the Dhamma, he speaks in praise of the Saṅgha, and he fulfills a lawful promise made to householders. Bhikkhus, if it wishes, the Saṅgha may revoke an act of reconciliation for a bhikkhu who possesses these eight qualities.” The ninth.

10. Sammāvattanasuttaṃ

10. The Discourse on Proper Conduct

90. cūḷava. 211 “Tassapāpiyasikakammakatena, bhikkhave, bhikkhunā aṭṭhasu dhammesu sammā vattitabbaṃ – na upasampādetabbo, na nissayo dātabbo, na sāmaṇero upaṭṭhāpetabbo, na bhikkhunovādakasammuti sāditabbā, sammatenapi bhikkhuniyo na ovaditabbā, na kāci saṅghasammuti [V 3.0166] sāditabbā, na kismiñci paccekaṭṭhāne ṭhapetabbo, na ca tena mūlena vuṭṭhāpetabbo. Tassapāpiyasikakammakatena, bhikkhave, bhikkhunā imesu aṭṭhasu dhammesu sammā vattitabba”nti. Dasamaṃ.

90. “Bhikkhus, a bhikkhu upon whom the Tassapāpiyasikakamma has been carried out should conduct himself properly in regard to eight matters: he should not give the higher ordination; he should not give dependence; a novice should not be made to attend on him; he should not consent to an appointment as an instructor of bhikkhunīs; even if appointed, he should not instruct bhikkhunīs; he should not consent to any appointment by the Saṅgha; he should not be placed in any position of authority; and he should not be the basis for another's rehabilitation. Bhikkhus, a bhikkhu upon whom the Tassapāpiyasikakamma has been carried out should conduct himself properly in regard to these eight matters.” The tenth.

Sativaggo catuttho.

The Chapter on Mindfulness, the Fourth.

Tassuddānaṃ –

The summary:

Satipuṇṇiyamūlena[M 3.0160], corasamaṇena pañcamaṃ;

Yaso pattappasādena, paṭisāraṇīyañca vattananti.

Sati, Puṇṇiya, and Mūla; Cora, and Samaṇa as the fifth; Yaso, Patta, and Pasāda; Paṭisāraṇīya and Vattana.

(10) 5. Sāmaññavaggo

(10) 5. The General Chapter

91-116. Atha [V 3.0167] kho ettha “atha kho”ti ca, “upāsikā”ti ca idaṃ aṭṭhakathāyameva dissati, na pāḷipotthakesu bojjhā bojjhaṅgā (ka. sī.) upāsikā ettha “atha kho”ti ca, upāsikā”ti ca idaṃ aṭṭhakathāyameva dissati, na pāḷipotthakesu, sirīmā, padumā, sutanā sudhanā (sī. pī.), sudhammā (syā.), manujā, uttarā, muttā, khemā, rucī rūpī (sī. pī.), cundī, bimbī, sumanā, mallikā[P 4.0348], tissā, tissamātā tissāya mātā (sī. pī.), soṇā, soṇāya mātā soṇamātā (syā.), kāṇā, kāṇamātā kāṇāya mātā (sī. pī.), uttarā nandamātā, visākhā migāramātā, khujjuttarā upāsikā, sāmāvatī upāsikā, suppavāsā koliyadhītā koḷiyadhītā (syā. pī.), suppiyā upāsikā, nakulamātā gahapatānī.

91-116. Then the female lay follower Bojjhā, Sirimā, Padumā, Sutanā, Manujā, Uttarā, Muttā, Khemā, Rucī, Cundī, Bimbī, Sumanā, Mallikā, Tissā, Tissā’s mother, Soṇā, Soṇā’s mother, Kāṇā, Kāṇā’s mother, Uttarā who was Nanda's mother, Visākhā who was Migāra’s mother, the female lay follower Khujjuttarā, the female lay follower Sāmāvatī, Suppavāsā the Koliyan daughter, the female lay follower Suppiyā, and Nakula’s mother, the householder's wife.

Sāmaññavaggo pañcamo.

The General Chapter, the fifth.

Dutiyapaṇṇāsakaṃ samattaṃ.

The Second Fifty is finished.

(11). Rāgapeyyālaṃ

(11) The Repetition Section on Passion

117. “Rāgassa[T 4.0360][M 3.0161][V 3.0168], bhikkhave, abhiññāya aṭṭha dhammā bhāvetabbā. Katame aṭṭha? Sammādiṭṭhi, sammāsaṅkappo, sammāvācā, sammākammanto, sammāājīvo, sammāvāyāmo, sammāsati, sammāsamādhi – rāgassa, bhikkhave, abhiññāya ime aṭṭha dhammā bhāvetabbā”ti.

117. “Bhikkhus, for the direct knowledge of passion, eight things are to be developed. What eight? Right View, Right Intention, Right Speech, Right Action, Right Livelihood, Right Effort, Right Mindfulness, and Right Concentration. Bhikkhus, for the direct knowledge of passion, these eight things are to be developed.”

118. “Rāgassa, bhikkhave, abhiññāya aṭṭha dhammā bhāvetabbā. Katame aṭṭha? Ajjhattaṃ rūpasaññī bahiddhā rūpāni passati parittāni suvaṇṇadubbaṇṇāni, tāni abhibhuyya ‘jānāmi passāmī’ti evaṃsaññī hoti. Ajjhattaṃ rūpasaññī bahiddhā rūpāni passati appamāṇāni suvaṇṇadubbaṇṇāni, tāni abhibhuyya ‘jānāmi passāmī’ti evaṃsaññī hoti. Ajjhattaṃ arūpasaññī bahiddhā rūpāni passati parittāni suvaṇṇadubbaṇṇāni, tāni abhibhuyya ‘jānāmi passāmī’ti evaṃsaññī hoti. Ajjhattaṃ arūpasaññī bahiddhā rūpāni passati appamāṇāni suvaṇṇadubbaṇṇāni, tāni abhibhuyya ‘jānāmi passāmī’ti evaṃsaññī hoti. Ajjhattaṃ [P 4.0349] arūpasaññī bahiddhā rūpāni passati nīlāni nīlavaṇṇāni nīlanidassanāni nīlanibhāsāni pītāni pītavaṇṇāni…pe… lohitakāni lohitakavaṇṇāni…pe… odātāni odātavaṇṇāni…pe… odātanibhāsāni, tāni abhibhuyya ‘jānāmi passāmī’ti evaṃsaññī hoti – rāgassa, bhikkhave, abhiññāya ime aṭṭha dhammā bhāvetabbā”.

118. “Bhikkhus, for the direct knowledge of passion, eight things are to be developed. What eight? Being percipient of form internally, one sees forms externally, limited, beautiful and ugly; having mastered them, one is percipient thus: ‘I know, I see.’ Being percipient of form internally, one sees forms externally, immeasurable, beautiful and ugly; having mastered them, one is percipient thus: ‘I know, I see.’ Not being percipient of form internally, one sees forms externally, limited, beautiful and ugly; having mastered them, one is percipient thus: ‘I know, I see.’ Not being percipient of form internally, one sees forms externally, immeasurable, beautiful and ugly; having mastered them, one is percipient thus: ‘I know, I see.’ Not being percipient of form internally, one sees forms externally that are blue, of blue color, of blue appearance, of blue lustre; or yellow, of yellow color…; or red, of red color…; or white, of white color… of white lustre; having mastered them, one is percipient thus: ‘I know, I see.’ Bhikkhus, for the direct knowledge of passion, these eight things are to be developed.”

119. “Rāgassa, bhikkhave, abhiññāya aṭṭha dhammā bhāvetabbā. Katame [T 4.0361] aṭṭha? Rūpī rūpāni passati, ajjhattaṃ arūpasaññī bahiddhā rūpāni passati, subhanteva adhimutto hoti, sabbaso rūpasaññānaṃ samatikkamā paṭighasaññānaṃ atthaṅgamā nānattasaññānaṃ amanasikārā ‘ananto ākāso’ti ākāsānañcāyatanaṃ upasampajja viharati, sabbaso ākāsānañcāyatanaṃ samatikkamma ‘anantaṃ viññāṇa’nti viññāṇañcāyatanaṃ upasampajja viharati, sabbaso viññāṇañcāyatanaṃ samatikkamma ‘natthi kiñcī’ti ākiñcaññāyatanaṃ upasampajja viharati, sabbaso ākiñcaññāyatanaṃ samatikkamma nevasaññānāsaññāyatanaṃ upasampajja [V 3.0169] viharati, sabbaso nevasaññānāsaññāyatanaṃ samatikkamma saññāvedayitanirodhaṃ upasampajja viharati – rāgassa, bhikkhave, abhiññāya ime aṭṭha dhammā bhāvetabbā”.

119. “Bhikkhus, for the direct knowledge of passion, eight things are to be developed. What eight? (1) One who has form sees forms. (2) Not being percipient of form internally, one sees forms externally. (3) One is resolved only on the beautiful. (4) With the complete transcendence of perceptions of form, with the passing away of perceptions of resistance, with non-attention to perceptions of diversity, percipient that ‘space is infinite,’ one enters and abides in the sphere of infinite space. (5) By completely transcending the sphere of infinite space, percipient that ‘consciousness is infinite,’ one enters and abides in the sphere of infinite consciousness. (6) By completely transcending the sphere of infinite consciousness, percipient that ‘there is nothing,’ one enters and abides in the sphere of nothingness. (7) By completely transcending the sphere of nothingness, one enters and abides in the sphere of neither-perception-nor-non-perception. (8) By completely transcending the sphere of neither-perception-nor-non-perception, one enters and abides in the cessation of perception and feeling. Bhikkhus, for the direct knowledge of passion, these eight things are to be developed.”

120-146. “Rāgassa[M 3.0162], bhikkhave, pariññāya…pe… parikkhayāya… pahānāya… khayāya… vayāya… virāgāya… nirodhāya… cāgāya… paṭinissaggāya…pe… ime aṭṭha dhammā bhāvetabbā”.

120-146. “Bhikkhus, for the full understanding of passion… for its complete destruction… for its abandoning… for its destruction… for its fading away… for dispassion towards it… for its cessation… for giving it up… for its relinquishment… these eight things are to be developed.”

147-626. “Dosassa…pe… mohassa… kodhassa… upanāhassa… makkhassa… paḷāsassa… issāya… macchariyassa [P 4.0350]… māyāya… sāṭheyyassa… thambhassa… sārambhassa… mānassa… atimānassa… madassa… pamādassa abhiññāya…pe… pariññāya… parikkhayāya… pahānāya… khayāya… vayāya… virāgāya… nirodhāya… cāgāya… paṭinissaggāya…pe… ime aṭṭha dhammā bhāvetabbā”ti.

147-626. “For hatred… for delusion… for anger… for resentment… for contempt… for rivalry… for envy… for avarice… for deceit… for craftiness… for obstinacy… for contention… for conceit… for arrogance… for vanity… and for negligence: for their direct knowledge… for their full understanding… for their complete destruction… for their abandoning… for their destruction… for their fading away… for dispassion… for their cessation… for giving them up… for their relinquishment… these eight things are to be developed.”

Rāgapeyyālaṃ niṭṭhitaṃ.

The Repetition Section on Passion is finished.

Aṭṭhakanipātapāḷi niṭṭhitā.

The Book of the Eights is finished.
